# Substrate capability and tractability: overall status

**Overall programme status (2026-10-06):** The measurement programme remains active. The capability map validates 28 areas, 422 source links, 72 linked test references, and 556 result-linked runners, with no unresolved source links or validation errors; generated JSON SHA-256 is `37754ba68845c4fd249192726047997f265f0e07648ae94c91b12b0bc7399e15`. Recent work strengthens bounded substrate mechanisms and self-change rehearsal, but current evidence still does not establish natural-task cheap-cut prevalence, broad tractability, general induction, or general self-improvement. The prospective ingress-frame and hotspot captures show that large out-of-frame traces combine first-pass materialization with recurring population work. A new 64/512-shard full-seed GenInfer comparison passes exact parity across 15 cases and 30 source-stable workers, with no reliable closure-cost difference; keep the 64-shard default. Native counters then show exactly 29,280 matrix attempts across N=4/16/64 at fixed B=5, while audit-enabled cost rises 16.15× from N=4 to N=64; candidate transformation and residual checking scan the relation set. A 27-worker bound × relation factorial confirms attempts follow the B fourth-power formula and GenInfer comparison units equal 4N per candidate; audit-enabled cost reaches 3.0 s at N=64/B=7. The implemented operator currently skips coordinate counts other than two, and natural bound prevalence remains unmeasured. The next steps are an exact-parity validator optimization comparison, a natural-task B/base census, a live whole-Substrate route, and continued thoughtexperiment settlement work.

**2026-10-06 indexed `AddNodeIfAbsent` lookup:** The synthetic source-locked ladder passes parity and integrity checks in 72 scale workers; warm closure medians improve 44.9–98.5% over the 12 population/lookup-count cells. At N=7,164/M=15, closure falls from 1.472 ms to 0.095 ms, with index build plus closure at 0.645 ms. The real full-seed unknown-word route preserves outputs, but its timing comparison is invalid because boot already creates the `Concept.name` index; a matching probe confirms this after 731 seeds and 10,794 Concepts. The implementation also raises CoW fork median from 0.220 ms to 3.007 ms at N=16,384/M=64. Treat this as a bounded mechanism result, not a live-route or general tractability result. Next make index state COW-friendly, finish semantic controls, and obtain a genuine live comparison. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ADD_NODE_IF_ABSENT_INDEXED_LOOKUP_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADD_NODE_IF_ABSENT_INDEXED_LOOKUP_V1_PREREG_20261006.md), and [boot probe](../../../evaluation/reports/add_node_if_absent_indexed_lookup_v1_20261006/live_index_presence_probe.json).

**2026-10-06 high-cardinality attribute-index COW follow-up:** `TypedGraph` now stores attribute indexes in nested sharded COW maps. The locked profile has one distinct value per node and confirms exact queries at 7,164, 16,384, and 50,771 nodes; shallow index clone medians are 30/30/40 ns against 0.530/1.089/5.326 ms for eager deep copies. The shallow timings approach measurement resolution, and this isolates clone cost only. The v3/v4 mutation panels now measure graph-level first-write cost and isolation; whole-Substrate fork/closure remains open. See [profile results](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_ATTR_INDEX_PROFILE_V2_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_ATTR_INDEX_PROFILE_V2_PREREG_20261006.md), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/typed_graph_cow_attr_index_profile_v2_20261006.json).

**2026-10-06 attribute-index mutation follow-up:** The source-locked v3 graph panel passes exact results and parent/sibling isolation across 12 cells and 21 paired repetitions. Shared fork-plus-write is 98.8–99.5% lower for attribute replacement/insertion and 61.9–81.9% lower for deletion/retyping than eager clone in this fixture. Deletion/retyping branch writes rise to 5.64–5.84 ms at 50,771 nodes because unindexing scans all value buckets. The next improvement is to use the node's before-image attributes to address only its relevant value buckets, then repeat the mutation gate and measure whole-Substrate fork/closure. See [v3 results](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_ATTR_INDEX_MUTATION_V3_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_ATTR_INDEX_MUTATION_V3_PREREG_20261006.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/typed_graph_cow_attr_index_mutation_v3_analysis_20261006.json).

**2026-10-06 targeted unindex v4:** The same locked 12-cell mutation matrix passes exact index results and parent/sibling isolation after deletion and retyping. Reading the node's before-image and removing only its matching value buckets cuts shared delete/retype write time 96.3–98.3% against v3 (50,771-node medians fall from 5.64–5.84 ms to 97–111 µs). Shared fork-plus-write is 98.7–99.5% below eager copy across the matrix. `by_type` vector removal still scales with typed population, and whole-Substrate closure remains unmeasured. See [v4 results](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_ATTR_INDEX_TARGETED_UNINDEX_V4_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_ATTR_INDEX_TARGETED_UNINDEX_V4_PREREG_20261006.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/typed_graph_cow_attr_index_targeted_unindex_v4_analysis_20261006.json).

**2026-10-06 ordered type-index COW v5:** `by_type` now uses COW-paged ordered slots plus NodeID positions, with tombstones and threshold compaction. All exact-order, NodeID reuse, compaction, index, and branch isolation gates pass. N=16,384 shared delete/retype writes are about 18% lower than v4, but at N=50,771 times remain about 100 µs, with no material deletion improvement and retype descriptively slower. Thus the vector removal was not the sole dominant cost. Value-shard copy amplification and node-page writes are candidates for direct attribution; whole-Substrate closure and memory remain open. The native extension was rebuilt and its engine provenance verified. See [v5 results](TYPED_GRAPH_ORDERED_TYPE_INDEX_COW_V5_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](TYPED_GRAPH_ORDERED_TYPE_INDEX_COW_V5_PREREG_20261006.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/ordered_type_index_cow_v5_analysis_20261006.json).

**2026-10-05 prospective pre-outcome frame capture:** Three locked workers record 21 outcome-blind ingress frames (6–9 nodes; state-graph induced-width upper bound 2) and complete post-checkpoint native rule traces. Capture integrity passes with no dropped rows or unmapped RuleUIDs, but each turn touches 7,204–11,814 distinct nodes outside the declared input cut, and the median trace contains 138,583 accesses. This does not establish locality: the message-root frame is only an ingress projection, not a complete task or factor interface. The positive held-answer utility gate also fails on all three “Can you tell me about copper?” turns. Next, hold the ownership rule fixed while expanding to independently sampled task families and reconciling native and host-side dependencies. See [results](SUBSTRATE_PREOUTCOME_TASK_FRAME_CAPTURE_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_PREOUTCOME_TASK_FRAME_CAPTURE_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/preoutcome_task_frame_capture_v1_analysis_20261005.json).

**2026-10-05 native hotspot attribution follow-up:** The per-prompt operation profiles reproduce across workers and source hashes match the capture's seed closure and native build manifest. The audit attributes 345,701 of 435,616 first-turn outside-cut accesses to three resident rules; adding the first-turn ShapeDecode ladder gives 402,758 (92.5%). `slang_variant_bind_denotes` scans 7,164 Concepts for each of 15 variants on the first turn; this candidate scan disappears on later turns. `curiosity_targets_ambivalent_nodes` and `gr_condition_lapse` each retain recurring population-wide work. Thus the prior broad access trace is partly first-pass materialization and partly ambient supervision, rather than a single large request boundary. This does not show those effects can be deferred. Next compare global closure with existing barrier/async per-area settlement on CoW branches, then address the missing request-priority residual interface only if full-settlement parity holds. See [hotspot results](SUBSTRATE_PREOUTCOME_TASK_FRAME_HOTSPOT_AUDIT_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md) and [machine analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/preoutcome_task_frame_hotspots_v1_analysis_20261005.json).

**2026-10-05 default-core termination-fragment census:** A source-locked read-only census of `boot_core()` finds 0/135 active rules accepted by the current finite-key insertion-only checker, including per-rule checks and an independent aggregate gate. 132 rules contain `where`; the active set also uses computed additions, deletion, attribute writes, and other effects excluded by the fragment. The result means the existing narrow termination bound cannot be extended to the default core by simply enabling that checker; it does not imply the core diverges. The next design choice is a broader proven admission class or an explicitly restricted profile. See [census results](SUBSTRATE_CORE_RULE_FINITE_KEY_ADMISSION_CENSUS_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md).

**2026-10-05 core seed-root follow-up:** A locked, read-only census of every one of the 17 `CORE_SEEDS` roots with dependency closure finds 13 nonempty profiles and zero profiles wholly admitted by that same finite-key fragment; four profiles are empty and not counted as candidates. An independent analyzer agreed on all 209 active-rule instances. Thus no existing core root selection supplies a useful restricted profile under the present checker. Rejections indicate that the sufficient fragment does not cover those rules, not that they fail to terminate. See [root-profile results](SUBSTRATE_CORE_SEED_PROFILE_ADMISSION_CENSUS_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md).

**2026-10-05 deferred patch effect attribution:** The `sc_canary_requests_commit` production seed rule now carries a uniquely sourced `ApplyRulePatch` effect index across queue drain; all 17 source-locked checks pass on a stale-patch fixture. Target-only attribution ranks the deferred source and identifies the curing omission in one branch, matching value tracing; full rematching needs two and blind search four. Target-only median is 0.839 ms / 3,521 report bytes versus 0.877 ms / 4,889 bytes for value trace and 1.013 ms for rematching, based on only three isolated reps per arm. Other deferred queues and multiple-origin convergence remain open. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_DEFERRED_PATCH_DIAGNOSIS_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md).

**2026-10-05 production-derived rule diagnosis:** The locked panel uses the four-edge `discourse_goal_resolve` seed rule and a single fault-injected outcome literal. An independent full graph/rule rematch finds one guarded binding; blind omission, rematching, target-only attribution, and value tracing all cure the injected error with exact equal branch graphs and no primary mutation. Value tracing selects effect 2 and takes one branch; the other approaches take three. Three fresh replicates per arm show 1.004 ms median value trace vs 1.134 ms blind and 1.257 ms target-only, too few runs for a speed claim. This is one authored fixture, not natural diagnosis prevalence; deferred effects remain unmeasured. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_PRODUCTION_RULE_DIAGNOSIS_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md).

**2026-10-05 Thoughtexperiment effect-target observer:** The target-only per-effect API compiles and passes 13 locked analysis checks across three workers and 27 isolated runs, including final-graph parity, effect-indexed target attribution, empty value arrays, and bounded truncation. At report cap it measures 326 ms / 163,672 KiB post-run RSS/PSS, between endpoint-only (231 ms / 118,048 KiB) and value tracing (600 ms / 276,292 KiB). Small-fixture medians are effectively tied with endpoint-only. This reduces but does not eliminate instrumentation cost; it is synthetic observer evidence, not natural-task diagnosis utility. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EFFECT_TARGET_COST_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md).

**Programme navigation (2026-10-05):** the remaining structural, natural-workload, learning, self-repair, and scaling evidence gates are consolidated in the [next-gates index](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_PROGRAMME_NEXT_GATES_20261005.md). It separates passed bounded results from open questions.

**2026-10-05 coupled-quadratic induction v1.3:** The source/native-locked 40-cell capture passes all 13 analysis gates. The enabled inducer acquires no target-compatible operator and makes no exact held-out transfer prediction on the new coupled quadratic relation in 0/10 runs; the validated linear control succeeds in 10/10. Every nonlinear observation adds one novelty `CapabilityAxis`; the five-entry `DimensionGenerator` codebook does not change. Transfer closure averages 1,909.989 ms enabled versus 0.420 ms frozen. Fixture-derived search cost is at least 781,248 matrix attempts per `GenInfer` evaluation on transfer versus 4,800 in training; actual call counts remain uninstrumented. This is evidence of a bounded-induction gap and costly search on this family, not evidence against all nonlinear learning. Next capture actual invocation/candidate counts and rejection stages. See [v1.3 results](SUBSTRATE_NONLINEAR_DIMENSION_INDUCTION_V1_3_RESULTS_20261005.md) and [search-cost analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_nonlinear_dimension_induction_search_cost_v1_20261005.json).

**2026-10-05 coupled-quadratic induction v1.5 instrumentation:** V1.5 closes the actual-call-count gap; its analysis passes all 17 gates. The enabled nonlinear transfer performs one GenInfer kernel call per run and tests 7,812,480 matrices across ten runs/20 eligible bases at B=12; all candidates fail residual validation. Training tests 48,000 at B=3. The linear control succeeds 10/10 and shows 20 cache hits from 40 requests during acquisition; frozen arms issue zero GenInfer requests. V1.4's substrate-Counters instrumentation was inert because rewrite effects do not wire the owning counter reference into EvalCtx; this failed measurement is preserved in its amendment. V1.5 uses the gated native scale audit and confirms both the search growth and the current linear-only operator acquisition boundary. Audit-enabled latency is not a clean counter-off performance estimate. See [v1.5 results](SUBSTRATE_NONLINEAR_DIMENSION_INDUCTION_V1_5_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_NONLINEAR_DIMENSION_INDUCTION_V1_5_PREREG_20261005.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_nonlinear_dimension_induction_v1_5_analysis_20261005.json), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_nonlinear_dimension_induction_v1_5_20261005.json), and [v1.4 amendment](SUBSTRATE_NONLINEAR_DIMENSION_INDUCTION_V1_4_AMENDMENT_20261005.md).

**2026-10-05 square-vs-cross dense-map morphology transfer:** The locked 12-worker, six-seed study passes all 14 capture and 10 independent-analysis gates. Both patch morphologies learn all nine trained sites and score 162/162 on trained probes. On held-out nearest-region classification, square patches score 288/288 while cross patches score 33/288; square wins all six paired seeds. Exact held-out coordinate localization is 0/288 for both. This is a strong effect for one authored map task, not semantic vision or precise coordinate inference; replication on another grid and patch scale remains open. See [results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_PHASE0_DENSE_MAP_MORPHOLOGY_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md).

**2026-10-05 event-value attribution baseline:** A locked three-worker probe uniquely reconstructs two direct-write changed fields from target events and before/after graph snapshots. In its deferred TrackIdentity fixture, three changed fields are ambiguous across 9,998 repeated events; closure reaches the 10,000-step budget in all execution modes. This is not a termination finding. It shows that settlement-level diffs do not recover ordered values for repeated deferred writes, so event-local before/after records with a memory cap are the next instrumentation gate. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EVENT_VALUE_ATTRIBUTION_BASELINE_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md).

**2026-10-05 bounded event-value attribution follow-up:** V1.1 stores raw before/after graph snapshots and independently derives the changed fields. All 14 analysis checks pass across three workers; ordinary, traced, and exact modes match. All eight changed fields in the three-step direct/deferred window uniquely map to one progressed event. The deferred window ends at its declared progress cap, so this demonstrates short-window attribution under non-overlap, not ordered values under long repeated writes. See [v1.1 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EVENT_VALUE_ATTRIBUTION_BASELINE_V1_1_RESULTS_20261005.md).

**2026-10-05 dense visual map and region generalization:** Six source-locked nine-unit workers acquired all nine sites on a 3×3 grid. Frozen held-out probes selected the unit assigned to the correct nearest trained-site region in 288/288 cases, while exact coordinate localization remained 0/288; winning fields stayed centered a median 2.83 cells from those between-grid probes. This supports discrete region generalization on the sampled geometry, not continuous interpolation. The raw runner's sole failed gate was a probe-count arithmetic defect (expected 225 instead of 75 probes/worker); the unchanged capture passes all corrected independent checks for its 450-probe matrix, activations, winners, provenance, and frozen weights. See [results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_PHASE0_DENSE_MAP_INTERPOLATION_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md) and [validation amendment](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_PHASE0_DENSE_MAP_INTERPOLATION_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261005.md).

**2026-10-05 six-seed four-site schedule replication:** Blocked training localized all four trained sites in every new seed (72/72 trained probes); one-step interleaving did so in two of six seeds, with partial coverage in three and none in one (39/72 probes). Both schedules scored 0/216 on strict held-out localization, while nearest-region correctness was 69/216 for each. Median native closure was 6.82 ms/step blocked and 6.70 ms interleaved; training input writes differed by schedule (63 vs 287,991 median). This replicates reliable blocked acquisition in this authored learner but confirms no held-out spatial interpolation. All 12 workers, 576 activation/winner reconstructions, source-lock and frozen-weight checks pass. See [replication results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_PHASE0_FOUR_SITE_SCHEDULE_REPLICATION_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md).

**2026-10-05 paired site-count × schedule result:** The four-site failure is schedule-dependent in this phase-0 task: blocked training localizes all four sites in all three seeds (36/36 probes), while one-step interleaving localizes 18/36; paired two-site controls pass under both schedules. Held-out interpolation remains 0/108 for four-site maps. Median closure remains 6.80–7.31 ms/step across the four cells. See [factorial results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_PHASE0_SITE_COUNT_SCHEDULE_FACTORIAL_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md).

**2026-10-05 four-site map scaling limit:** With four WUnits trained on four locations (4,000 exposures/site), only 20/60 trained-site probes localized and 0/180 held-out probes did; two seeds represented two training sites each and one represented none. Median rule-closure time remained 6.96 ms/step. This identifies a representation/retention failure in the tested regime, not a proven hard capacity bound. See [results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_PHASE0_FOUR_SITE_MAP_INTERPOLATION_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md).

**2026-10-05 spatial transfer boundary:** On three new seeds, the unseen X shape remained correctly localized at trained sites in 30/30 frozen probes, but transferred to two untrained sites in 0/30. Median winning-field distance at novel sites was 5.10 cells. This is consistent with location-specific receptive fields rather than zero-shot translation invariance in the tested two-site map. See [results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_PHASE0_NOVEL_SHAPE_LOCATION_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md).

**2026-10-05 frozen held-out-shape response:** After training on square/plus inputs at two positions, the correct location-specific WUnit won all 30 frozen probes using an unseen X morphology at those trained positions. Seen-shape controls also pass 30/30; recomputed weight dot products match native activations/winners for all probes, and exact receptive-field weights remain unchanged during testing. This is morphology tolerance at known locations, not shape classification or new-location transfer. See [v1.2 results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_PHASE0_HELDOUT_SHAPE_RESPONSE_V1_2_RESULTS_20261005.md); initial setup failures are disclosed there.

**2026-10-05 spatial feature-geometry transfer:** Under rapid alternation and eta 0.005, the learner localized both sites in 3/3 seeds for solid 5×5 controls and 3/3 for a mixed 3×3-square/five-cell-cross task. Exploratory captured receptive-field spreads matched the binary patterns’ analytic RMS spreads. This is spatial geometry tracking, not shape classification. See [results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_PHASE0_SPATIAL_FEATURE_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md).

**2026-10-04 eta-rescue held-out replication:** With three new seeds, eta 0.005 retained both locations in 3/3 workers on both the original and a held-out coordinate pair; baseline eta retained both in 2/3 on each. This supports a local rate effect beyond the initial seeds, but remains preliminary and within one feature family. See [held-out results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_PHASE0_ETA_HELDOUT_POSITIONS_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md).

**2026-10-04 plasticity-rate × switching interaction:** On a fixed one-step stimulus schedule with identical switch/write counts, two-location retention rose from 1/3 seeds at `ph0_eta` 0.0005–0.002 to 3/3 at 0.005. The 100-step baseline at 0.0005 also passed 3/3. This indicates a plasticity-rate contribution in the measured visual learner; the threshold is provisional at three seeds per condition. See [interaction results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_PHASE0_ETA_SWITCH_INTERACTION_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md).

**2026-10-04 phase-0 switching-dose follow-up:** In 15 source-locked runs, two-location retention was 1/3 at 1- and 10-step blocks and 3/3 at 100, 1,000, and 4,000 steps. Rule-closure timing stayed around 6.7–7.0 ms/step while input writes ranged from 399,975 to 75 per worker; this is a provisional temporal boundary with transition work still coupled to learning. See [dose results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_PHASE0_SWITCHING_DOSE_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md).

**2026-10-04 phase-0 interleaved-retention boundary:** At equal exposure counts, blocked training retained both locations in 3/3 seeds; stepwise interleaving did so in 1/3. Closure timing stayed near 7.1 ms/step, but interleaving caused roughly 400k input writes per worker versus 75 in blocked schedules. The result flags switching sensitivity while leaving forgetting versus input-transition effects unresolved. See [results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_PHASE0_INTERLEAVED_RETENTION_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md).

**2026-10-04 sequential visual-location retention:** Across six source-locked workers, three seeds crossed with both training orders, the native phase-0 learner acquires a second stable 5×5 burst location and retains the first in every case. Independent RF scoring finds 2.00-cell best-unit spread at both trained locations after stage two. Median native closure time is 7.21/7.30 ms per step for the two stages. This demonstrates two-pattern sequential acquisition/retention in one small visual family, not open-ended capacity or natural-image learning. The post-capture analyzer input-shape correction is disclosed in an amendment; raw data and thresholds are unchanged. See [v1 results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_PHASE0_SEQUENTIAL_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md).

**2026-10-04 phase-0 visual learning transfer:** A source-locked 12-case native rule panel crosses two fixed patch positions, three initialization seeds, and stable versus temporally shuffled activity with exactly matched active-cell counts. Stable inputs localize a learned receptive field in 6/6 cases; shuffled controls do so in 0/6. Median best-unit spread is 2.00 versus 6.48 cells. This supports one bounded visual learning mechanism under a controlled correlation contrast, not natural-image learning or general induction. See [v1 results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_PHASE0_PATTERN_LEARNING_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md).

**2026-10-04 phase-0 vision rule scaling:** A source-locked three-replicate panel scales an authored 16×16 phase-0 vision workload from 4 to 256 WUnits. Across the endpoints, candidate work grows 51.4×, Term work 48.5×, and uninstrumented median closure latency 41.9× for a 64× population increase. All audited twins match graph outcomes and progress counts, and all cases reach Argmax with one terminal cache entry. This is evidence of near-linear bounded scaling for this workload family, not a general asymptotic or natural-task claim. See [v1 results](SUBSTRATE_VISION_EYE_WUNIT_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md).

**2026-10-04 source-authored Argmax checkpoint transfer:** A source-locked phase-0 vision-eye run executes `ph0_wunit_winner` over four fixed-patch steps, records three Argmax evaluations, and captures one nonempty evaluator-generated Argmax entry. Two independent restores round-trip exactly; forced dirty no-op closure matches live aggregate/per-rule work and post-state graph/scheduler exactly. This closes one authored subsystem transfer gate while leaving speedup, natural prevalence, and scaling unmeasured. See [v1 results](SUBSTRATE_SCHEDULER_CACHE_VISION_EYE_ARGMAX_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md).

**2026-10-04 Argmax eligibility census:** A source-locked static pass classifies 204 of 327 executable seed Argmax sites as eligible under the current memoization whitelist, across 52 seed/rule pairs; 123 sites across 79 pairs are ineligible. Frequent exclusions include `NodesOfType`, `IfThenElse`, `Union`, and `SetT`. This turns the next transfer search from a broad census into a runtime validation of eligible source rules; `vision_eye.ph0_wunit_winner` is a candidate, not yet a runtime result. See the [census results](SUBSTRATE_ARGMAX_ELIGIBILITY_CENSUS_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md).

**2026-10-04 source-authored Argmax diagnostic:** The `vision_growth` rule path was exercised with four synthetic one-patch inputs; the Rust audit recorded 13 rule applications and four Argmax evaluations, but no persistent Argmax cache entry. Inspection shows the ranking expression includes `Dot`, outside `is_stable_graph_query`'s memoized subset, and Oja's graph writes clear graph-dependent caches. This rules out the vision WTA rule as a nonempty-cache checkpoint transfer case under current policy; it does not show a replay failure. The diagnostic is not source-locked and uses synthetic inputs. Next classify source-authored Argmax expressions for cache eligibility and choose a real workload that reaches an eligible expression, while keeping learning-rule cacheability as a separate design question. See the [cache coverage audit](SUBSTRATE_EVAL_CACHE_CHECKPOINT_COVERAGE_AUDIT_20261004.md).

## 2026-10-02 real-export inference evidence

The exact-inference envelope now includes a small connected factor graph built from all ten held-out inputs in one Qwen3-0.6B layer-12 mode export. Three fresh workers pass all 30 JT/independent-VE/direct-rescore comparisons at certified width 4, with mean dominant-mode sign agreement 0.75. This is evidence for correct inference integration on real model-derived weights; graph incidence remains authored and the result does not establish language-task utility, learned frame boundaries, or natural-task width prevalence. The next generalization gate is a preregistered model/layer/task sample with outcome-independent graph construction and task utility measured separately.

A second real-export study applies exact 3-state MAP to a learned L12→L13 transport graph. Across 60 worker-condition cells at width 8, exact MAP matches an independent VE oracle; learned-transport agreement is 0.800, versus 0.413 after shuffling, but equals BP and trails the linear baseline (0.863). Median exact time is 3.45 s with Python allocation tracing active. This extends exact inference evidence to a distinct connected graph family while showing no accuracy gain from exact MAP on this sample. It still does not measure natural frame discovery or task utility. See [transport census results](LLM_TRANSPORT_TASK_GRAPH_EXACT_CENSUS_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md).

The separated-cost follow-up measures the same width-8 family without allocation tracing on timed JT calls: median exact JT is 539.6 ms, BP 76.7 ms, graph build 2.35 ms, extraction 0.579 ms, and min-fill ordering 0.431 ms. Allocation tracing is sampled separately (12.61 MB median peak). This makes the prior instrumented cost discrepancy explicit; width-8 exact inference is feasible on this fixture, but exact MAP is roughly seven times slower than BP without a prediction advantage. These timings remain one-host, one-topology evidence. See [cost profile results](LLM_TRANSPORT_TASK_GRAPH_COST_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md).

A preregistered width sweep measures k={1,2,4,6,8,10} over all ten held-out transitions and three fresh workers. Certified width tracks k; exact JT rises from 35.7 ms at width 6 to 539 ms at width 8 and 7.13 s at width 10, with exact energies matching an independent exhaustive bipartite oracle for all 180 cases. This supports the expected treewidth cost dependence for this constructed dense family. It does not test the cheap-cut hypothesis on natural task graphs. See [width-scaling results](LLM_TRANSPORT_TREEWIDTH_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md).

At fixed size, a further 120-case panel prunes each L13 target to its strongest 1/2/4/8 incoming train-fitted edges. This creates a measured width profile of 1/2/5–6/8. Fan-in 1 or 2 has higher held-out internal sign agreement (0.863) than the dense fan-in 8 condition (0.800), while median exact JT falls from 541.5 ms to 0.153/0.399 ms. Exact and BP predictions match in all cells. This supports a topology/cost connection on this learned transport family, but edge pruning was explicit and task-graph cut prevalence remains unmeasured. See [fan-in topology results](LLM_TRANSPORT_TOPOLOGY_FANIN_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md).

The fan-in effect has now been repeated across four separately fitted adjacent transitions in the same Qwen3-0.6B layer chain, with 480 cases total. Certified widths and median times track fan-in at 1/2/4/8 edges per target: widths 1/2/4–7/8 and JT 0.156/0.397/11.08/535.7 ms. Exact MAP matches BP's prediction in every cell. Per-hop accuracy varies, and all four transitions share one model and corpus. This supports repeatability of the measured cost/topology relationship within one layer chain, while cross-model transfer and natural graph sampling remain open. See [chain replication results](LLM_CHAIN_TRANSPORT_FANIN_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md).

## 2026-10-02 learning handoff boundary measured

A four-worker paired run follows raw navigation errors into the current concern/grounding path. The task rule returns Down where Up is expected or Right where Left is expected. `induce_concern`, with no caller-supplied name hint or edit cue, mints `induced_actual_expected` and records `['actual','expected']`. `ground_concern` then refuses all four because these generic fields map to no held structural relation. A fresh matched positive-control context with the authored A8 cue builds the expected `flip_comparison` proposal in every worker while preserving the primary rule. This means observation capture and novel-axis creation are operational; the learned handoff from a raw discrepancy to task-relevant predicates is not. The next learning gate is to develop and evaluate residual grounding with ambiguous/noisy controls and false-proposal costs, not to claim autonomous self-improvement. See the [protocol](RESIDUAL_TO_COMPOSER_GROUNDING_BOUNDARY_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_residual_to_composer_grounding_boundary_v1.py), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_to_composer_grounding_boundary_v1_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 exact inference rerun against current native engine

The prior 36-worker exact-inference result remains reproducible against its 2026-09-29 lock, but live validation showed its native binary and build-manifest hashes had changed. The native source/build is currently fresh, so the same grid/q-tree experiment has been rerun under a new source lock. All 36 workers and ten gates pass exact extraction, width certificates, both MAP orders versus the independent oracle, direct returned-state energies, no mutation, and stable fingerprints. The q=5/6 grid false-refusal pattern repeats: min-fill estimates width above q in 6/6 grids at each width, while sparse partial q-trees are accepted in all cases. At q=5/6, grid JT median times are 112/135 ms and 200/279 ms (min-fill/certified order), versus 38/35 ms and 52/57 ms for q-trees. The exact JT routine runs in Python after native extraction. This is current-build evidence for two synthetic binary pairwise graph families; natural graph prevalence and other inference vocabularies remain open. See the [v1.3 protocol](CAN_EDGE_MATCHED_INFERENCE_COST_V1_3_PREREG_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_3_20261002_analysis.json), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_3_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 matched graph topology sensitivity

At 100,000 nodes and 400,000 unrelated edges, four layouts (cyclic, 100-hub, clustered, random-regular) were tested against four structural proposal families. All 48 trials selected the expected proposal and preserved primary rules. Three-replicate results included an actor-identity hub anomaly, so an eight-replicate follow-up was run for that family. Its medians are 17.10 ms cyclic, 17.91 ms hub, 17.41 ms clustered, and 17.96 ms random regular. Occasional tails reached 25.71 ms (hub) and 20.68 ms (random); evidence suggests a modest potential hub effect mixed with runtime variation. These are unrelated noise edges and do not measure inference when a queried relation itself has high degree. See [v1 protocol](STRUCTURAL_COMPOSER_TOPOLOGY_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [v1.1 follow-up](STRUCTURAL_COMPOSER_TOPOLOGY_V1_1_PREREG_20261002.md), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_structural_composer_topology_v1_1.py), and [captures](../../../evaluation/reports/structural_composer_topology_v1_20261002.json), [follow-up](../../../evaluation/reports/structural_composer_topology_v1_1_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 edge-density composer gate

The next graph-shape gate fixes 100,000 unrelated nodes and adds 0/100,000/400,000 directed typed edges. Across recurrence, reply, actor relation, and actor identity proposals, all 36 trials produce the expected proposal and preserve primary rules. Median composer latency shifts by at most 0.9 ms from zero to four unrelated edges per node. Edge insertion adds about 10.3 MiB RSS per 100,000 edges in the synthetic graph representation. This supports weak sensitivity to unrelated cyclic edges at these densities, not to graph structure generally: hub, cluster, and inference behavior remain open. See the [protocol](STRUCTURAL_COMPOSER_EDGE_DENSITY_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_structural_composer_edge_density_v1.py), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/structural_composer_edge_density_v1_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 native graph storage baseline

Native storage build throughput and memory now have a separate source-locked baseline. Across 100,000 and 1,000,000 nodes, median `add_node` throughput is 1.05–1.08 million nodes/s. Unchecked `add_edge_unchecked` throughput is 0.75–0.89 million edges/s for the tested one- and two-edge-per-node shapes. At one million nodes, process peak-RSS delta grows from 173.2 MiB (no edges) to 478.7 MiB (two million edges). These measurements isolate the Rust graph store's unchecked insertion path and include Python `NodeID` references plus process/runtime memory; checked edge admission and cognitive inference remain separate costs. See the [protocol](NATIVE_GRAPH_BUILD_SCALING_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_native_graph_build_scaling_v1.py), and [raw results](../../../evaluation/reports/native_graph_build_scaling_v1_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 one-million-node composer result

The four-family prompted composer panel now has a million-node checkpoint. Twelve of twelve trials at 1,000,000 unrelated nodes select the preregistered proposal and preserve the primary active rules. Median composition is 16.62–30.35 ms by family; one of three reply trials is 44.69 ms, with the other two near 30 ms. Insertion increases current RSS by about 143.2–143.9 MiB, or roughly 150 bytes per simple node in this process measurement. Composer latency remains near the lower-scale range for these cases. This is useful bounded CPU/graph evidence, while million-node graph construction throughput, precise/peak memory, edge-density scaling, broader rule-count effects, 10⁷–10⁸-node runs, and natural-task locality remain open. See the [v1.3 protocol](STRUCTURAL_COMPOSER_FAMILY_SCALING_V1_3_PREREG_20261002.md), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_structural_composer_family_scaling_v1_3.py), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/structural_composer_family_scaling_v1_3_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 100,000-node composer scaling extension

The four-family source-locked panel extends the unrelated-node axis to 100,000. All 48 trials across four proposal families and four graph sizes select the expected target/operator/site and preserve the primary active rules. Median latency shifts by less than 1 ms from the zero-noise baseline in each family (one family is slightly faster, within runtime noise). Process RSS grows by a median 13.2–13.9 MiB while inserting 100,000 nodes. This is evidence for stable prompted-composer latency through 100,000 unrelated nodes across these four cases; it does not establish million-node performance, general natural-task locality, or exact graph memory cost. See the [v1.2 protocol](STRUCTURAL_COMPOSER_FAMILY_SCALING_V1_2_PREREG_20261002.md), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_structural_composer_family_scaling_v1_2.py), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/structural_composer_family_scaling_v1_2_20261002.json).

The current capability-map rebuild/check also passes: 28 rows, 397 resolved source links, 72 linked test references, 546 result-linked runners, and no unresolved links or validation errors. This is inventory consistency evidence, not a completeness or test-coverage claim.

## 2026-10-02 broader composition and memory panel

The source-locked scaling panel now spans four known structural proposal families and 0/1,000/10,000 unrelated nodes, with three fresh-process trials per cell. All 36 trials selected the preregistered proposal and preserved the primary active rules. Family medians move by roughly 0.3–1.6 ms from 0 to 10,000 nodes. V1.1 measures RSS around both noise-node insertion and composition; insertion-phase median RSS deltas at 10,000 nodes are 240–900 KiB, while 1,000-node deltas are often hidden within already resident pages. This is coarse RSS evidence, not precise object-level memory accounting. The results strengthen a bounded claim of low latency sensitivity to unrelated nodes across these four prompted cases, while autonomous residual discovery, larger graphs, broader task coverage, and reliable peak-memory scaling remain open. See [protocol](STRUCTURAL_COMPOSER_FAMILY_SCALING_V1_1_PREREG_20261002.md), [analysis runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_structural_composer_family_scaling_v1_1.py), and [raw results](../../../evaluation/reports/structural_composer_family_scaling_v1_1_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 full structural composer scaling result

The complete prompted composer has now been measured on the same unrelated-node scale axis. The corrected source-locked v1.1 run warmups each cell and shuffles order; every one of 15 trials selected the expected `actor+verb` repair and left the active primary rule store unchanged. Median latency was 15.28 ms at 0 noise nodes, 15.67 ms at 1,000, and 15.93 ms at 10,000; one 10,000-node trial took 24.13 ms. The first v1 run's zero-noise cell included a first-use penalty, so only its diagnostic observation is retained. This is evidence for one prompted rule on one host, not a broad complexity claim. Memory scaling, more rule families, and autonomous residual discovery remain open. See the [v1.1 preregistration](FULL_STRUCTURAL_COMPOSER_GRAPH_SCALING_V1_1_PREREG_20261002.md), [analysis runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_full_structural_composer_graph_scaling_v1_1.py), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/full_structural_composer_graph_scaling_v1_1_20261002.json).

## 2026-10-02 graph-resident structural-site scaling update

The next scaling layer is measured. In a locked 3×3 grid, `_ensure_edge_identity_sites` created the exact expected candidates for 8/32/64-field synthetic signatures in graphs with 0/1,000/10,000 unrelated nodes. At widths 32 and 64, median materialization varies by at most 1.9% across that graph-size range; the timings are about 10 ms and 41 ms, respectively. Width 8 remains sub-millisecond but noisier. This indicates that typed site insertion cost is primarily driven by candidate count in these cases and is not strongly affected by unrelated node count. Complete composer cost, native selection under these loads, and memory remain unmeasured. The source-locked runner, analyzer, protocol, and raw data are linked from the [capability map](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_MAP.md).

## 2026-10-02 edge-identity extraction scaling update

The attribute-scoped edge-identity site generator had quadratic candidate count but repeated list-based duplicate checks. That avoidable cost is now set-backed. A source-locked nine-point single-host microbenchmark (1–256 direct bound fields) matches predicted candidate counts throughout; median extraction is 1.39 ms at 64 fields and 45.5 ms at 256 fields. The measurement is limited to candidate extraction on synthetic signatures. It does not yet answer graph-resident `EditSite` materialization, native Term selection, full composer cost, or unrelated graph-size sensitivity. The broader goal remains active. See the [scaling preregistration](EDGE_IDENTITY_SITE_SCALING_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [results](../../../evaluation/reports/edge_identity_site_scaling_v1_20261002.json), and [updated capability map](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_MAP.md).

## Warm live-route CPU attribution update (2026-10-02)

The thread-limit mystery is narrowed. A diagnostic-only native getter confirms the live Rayon pool has 24 workers by default and 1 under `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1`. The target conversation adds 23 OS threads under either setting. A separate locked caller-thread split places only 85–86 ms of the roughly 1.64–1.68 s target process CPU on the caller; 94.8% is aggregate CPU from other process threads in both arms. Wall time remains about 111 ms. This is strong evidence of off-caller concurrent work, but not an attribution to a rule, Rayon, or a particular scheduler. The next evaluation must measure per-thread CPU and task/matcher work during the call, identify the thread producer, then intervene on that producer under exact semantic parity. See [Rayon-pool results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_WARM_CONVERSE_RAYON_POOL_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [caller-CPU results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_WARM_CONVERSE_CALLER_CPU_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_warm_converse_caller_cpu_v1_raw_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_warm_converse_caller_cpu_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## Capability inventory evidence-link refresh (2026-10-02)

The source-linked capability map now validates 28 rows, 397 resolved source references, 72 linked test references, 298 result documents, 208 protocol/lock references, 366 raw/analysis records, and 587 linked runners/analyzers. No capability row is missing a test link, unresolved source link, or required evidence field. The visual-ingress and planning rows now point to existing functional tests, with explicit limits on what those tests exercise. Link presence establishes navigability, not assertion relevance or executed coverage; the map continues to mark coverage as unaudited. The machine-readable mirror passes `scripts/build_substrate_capability_map.py --check` with zero validation errors.

**A8-to-Thoughtexperiment bridge gate (2026-10-02):** v1 and v1.1 stopped before scoring because the graph path fell back to Python. v1.2's canonical clause comparison fixed source attribution, verified all three composer Rules after firing, and rejected four controlled clause mutations; its scoring attempt then exposed a runner source-control unpacking defect before persisting outcomes. v1.3 corrected that defect and passed order attribution/abstention gates. Its graph proposals passed both adjacent held-out directions, but the preregistered raw digest-equivalence prediction failed because the digest retained engine metadata. v1.4 preregistered a semantic projection excluding only generated rule metadata and evaluated eight previously unscored cardinal positions at offsets 2 and 4. All analyzer gates passed: graph proposals pass 32/32 worker/state outcomes, semantic candidate identity and receipts pass, primaries remain unchanged, and no-cue graph calls abstain. Exactly one of six host candidates succeeds per worker. Median graph selection remains slower than default order (140.3 ms vs 79.1 ms), so the proposal has no measured economic advantage. The workers are isolated repetitions of one authored rule/coordinate fixture, not independent tasks. This is bounded cue-conditioned proposal generalization, not autonomous diagnosis or self-improvement. Next gates are obstacle/topology variation, new target rules and task families, broader proposal coverage, and measuring learning/adoption without caller-supplied cues or oracles. See [v1.4 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_A8_PROPOSAL_BRIDGE_V1_4_RESULTS_20261002.md), [v1.3 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_A8_PROPOSAL_BRIDGE_V1_3_RESULTS_20261002.md), [v1.3 analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_a8_proposal_bridge_v1_3_analysis_20261002.json), and [v1.4 analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_a8_proposal_bridge_v1_4_analysis_20261002.json).

## Thoughtexperiment harness status (2026-10-02)

The thoughtexperiment harness now demonstrates isolated candidate execution over shared primary state, exact branch residual production, and explicit revision-checked adoption. In a productive synthetic fixture, 225 branches across eager-copy, direct shared-page, and reusable-snapshot backends produce identical nonempty residuals while leaving the primary unchanged. At 50k unrelated nodes, reusable snapshots reduce the five-branch batch from 60.1 ms eager to 1.06 ms including capture (about 57×); the distinct full-harness six-candidate benchmark shows a smaller 1.46–1.51× batch gain over standalone at 50k for shared-page/reusable backends. A separate 15-worker adoption panel accepts exact receipts, rejects stale revisions and corrupted before-images atomically, and preserves full-primary graph parity. High-churn and degree/placement panels also show lower allocator use for shared snapshots across their tested synthetic cells. These are substantial mechanism and controlled-throughput results, not general task capability or a universal speedup law. See [productive residual results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_PRODUCTIVE_RESIDUAL_SNAPSHOT_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [residual adoption results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RESIDUAL_ADOPTION_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [reusable batch results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_REUSABLE_SNAPSHOT_BATCH_V2_RESULTS_20261001.md), and [high-churn lifecycle results](COW_HIGH_CHURN_LIFECYCLE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md).

**Four-family proposal-utility panel (2026-10-02):** 16 worker/family order records were frozen before held-out scoring; source fingerprints remained stable, order files contain no held-out labels, and all five arm orders are complete candidate permutations. All 80 policy cells eventually find a receipt-verified candidate while preserving primary state. Experience rank is better than zero-history ranking on first-choice utility (8/16 vs 0/16), but unchanged default order and target memoization succeed first in all 16 cells. Experience ranking carries a median 3.63 s selection cost, mostly training-record acquisition; default order is 37.9 ms. The preregistered exhaustive held-out outcome gate failed because trials stopped at the first success: only 70/104 candidate instances were evaluated. Treat the panel as descriptive and non-confirmatory; do not infer an economic benefit or self-improvement. A confirmatory rerun needs exhaustive held-out candidate scoring before analysis. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_BLINDED_PROPOSAL_UTILITY_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [analyzer](../../../scripts/analyze_thoughtexperiment_blinded_proposal_utility_v1.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_blinded_proposal_utility_v1_analysis_20261002.json), [frozen orders](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_blinded_proposal_utility_v1_orders_20261002.json), and [scores/cost ledger](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_blinded_proposal_utility_v1_scores_20261002.json).

**Confirmatory four-family rerun (2026-10-02):** v1.1 repaired held-out coverage but did not lock its analyzer hash, so it is retained as a descriptive replication. v1.2 includes the analyzer hash in the pre-capture lock and passes all seven analyzer gates: stable locked sources, complete blinded orders, exact score binding, full 104/104 candidate coverage, valid receipts and primary isolation, complete policy prefixes, and reconciled selection/audit costs. Experience rank reaches 8/16 first-choice successes versus 0/16 frozen; default and target memoization each reach 16/16. Median policy-selection cost is 3.64 s for experience versus 36.4 ms default, including required training acquisition. The selection signal is real on this authored candidate set, but there is no advantage over the strongest controls and no economic benefit. Candidate enumeration and oracles remain host-authored; this does not demonstrate self-improvement. See [v1.2 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_BLINDED_PROPOSAL_UTILITY_V1_2_RESULTS_20261002.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_blinded_proposal_utility_v1_2_lock_20261002.json), [orders](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_blinded_proposal_utility_v1_2_orders_20261002.json), [scores](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_blinded_proposal_utility_v1_2_scores_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_blinded_proposal_utility_v1_2_analysis_20261002.json).

**Independent adequacy receipt at code-shape adoption (2026-10-02):** `teach_write_shape_from_example` now has strict opt-in receipt enforcement. A locked three-process panel through full-seeded `WorldAdapter` calls passed all 18 checks: rejected, ambiguous, incomplete, invalid-provenance, and missing receipts caused no code-shape or epoch growth; the matching accepted receipt added exactly one shape and advanced the epoch once in every worker. Median full-seed boot was 17.57 s, refusal paths 3.4–5.8 ms, and accepted teaching/adoption 32.7 ms. The initial incomplete-evidence fixture was corrected under an explicit amendment and a refreshed source lock. This is authored single-function integration evidence; the external labels are harness-supplied and their identity/truth is not authenticated. Legacy calls remain ungated unless the strict option is enabled. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_RECEIPT_ADOPTION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_RECEIPT_ADOPTION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_RECEIPT_ADOPTION_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_receipt_adoption_v1_analysis_20261002.json), and the implementation in [`code_write_growth.py`](../../../domains/code_write_growth.py).

**Receipt persistence/replay and teach idempotence (2026-10-02):** a locked three-pair process panel shows an accepted candidate survives persistence and a full-seed restart with exact shape-attribute parity. Replaying its receipt succeeds without duplicate live nodes or seed entries; altered source or request bindings are refused. The first capture found a duplicate live shape despite a stable epoch and seed file; generic [`teach()`](../../../domains/teach.py) now reuses exact `(type, attrs)` matches. The amended panel passes 21/21 checks. Current medians are 16.67/19.42 s writer/reader boot, 59.5 ms durable teach/write, and 32.8 ms restored-graph replay. This is one authored function; replay is reusable and candidate-bound, not authenticated or one-shot. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_RECEIPT_PERSISTENCE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [amendments](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_RECEIPT_PERSISTENCE_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md) and [v1.2 performance amendment](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_RECEIPT_PERSISTENCE_V1_2_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [diagnostic capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_receipt_persistence_v1_initial_diagnostic_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_receipt_persistence_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

**Selective information-commitment rule read (2026-10-02):** profiling traced the multi-second durable-teach path to `_ensure_loaded()` exporting and JSON-decoding all 2,357 native rules to check for one gate. It now uses the existing native prefix export with a compatibility fallback. The amended, source-locked process panel still passes persistence/restart/replay; durable teach plus disk write measures 59.5 ms median. Same-substrate diagnostics measure 3.36 s for full export and 180.7 ms median for prefix export, with substantial order sensitivity (0.155 ms prefix-first, 180.7–224.5 ms after the full export). The earlier 3.315 s writer measurement is from a capture that failed the replay-idempotence gate and did not directly hash this Python source, so it is context rather than a formal paired speedup estimate. See the [performance amendment](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_RECEIPT_PERSISTENCE_V1_2_AMENDMENT_20261002.md) and [confirmatory raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_receipt_persistence_v1_raw_20261002.json).

**Receipt-gated code-write transfer (2026-10-02):** a fresh three-worker source-locked panel applies the strict receipt gate to parity and clamp example acquisition and an identity-as-square negative control. Both accepted Terms and emitted Python transfer exactly across 183 held-out inputs; the identity candidate agrees on its single acquisition example but fresh square probes reject it without codebook growth. Labels are recomputed by a distinct source-hashed oracle module, improving provenance traceability without authenticating an external oracle. Median teach/adoption is 21.6–22.2 ms; full-seed boot is 16.68 s. This broadens evidence from one example to two positive authored task families, while remaining example-supervised and non-naturalistic. See [results](SUBSTRATE_INDEPENDENT_ADEQUACY_CODEWRITE_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_INDEPENDENT_ADEQUACY_CODEWRITE_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_independent_adequacy_codewrite_transfer_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

**Adequacy-probe coverage boundary (2026-10-02):** in three matched gated/ungated repetitions, both arms use the same identity candidate, one `x=0` acquisition example, and identical source-hashed oracle evidence. With only fresh `x=1`, the strict gate accepts the false identity-as-square candidate in 3/3 runs; both arms then score 0/4 on `x=2..5`. With fresh probes from `{1,2,3,4}`, the receipt exposes the mismatch: strict mode refuses in 3/3, while ungated mode still adopts and scores 0/4. The 29-check panel thus shows the actual guard's value and its finite-domain limit: acceptance is relative to the caller-provided probe domain. It does not estimate how often naturally selected domains are inadequate. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_PROBE_COVERAGE_AB_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_PROBE_COVERAGE_AB_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_probe_coverage_ab_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

For general ability, the current system can rehearse and rank authored candidate rules, test convergence against task-specific oracles, return receipts, and in selected paths commit those receipts without silently changing the primary during rehearsal. The v1.2 four-family panel passes its source, blinding, exhaustive held-out scoring, receipt, isolation, and cost-reconciliation gates. Its graph-resident ranker beats zero-history ranking on 8/16 first choices, but default ordering and target memoization succeed on 16/16 and experience ranking has substantially higher measured selection cost. This establishes a bounded ranking response, not a useful general search advantage. Concurrent throughput is backend- and workload-dependent: shared-page recurrence repair reaches 1.64–1.75× sequential throughput at fanout 8, while a shape-decoding path initially regresses under concurrency and improves only after targeted native binding changes; other task-shaped receipt routes remain slower than direct execution. The A8 bridge v1.4 passes all gates on eight new held-out cardinal displacements across four isolated workers, with matching semantic identity, receipts, primary isolation, and no-cue abstention. Graph selection remains slower than host default, and the fixture retains one rule family without obstacle variation. This is narrow proposal generalization, not autonomous diagnosis or self-improvement. Later gates still need task/topology diversity, new target rules, semantic correctness without supplied contracts/oracles, lifecycle/cancellation and concurrent-adoption coverage, and independent-host replication. See [v1.4 bridge results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_A8_PROPOSAL_BRIDGE_V1_4_RESULTS_20261002.md), [v1.2 panel results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_BLINDED_PROPOSAL_UTILITY_V1_2_RESULTS_20261002.md), [navigation repair results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_NAVIGATION_POLARITY_REPAIR_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [A8 structural-composition results](MAC_FAC_A8_RESULTS.md), [recurrence parallel results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_PARALLEL_WAVE_V2_RESULTS_20261001.md), and [task-generated receipt results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_TASK_GENERATED_RECEIPT_TRANSFER_V1_1_RESULTS_20261001.md).

## Active query selection optimization (2026-10-02)

Following negative memoization results, an eager sequence-local pure-Term prediction matrix was measured at 1.78–1.87× median speedup on three binary-coded workloads. A subsequent partial-domain/operator panel led to a lazy update that filters existing observations first and only evaluates current survivors. The lazy revision passes exact parity across eight pure-Term operators with complete and partial x/y inputs; it selects 12 partial inputs across the 16 paired target runs and refuses graph-dependent `Holds` terms before evaluation. A 36-worker crossover panel now measures the lazy version itself: it is 1.51–1.99× faster when 3–9 follow-up queries remain, and 0.63–0.75× as fast when initial evidence already identifies the singleton. Peak RSS is indistinguishable at current sampling resolution. Both versions preserve the off-grid false-singleton limitation; neither improves hypothesis generation or model adequacy. Matrix selection remains opt-in, with the lazy route indicated only for multi-query sequences in finite pure-Term tasks ([eager results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_BATCHED_PREDICTIONS_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [lazy coverage results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_BATCHED_PARTIAL_DOMAIN_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [crossover results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_LAZY_MATRIX_CROSSOVER_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_lazy_matrix_crossover_v1_analysis_20261002.json)).

**Scope correction and production-path update (2026-10-01):** the prior grammar-escalation modulo no-fit used `domains.generator.synthesize`, whose Python operator table omits `Mod`; do not generalize that result to the production substrate. The production Rust inventory includes `Mod`, and graph-authored Γ selects it for a generic integer task. In a locked 10-worker production study, modulo is exact on 40/40 held-out calls; factorial, absent from the native operator inventory, gaps at the 200,000-candidate cap in 5/5 workers. This confirms finite operator selection and application, but no primitive invention. See the [scope amendment](SUBSTRATE_SYNTHESIS_GRAMMAR_ESCALATION_V1_SCOPE_AMENDMENT_20261001.md), [production results](SUBSTRATE_PRODUCTION_GAMMA_OPERATOR_SELECTION_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/production_gamma_operator_selection_v1_20261001_analysis.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, bounded grammar escalation v1):** five workers on each of two tasks completed the locked grammar ladder; all 10 analyzer gates pass. The division target is recovered at the caller-supplied `Div` stage and transfers to 35/35 held-outs. For `x % 3`, the three grammars supplied to the standalone Python helper omit `Mod`, and all searches complete through size 5 without a fit (215 ms median total); this is not a production-path result. This is evidence for bounded gap reporting in the standalone helper; that ladder receives every grammar stage from the runner. It does not measure the separate production Γ operator selector, now covered by the production-path study above. See [results](SUBSTRATE_SYNTHESIS_GRAMMAR_ESCALATION_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_SYNTHESIS_GRAMMAR_ESCALATION_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/synthesis_grammar_escalation_v1_20261001_analysis.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, declared grammar expansion v1):** a locked ten-worker panel passes all 10 gates. With division omitted, the bounded size-5 synthesis returns no fit in all five workers (median 1,116 candidates, 29.4 ms); with division supplied, it returns `x / 2` and transfers to 35/35 odd/negative held-outs (median 94 candidates, 2.02 ms). This measures grammar-conditioned synthesis and an honest bounded no-fit; it does not show the substrate inventing or selecting the missing operator. The next gate is a charged grammar-escalation ladder that also includes a target absent from all registered grammars. See [results](SUBSTRATE_SYNTHESIS_DECLARED_GRAMMAR_EXPANSION_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_SYNTHESIS_DECLARED_GRAMMAR_EXPANSION_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/synthesis_declared_grammar_expansion_v1_20261001_analysis.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, synthesis off-support ambiguity v1.1):** the 15-worker locked replication passes all 10 integrity/behavior gates. With sparse examples compatible with identity and square, the default route consistently accepts identity and predicts 0/20 analytic square holdouts correctly; when examples disambiguate square, it transfers exactly on 20/20 holdouts. Contradictory labels produce a gap only at the 200,000-candidate cap in all five workers, with 266 ms median wall time. This is evidence of bounded synthesis and a specific failure mode under underdetermination, not a general ambiguity policy or evidence that intended rules can be identified from insufficient data. Next compare explicit-prior choice, discriminating-query selection, and abstention under matched search/cost limits. See [results](SUBSTRATE_SYNTHESIS_OFFSUPPORT_AMBIGUITY_V1_1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_SYNTHESIS_OFFSUPPORT_AMBIGUITY_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/synthesis_offsupport_ambiguity_v1_1_20261001_analysis.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, mixed-act task receipt transfer v1.1):** the second, independent task family replay ran eight fresh workers (24 total Direct/eager/shared episodes). Per-event closure progress and canonical graph facts match Direct at all checkpoints; the two Goals resolve on `[1,2,3]` and `[6,7,8]`, common-ground claims and deferred epistemic residual match, terminal probes are fixed points, and all 128 receipts per branch arm adopt to primary states matching their producing branches. Both receipt arms have 48 nonempty receipts and 512 total graph effects. Serialized wire order differs from Direct at 72/80 event states per arm, increasing the priority of an order-compatibility audit. Median measured route times are 6.03 ms Direct, 22.15 ms eager receipt, 20.41 ms shared receipt; CoW's measured branch setup is lower than eager, but receipt/adoption remains slower than Direct. This is one existing authored task fixture, not natural-task prevalence. The first v1 capture is retained as a JSON serialization failure diagnostic and makes no result claim. See [v1.1 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_MIXED_ACT_TASK_RECEIPT_TRANSFER_V1_1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_MIXED_ACT_TASK_RECEIPT_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_MIXED_ACT_TASK_RECEIPT_TRANSFER_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261001.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_mixed_act_task_receipt_transfer_v1_1_raw_20261001.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_mixed_act_task_receipt_transfer_v1_1_analysis_20261001.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, task-generated receipt transfer v1.1):** eight fresh workers replayed the frozen ten-event common-ground task through direct closure, eager-copy branch plus journal/adoption, and shared-snapshot branch plus journal/adoption. All 24 episodes pass event/goal identity, per-event closure progress, canonical graph facts, two-goal path, common-ground, terminal fixed-point, source-lock, and branch-to-adopted-primary parity gates. Each receipt arm generates 48 nonempty event receipts across 80 events, with 128 recorded effects. Full serialized edge order differs from Direct in 32/80 event states per receipt arm even though canonical facts match, so wire-order compatibility remains visible. After amending a cold-import timing confound, median measured route times are 5.62 ms direct, 13.65 ms eager receipt, and 11.01 ms shared-snapshot receipt; this is one existing authored fixture, not natural-task prevalence or an end-to-end CoW speedup. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_TASK_GENERATED_RECEIPT_TRANSFER_V1_1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_TASK_GENERATED_RECEIPT_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_TASK_GENERATED_RECEIPT_TRANSFER_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261001.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_task_generated_receipt_transfer_v1_1_raw_20261001.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_task_generated_receipt_transfer_v1_1_analysis_20261001.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, CoW high-churn lifecycle v1):** the source-locked native panel completed 200 waves across 10k/50k-node bases, fanout 4/16, shared/eager branches, and 25 repeated waves per cell while retaining each primary and reusable snapshot. All semantic and primary/later-fork checks pass; exact branch-added NodeIDs agree in all 100 backend pairs; every mutation-adjusted post-drop allocation delta is zero, and live allocation at the first and final wave is identical within each cell. Shared snapshots use 16.04×–22.75× less live requested allocator payload and complete the full concurrent wave 7.66×–13.16× faster across these four synthetic cells. One host, node-only fixtures, allocator payload rather than RSS/PSS, and no commit/adoption; this supports high-churn lifecycle under the measured conditions, not a general leak or workload claim. See [results](COW_HIGH_CHURN_LIFECYCLE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](COW_HIGH_CHURN_LIFECYCLE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/cow_high_churn_lifecycle_v1.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/cow_high_churn_lifecycle_v1_analysis.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, concurrent CoW degree/placement v1):** the 384-wave/192-pair source-locked native panel crosses four degree profiles (path, regular-four, star, grid), N=10k/50k, 1%/10% write density, four target policies (clustered, spread, low out-degree, high out-degree), and eager/shared snapshots. All 64 cells pass semantic parity, branch-ID, primary/sibling/later-fork isolation, cancellation, and zero mutation-adjusted allocation-after-drop gates. Shared snapshots use less total live allocator payload and win median wave time in all 64 cells (eager/shared allocation ratio 1.73×–7.80×). On the regular-four graph, degree is fixed at four yet 10% clustered writes use 62.0 MiB shared total-live allocation versus 128.7 MiB spread, isolating page placement. On the 50k star, hub-including spread uses 101.2 MiB versus 34.5 MiB for a contiguous leaf cluster; grid low-degree writes also cost more than high-degree writes. Thus target degree and page placement jointly affect write amplification. This remains one-host synthetic evidence with three repeats, not a workload law. Next use randomized degree distributions/target sets and task-generated receipts. See [results](COW_CONCURRENT_DEGREE_PLACEMENT_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [preregistration](COW_CONCURRENT_DEGREE_PLACEMENT_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/cow_concurrent_degree_placement_v1.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/cow_concurrent_degree_placement_v1_analysis.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, concurrent CoW topology/write-density v1):** a source-locked 144-wave/72-pair panel crosses path/star/grid, 10k/50k nodes, 1%/10% writes, fanout 4/16, and shared-snapshot/eager-copy. Every semantic, exact branch-ID, sibling-survival, primary/later-fork, cancellation, and mutation-adjusted reclamation gate passes; zero mutation-adjusted live requested bytes remain after drop in all rows. Shared snapshot reduces total all-live allocator payload by 1.73×–3.12× and wins median wave time in all 24 cells. At 50k/fanout16/10% writes, however, shared mutation increment is 85.9–110.8 MiB versus 6.9–19.3 MiB eager; fork-only savings still leave lower total shared allocation in this panel, but first-write amplification is substantial. This is one host, 3 repeats, deterministic even-spread targets, and fixed 128-byte payloads—not a general scaling law. Next vary clustered/spread target placement and degree distributions, then replicate on a second host or task-generated graphs. See [results](COW_CONCURRENT_MUTATION_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [preregistration](COW_CONCURRENT_MUTATION_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/cow_concurrent_mutation_scale_v1.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/cow_concurrent_mutation_scale_v1_analysis.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, native concurrent CoW lifecycle v1):** a source-locked Rust panel completes 60 paired waves across 10k/50k-node bases, fanout 1/4/16, and shared-snapshot/eager-copy backends. All branch semantics, primary and later-fork isolation, sibling survival after cancelling half the branches, and exact branch-ID parity pass. An amended allocator protocol uses both a thread-only control and a same-backend fork/drop/no-write control after the initial run exposed -24 bytes per worker of raw post-join drift. In every measured wave, mutation-adjusted live requested bytes return to zero after drop. At 50k/fanout16, shared/eager all-live allocator deltas are 3.43/78.14 MiB, half-cancelled deltas 1.72/39.04 MiB, and median full-wave times 4.13/37.56 ms. This establishes concurrent native branch lifecycle and allocator-level mutation reclamation for one synthetic isolated-node topology, not serving performance or general scaling. Next vary topology and write payload/density, then run high-churn lifecycle with a retained-base/reclamation stress bound. See [results](COW_CONCURRENT_BRANCH_LIFECYCLE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [preregistration](COW_CONCURRENT_BRANCH_LIFECYCLE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [amendments](COW_CONCURRENT_BRANCH_LIFECYCLE_V1_AMENDMENT_20261001.md) and [amendment 2](COW_CONCURRENT_BRANCH_LIFECYCLE_V1_AMENDMENT_2_20261001.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/cow_concurrent_branch_lifecycle_v1.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/cow_concurrent_branch_lifecycle_v1_analysis.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, CoW shared-prefix consumer v1):** the first storage-independent fake prefix/block consumer completes 30 source-locked fresh workers across 0/1k/10k background nodes, five repeats, and public-default/eager-copy snapshot paths. All seven isolation, revision refusal, cancellation, later-fork, and reuse gates pass, and the independent analyzer confirms exact semantic parity in every cell. At 10k nodes, median fork for two requests is 0.105 ms public-default versus 5.563 ms eager. Logical active branch blocks reach zero after cancellation, but process PSS remains above baseline; object-level reclamation is not proven. This demonstrates shared-prefix-shaped lifecycle compatibility only—not vLLM, KV-cache, concurrent cancellation, scheduler, throughput, or serving-memory compatibility. Next probe repeated and concurrent request lifecycles with allocator-level reclamation accounting, then design an actual adapter only against a named serving runtime. See [results](COW_SHARED_PREFIX_ADAPTER_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [preregistration](COW_SHARED_PREFIX_ADAPTER_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/cow_shared_prefix_adapter_v1.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/cow_shared_prefix_adapter_v1_analysis.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, targeted commit factorial v1.1):** the follow-up independently crosses 256/1k/5k nodes, background out-degree 0/2/8, and deletion aperture 1/4/32/128; every non-focal live node participates in the density-controlled graph. All 432 clean pairs pass exact complete-graph parity, tombstone allocation parity, write-kind, and isolation gates; all 108 stale-writer injections reject atomically while preserving the competing write. Pooling aperture levels, targeted/legacy medians at N=5,000 are 7.19/32.05 ms (out-degree 0), 8.72/99.21 ms (2), and 15.34/324.73 ms (8). This supports a large density- and size-dependent gain from skipping full graph materialization, on one synthetic topology/host. The v1 diagnostic was not used for independent-factor claims because its eligible background population shrank as aperture grew. Next repeat on a second topology and host, then use task-generated receipts. See [v1.1 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_TARGETED_COMMIT_FACTORIAL_V1_1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [preregistration](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_TARGETED_COMMIT_FACTORIAL_V1_1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_targeted_commit_factorial_v1_1.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, targeted residual commit reads):** the paired 72-trial result replaces the previous profiling note's proposed next step: targeted native node/edge lookups and native next-live-ID allocation now avoid `graph_to_dict()` inside commit, preserve legacy allocation across trailing tombstones, and match the independent whole-graph oracle for all four rule families. Counterbalanced uninstrumented medians at 1,000 nodes fall 4.57–5.44× for attribute/add/mixed families (9/9 pair wins per cell); hub deletion falls 1.40× (8/9). Small-graph gains are modest. This is synthetic one-host evidence with family-confounded edge counts, not a scaling bound. Next vary node count, edge density, and aperture independently and exercise randomized tombstones and stale-writer conflicts before applying it to held-out task receipts. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_TARGETED_COMMIT_READS_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_TARGETED_COMMIT_READS_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_commit_phase_profile_v1.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, generated-residual commit cost attribution):** a three-worker, 72-trial instrumented replay passes all semantic/oracle gates. At 1,000 nodes, `commit_imagination`'s full `graph_to_dict()` costs 11–42 ms median and dominates the measured commit; native staged delta apply costs 1.2–1.8 ms. At 64 nodes, future-manifold assessment costs 12.5–16.9 ms and dominates. This separates a graph-size-sensitive serialization cost from a mostly size-independent assessment cost. Instrumented nested timings overlap and include wrapper overhead; they are localization evidence, not additive phase accounting. Next test targeted native reads and safe ID allocation to remove whole-graph serialization, with exact atomicity/oracle parity and uninstrumented matched timing. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COMMIT_PHASE_PROFILE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [preregistration](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COMMIT_PHASE_PROFILE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_commit_phase_profile_v1.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, generated residual adoption across effects and apertures):** the 72-trial v1.1 panel passes full-graph oracle parity, isolation, gate, and writeback checks across four actual rule families and 64/1,000-node fixtures, including degree-32 hub deletion. Snapshot/setup rises with graph size for attribute/add/mixed cases; deletion aperture increases residual readout. Approved commits take 18–60 ms median versus sub-millisecond reground/refusal, so commit cost now needs subphase attribution. This is one-host synthetic evidence, not learned-rule quality, natural-task prevalence, or a scaling law. Next measure held-out task-generated receipts and cross node count, background-edge density, and hub degree. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERATED_RESIDUAL_ADOPTION_V1_1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [preregistration](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERATED_RESIDUAL_ADOPTION_V1_1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_generated_residual_adoption_v1_1.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, exhaustive CAN clause-order oracle):** an exact 24-permutation simulation and 36-worker native capture pass all 9 gates for the production Hebbian match pattern. The current install order is never minimum-work, with 2.58–8.33× regret; at N=32,Q=248 the installed sequence performs 70,496 candidate tests, while `mt has_concept a → a coactivated_with coact → coact coactivation_of b → mt has_concept b` performs 8,464. Prefix counters match the independent typed-graph simulator. Uniform cyclic and source-skewed layouts give identical work vectors in this one-Microtheory fixture, so the measured loss is clause sequencing rather than the tested source-degree distribution. This is one effect-free matcher pattern at N≤32; production guards/effects, full rule interactions, natural graph shapes, and selector-statistics cost remain unmeasured. The evidence now isolates the engine gap as intermediate-prefix estimation, beyond first-edge cardinality. See [results](CAN_MIXED_DEGREE_JOIN_ORDER_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](CAN_MIXED_DEGREE_JOIN_ORDER_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/can_mixed_degree_join_order_v1_analysis_20261001.json).
