**Update (2026-10-01, mixed-relation join selector positive control):** all 9 gates pass in 90 source-locked workers across path/star/cycle patterns, four sparse-relation rotations, a balanced control, and N=16/32. Native prefix counters match an independent adjacency simulator exactly; exhaustive evaluation of all 24 clause permutations finds the installed rarest-edge/connected-edge order is minimum-work in 90/90 cases. A single N-sized relation among three 4N relations cuts candidate tests 75% versus the balanced control; doubling N doubles work. This is a deliberately regular, uniform, nested-relation positive control using synthetic relation names and graph loading, not evidence of general optimizer quality. It contrasts with the CAN repeated-edge/two-hop shape where the cardinality heuristic loses 2.28–2.89×. The next decisive selector test should use nonnested, skewed relation incidence and compare the same four-clause CAN pattern against all 24 orders. See [results](SUBSTRATE_MIXED_RELATION_JOIN_COST_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_MIXED_RELATION_JOIN_COST_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_mixed_relation_join_cost_v1_analysis_20261001.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, matcher prefix work):** a 108-worker panel covers path, star, and cycle patterns on path/star/ring graphs at N=16/32. All 9 gates pass; the native per-clause candidate-test counters exactly match an independent adjacency-based join enumerator at every prefix, and all prefix sums reconcile with global work. Work is topology-dependent: star4-on-star rises from 54,240 to 954,304 candidate tests when N doubles, while path4-on-path and cycle4-on-ring roughly double. Reversing clauses has no effect in these all-same-relation controls, showing that order changes do not automatically help. This validates the work instrumentation and motivates mixed-relation/selectivity trials; it is not a natural workload or large-scale result. See [results](SUBSTRATE_MATCHER_PREFIX_WORK_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_MATCHER_PREFIX_WORK_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_matcher_prefix_work_v1_analysis_20261001.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, install-time CAN join-order policy):** an additional source-locked 45-worker study passes all 9 gates and independently predicts all 180 installed rule orders. When rules are imported after data, the cardinality heuristic chooses `has_concept` first for every Q>N cell, including when clauses were authored sparse-first. The productive closure reference shows that this costs 2.28–2.89× the candidate tests of explicit coactivation-first order across those cells; the graph-first arms still match the exact productive outcome. At Q=N, clause-order ties preserve the input first edge; at Q=0 the zero-cardinality coactivation edge eliminates empty matcher work. This shows the install-time count heuristic is not an adequate proxy for downstream join work in this rule shape; it does not establish the best ordering for other patterns. Next compare runtime/intermediate-binding estimates across independent rule families before changing the engine policy. See [results](CAN_INSTALL_TIME_JOIN_ORDER_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](CAN_INSTALL_TIME_JOIN_ORDER_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/can_install_time_join_order_v1_analysis_20261001.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, productive coactivation closure):** the source-locked 45-worker panel passes all 10 gates across 15 N×Q cells. Both production Hebbian rules perform their intended mint/reweight writes; independent pair and graph-growth oracles pass; every nonempty closure reports two progressing rewrite steps and every terminal probe reports zero; order variants and audited/uninstrumented runs agree on canonical outcomes. Sparse-first reduces candidate tests by 72.74–99.19% for nonempty ledgers. At N=128,Q=16,256, candidate tests fall 667,968,192→5,396,992 and audited closure time 57.3s→2.37s. This demonstrates a substantial avoidable order cost in the controlled rule family, while the 57s authored-order tail also shows that broad joins can overwhelm the baseline at only 16k records. It remains one synthetic family/host and does not establish natural distribution tractability or asymptotic scaling. Next evaluate whether the production install-time ordering policy selects sparse-first under realistic densities, then repeat productive closure with independently structured pair distributions and full CAN rule interactions. See [results](CAN_PRODUCTIVE_COACTIVATION_CLOSURE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](CAN_PRODUCTIVE_COACTIVATION_CLOSURE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/can_productive_coactivation_closure_v1_analysis_20261001.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, coactivation population × Hebbian join order):** the source-locked 45-worker panel passes all eight gates, including actual installed-order verification, read-only fixed points, exact graph parity, and reconciliation of all 90 arms' per-rule candidate counts with global totals. Across N=32/64/128 and Q=0 through N(N−1), sparse-first reduces candidate tests by 90.79–100%; at N=128,Q=16,256 the count falls from 4,178,304 to 97,536. Even Q=0 baseline work is quadratic-looking in N due to the broad `has_concept` self-join. This is finite evidence on two synthetic read-only Hebbian matcher rules, not an asymptotic or natural-task claim. It identifies join order and relation population as material cost controls for follow-up productive-closure measurements. See [results](CAN_COACTIVATION_POPULATION_JOIN_ORDER_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [preregistration](CAN_COACTIVATION_POPULATION_JOIN_ORDER_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/can_coactivation_population_join_order_v1_analysis_20261001.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, matcher index versus graph persistence order):** source inspection finds the matcher scans `edges_by_type`/adjacency vectors, while `graph_to_dict()` emits live edge slabs by slot. The locked 27-worker shared-sink probe selects the same last-match NodeID in full and scoped-plus-fallback arms in all 27 workers, despite exact graph wire equality in only 9/27. The v4 preregistered check equating selected match to the last serialized edge was an invalid oracle; six direct-full choices differ from that slab tail. Canonical graph facts match 27/27. The strict 330/354 outcome and oracle correction are disclosed; no general order-insensitivity claim follows. A next probe should delete a later binding's unique driver edge, since prior fixtures delete a trigger edge shared by all bindings. See [v4 results](SUBSTRATE_ALL_QUEUE_SCOPED_FALLBACK_V4_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [amendment](SUBSTRATE_ALL_QUEUE_SCOPED_FALLBACK_V4_1_AMENDMENT_20261001.md).

**Update (2026-10-01, order-sensitive continuation):** an order-sensitive shared-sink rule consumes all R×M distinct handled edges and selects the same final NodeID under full and scoped-plus-fallback closure in 27/27 workers. The v2 edge-vector insertion-order difference therefore did not change this matcher-driven continuation's result. Strict exact graph-wire parity remains 9/27, with identical node arrays and canonical edge-fact multisets in 27/27. The result narrows the risk to untested consumers/order paths; it does not prove edge order irrelevant. An unguarded pilot oscillated and was stopped before capture; v3's locked continuation uses a one-shot marker, with the pilot disclosed in its amendment. See [v3 results](SUBSTRATE_ALL_QUEUE_SCOPED_FALLBACK_V3_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [state/order analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/all_queue_scoped_fallback_v3_state_order_20261001.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, distinct writes and dependent scope rule):** a preregistered 27-worker follow-up adds a verifier rule activated by R×M independent writes. All queue totals, handled/verified edge counts, and target/frame outcomes are correct in full and scoped-plus-fallback arms. Strict byte-order graph parity fails in 18/27 workers (all R>1), while node arrays and canonical edge-fact multisets match in 27/27. The `handled_mid` edge subsequence itself is reordered in the six R>1,M=1 workers, so ordering may be operationally visible; no downstream order-sensitive continuation has yet been measured. Preserve the strict failure and test that next before broadening scoped-equivalence claims. See [v2 results](SUBSTRATE_ALL_QUEUE_SCOPED_FALLBACK_V2_RESULTS_20261001.md), [strict analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/all_queue_scoped_fallback_v2_analysis_20261001.json), and [state/order analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/all_queue_scoped_fallback_v2_state_order_20261001.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, scoped multi-binding repair):** the initial multi-binding probe exposed that scoped non-batch closure stopped after one progressing binding, unlike the live scheduler's snapshot drain. The scoped path now folds all distinct bindings captured before mutation and avoids graph-dependent cache reuse during the fold. After rebuilding the native extension, the same 27-worker R×M comparison passes all 300/300 checks: exact full/scoped-plus-fallback final-graph parity, explicit scoped pending state, and five-family queue/trace reconciliation. The repair resolves the observed discrepancy for this fixture; distinct per-binding targets, write-dependent bindings, projection deduplication, rule interactions, and natural frame scopes remain unmeasured. The initial raw capture was overwritten by output-path reuse and is not independently auditable; the limitation is disclosed in [amendment v1.1](SUBSTRATE_ALL_QUEUE_SCOPED_FALLBACK_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261001.md). The preserved confirmatory result and capture are linked from [results](SUBSTRATE_ALL_QUEUE_SCOPED_FALLBACK_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md).

**Update (2026-10-01, unified deferred-family/join/mutation trace):** one source/native-locked factorial workload now exercises all five queue families together with positive two-edge joins and trigger deletion across 27 fresh workers. All 300 checks pass, including audit-on/off semantic parity, fixed-point completion, mutation outcomes, and exact operation-work/family-counter equality. RulePatch deduplicates to one item per writer; the other four queues scale as `R×M`; all five drain in one batch. Candidate-test cost increases with M, but its curve changes with R, so this does not establish an asymptotic law. This closes the integration gap for one authored full-closure fixture; natural-task prevalence, trace coverage beyond instrumented hooks, scoped/fallback across all five in one trace, and general partition safety remain open. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ALL_QUEUE_MULTIPLICITY_WORK_TRACE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/all_queue_multiplicity_work_trace_v1_analysis_20261001.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, positive-join operation-work scaling):** a 27-process source/native-locked sweep traces `rule_edits` and `fb_samples` during positive two-edge joins at `R={1,8,32}` writer/target pairs and `M={1,4,16}` successful bindings per writer. All 219 analysis checks pass; operation work reconciles exactly with family counters and audit-on/off graph and report states match. Queue items are exactly `R×M` per family. Scheduler passes remain three, while selected-rule, dirty-vector, and external-poll units equal `6R` across the tested M values. Candidate tests grow more sharply with M (median 7→277 for R=1, 56→2,216 for R=8, 224→8,864 for R=32 from M=1 to 16). This is one authored join shape, not an asymptotic or natural-task complexity result. See [results](SUBSTRATE_JOIN_MULTIPLICITY_WORK_TRACE_V2_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_join_multiplicity_work_trace_v2_analysis_20261001.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, deferred queue operation trace):** a new source/native-locked 18-worker panel connects per-operation work rows to family counters for RulePatch, TrackIdentity, and NetCollapse in full, scoped-only, and fallback phases. All 229 analyzer checks pass. Every full phase enqueues and drains three items; scoped-only enqueues three and leaves them pending; fallback drains all three without re-enqueueing. This required adding a work-operation guard to scoped-delta closure, whose queue work had previously been invisible to the trace. Earlier independent studies already exercise the remaining families, `rule_edits` and `fb_samples`; coverage across all five is composite rather than one unified trace. Scheduler counters are fixed in this tiny fixture as unrelated target count varies, so no broad scaling claim follows. See [results](SUBSTRATE_DEFERRED_QUEUE_WORK_TRACE_V2_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_deferred_queue_work_trace_v2_analysis_20261001.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, whole-worker scheduler/deferred trace):** a corrected source-locked trace repeats across three fresh workers and reconciles whole-worker records with per-turn segments. It captures 51 `fb_samples` drain batches per worker, but zero deferred enqueue-item and drained-item rows; these are empty drain attempts, so queue item flow was not exercised. An independent audit corrects the original analyzer's vacuous queue-balance pass. Task quality remains 18/21. This provides repeatable cost counts for one route, not complete work accounting or general tractability. Next exercise a route that creates real deferred items, and require nonempty enqueue/drain item events before evaluating queue balance. See [results](SUBSTRATE_HELD_TOPIC_WHOLE_WORKER_TRACE_V1_8_RESULTS_20261001.md), [queue audit](../../../evaluation/reports/held_topic_runtime_interface_trace_v1_8_20261001/queue_item_audit_v1.json), and [capture analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/held_topic_runtime_interface_trace_v1_8_20261001/analysis_v2_0.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, runtime boundary/access capture):** three current-source workers captured dynamic native access events and boundary-node attribute schemas for seven live held-topic turns. Trace integrity passes across all 21 turns with zero dropped events, stable structure, mapped rule UIDs, and reconciled scope counters. The protocol-declared task frames are 10–13 nodes with 40–51 boundary incidences, while 112k–309k native events per turn touch nodes outside the direct cut; this shows these small projections omit most observed closure activity, not that every outside access is necessary. Task quality independently fails on the honest-capability copper prompt in all three workers (18/21 pass). Host/scheduler work outside trace hooks and learned boundary discovery remain open. See [results](SUBSTRATE_HELD_TOPIC_RUNTIME_INTERFACE_TRACE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/held_topic_runtime_interface_trace_v1_20261001/analysis_v1_1.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, static/dynamic access reconciliation):** a fresh v1.1 capture preserves both wildcard flags in every per-rule declaration and reconciles 3,385,326 concrete-resource native observations. The analyzer classifies 3,231,108 as explicit, 153,966 as conservatively wildcard-covered, and 252 as uncovered, plus 36 type-index queries without concrete resource keys. Every uncovered event is an effect-time read of `assessment.status` or `assessment.reason` by `optic_flow_refuse_no_translation`, whose conditional-write effect is absent from the derived read set. This establishes a specific static dependency metadata gap that must be fixed before declarations can serve as a sound partition boundary. Capture integrity passes; the same copper clarification task-quality failure persists (18/21). See [v1.1 results](SUBSTRATE_HELD_TOPIC_RUNTIME_INTERFACE_TRACE_V1_1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/held_topic_runtime_interface_trace_v1_1_20261001/analysis_v1_2.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, effect-time dependency repair):** `SetAttrIfDifferent` now declares its target as a read as well as a write, and a rebuilt native extension confirms the repair on the same held-topic workload. Across three fresh workers, all 21 traces pass integrity; 3,232,224 concrete-key observations are explicit, 153,966 remain wildcard-covered, and none are uncovered. Thirty-six type-index queries remain without concrete type identity, wildcard accesses remain unbounded, and other effect kinds still need audit. The copper clarification quality failure is unchanged (18/21). This makes the observed static metadata more complete for one operation and task route; it does not establish cheap cuts or safe execution partitioning. See [v1.2 results](SUBSTRATE_HELD_TOPIC_RUNTIME_INTERFACE_TRACE_V1_2_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/held_topic_runtime_interface_trace_v1_2_20261001/analysis_v1_3.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, broader finite-effect audit):** static dependencies and native traces now include the old-value comparison for `SetAttr`, candidate-attribute comparisons for `AddNodeIfAbsent`, and edge-existence probes for `AddEdge`/`DelEdge`. In a rebuilt, source-locked three-worker replay, all 3,739,776 concrete-resource events reconcile: 3,585,810 explicit and 153,966 wildcard-covered, with zero uncovered; 36 type-index queries remain without concrete type identity. Trace-integrity gates pass, and task quality remains 18/21. This is one live task route; broad/deferred effect coverage, bounded interfaces for wildcard operations, index population attribution, and partitioned execution remain open. See [v1.3 results](SUBSTRATE_HELD_TOPIC_RUNTIME_INTERFACE_TRACE_V1_3_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/held_topic_runtime_interface_trace_v1_3_20261001/analysis_v1_4.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, typed-population work attribution):** the 36 prior unresolved query events are ShapeDecode typed-population cache fingerprints. The rebuilt trace records 36 queries and 8,625 candidate-node visits by type (`ShapeSignature`: 21/8,589; `DimensionGenerator`: 6/36; the `·?·` sentinel: 9/0). The scans remain wildcard-covered and unbounded. No `NodesWhere` indexed or fallback query occurred on this route. Across the complete trace, 3,748,437 concrete-resource events reconcile (3,585,810 explicit; 162,627 wildcard) with zero uncovered or unmapped; task quality remains 18/21. See [v1.5 results](SUBSTRATE_HELD_TOPIC_RUNTIME_INTERFACE_TRACE_V1_5_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/held_topic_runtime_interface_trace_v1_5_20261001/analysis_v1_6.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, conditional-grammar scaling):** the opt-in candidate-cohort streaming comparison now includes comparison operators and ternary `IfThenElse`. All 12 paired cells across triangular, square, and Euler fixtures complete the bounded search and preserve exact ordered-pool and held-out-support parity. Python-traced peak allocation falls in each task/size group by 1.09×–3.83×; wall time remains near parity with only two repeats per group. This supports temporary-memory reduction on the added ternary path, not faster synthesis or generalization beyond the three authored task adapters. Native allocation and isolated-process RSS remain open. See [results](SUBSTRATE_GENERATOR_STREAMING_GRAMMAR_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_generator_streaming_grammar_v1_20261001_analysis.json).

**Update (2026-10-01, synthesis cohort streaming):** an opt-in eager-versus-streaming comparison completed 120 paired cells with exact ordered-pool, work-count, halt-status, and held-out-support parity. Streaming removes the full same-size temporary candidate list, lowering median Python-traced peak allocation 2.59×; paired median wall time is effectively unchanged (eager/streaming 1.014×). This is a memory improvement for the measured grammar, not a speedup or reduction in combinatorial search. Native allocations were not observed and tracing perturbs absolute timings, so retain streaming as opt-in until process/native-memory and production-workload evidence is available. See [results](SUBSTRATE_GENERATOR_STREAMING_PROBE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_generator_streaming_probe_v1_20261001_analysis.json), and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

**Update (2026-10-01, generated-pool scale ladder):** the preregistered 120-worker grammar-size × probe-diversity ladder completed with no worker errors or timeouts; all source, coverage, and cell-invariant analyzer gates pass. Within these three small authored tasks, synthesis rises from milliseconds to 0.3–0.8 seconds at larger grammar ceilings; more probe inputs expose more distinct behaviors, increase pool size/work, and improve target retention in some cells. This is an early cost-cliff signal for complete generated-pool induction, not natural-task scaling evidence or an asymptotic estimate. Per-worker RSS high-water deltas are too coarse to conclude memory scaling. Next: compare incremental pool construction with full enumeration and current resynthesis under fixed charged-work budgets, broaden independent task families, and include a target absent from the grammar. See [scale results](SUBSTRATE_GENERATED_POOL_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_generated_pool_scale_v1_20261001_raw.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_generated_pool_scale_v1_20261001_analysis.json), and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

**Update (2026-10-01, generated-pool versus resynthesis comparison):** a five-block source-locked study across triangular number, square, and Euler tasks passes all 215 analyzer checks, with all arms exact on held-out inputs and every generated pool complete with the target behavior present. Entropy uses 1/0/2 query labels against the MDL loop's 11/10/17. After separating learner work from held-out evaluation, total charged learning time remains about 6×/77×/32× higher for generated-pool entropy; pool enumeration dominates, and entropy matches seeded-random query counts on these three tasks. The MDL arm is an inline replay under a matched fixed grammar rather than a call to `cegis_synthesize`, whose current API does not expose those grammar controls. This is a negative end-to-end efficiency result on three authored functions, not a general learner verdict. Next scale pool size and task inputs, measure complete/incomplete pool rates and resource tails, then test incremental pool construction before production-loop integration. See the [results](SUBSTRATE_GENERATED_POOL_CEGIS_COMPARISON_V1_5_RESULTS_20261001.md), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_generated_pool_cegis_comparison_v1_5_20261001_analysis.json), and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

**Update (2026-10-01, CoW as a general substrate capability):** copy-on-write is being developed as a consumer-neutral facility for isolating speculative mutable branches over shared immutable state. Its value is broader than Thoughtexperiment speed: callers can obtain predictable isolation and a stable branch contract while storage backends evolve, and multiple consumers can share a base without copying it wholesale. Thoughtexperiment is the first measured consumer. Solver/planner search, simulation, sandboxed execution, and a vLLM-style shared-prefix interface are future compatibility targets, each requiring its own adapter and workload evidence; graph snapshots are not model KV-cache blocks. The selective typed-adjacency CoW stage now passes 81 mutation parity/isolation cells and shows a 1.81× median six-candidate batch speed ratio at +50k unrelated nodes (5/5 paired blocks), but no stable gain at baseline or +1k and no consistent extra gain from reusable snapshots. This supports a useful capability with a scaling benefit in one workload regime; it does not establish broad serving compatibility or general performance. See the [design and measurements](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COW_SUBSTRATE_DESIGN.md) and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).
**Clarification (2026-10-01, CoW value and compatibility):** CoW is useful on its own for isolation, design purity, and future compatibility; its justification does not depend on Thoughtexperiment becoming faster. Keep the branch lifecycle and receipts independent of page layout and consumer. Add a distinct shared-prefix adapter compatibility gate, shaped for vLLM-style ownership and reuse semantics, without treating graph snapshots as KV-cache storage or implying a serving integration. The next implementation plan should measure correctness, cancellation, branch reclamation, concurrent readers, and memory amplification at this API boundary as well as task-specific speed.

**Update (2026-10-01, where-aware prefix-cost selector v2):** the frozen selector now evaluates supported production `where` guards as soon as their variables bind. Three source-locked workers on a new synthetic N=20 retinal profile each measured all 120 orders and selected the exact minimum, 44 candidate tests, tying the default. All 8 semantic/integrity gates passed; index-plus-search median was 344–348 μs and estimated work 5 versus actual 44. This resolves the specific held-out failure mechanism seen in v1, but does not show a work reduction or calibrated estimator. Evaluation on new production rule families with charged selector overhead remains open. See [results](PREFIX_COST_SELECTOR_V2_RESULTS_20261001.md) and the [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

**Update (2026-10-01, consumer-neutral snapshot API):** the Rust and Python application APIs now use `Substrate.snapshot()` and choose their backend internally; snapshots no longer expose backend identity, and consumers use the same `fork()` contract. Thoughtexperiment and self-correction production callers use that API. Backend choice remains available only through private measurement hooks for controlled comparisons. The 5,000-node source-captured fanout panel passes sibling, primary, and captured-base isolation in all 9 backend × fanout cells (5 repeats each). Median fork time at fanout 16 is 1.10 ms for full copy, 0.79 ms for shared slabs, and 0.0043 ms for shared graph pages. This validates the consumer boundary and a local fork-cost comparison, not cancellation, reclamation under pressure, concurrent access, total memory/RSS, or a vLLM adapter. See the [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_consumer_neutral_snapshot_api_v1.json), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_snapshot_fork_contract_v1.py), and [design](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COW_SUBSTRATE_DESIGN.md).

**Update (2026-10-01, snapshot lifecycle memory v2):** a preregistered 45-worker fresh-process panel adds snapshot retention/drop stages to the existing branch PSS comparison. All 8 independent integrity/semantic gates pass, including the post-discard later-fork check. At 50,771 total nodes, median snapshot-live/branch-live PSS deltas are 10,996/27,832 KiB full copy, 3,004/11,844 KiB shared slabs, and 284/772 KiB shared graph pages. This strengthens the evidence for low marginal resident cost of graph-page snapshots at large fixture size. Drop-stage PSS is not stable enough to demonstrate allocator/kernel reclamation; the full-copy process stays near its high-water sample and shared-page results fluctuate below baseline. The result establishes lifecycle semantics and resident footprint on one synthetic fixture, not bounded-memory reclamation, cancellation, concurrency, or natural workload performance. See the [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SNAPSHOT_RECLAMATION_V2_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

**Update (2026-10-01, native concurrent snapshot branches):** compile-time bounds confirm `ExperimentSnapshot: Send + Sync`, and the native threaded probe passes primary, branch, and captured-base isolation across all 9 size × fanout cells. Concurrent fork-plus-one-node-write loses at all 771-node cells and at 10,771/fanout 1; it wins 5/5 paired blocks at fanout 4 and 16 on 10,771 nodes, and at fanout 4 and 16 on 50,771 nodes. At 50,771 nodes and fanout 16 the median concurrent batch is 1.17 ms versus 5.03 ms serial (4.28×). This demonstrates thread-safe immutable snapshot sharing and a scale-dependent branch setup gain. Rule execution, one live shared `Substrate` receiving concurrent writes, bounded memory under contention, and Thoughtexperiment end-to-end throughput remain unmeasured. See the [results](COW_SNAPSHOT_CONCURRENT_FORK_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/cow_snapshot_concurrent_fork_v1.json), and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

**Update (2026-10-01, generated active-query learning):** the learner now has source-locked evidence beyond the earlier finite authored-hypothesis selector. An opt-in generated-pool Euler experiment completes pools of 218–662 hypotheses under the declared size bound; entropy identifies a behavior exact on six held-out complexes in 2–3 oracle calls, versus 5–7 for fixed-first and median 4 for seeded random. The query reduction costs more local compute (31.8 ms versus 3.2/3.4 ms median selector-plus-oracle time). Corrected cached filtering preserves exact query/survivor parity, and a serial branch-reuse panel measures 2.70×/6.53× speed at fanout 5/20 after charging the shared index build. These results are still one authored Euler task family and do not show a generally better learner, independent task-family transfer, or integration into `cegis_synthesize`'s normal resynthesis loop. Next compare full-budget active querying with that loop on fresh functions, counting pool generation, query selection, oracle calls, and resynthesis together. See the [results and protocol trail](SUBSTRATE_GENERATED_EULER_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_2_1_RESULTS_20260930.md), [branch-reuse results](SUBSTRATE_QUERY_INDEX_BRANCH_REUSE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260930.md), and the [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

**Update (2026-09-30, Thoughtexperiment snapshot reuse):** the fixed-revision six-candidate harness now accepts `native_reusable_snapshot`: it captures one graph-page-backed snapshot after baseline prediction, verifies the captured graph/rule revision, and forks independent candidate branches from it. Across 0 and +1k unrelated nodes, 2 measured randomized blocks plus 1 warmup preserve exact candidate outcomes, nested residuals, and primary isolation against the direct shared-graph-page backend. Batch medians are 686.7/639.3 ms at baseline and 608.2/587.5 ms at +1k (reusable/direct), with one useful candidate per batch. This first integration does not make the harness faster; it gives it a stable reusable isolation boundary while current branch forks still copy indexes and non-graph state. The capture is one synthetic family and host with short repeats. Focus next on complete branch-local ownership of graph indexes and shared rule/term state, and retain lifecycle/reclamation and memory gates. See the [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_fixed_revision_six_candidate_batch_v1.py), compressed [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_reusable_snapshot_batch_v1.json.gz), and [design](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COW_SUBSTRATE_DESIGN.md).

**Update (2026-09-30, initial reusable CoW snapshot contract):** the initial `Substrate.snapshot(backend)` prototype exposed a reusable immutable base with opaque identity and captured graph/rule revision; independent mutable branches used the same `snapshot.fork()` interface across eager, shared-slab, and shared-graph-page backends. The source-locked lifecycle probe passes sibling, primary, and read-only isolation for all 12 backend/fanout cells at 10,000 path nodes and fanouts 1/4/16/32. Shared graph pages reduce median branch-fork time to roughly 0.25–0.27 ms through fanout 16; the fanout-32 cell is noisier at 0.74 ms. This is API and fork-cost evidence, not a Thoughtexperiment end-to-end speedup: indexes and non-graph substrate state still copy eagerly. Reclamation, cancellation, adoption/conflict policy, concurrent consumers, receipts, memory amplification, and actual candidate-evaluation throughput remain open. The consumer API has since been simplified to hide backend selection; see the later consumer-neutral API update, [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_snapshot_fork_contract_v1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_snapshot_fork_contract_v1.json), and [design](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COW_SUBSTRATE_DESIGN.md).

**Status date:** 2026-09-30
**Overall goal:** active; the substrate has a source-linked capability inventory and a broad empirical programme, but it does not yet support a general tractability or general-capability claim.

**Update (2026-09-30, query selection and CoW scope):** an audit of the legacy 125-task P7-D run finds 19 accepted holdout failures receiving benchmark-supplied feedback; one repairs to a heldout pass, two remain failures, and 16 become gaps. The current `cegis_synthesize` path selects the first disagreement in a caller-supplied order and charges an oracle call for each inspected candidate. A separate finite-pool entropy ranker now ranks candidate inputs without oracle access when supplied four hypotheses. The v1/v2 probes found order sensitivity on triangular numbers. A 20-worker cross-task panel now repeats the comparison on triangular numbers and population variance with fixed base literals. Seeded permutations use fewer median query rows in both tasks; heldout passes are 5/5 for both policies on triangular numbers, and 5/5 fixed-order versus 4/5 seeded on variance. Variance search costs 2.8M candidates and 84–119 seconds median. This shows order sensitivity across two authored families, but not a generally superior policy; the seeded variance arm is less reliable. A 120-worker selector panel across triangular numbers, variance, and Euler characteristic gives 10/10 unique true-hypothesis identification for entropy ranking, versus 0/10 fixed-first and 2/10 seeded-random on Euler. This is a bounded mechanism result over authored hypothesis sets; it is not integrated into CEGIS and does not generate its own version space. Next integrate query ranking with generated hypotheses and evaluate on fresh task functions under matched search budgets. P7 feedback and CEGIS are distinct evidence; P7 remains historical (2026-07-31, older source revision). CoW is framed as a general substrate capability for isolation, stable API/future backend compatibility, and reuse by multiple consumers; thoughtexperiments are one consumer. Shared-prefix/KV-block serving (including a vLLM-style interface) is a plausible adapter, but needs its own block ownership, reuse, mutation, cancellation, and reclamation evidence. See [finite-pool query-ranking results](SUBSTRATE_LABEL_BLIND_QUERY_RANKING_V1_RESULTS_20260930.md), [cross-task CEGIS results](CEGIS_CROSS_TASK_QUERY_ORDER_V1_RESULTS_20260930.md), and [single-task fixed-vocabulary results](CEGIS_QUERY_ORDER_SENSITIVITY_V2_RESULTS_20260930.md).

**Update (2026-09-30, thoughtexperiment CoW purpose and phase profile):** treat copy-on-write as a general substrate capability for isolated branches over shared immutable state, with possible future use in thoughtexperiments, solver search, simulations, sandboxing, and shared-prefix serving interfaces. Its value includes isolation and API compatibility even where it does not improve this workload's speed. A matched six-candidate phase profile at 0/1k/10k unrelated nodes (two measured repeats plus one warmup, one synthetic task, one host) preserves exact batch/standalone semantic parity and primary-state isolation. Batch medians are 734/409/649 ms versus 1,221/631/1,042 ms standalone (1.66×/1.54×/1.61×). Across candidate phases, branch snapshot/setup medians are 12.70/7.11/12.04 ms, rule patch 14.02/7.31/11.65 ms, and model-after prediction 76.24/42.96/65.91 ms. These low repeat counts show noticeable timing variation, but prediction remains the largest of the three measured phases; optimizing outer branch setup alone cannot deliver the 10× objective. The next implementation should define shared immutable state and isolated overlay branches as reusable primitives, and separately reduce repeated predictive-look work while validating exact nested results. The compressed, source-locked [phase profile capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_fixed_revision_six_candidate_phase_profile_v1.json.gz) retains the raw trials; the [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_fixed_revision_six_candidate_batch_v1.py) can reproduce the protocol.

**Update (2026-09-30, nested prediction phase profile):** instrumentation splits `predict_collapse` into frontier scanning, bounded forward look, and trace minting, and each nested imagination into branch/rule setup, preparation, collapse, residual readout, and trace. Across five measured repeats plus two warmups at 0/1k/10k unrelated nodes, model-after prediction is consistently dominated by bounded looks (58–64 ms) plus structural frontier scans (17–20 ms); trace minting is under 0.1 ms. Each look executes three rounds; nested branch snapshot/rule setup takes 12–14 ms per round, versus roughly 1.2 ms collapse and 1.4–1.5 ms readout. This makes repeated internal snapshots a concrete CoW target, while structural frontier enumeration remains a separate cost. Batch medians are 750–781 ms versus 1,182–1,314 ms standalone (1.55×–1.75×); exact parity and primary-state isolation pass. This is stronger repeat evidence but still one synthetic family/host. The [compressed phase capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_fixed_revision_six_candidate_prediction_phase_v1.json.gz) contains raw trial data.

**Update (2026-09-30, native CoW primitive):** the first Rust `CowPagedVec<T>` prototype uses a shared radix root and copy-on-write 64-slot leaf pages. A source-locked probe covers 20 cells (771–50,771 slots × fanout 1–32) with 25 repeats; exact values, base isolation, divergent sibling writes, and untouched-page sharing pass in every cell. At 50,771 slots/fanout 32, one-slot fork-plus-write takes 35.22 μs versus 8.14 ms for cloning and writing a `Vec<u64>` (about 231×); at 771/fanout 32 it is slower (10.36 vs 3.71 μs). Indexed reads are about 5.2–18.4 ns versus 0.5–1.7 ns for vector indexing, so this storage structure trades read cost for cheap sparse branches. This is an isolated primitive, not yet integrated with graph slabs/indexes; base conversion is excluded, values are fixed-size, and memory bytes are not yet measured. The [probe capture](../../../evaluation/reports/cow_paged_vec_probe_v1.json) informs a gated prototype direction but does not establish graph-level speed.

The integrated Rust build now exposes this primitive as an experimental library module and supplies a standalone optimized probe. This is implementation progress, not completion of the substrate CoW capability: the probe’s favorable large/fanout result is sensitive to the synthetic `u64` workload, small graphs lose, indexed reads and branch drop cost more, and the graph’s adjacency/index stores remain deeply copied. The next gate is to measure payload-sized pages and memory, then integrate the slab and query-index policies while keeping eager-copy output parity as the control.

**Update (2026-09-30, shared slabs in thoughtexperiment branches):** node and edge slabs now use persistent pages in the graph, and fork APIs distinguish an eager-copy oracle from a partial backend sharing only those slabs while copying all query indexes. Five randomized measured blocks plus two warmups at 0/1k/10k unrelated nodes match exact nested candidate residuals/results across backends and preserve primary graph/rule state. Shared/eager paired batch ratios have medians 0.99/1.19/0.94; candidate snapshot/setup improves at +1k/+10k, but batch latency is variable and gives no stable overall gain in this small one-host study. The branch API works and isolation is measured; the performance target is not met. Query-index sharing, real graph payload/memory accounting, broad workloads, and independent-host replication remain open. See the [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_cow_shared_slab_comparison_v1.json.gz) and [design](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COW_SUBSTRATE_DESIGN.md).

## Assessment

**Update (2026-09-30, thoughtexperiment throughput):** the Rust mutation journal returns exact `conclusions` and `derived_only` residuals from touched objects. A nonzero-inference, source-locked comparison covers node addition/change/removal and edge addition/removal, passes exact residual and primary-state preservation gates at 0/1k/10k/50k/100k unrelated nodes, and has three paired repeats plus one warmup. At 100k, its scoped branch plus sparse readout takes a median 9.66 ms (about 103 timed branch operations/s) versus 3.14 s (0.32/s) for whole-graph snapshot/diff, a 325× speedup on this one-rule probe; fixture construction is excluded. The matched self-correction runner captures nested baseline and candidate residuals directly. At baseline and +10k, two measured repeats plus one warmup pass exact nested residual parity across serialization, native copy, scoped copy, and scoped legacy-diff backends, with positive/negative outcomes and primary-graph/rule preservation intact. At +10k, journal scoped rehearsal takes 239.62/215.52 ms (positive/negative): 8.4×/9.0× faster than scoped legacy diff, 5.4×/5.8× faster than serialization, and 3.2×/3.5× faster than full native copy. A bounded candidate expansion now produces six variants instead of two; all six pass journal/legacy nested-residual parity and isolation. On this fixture only the designated dedup fix converges (2/2); the guard and four new key swaps diverge (0/2). Summed candidate medians are 1.12 s for journal versus 12.34 s for legacy at +10k (fixture setup excluded), showing about 11× more trials in the timed budget but no added useful outcome from the new variants. A separate 10k path/star/grid sweep passes all six positive/negative parity gates; journal speedups range 8.7×–11.4×, so the effect is not limited to path-shaped background graphs. Evidence is still from one host and synthetic task families with small exploratory repeat counts; natural workloads and independent-host replication remain open. Earlier edge-scope, zero-step readout, and two-candidate batching results remain useful; process-CoW did not beat native deep copy on its tested end-to-end workload and dirtied about 100 MiB. Next: broaden candidate families against independent outcome oracles, replicate nested residuals across more topologies and hosts, and measure useful discoveries per time budget with memory and fixed-revision costs. See the [nonzero-inference journal capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_delta_journal_inference_v1.json), [matched self-correction capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_branch_delta_journal_v1.json), [candidate-diversity capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_candidate_diversity_v1.json), [topology-scaling capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_topology_scaling_v1.json), [fixed-revision batch capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_process_batch_amortization_v1.json), and [CoW capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_process_cow_scoped_v2.json).

The assessment now has a substantial, auditable evidence base. The capability map contains 28 capability rows, 290 source links, 65 linked test references, and 513 result-linked runners (546 runner/analyzer references). Its automated consistency check reports no unresolved source links, missing evidence, or validation errors. The measurement plan has accumulated source audits, locked protocols, raw captures, analysis reports, and repeated results across structural width, closure, exact inference, visual processing, transfer, synthesis, and selected resource envelopes. These measurements make the next experiments concrete and expose where implementation evidence ends and a theoretical or empirical assumption begins.

The tractability argument is supported in bounded regimes. Supplied small separators enable exact factorization in the tested synthetic frame constructions; selected bounded-width and task-derived operations have measured scaling evidence. Finite-key closure has a proven restricted termination envelope and generated cases that agree with independent closure oracles, while unrestricted minting remains outside a mandatory admission gate. Fixed-basis synthesis and authored operator-shape selection transfer on several small scalar families, with costs and failure controls recorded. These results support conditional claims about particular representations, source revisions, and task families. They do not establish that natural workloads reliably admit cheap cuts, that the system discovers those cuts, or that all production routes obey the restricted closure language.

The largest capability gap remains autonomous induction of useful new structure, but prior full-seed GenMint work does demonstrate bounded in-sequence concept/dimension acquisition and later reuse. In the three-matrix reuse study, acquired generators transferred exactly when selected, and the pooled acquisition-plus-application cost first crossed the disabled arm at 16 held-out applications; a broader six-matrix panel found family-specific effects and essentially pooled parity at 16 applications (27/54 enabled-faster pairs). Thus reuse is real in a controlled 2-D integer-linear domain, while reliable acquisition and amortization across families are not. Durable export/restore across process restarts and broad independent-family benefit remain unestablished.

The current integrated program-synthesis panel also transfers linear and quadratic programs but accepts a hinge shortcut that fails every held-out prediction and exhausts its candidate budget on absolute value. A balanced hinge task transfers when the harness supplies a narrow operator grammar; that contrast isolates grammar choice as a useful lever, but does not show the substrate learned that choice. SynthEnumerate succeeds when the required grammar is supplied. These scalar synthesis results do not yet establish learned grammar choice, concept/dimension adoption, or reuse by later tasks in the program-synthesis route. The evidence therefore supports both (a) a narrow, demonstrated acquisition-and-reuse capability for 2-D generators and (b) bounded synthesis with unresolved false acceptance and autonomous grammar selection—not general Rung 3/4 learning.

The broader program-synthesis comparisons temper that result. On the fixed P6 suite, the current arm passes 83/125 tasks versus 82/125 with the learned layer frozen; 25 additional accepted programs fail held-out evaluation. On MBPP, full R3 passes 88/257 versus 85/257 without retrieval, while 59 accepted programs fail held-out assertions. The paired task reanalysis finds one P6 gain and no losses (exact McNemar p=1.0), and five MBPP gains and two losses (p=0.453). These are small differences on known fixed suites, not evidence that acquired structure improves general performance. Other recorded composition and string ablations also show no measured gain. Thus distinguish **task-solving capability** (demonstrated on bounded families) from **incremental learning benefit** (not established).

## What is established and what remains open

| Area | Evidence now available | Remaining question |
|---|---|---|
| Inventory and provenance | 28-row map with source, test, runner, and result references; automated link/evidence validation passes. | Runtime reachability and semantic coverage are incomplete; production visual ingestion/settling and planning/action selection have no linked tests in the map. |
| Structural locality and inference | Synthetic width, boundary, topology, and separator-factorization studies; exact inference agrees with conditional-frame solvers on constructed separators. | How often natural tasks have useful low-aperture frames; whether the substrate can discover semantically valid cuts; costs on larger natural instances. |
| Rule closure | Restricted finite-key termination result, parser/source crosswalk, generated positive and refusal cases, and unrestricted-mint stress evidence. | Enforce the restriction fail-closed across authoring, import, revision, compilation, and self-mint paths; prove or measure production behavior under that gate. |
| Rungs 1–2 and fixed-basis execution | Selected matching, transform, vision, and task-transfer panels with exact controls and resource measures. | Broader task distribution, unsupported-family behavior, and comparable end-to-end measurements across capabilities. |
| Rungs 3–4 | Small exact transfer for supplied/fixed grammars; authored selectors help on tested threshold tasks; unshaped clamp composition succeeds. Full-seed GenMint acquires and reuses selected 2-D generators across staged tasks; cost benefit varies by matrix family. P6 and MBPP show functioning task solving, but only +1/125 and +3/257 paired pass differences against frozen/no-retrieval arms, with 25 and 59 accepted holdout failures respectively. | Reliable acquisition and economic benefit across independent families; restart durability; autonomous grammar choice; explicit ambiguity handling; novel primitive induction beyond the represented 2-D operator family. |
| Scaling | Several bounded structural, inference, and process-resource envelopes, including large corpus startup observations. | Independent-host repeats, controlled CPU/memory scaling over representative end-to-end workloads, and defensible estimates at much larger graph sizes. |

## Evidence limits

The results are heterogeneous and must not be collapsed into a single capability score. Most are finite, preregistered panels on synthetic or deliberately authored tasks; success on them estimates those task families and source revisions. Oracle-supplied grammars, hand-authored selector rules, and supplied separators are diagnostic conditions, not evidence of autonomous acquisition. Several measurements isolate a kernel or adapter rather than the full operating loop. Repeated workers on one function estimate repeatability and cost, not generalization across distinct functions. Performance values are host- and instrumentation-specific. The paired P6/MBPP reanalysis repairs an inferential-label issue in the older result text: their Newcombe intervals came from marginal arm intervals and were not paired intervals. The exact discordance analysis is reported separately, makes no natural-task population claim, and does not adjust for task-family clustering. The underlying plan records protocol deviations, capture repairs, and excluded runs alongside final reports; those distinctions should remain visible in future summaries.

## Next gated work

**Priority decision (2026-09-30):** focus capability work on measured substrate improvement rather than adding more static baseline panels, while preserving the present division of labor: the LLM directs meta-level work (task selection, experiment design, and which proposal to examine); the substrate improves object-level representations and performance. Two local candidate-snapshot families are measured. GenMint transfers an acquired linear generator exactly in 400/400 calls across 25/30 acquired workers (five acquisition misses); frozen and exposure/no-consolidation controls score 0/480. Scalar synthesis stores an executable Program/IR in ten candidate snapshots and transfers exactly on 60/60 held-outs. A hinge ambiguity panel shows all five sparse/default workers accept a fitting shortcut that fails 30/30 held-out points; one externally chosen counterexample changes the default route to a 200,000-candidate gap in 5/5, while a supplied compact grammar transfers exactly in 5/5. The latter is oracle information, not learned query/grammar selection. All three studies preserve candidate-branch isolation and eager/shared-page semantic parity. This is bounded authored-family evidence, not broad capability or durable-adoption authorization. Nonlinear GenMint negatives raise novelty axes whose appropriateness is unadjudicated. Formal P3-E1 still depends on Phase 2 and custody/release prerequisites; the [execution ledger](GENERAL_INTELLIGENCE_EXECUTION_LEDGER.md) records those gates as not passed. Next work should broaden ambiguity/query-selection across independent functions, add forgetting/reset controls, and adjudicate novelty-axis mint quality before meta-policy or adoption claims.

**Implementation direction (2026-09-30): build toward self-improvement through isolated experiments.** The intended outcome is for the substrate to build and improve its own bounded capabilities, not to describe current behavior as general self-improvement. Today the LLM supplies most meta-level direction, while the substrate performs some object-level acquisition and application. A gradual transfer needs a closed evidence loop: substrate-readable correction outcomes, isolated candidate experiments, residuals returned to the primary graph, and graph-authored policies that learn how to rank/allocate future experiments. The correction-dispatch path now records a compact `SelfCorrectionExperienceV1` trace on the source Impasse, including candidate digests, tested candidate outcomes, rank/cost, adoption/escalation, and before/after summaries. Attribution is deliberately cautious: presence of a graph policy does not prove it produced the trigger flag. This code change is unverified; no tests or experiment were run.

The **thoughtexperiment harness** is the systems path toward isolated substrate-led improvement: run candidate changes against matched probes in a branch, then return a provenance-locked residual and outcome record to the primary graph. Candidate mutations are not merged implicitly; adoption is separate. The current implementation includes serialization and native deep-copy routes plus a Linux process-CoW prototype; it does not yet provide a portable Rust CoW overlay. A versioned immutable base with branch-local overlays remains a design candidate. Evidence now demonstrates branch-isolated rehearsal and residual return on one synthetic fixture, but the randomized-block process-CoW comparison is now near native deep-copy parity only at +10k nodes and slower at smaller sizes. This harness can support a later substrate-authored experiment policy; it does not itself transfer meta-level selection or establish self-improvement.

**Fork boundary source audit (2026-09-30):** the current `TypedGraph` declaration has 25 fields and `Substrate` has 50. The field-inventory runner was rerun against current Rust sources after the graph mutation-version field was added; its capture now records 25/50 and source hashes matching the live declarations. This sharpens the implementation risk: semantic graph content and its maintained indexes must fork consistently, while rules/terms/interner/checker state, execution caches and cursors, telemetry, deferred effect queues, and framebuffer handles need explicit branch policies. The [CoW design](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COW_SUBSTRATE_DESIGN.md) treats CoW as a reusable isolation/shared-state capability across thoughtexperiments and future consumers, and separates that goal from this fixture's speed results. The policy labels remain a checklist, not proof of semantic isolation.

**Thoughtexperiment baseline measured (2026-09-30):** a source-locked runner measures the native graph serialization/restore route used by detached imagination. On this host, median export-plus-restore was 10.33 ms (1k nodes), 124.56 ms (10k), and 729.14 ms (50k); the 50k graph serialized to 12.0 MB. This is synthetic, graph-only, one-host evidence and includes no inference, rule import, residual extraction, RSS, or CoW arm. See the [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_snapshot_baseline_v1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_snapshot_baseline_v1.json), and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

**Rehearsal route repaired and measured:** source tracing showed detached imagination omitted the native rewrite-rule store, then forward look used the Default tick plan and failed to execute graph-resident rules. The detached path now copies active rewrite rules, applies candidate edits only in the branch, and runs graph-then-struct execution. On the existing self-correction fixture, the designated positive candidate now converges 3/3 and the wrong candidate remains divergent 3/3 at baseline, +1k, and +10k unrelated nodes. Median rehearsal latency rises from about 0.54 s on the 771-node base graph to 2.37–2.38 s at 10,771 nodes; this is a single-host synthetic chain and the branch still uses serialization copies, so it is not a CoW result. Source changes are in [`imagination.py`](../../../domains/imagination.py), [`collapse_model.py`](../../../domains/collapse_model.py), [`self_correction.py`](../../../domains/self_correction.py), the Python API wrapper, and [`py.rs`](../../../runners/dsl/src/py.rs). The current engine was rebuilt with the repository procedure and records source revision `7bf9a7cb9815`. The [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_self_correction_baseline_v1.py) and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_self_correction_baseline_v1.json) preserve the matched outcomes and scaling data. No test suite was run; direct measurement plus source-level checks are the evidence so far.

**Native full-copy control measured (2026-09-30):** the same self-correction fixture now runs through serialization and a Rust deep-copy branch. All six positive/negative × size parity gates pass on prediction metrics, branch steps/conclusions, and primary graph/rule counts. The expected correct candidate converges 3/3 and the wrong candidate diverges 3/3 at baseline, +1k, and +10k unrelated nodes. Full rehearsal median time is 788.98/800.81 ms for serialization positive/negative at 771 nodes and 3,329.29/3,179.42 ms at 10,771 nodes; the native deep-copy arm is 424.87/384.57 ms and 2,870.60/2,796.89 ms. The runner also verifies unchanged pre-existing primary node values/types, edges, and native rules, with only the allowed durable trace records added. This suggests native copying removes substantial serialization overhead on this fixture, with a smaller relative benefit as unrelated graph size grows. It is not a CoW result, has fixed backend order, and does not measure RSS or prove parity across all audited fields. See the [comparison runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_self_correction_copy_comparison_v1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_self_correction_copy_comparison_v1.json), and staged limits in the [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

**Linux process-CoW thoughtexperiment harness v1 (2026-09-30):** the [source-locked runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_process_cow_self_correction_v1.py) forks the guarded substrate, executes correction in-place in the child, returns the residual over a pipe, and persists a namespaced `ThoughtexperimentReceipt` in the parent without merging branch state. Six size × candidate comparisons at baseline, +1k, and +10k nodes match prediction/residual outcomes against serialization and native deep-copy controls; the positive candidate converges and the wrong candidate remains divergent. All six semantic-parity, expected-control-outcome, and parent-isolation gates pass. Three seeded randomized complete blocks per graph size (seed `20260930`) place both candidate arms and all backends in every repeat block. Process-CoW total medians at baseline/+1k/+10k are 407/439, 700/621, and 2,930/2,881 ms (correct/wrong), against same-run native deep-copy medians 389/394, 587/538, and 2,810/2,764 ms. This is 5–11% slower at baseline, 15–19% slower at +1k, and about 4% slower at +10k. Same-run serialization medians are 786–789, 965–985, and 3,291–3,542 ms. The prototype demonstrates isolated child execution and residual return but does not deliver the large experiment throughput gain required for open-ended exploration. At +10k, the repeated model-before prediction costs 1.16–1.19 s and child private-dirty deltas are 22–107 MB. Source tracing also identifies full-graph focus pruning and repeated full graph serialization for residual extraction as candidate costs. The [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_process_cow_self_correction_v1.json) reports the randomized schedule and timing components.

**Thoughtexperiment throughput target and current boundary:** the stretch target remains at least 10× lower marginal candidate-evaluation time than native full-copy rehearsal on sparse candidates at a fixed primary revision. The new journal path passes exact direct and nested residual gates; it is 3.2×/3.5× faster than full native copy at +10k on the matched self-correction fixture, and 8.4×/9.0× faster than the same scoped branch using whole-graph diff. Thus the readout bottleneck is substantially reduced, but the 10× target against the strongest native-copy control is not yet met. The six-candidate panel also improves potential trials per timed budget by about 11× against legacy readout, but only the original dedup candidate is useful on this fixture. Fixed-revision baseline reuse remains modest at 1.15×–1.27×. Whole-state branch copying, memory cost, and task-distribution coverage remain the primary systems questions; a portable Rust CoW overlay is still unimplemented. See the [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md), [matched residual capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_branch_delta_journal_v1.json), and [candidate-diversity capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_candidate_diversity_v1.json).

**Fixed-revision candidate batch v1 (2026-09-30):** `imagine_candidate_batch` now computes one baseline prediction, suppresses candidate-level primary traces, reuses that baseline on each isolated native deep-copy branch, and verifies the graph/rule digest remains unchanged during the candidate phase. A source-locked [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_candidate_batch_amortization_v1.py) compares this path with standalone candidate trials that recompute the baseline on a fresh matched fixture. Across three randomized blocks, the two-candidate batch exactly matches positive/negative prediction and residual outputs and passes the primary-isolation gate. Median batch versus per-candidate-baseline costs are 626/719 ms at baseline, 950/1,189 ms at +1k, and 4,605/5,863 ms at +10k, for 1.15×/1.25×/1.27× speedups. Baseline reuse helps, but the gain is modest because each branch still copies the full state and runs whole-graph costs; the 10× stretch gate remains unmet. See the [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_candidate_batch_amortization_v1.json).

**Fixed-revision six-candidate batch v1 (2026-09-30):** the batch now reuses one baseline across six candidate rehearsals and guards primary graph/rule isolation using an O(1) native semantic revision token (graph mutation version, rule revision epoch, and rule-slot count), instead of serializing and hashing the whole graph twice. A source-locked comparison uses five randomized measured blocks plus two warmups at 771, 1,771, and 10,771 nodes; exact nested residual/outcome parity, positive/negative controls, and primary-isolation gates pass at every size. Median batch times are 733/769/903 ms versus 1,183/1,328/1,618 ms standalone: 1.61×/1.73×/1.79× more candidate throughput. At 50,771 nodes, a separate three-block capture gives 956 ms versus 1,436 ms (1.50×). All six candidates run, but only the designated dedup fix converges on this task. This confirms a useful fixed-revision batch gain, not a large per-candidate speedup: each candidate rehearsal remains around 0.1–0.16 s and one synthetic family on one host does not establish open-ended task coverage. The revision token covers graph/rule semantic mutation, not every cache, telemetry, or external side effect. See the [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_fixed_revision_six_candidate_batch_v1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_fixed_revision_six_candidate_batch_v1.json), [50k capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_fixed_revision_six_candidate_batch_50k_v1.json), [`Substrate::experiment_graph_rule_revision`](../../../runners/dsl/src/substrate.rs#L1225), and [`TypedGraph::mutation_version`](../../../runners/dsl/src/graph.rs).

**Fixed-revision process-CoW candidate batch v1 (2026-09-30):** five randomized measured blocks plus two warmups compare six candidates under a shared baseline at 771/1,771/10,771 nodes. All cells preserve exact nested residual/outcome rows and pass primary graph/rule isolation. Process-CoW batch medians are 773/1,064/1,042 ms versus native full-copy batch medians 452/662/777 ms, or 0.58×/0.62×/0.75× native throughput. Process is slower in 17 of 18 paired measured blocks across 771–50,771 nodes. At 50,771 nodes, a three-block capture is 1,348 ms process-CoW versus 941 ms native copy (0.70×). To preserve semantics, each child uses process-CoW for the outer candidate branch and native-copy snapshots for chained internal predictive looks; direct in-place prediction changed nested derived node IDs and failed exact parity. This rejects fork-per-candidate as a full-batch optimization on this fixture, but not CoW as a general substrate primitive. A separate setup-only comparison has eight measured blocks plus two warmups and exact focus-view/primary-state parity: native copy plus scope costs 1.17/2.21/3.03/13.06 ms at 771/1,771/10,771/50,771 nodes, versus process fork plus scope/pipe at 3.32/3.25/3.38/6.83 ms. Process setup crosses over only at 50k nodes, where it is 1.91× faster; this setup gain is outweighed in the current candidate batch by full prediction and per-child transfer costs. CoW remains useful beyond this speed comparison as an isolation primitive and as a path to shared immutable bases for future interfaces; the immediate engineering target is native branch-local mutation without process/pipe overhead, with parity for chained looks. See the [batch runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_fixed_revision_process_cow_batch_v1.py), [batch capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_fixed_revision_process_cow_batch_v1.json), [50k capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_fixed_revision_process_cow_batch_50k_v1.json), [branch setup runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_branch_creation_cost_v1.py), and [setup capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_branch_creation_cost_v1.json).

**Thoughtexperiment 50k-node batch extension (2026-09-30):** the six-candidate native batch was extended to 50,771 nodes with three randomized blocks plus one warmup. Exact nested parity and primary isolation pass; latest median batch time is 956 ms versus 1,436 ms for standalone trials (1.50× throughput), with candidate rehearsal medians measured in its capture. The matched process-CoW batch also passes parity and isolation but takes 1,348 ms versus 941 ms native (0.70× throughput). From 10,771 to 50,771 nodes the refreshed native batch rises from 903 to 956 ms; the current scoped task's total rises much less than graph size, so the setup-only fork advantage at 50k does not explain full-batch timing. This materially narrows the optimization target toward predictive-look work, repeated branch-local execution, and process-transfer overhead, while full-state memory and larger-scale behavior remain open. See the [native 50k capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_fixed_revision_six_candidate_batch_50k_v1.json) and [process-CoW 50k capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_fixed_revision_process_cow_batch_50k_v1.json).

### CoW as a general substrate capability (2026-09-30)

The goal is now explicitly broader than accelerating thoughtexperiments. CoW gives isolated mutable branches over a shared immutable revision, supports parallel consumers without multiplying the entire base state, and creates a storage-independent branch contract. Those properties have value even when a particular route is not faster. Future consumers could include solver alternatives, planning, simulations, tool sandboxes, and a vLLM-style shared-prefix interface; the latter is an architectural direction only, with no integration or serving benchmark yet.

The current implementation is partial: `TypedGraph` node/edge slabs use `CowPagedVec`, while query indexes and other `Substrate` state clone eagerly. A source-locked release probe on four directed-path sizes (25 repeats each) passes value/query checks and primary-state isolation. At 50,771 nodes, eager versus shared-slab clone medians are 8.08 versus 2.44 ms; clone-plus-one-attribute-write is 7.75 versus 6.15 ms. This confirms slab sharing can reduce actual graph clone cost, while showing first-write and copied-index costs remain. The matched six-candidate batch still has no stable end-to-end shared-slab advantage. Therefore current evidence supports an architectural capability and a local clone-cost improvement, not a general speed claim or full-state snapshot.

A follow-up source-locked graph v2 probe broadens the fixture to path, star, and grid topologies, at 771/1,771/10,771/50,771 nodes, with clone-only, one-write, and spaced 1%-write arms. Every one of 12 cells passes graph/query parity and divergent sibling plus primary isolation checks (25 repeats per cell). At 50,771 nodes, clone-only shared-slab medians range 1.47–2.42 ms versus 6.45–10.65 ms eager; with 1% writes, 2.27–3.42 ms versus 7.65–10.38 ms. This strengthens the evidence that slab sharing is useful across these synthetic graph shapes and write densities. It still does not measure indexes as CoW, total Substrate forks, memory, or natural workload distributions. See the [v2 capture](../../../evaluation/reports/typed_graph_cow_probe_v2.json) and [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/typed_graph_cow_probe_v2.py).

A separate source-locked allocation probe repeats the 12 graph cells seven times with a counting allocator. Every branch drop restores logical live-byte accounting to the operation's pre-branch baseline; sibling isolation also passes. At 50,771 nodes, eager clone branches retain 8.23–10.22 MiB and shared-slab branches retain 5.84–7.03 MiB, depending on topology. One shared branch write adds 3,936 bytes; 1% spaced writes add about 1.08 MiB. This directly measures heap payload allocation and reclamation for `TypedGraph`, while allocator overhead, RSS/PSS, resident pages, and non-graph `Substrate` memory remain unmeasured. The result quantifies significant remaining copied graph state and strengthens the case for the next index-sharing stage. See the [memory capture](../../../evaluation/reports/typed_graph_cow_memory_probe_v1.json) and [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/typed_graph_cow_memory_probe_v1.py).

The graph-page increment shares per-node adjacency pages in addition to node/edge records. The graph-level source-locked probe passes query parity and sibling isolation over 12 path/star/grid × size cells; at 50,771 nodes it cuts clone time from 7.14–9.62 ms records-only to 0.89–1.58 ms. A counting-allocator follow-up lowers clone-only retained memory from 6.68–7.87 MiB to 3.26–4.70 MiB; every branch returns to its live-byte baseline after drop. This verifies the local engineering hypothesis that adjacency page copies were a major cost. The full six-candidate matched comparison also passes cross-backend exact-output and primary-isolation gates, but at baseline/+1k/+10k it measures graph-page batch medians 879/1,258/981 ms versus records-only 856/878/940 ms. Thus clone and graph-memory gains still do not demonstrate a stable thoughtexperiment throughput gain. The run has three measured repeats and one warmup, one task family, and one host. See the [graph timing capture](../../../evaluation/reports/typed_graph_cow_graph_pages_probe_v1.json), [graph memory capture](../../../evaluation/reports/typed_graph_cow_graph_pages_memory_probe_v1.json), and [compressed end-to-end capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_shared_graph_pages_batch_v1.json.gz).

The follow-up fixes the low-repeat ambiguity: five randomized measured blocks plus two warmups at baseline/+10k/+50k all pass exact cross-backend nested results and primary isolation. At baseline the graph-page arm ties (849 ms; 2/5 wins against eager, 3/5 against records-only). At +10k it is 866 ms versus 1,009 ms eager and 966 ms records-only, winning 5/5 paired blocks (median paired ratios 1.27× and 1.16×). At +50k it is 912 ms versus 1,426 ms eager and 2,030 ms records-only, winning 5/5 (1.65× and 2.15×). This establishes an end-to-end gain on the one synthetic self-correction family tested, and supports the hypothesis that sharing adjacency matters to full candidate throughput. The result remains one host and one task family; RSS/PSS, broad task mix, and independent-host replication remain open. See the [higher-repeat compressed capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_shared_graph_pages_batch_v2.json.gz).

Next gates: measure graph clone components and full-state fork costs; account for changed-page/index allocations and RSS; share indexes through branch-local overlays; validate divergent sibling writes and operation-order/ID parity; define the snapshot/fork/receipt boundary for independent consumers; then test a second consumer such as solver alternatives. The candidate consumer interface should remain independent of page CoW, process CoW, or persistent-map internals.

See the [CoW design](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COW_SUBSTRATE_DESIGN.md), [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md), [actual-graph capture](../../../evaluation/reports/typed_graph_cow_probe_v1.json), and [actual-graph runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/typed_graph_cow_probe_v1.py).

### Responsibility transition

Measure the handoff as a sequence of separately attributable decisions, rather than a binary “autonomous/not autonomous” label:

| Stage | LLM responsibility | Substrate responsibility | Gate to advance |
|---|---|---|---|
| Current object-level learning | Choose task families, budgets, and hypotheses to probe. | Detect residuals, acquire/use representations, and execute held structure. | Show strict held-out benefit or an explicit, costed capability gain against frozen/reset controls; expose complete rung, provenance, and cost reports. |
| Substrate-assisted diagnosis | Select and approve the next diagnostic question. | Report uncertainty, residual class, failure cause, and evidence-backed candidate experiments. | Candidate reports reproduce from state and distinguish task evidence from unsupported conjecture. |
| Co-directed search | Approve a bounded proposal set and sealed evaluation. | Rank hypotheses and allocate search within a fixed budget; produce isolated candidates. | Candidate ranking beats a fixed portfolio/frozen selector under matched cost, with false-accept and refusal limits met. |
| Delegated meta-policy | Audit and veto; retain independent custody and adoption authority. | Select experiments and propose meta-policy changes inside the sandbox. | Repeated held-out gains, harm/forgetting checks, exact rollback, and the applicable upstream P3 gates. |
| Durable authority transfer | Retain external approval until the formal gate permits a defined delegation. | Only after that gate, update bounded learning policy under explicit authority. | No transfer claim before P3-E1 and its required dependencies pass; this stage is not currently eligible. |

For every stage, log who chose the task, hypothesis, experiment, budget, candidate, score interpretation, and adoption decision. Report the fraction of decisions made or changed by each actor, LLM intervention/veto rate, paired held-out performance, false acceptance/mint, forgetting, compute/resource cost, and rollback outcome. A substrate that learns an object-level transform while the LLM chooses the experiment counts as substrate learning, not substrate meta-learning.

1. **Completed local candidate-snapshot and ambiguity gates:** GenMint `DimensionGenerator` and scalar-synthesis Program/IR snapshots transfer exactly under their fixed oracle tasks. The hinge contrast establishes one known false-fit/counterexample/search-cap response, with a manually supplied compact grammar as an upper bound. These panels do not meet project-held split/custody requirements or formal P3-E1. `domains/cegis_faculty.py` additionally implements first-disagreement selection over a caller-supplied ordered family, but that policy has no matched selector comparison and can inspect oracle labels for candidate inputs before choosing which label to add. Next, vary functions, family order, and counterexample locations; compare this baseline with a label-sealed substrate-selected policy and fixed/random controls; add reset/forgetting controls; and adjudicate novelty-axis mint quality ([GenMint results](SUBSTRATE_OFFLINE_CANDIDATE_SNAPSHOT_EFFICACY_V1_RESULTS_20260930.md), [Program/IR results](SUBSTRATE_PROGRAM_IR_CANDIDATE_SNAPSHOT_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20260930.md), [ambiguity results](SUBSTRATE_SYNTHESIS_AMBIGUITY_COUNTEREXAMPLE_V1_RESULTS_20260930.md), [ambiguity protocol](SUBSTRATE_SYNTHESIS_AMBIGUITY_COUNTEREXAMPLE_V1_PREREG_20260930.md)).
2. **Test durability as a supporting component:** preserve the GenMint matched enabled/disabled acquisition curve, then export/restore the learned graph across a process restart before new related tasks. Verify the acquired object is actually selected. Compare acquisition, checkpoint/reload, and application costs; include one-off novelty and shuffled order. Report acquisition success, false mint, route selection, transfer, reuse count to break even, and tail cost. Existing v2/v3 curves are baselines, not unrun pilots.
3. **Define ambiguity behavior and test transfer:** on finite domains, construct training examples consistent with multiple programs that disagree elsewhere. Predeclare whether the system predicts under an explicit prior, refuses, or requests a discriminating example. Measure accepted-answer error, coverage, query/search cost, and false acceptance; test novel functions and unsupported operators. Count distinct functions separately from process repeats.
4. **Compare grammar selection with a fixed portfolio:** under a matched total time/memory budget compare compact-grammar portfolio, broad default, authored selector, proposed learned selector, and oracle grammar. Use neutral paraphrases, misleading trigger words, and counterexamples. Report candidate work and wall time, especially when cap-hit arms are censored.
5. **Move cheap-cut work to representative traces:** synthetic supplied-separator work already confirms conditional factorization. Compare monolithic solving, generic partitioning, substrate-discovered frames, oracle frames, and size-matched random frames on a predefined sample of real workload graphs.
