Charge cut discovery, communication, maintenance, internal solving, and readout; reject cuts that lose cross-frame semantics.
6. **Measure the closure envelope before enforcing it globally:** census active production rules and classify which behaviors fit the certified finite-key fragment. Then define a certified mode with audited install/import/revision/self-mint routes; preserve broader rules under separately measured execution limits. Check environmental assumptions as well as syntax.
7. **Scale only the validated operating routes:** use an independent host and vary graph size, width, frame count, and boundary cardinality. Report CPU, memory, wall time, discovery/maintenance overhead, and completion/refusal. Keep task-family and source-version replication distinct from fresh transfer.

Each gate should be source/task locked and have a preregistered wall-time, memory, and candidate/instance cap before execution. The decision rules are:

| Gate | Minimum evidence to advance the claim | If inconclusive or failed |
|---|---|---|
| Persistent acquisition/reuse | Acquired object is invoked after restart; at least one preregistered transfer set improves strict held-out performance or total cost over matched frozen and memoization controls, without increasing false acceptance beyond its locked limit. | Report acquisition/persistence without learning benefit; diagnose representation/use failure before adding another learner. |
| Ambiguity and selector transfer | Ambiguous cases follow the predeclared predict/refuse/query policy; fresh functions show held-out accuracy and coverage under the locked total budget. | Keep claim to fixed grammar/operator coverage; revise the inference contract, not just the search budget. |
| Natural-workload cheap cuts | Discovered-frame execution preserves oracle semantics and reduces end-to-end total cost against monolithic and size-matched controls on the predefined trace sample. | Report frame factorization as a supplied-structure result only. |
| Certified closure mode | Active-rule census establishes scope; every ingress path in the declared mode enforces the assumptions and bounded closure result. | Keep unrestricted routes outside the theorem and report their measured budgets separately. |
| Independent-host scale | Validated routes complete within preregistered resource limits with semantic parity as dimensions increase. | Narrow the supported operating envelope to the largest passing width/size; do not extrapolate beyond it. |

- Machine check: `python3 scripts/build_substrate_capability_map.py --check` reports 28 rows, 290 source links, 65 linked test references, 513 result-linked runners (546 runner/analyzer references), zero unresolved source links, zero missing evidence, and zero validation errors. Three inventory rows have no linked tests: production visual image ingestion/frame-scoped settling, the thoughtexperiment harness, and planning/action selection.

**Full-process memory accounting for graph-page CoW (2026-09-30):** the new fresh-process runner measures Linux `smaps_rollup` around complete substrate branch creation, a branch-local node insertion, and branch destruction for eager deep-copy, shared-record-page, and shared-record-plus-adjacency-page routes. Across 45 measured processes (three graph sizes × three backends × five repetitions), all primary revision and branch node/edge isolation checks pass. At +50k unrelated nodes, median branch-creation PSS deltas are 11,308 KiB eager, 4,868 KiB shared-records, and 4,448 KiB shared graph pages. At baseline and +10k, the observed deltas are approximately 212 KiB and 1.27 MiB for all three; therefore this measurement does not show a consistent small-graph memory advantage. The +50k graph-page result supports a reduction in process-resident marginal branch memory on this fixture, alongside the earlier allocator and timing captures. Linux process deltas include Python, runtime and fixture effects; small writes do not produce a resolvable `smaps_rollup` delta, post-drop memory can remain in allocator arenas, and this is one host and one synthetic graph family. It is a bounded memory result, not a universal memory or speed claim. See the source-locked [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_full_substrate_pss_v1.py) and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_full_substrate_pss_v1.json).

**Graph-page CoW mutation semantics expanded (2026-09-30):** eager, shared-record, and shared-graph-page `TypedGraph` forks now have source-locked parity evidence for node attribute update/insertion/removal, retyping, edge delete, cascading node delete, freelist ID reuse, edge append, nested branching, and divergent siblings at 771/10,771/50,771 nodes. All nine rows match canonical graph state and pass base, sibling, and nested-branch isolation. This strengthens the case for CoW as a semantic branch capability with future independent consumers. It remains graph-level evidence from one path topology; complete derived-index coverage, arbitrary mutation order, concurrency, full `Substrate` field semantics, and independent-host confirmation remain open. The [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/typed_graph_cow_mutation_semantics_v1.json) and [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/typed_graph_cow_mutation_semantics_v1.py) retain the evidence.

**CoW derived-index parity expanded (2026-09-30):** a v2 mutation evaluation declares attribute indexes across the original/retyped node classes and checks indexed lookups, type membership, edge-type buckets, and incoming/outgoing adjacency after mutation, deletion/reuse, nested fork, and sibling divergence. Eager, shared-record, and shared-graph-page routes match across all nine backend/size rows (771/10,771/50,771 nodes). The result covers selected derived indexes that remain eagerly copied alongside CoW pages; randomized mutation order, additional index layouts/topologies, concurrency, full Substrate-wide auditing, and cross-host confirmation remain open. See the [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/typed_graph_cow_mutation_semantics_v2.py) and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/typed_graph_cow_mutation_semantics_v2.json).

**CoW semantic gate broadened across topology and operation order (2026-09-30):** the v3 capture contains 81 backend × size × topology × schedule rows. Across path, star-augmented, and grid-augmented synthetic graphs at 771/10,771/50,771 nodes, all eager/shared routes agree on graph state and selected derived-index queries under three mutation schedules; primary, nested, and sibling isolation and node-ID reuse gates pass. A no-build replay reproduced semantic rows and source/runner/binary locks. The fixtures share a path backbone, so this supports controlled graph-level robustness, not natural-workload generality or full-Substrate safety. [Runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/typed_graph_cow_mutation_semantics_v3.py) and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/typed_graph_cow_mutation_semantics_v3.json).

**GenMint learned-state restart gate completed (2026-09-30):** 90 fresh-process sequences pass the amended integrity audit. Graph and grammar restore exactly; native rules restore semantically. Intended matrix acquisition succeeds in 25/30 enabled sequences, with a consistent five-cell miss on shearB/adjacent layout. When acquired, the training-made generator is selected after restart and predicts all 400/400 held-out calls exactly; continuous and restart arms have exact semantic parity. Restart incurs about 410 ms extra versus continuous operation; versus induction-disabled restart, the pooled advantage is small (−28.8 ms, 19/30 paired wins; −44.6 ms among acquisition-success pairs). This confirms durable bounded object-level reuse, not general learning benefit or meta-learning. See [results](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_PROCESS_RESTART_REUSE_V1_RESULTS_20260930.md), [amended analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/genmint_process_restart_reuse_v1_1_analysis_20260930.json), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_PROCESS_RESTART_REUSE_V1_PREREG_20260930.md), and [amendment](SUBSTRATE_GENMINT_PROCESS_RESTART_REUSE_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260930.md).

**Generated version-space selector gate (2026-09-30):** the new opt-in synthesis mode collected probe-distinct fitting terms with fixed literals, separating training labels from unlabeled probe inputs. A source-locked five-anchor triangular-number pilot completed at 7,905 term evaluations and a median 0.647 s per anchor. Entropy, fixed-first, and seeded-random each identified the held-out-correct hypothesis on every trial; oracle-best used 10 oracle labels and picked the same `n=2` query. This is no demonstrated selector advantage. The list-variance stress probe timed out at 20 seconds after 102,703 terms, returning an incomplete pool, so the current result also identifies a tractability boundary for generated version spaces. See [results](SUBSTRATE_GENERATED_VERSION_SPACE_V1_RESULTS_20260930.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_GENERATED_VERSION_SPACE_V1_PREREG_20260930.md), [runner](../../../scripts/substrate_generated_version_space_v1.py), and [analysis capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_generated_version_space_v1_20260930_analysis.json).

**Generated Euler active-query gate (2026-09-30):** under three anchors, generated pools of 218–662 arithmetic hypotheses were complete at the configured size bound and each contained a behavior correct on six independent held-out complexes. Repeated entropy queries identified that behavior in 2–3 oracle calls (3/3); fixed-first needed 5–7, seeded random had a median of 4 across 60 runs. The current entropy implementation costs more local compute: median decision plus oracle time was 31.8 ms, versus 3.2 ms fixed-first and 3.4 ms random, because it evaluated 6,026 candidate predictions per run while the local oracle was cheap. This is a measured query-efficiency gain, not an end-to-end efficiency gain. Optimize via prediction caching/incremental entropy and include memory cost before considering CEGIS integration. The timed [v1.1 results](SUBSTRATE_GENERATED_EULER_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_1_RESULTS_20260930.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_GENERATED_EULER_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260930.md), and [analysis capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_generated_euler_active_query_v1_1_20260930_analysis.json) retain the evidence.

**Cached active-query index and branch reuse (2026-09-30):** synthetic alternate-hypothesis branches exposed and fixed a numeric-tolerance mismatch in cached filtering; the corrected [v1.2.1 capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_generated_euler_active_query_v1_2_1_20260930_analysis.json) has exact cached/uncached query and survivor parity on the three Euler anchors. A source-locked serial reuse benchmark then compared 1/5/20 independent hypothesis branches sharing one pool. At one branch the cache is slower (59 ms versus 40 ms); at 5 and 20 branches it is 2.70× and 6.53× faster, with a fixed ~642 KB Python-heap estimate. All 390 branch targets remain in both survivor sets, while 357 become unique and 33 remain tolerance-equivalent. This is in-process symbolic branch evidence, not Rust graph CoW or concurrent serving. See the [corrected Euler results](SUBSTRATE_GENERATED_EULER_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_2_1_RESULTS_20260930.md), [branch-reuse results](SUBSTRATE_QUERY_INDEX_BRANCH_REUSE_V1_1_RESULTS_20260930.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_QUERY_INDEX_BRANCH_REUSE_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20260930.md), and [analysis capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_query_index_branch_reuse_v1_1_20260930_analysis.json). Full graph-level CoW integration, independent host replication, concurrency, and RSS measurement remain open.

## 2026-10-01 update — scoped queue behavior and thoughtexperiment CoW

The scoped-closure measurement now includes a corrected cross-binding deletion probe: when one snapshotted match deletes another match's unique driver edge, the tested scoped-plus-fallback path reaches the same canonical facts as full closure in 27/27 workers; queue counts and traces pass 300/300 gates. Exact wire order agrees in only 9/27, so the report keeps serialization parity distinct from semantic fact parity. The v5 invalid M=1 self-loop and inherited order oracle are explicitly disclosed; v5.1 is the confirmatory capture. This is still one authored cyclic fixture, not a general scope theorem.

Copy-on-write remains a general substrate capability with independent semantic and resource acceptance dimensions. Snapshot/fork isolation and a backend-neutral Rust contract are useful for speculative experiments and future consumers; thoughtexperiment throughput is one use, and a vLLM-style shared-prefix adapter remains a future compatibility target with no integration evidence. Current graph-page and reusable-snapshot evidence supports branch isolation and partial sharing, while full-workload speed remains mixed and substrate/index copies remain. Next capability gates are broader real-consumer integration, concurrent branch lifecycle/cancellation/reclamation, and end-to-end memory/throughput measurement. Detailed design and evidence: [Thoughtexperiment CoW design](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COW_SUBSTRATE_DESIGN.md), [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md), and [v5.1 scoped-drain results](SUBSTRATE_ALL_QUEUE_SCOPED_FALLBACK_V5_1_RESULTS_20261001.md).

The next scoped-closure boundary, where-clause invalidation by an earlier snapshotted binding, now has a corrected source-locked panel. All initially eligible bindings execute in full, scoped-only, and scoped-plus-fallback arms even when earlier effects invalidate later where truth and delete their match edges; 300/300 gates pass, and canonical facts agree in 27/27 workers. The evidence is limited to one attribute predicate and cyclic workload. General predicates, external dependencies, cross-frame scope boundaries, natural workload frequency, and full scope-completeness remain open ([v6 results](SUBSTRATE_ALL_QUEUE_SCOPED_FALLBACK_V6_RESULTS_20261001.md)).

## 2026-10-01 update — reusable snapshot lifecycle in GenMint evaluation

The reusable immutable-base interface now has a second application consumer: acquired GenMint candidates are evaluated through children from `snapshot.fork()`, paired against eager-copy and direct shared-page controls. All 30 workers complete with zero failures and 15/15 analysis checks pass; semantic outputs and isolation match. The fork median is lower in this fixture, but end-to-end validation is not faster, so this is compatibility evidence rather than a performance win. The learning result remains bounded to an authored linear-generator family (25/30 acquisitions; 400/480 candidate held-outs vs 0/480 controls). Solver/planner integration, concurrent lifecycle, cancellation/reclamation, revision conflict/adoption, and a vLLM-style adapter remain open ([results](SUBSTRATE_OFFLINE_CANDIDATE_SNAPSHOT_EFFICACY_V1_1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [CoW design](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COW_SUBSTRATE_DESIGN.md)).

## 2026-10-01 update — reusable snapshot in a native solver

A bounded solver-alternative consumer now forks four CAN perturbations from one immutable snapshot and compares them against eager-copy branches. The corrected v1.1 source-locked panel passes 8/8 gates across 20 fresh processes with exact solver and graph parity and no primary/sibling leakage. Fork timing is lower for CoW in the tiny fixture, but solver timing is higher and the sub-millisecond observations do not support performance claims. This closes only the bounded solver-integration example; it does not establish planning quality, solver optimality, scale, or vLLM-style serving. V1's omitted timing is disclosed, with v1.1 retained as the complete capture ([results](COW_SOLVER_ALTERNATIVES_SNAPSHOT_V1_1_RESULTS_20261001.md)).

## 2026-10-01 update — scaled solver-alternative branches

The CAN snapshot consumer now has an 18-worker scale panel over path/banded graphs with 32, 128, and 256 Concepts. All 72 CoW/eager alternative pairs match exactly and pass fixed-point and isolation checks. At 256 Concepts, both fork routes are about 2.3 ms, compared with roughly 3.8–4.0 s solver settle. Closure time rises steeply across the measured points and is the next bottleneck to attribute; this is not an asymptotic or natural-task claim. A serial v1 attempt ended before producing a capture; only the corrected v1.1 bounded-parallel artifacts support the result ([results](COW_SOLVER_ALTERNATIVES_SCALE_V1_1_RESULTS_20261001.md)).

## 2026-10-01 update — solver closure work attributed

The steep closure cost seen in the 32–256 Concept solver panel is now attributed to native work counters. In both path and banded2 fixtures, candidate tests grow roughly 62× from 32 to 128 concepts and roughly 8× from 128 to 256, while rule enumeration stays near-constant. Per-rule candidate tests exactly reconcile to global counts in 144 branches; local bucket enumeration accounts for about 99.99% of candidate work, led by two Hebbian rules that join the concept population and coactivation links. This is consistent with cubic work on the measured finite sizes, not a complexity theorem or a natural-task distribution claim. The immediate engineering target for this fixture is the repeated join/match work, not its CoW branch fork ([v1.2 attribution](COW_SOLVER_ALTERNATIVES_SCALE_V1_2_RESULTS_20261001.md)).

## 2026-10-01 update — productive join-order validation

The CAN matcher result has now been checked with the production guards and productive mint/reweight effects restored. The source/native-locked 45-worker three-order panel passes all 10 gates: the exact default, sparse-first, and measured-best sequences are verified; productive per-pair outcomes and canonical graph facts agree; all arms reach fixed point; and instrumentation parity and counters reconcile. At N=128,Q=16,256, the measured-best sequence reduces audited candidate tests from 667,968,192 under the default install order to 5,308,096, with 5,396,992 for sparse-first. Median audited closure times are 40.58 s, 1.57 s, and 1.71 s respectively. This supports the measured ordering for this synthetic CAN family, not an online optimizer or general tractability claim. An explicit order-preserving install method was needed because the existing auto-order's tie handling cannot express this sequence from clause permutation; default installation behavior remains unchanged. The next tractability step is replication on another productive rule family and topology, followed by prefix-cost selector measurements including its own overhead ([v2 results](CAN_PRODUCTIVE_JOIN_ORDER_V2_RESULTS_20261001.md)).

## 2026-10-01 update — productive matcher topology sensitivity

The CAN productive-order result has been checked across three matched pair layouts: cyclic, hub-biased, and clustered. The corrected source/native-locked v1.1 replay has 54 fresh workers and passes 12/12 gates, including distinct layout hashes, same-input order arms, production effects, exact outcome and graph parity, terminal closure, and counter reconciliation. Measured-best cuts candidate work by 72.6%–94.0% versus default installation and 0.9%–5.4% versus sparse-first; the latter two orders have almost identical work across the generated layouts. The first replay's protocol said 36 workers while its implemented design ran 54; its failed schedule gate and preserved pilot artifacts are disclosed in the [amendment](CAN_PRODUCTIVE_JOIN_TOPOLOGY_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261001.md). The 54-worker corrected replay is the confirmatory evidence. This strengthens only topology robustness for the same synthetic CAN family. Independent-family, natural-task, and online-selector evidence remain open ([v1.1 results](CAN_PRODUCTIVE_JOIN_TOPOLOGY_V1_1_RESULTS_20261001.md)).

## 2026-10-01 update — independent retinal matcher family

The join-order programme now includes a second production rule family: five-clause retinal percept synthesis with its real epoch guard and productive attribute writes. The v1.1 source/native-locked exhaustive panel passes 13/13 gates in nine workers and checks all 120 clause permutations under uniform, correlated, and rotated support profiles. The rotated profile preserves identical global relation cardinalities and per-relation multiplicity histograms while changing their alignment across columns; default work is 88 candidate tests and the exact offline minimum is 57 (35.2% regret). Across all orders, candidate work ranges up to about 29× between best and worst in the rotated profile. This is evidence that the current global-cardinality heuristic can miss a substantially cheaper exact sequence even when the rule's semantics are preserved. The initial v1 analyzer failed before reporting; its raw data is disclosed as pilot and the corrected v1.1 replay is authoritative ([results](PHOTON_RETINAL_JOIN_ORDER_V1_1_RESULTS_20261001.md)). An online prefix estimator, its overhead, held-out ranking performance, natural inputs, and broader rule coverage remain open.

## 2026-10-01 update — bounded selector held-out result

A bounded-sample prefix selector was evaluated against the captured CAN and retinal panels and on a new N=16 retinal support alignment. It reproduces the measured CAN best order in 22/22 nonempty cases and is exact or within 1.75% of the exhaustive minimum on the original retinal profiles. On the held-out profile it selects 92 candidate tests, compared with 65 exhaustive-best and 72 under the ordinary default order; that is 41.5% regret against best and 27.8% more work than default. The choice and result repeat across three source-locked workers, and 14/14 gates pass for semantics, order coverage, fixed-point closure, and provenance. The Python index-plus-search cost is about 2.33 ms median, while earlier N=12 rule applications took about 0.12–1.40 ms. The first selector is therefore not ready for engine integration. This negative result identifies a remaining estimator problem: local samples and beam pruning can miss a cheaper multi-clause suffix even when historical cases fit. Keep the default as control; investigate calibrated uncertainty or budgeted prefix probes, then measure on a larger held-out set before optimizing the Rust matcher ([results](PREFIX_COST_SELECTOR_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md)).

## 2026-10-01 update — CoW contract and revision guard

Copy-on-write is being treated as general-purpose isolation and lifecycle infrastructure, with speed as an additional measurable benefit. Current Rust CoW results establish graph-page sharing, branch isolation, concurrent closure on a synthetic rule family, and guarded residual adoption on one productive Thoughtexperiment family. A vLLM-style shared-prefix consumer remains a compatibility target; serving-specific ownership, scheduling, cancellation, and reclamation are not implemented.

The adoption revision token now has a closed downstream Rust mutation surface: semantic stores are crate-private, public access is read-only, and supported term/rule/grammar/concept/shape mutations advance the tracked epoch. A full semantic fingerprint was measured at 3.4 ms for 100 and 179.6 ms for 5,000 entries per store, so it was rejected for branch capture/adoption; the tracked token stays near 0.00002–0.00003 ms. The rebuilt, source-locked adoption panel passes 9/9 gates across 15 workers, with exact full-primary parity and stale/conflict rejection atomicity. The mediated Thoughtexperiment path now persists the injected hypothesis, labels assumption nodes/edges as noncommittable, regrounds edge-attribute changes against live before-images, and routes approved writes through the commitment gate; a five-worker, 16-check probe passes ([results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EPISTEMIC_COMMIT_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_epistemic_commit_v1.json)). Remaining work includes deletion/writeback semantics, internal mutation discipline, and concurrent lifecycle behavior. Overall capability work remains active; these results improve isolation, auditability, and selected operating costs, but do not establish general self-improvement or tractable open-ended induction.


**Copy-on-write general-purpose consumer and parallel closure evidence (2026-10-01):** the 45-run native rule panel passes semantic parity, fixed-point, primary/snapshot isolation, and fresh-process gates across 771/10,771/50,771 nodes and fanouts 1/4/16. Independent Rust branches execute closures concurrently; fanout 4/16 reduces median wall time for both shared-page and eager branches. CoW is modestly faster in the measured medians, but this single-host synthetic result does not establish general speedup. PSS is workload-sensitive: CoW is lower at 10,771 nodes with fanout 4/16, but higher at 50,771 nodes for all fanouts after dense edge writes. The rule is not `batch_safe`, so this measures outer branch concurrency, not within-rule batching. This supports CoW as general-purpose isolation/lifecycle infrastructure, including future serving compatibility work, while vLLM-style KV-block reuse, request scheduling, cancellation, and reclamation remain unimplemented and unmeasured. Next measure branch write density/index touch locality, then define a storage-independent shared-prefix adapter contract. See [results](COW_SNAPSHOT_PARALLEL_RULE_CLOSURE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [preregistration](COW_SNAPSHOT_PARALLEL_RULE_CLOSURE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/cow_snapshot_parallel_rule_closure_v1.py), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/cow_snapshot_parallel_rule_closure_v1_analysis.json).


**CoW write-density/locality scale gate (2026-10-01):** 70 fresh processes complete the fixed-size 1/10/50/100% active-edge sweep at 10,771 and 50,771 nodes, with contiguous versus evenly spaced writes where applicable. All 14 cells pass semantic closure, fixed-point, primary/later-fork isolation, and source capture. At 50,771 nodes, CoW fork medians stay around 0.07–0.11 ms versus 11.5–14.2 ms eager; CoW concurrent wall time wins 34/35 paired rows at both graph sizes. Sparse spread writes increase CoW closure time over contiguous writes, and PSS varies by density/layout rather than consistently favoring CoW. The speed gain is principally branch-copy avoidance; execution and memory remain mutation-dependent. This is one ring workload, one host, and one AddEdge rule, not a universal or natural-task claim. See [COW_SNAPSHOT_WRITE_DENSITY_LOCALITY_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md](COW_SNAPSHOT_WRITE_DENSITY_LOCALITY_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [preregistration](COW_SNAPSHOT_WRITE_DENSITY_LOCALITY_V1_PREREG_20261001.md).


## Full Thoughtexperiment reusable-snapshot workload gate complete

The five-repeat +10k/+50k live-harness comparison covers eager, direct shared-page, and reusable snapshot branches with exact outcomes/residual parity across all backends. At +50k, direct and reusable shared-page batch medians are 420 and 423 ms versus 768 ms eager; direct wins 3/5 paired blocks against reusable. Reusable capture costs about 0.28 ms. The exact graph `conclusions` and `derived_only` deltas are empty for all six candidates, so this is real candidate-prediction throughput evidence but not a nonzero graph writeback or touched-page measurement. Next add a productive candidate fixture with a guaranteed nonempty graph residual, then compare extraction and explicit adoption separately under eager/direct/reusable isolation and exact-parity gates. Do not use the empty residual result to claim writeback is cheap. See [v2 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_REUSABLE_SNAPSHOT_BATCH_V2_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [preregistration](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_REUSABLE_SNAPSHOT_BATCH_V2_PREREG_20261001.md).


## Productive residual branch gate complete; adoption remains open

The amended 15-worker panel executes a real hypothesis-triggered native rule across eager, direct shared-page, and reusable snapshot branches at 0/10k/50k unrelated nodes. All 225 executions return the same nonempty total and derived-only residual, change one Fact, add a Conclusion, delete the stale fact, and pass one-step convergence plus terminal no-progress checks. At 50k, five reusable branches take 0.987 ms median plus 0.074 ms snapshot capture, versus 60.072 ms eager and 2.445 ms direct-page batching. This establishes residual production and caller return over reusable branches on one fixture; residual adoption into primary remains a distinct capability. Source review found no guarded graph-delta apply/commit API. Next design a revision-checked atomic adoption path, with stale-revision rejection and exact node-ID/edge state parity, then measure its receipt and apply cost separately. See [productive residual results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_PRODUCTIVE_RESIDUAL_SNAPSHOT_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [base protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_PRODUCTIVE_RESIDUAL_SNAPSHOT_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and the [v1.1/v1.2 amendments](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_PRODUCTIVE_RESIDUAL_SNAPSHOT_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261001.md).

## 2026-10-01 update — atomic residual adoption

`Substrate.apply_experiment_graph_delta` now checks an exact graph/rule revision, validates required before-images and graph references, applies against staged CoW graph/interner state, and publishes only on success. A source-locked 15-worker panel passes 9/9 gates: complete `conclusions` receipts match an independent full-primary delta oracle; stale revisions and malformed before-images reject without changing primary state. After the revision API and edge-attribute receipt changes, a fresh panel measures median apply at 0.380/0.791/1.474 ms for 0/10k/50k unrelated nodes. The measured branch `derived_only` receipt is not always closed because edges may reference injected hypothesis nodes it excludes; the mediated addition/edge-update path now keeps assumptions explicit and noncommittable; deletion and concurrency policy remain open. A separate five-worker source-locked probe now covers added, removed, and changed edge attributes, journal round-trip parity, and malformed before-image atomicity. This is a single productive fixture, not a general merge guarantee. The follow-up audit fixed the missed tracked mutation (`set_rule_area`), closed downstream direct-field bypasses by making semantic stores crate-private, and measured the full-fingerprint cost before retaining the O(1) token. See the [revision-token audit](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_REVISION_TOKEN_AUDIT_20261001.md). A five-worker epistemic-commit probe now verifies assumption-preserving records, stale edge-change refusal, default gate refusal, and explicitly approved writeback. Next cover deletion proposals and concurrent commit atomicity, then continue natural-task and shared-prefix lifecycle evaluation. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RESIDUAL_ADOPTION_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RESIDUAL_ADOPTION_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_residual_adoption_v1.json).


## Overall goal status update (2026-10-01)

The Thoughtexperiment harness has moved from isolated counterfactual execution toward a reviewable proposal lifecycle: exact graph deltas can be represented, regrounded against current state, rejected when stale, and committed behind an explicit gate. The latest mixed-commit probe passes 20/20 checks in each of five fresh workers. A batch containing node attribute change, node and edge addition, and edge and node deletion now passes through the native expected-revision, staged graph-delta transaction. Deterministic interleaving confirms stale-revision rejection; injected late before-image corruption leaves the complete mixed batch unapplied. Together with prior addition/change and edge-attribute probes, the substrate demonstrates useful pieces of residual adoption and epistemic gating on authored fixtures.

This is capability plumbing, not evidence of self-improvement or general capability. Broad mixed-operation receipt coverage, protected-operation policy integration, and deletion-hook parity remain open; one constructed mixed operation batch is covered. The broader goal also lacks evidence that substrate-generated proposals improve held-out task performance, that structural induction reaches useful depth, or that cheap-cut locality is common on natural tasks. Next: expand mixed operation combinations and scale, integrate policy and hooks, add randomized differential/property coverage against an independent graph oracle, measure full reground latency across deletion counts and topologies, then evaluate proposal generation/adoption on held-out task families. Continue shifting responsibility gradually: LLM assistance can author candidate mechanisms and adjudicate plans, while the substrate increasingly generates, tests, ranks, and retains proposals through reproducible harness runs.


The capability-map consistency check currently reports 28 rows, 314 source links, 65 linked test references, 529 result-linked runners, and zero unresolved sources, missing evidence, or validation errors. These inventory counts are not a completeness proof; three rows still lack linked tests.


The incident-edge access-path probe now has a preregistered five-worker capture with exact parity to a full graph-scan oracle. At 50k background nodes, local medians are 0.064–0.106 ms for target degree 4/32, versus 708–710 ms for full serialization. This supports the local accessor path on the synthetic workload; natural topology distributions remain unmeasured. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_INCIDENT_EDGE_REGROUND_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_INCIDENT_EDGE_REGROUND_V1_PREREG_20261001.md).


The first complete-reground scale panel now passes three fresh-worker survivor oracles over chain/star graphs from 1k to 50k nodes. A 50k graph with 128 low-degree deletions takes 5.535–6.193 ms median; deleting a 49,999-edge star hub takes 267.783 ms. This identifies incident-edge aperture as a material cost dimension. The panel is synthetic, has only three workers, and confounds graph size with deletion count in the chain ladder; see [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_FULL_REGROUND_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [preregistration](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_FULL_REGROUND_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md).


A balanced 3×3 reground factorial now separates graph size from deletion count on degree-2 chain targets. Five fresh workers pass all 45 worker/cell oracle checks. At 1/16/128 deletions, medians remain approximately 0.07/0.76/5.9–6.1 ms across 1k/10k/50k node graphs. This is evidence of near-independence from unrelated graph size for this local reground operation under the constructed topology, not a natural-task locality estimate. The 50k/128 maximum worker median reached 7.90 ms; tail characterization is weak. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_FULL_REGROUND_SCALE_V1_1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [preregistration](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_FULL_REGROUND_SCALE_V1_1_PREREG_20261001.md).


A seeded random-sparse conflict panel also passes exact model-oracle parity across five fresh workers, two seeds, and 1k/10k/50k nodes: 100 clean proposals survive and 400 conflicts are rejected at each graph size across node and edge edits/adds/removals. Median reground for 50 proposals is 3.53/4.10/5.57 ms. This is a seeded harness distribution with non-adjacent nonzero-degree targets, not a natural-task sample. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RANDOM_SPARSE_REGROUND_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RANDOM_SPARSE_REGROUND_V1_PREREG_20261001.md).


The randomized mixed-writeback oracle now passes 50 approved full-graph comparisons and 50 paired stale-edge conflict refusals across five fresh workers. Each approval combines node/edge addition, changes, explicit edge removal, and cascading node removal; each conflict arm preserves a concurrent update without applying proposal writes. This isolates commit semantics on manual 64-node receipts and says nothing about whether the substrate generates useful such receipts. Next connect generated rule residuals to the oracle, scale receipt sizes/degrees, and test held-out task utility. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_MIXED_RECEIPT_ORACLE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [preregistration](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_MIXED_RECEIPT_ORACLE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md).


The manual mixed-receipt gap is narrowed by an end-to-end rule-generated probe: five workers and 50 actual `imagine()` trials pass complete semantic-graph oracle parity through default refusal and approved commit. The graph-resident rule produces node change, node/edge addition, edge removal, and cascading node deletion; rehearsal/refusal preserve semantic primary state. This is one authored rule and locality family, not evidence of learned rule quality or natural-task utility. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERATED_RESIDUAL_ADOPTION_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERATED_RESIDUAL_ADOPTION_V1_PREREG_20261001.md).

The receipt edge-order diagnostic narrows the remaining transfer-compatibility question. On the mixed-act fixture, edge-slab order diverges from Direct in 72/80 event states and incident-edge order in 72/80; typed `neighbours`/`in_neighbours` order matches in all 26,968 captured buckets. The only direct `incident_edges()` caller found compares edge maps and is order-insensitive. `graph_to_dict()` remains broadly consumed and its list ordering is observable, so an explicit ordering contract and consumer audit are still required. This is not evidence of task-semantic failure. See [diagnostic results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECEIPT_EDGE_ORDER_DIAGNOSTIC_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md).

The broader consumer audit found an order-sensitive ambiguity path: a name-keyed helper selected the first ownership edge. A source-locked census found 224/8,340 named ownership groups with distinct Microtheory owners. The harvester now carries the selected Concept/Rule node ID into owner resolution and emits explicit owner candidates. A held-out full-boot `sift` continuation passes eight gates through `synthesize_gaps()`: Direct and receipt arms derive the same scale-cancellation implication through the exact `computer_vision` source lineage, while surface-only controls do not. Thus the corrected provenance preserves a real structural transfer across distinct held and donor Concepts. The gap is still a derivation with openness pruning disabled, not evidence of utility or improvement on an external task. One earlier nonempty machine-learning-lineage expectation failed because it encoded the old incorrect name attribution; this is disclosed in the lineage report. Next test normal selector pruning and blinded task utility, audit the new provenance schema consumers, and continue the separate serialized graph-order contract. See the [lineage v2 result](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_HARVEST_SHAPE_RECEIPT_LINEAGE_V2_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [held-out postulate result](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_HELDOUT_POSTULATE_RECEIPT_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md).

CoW is tracked as a general substrate capability: isolated branches, shared-prefix lifecycle, reclamation, and backend-independent branch semantics are useful independently of thoughtexperiment speedups. A vLLM-style shared-prefix adapter is a future compatibility target only; no KV-block implementation, serving integration, or serving-performance claim exists. The next architecture step is to keep branch ownership and isolation semantics independent of graph storage, then specify and measure adapters for concrete consumers. See the [CoW design](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COW_SUBSTRATE_DESIGN.md).

The held-out selector v2 closes the next gate: all nine preregistered checks pass across three fresh workers, with Direct and receipt arms agreeing on graph, shape, and gap outputs. The derivation-only arm still finds the scale-cancellation implication; normal openness pruning removes it because `sound_graph.py` already implements it. The selector-reason follow-up confirms this is the explicit “already in speech codebase” rule and identifies the covered source. This supports selector and receipt consistency, while showing no new gap, utility gain, or self-improvement. The next capability experiment should use a genuinely uncovered held-out candidate and evaluate it against a separately specified task-utility oracle. See the [selector v2 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_HELDOUT_POSTULATE_SELECTOR_V2_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [selector reason protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_HELDOUT_SELECTOR_REASON_V1_PREREG_20261001.md).

An exploratory default-selector scan of all 33 computer-vision postulates finds three retained order-1 gaps from a 24-shape harvested library. Source inspection shows one candidate, reliability-weighted fusion for `vision_language_model`, overlaps existing graph-native fusion in `domains/combinatorial_recognizer.py`; it is a likely selector false-open, not a novel capability. Two generic compositional-generation candidates remain underspecified as task capabilities. A three-worker default-versus-cached panel now passes exact gap-payload parity for all three candidates and semantic graph non-mutation in each worker. Median call times show only a descriptive 4–7% reduction on this fixed panel, with no scaling claim. The next learning gate is semantic selector calibration plus an explicit task utility oracle; a retained gap alone is insufficient. See the [discovery results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_OPEN_GAP_DISCOVERY_CV_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [coverage cache parity results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COVERAGE_CACHE_PARITY_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md).

A source-locked four-case selector calibration replicates that false-open: speaker normalization is correctly closed, diffusion image generation and VAE reconstruction remain open, and implemented reliability-weighted fusion is also left open. The direct cause is known: speech-surface discovery omits `domains/combinatorial_recognizer.py`, the module implementing the fusion operation. This is a selector scope/recall defect, not novel capability evidence. The next change should make implementation-surface lookup domain-aware, preserve this panel as a regression gate, and rerun absent-capability controls. See the [selector calibration report](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SELECTOR_CALIBRATION_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md).

A follow-up measurement-only override scans all 806 Python domain modules. It correctly closes both implemented mechanism cases and leaves both absent controls open across three fresh workers. This justifies a broader lookup experiment, but not yet a production policy: one matching mechanism key does not establish that a compound gap such as full VLM capability is implemented. The visual modality and partial-coverage semantics remain unmeasured. See the [all-domain surface report](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SELECTOR_DOMAIN_SURFACE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md).

The all-domain scope was replayed through actual `synthesize_gaps()` for the held-out VLM postulate. Three workers each retained the Kalman-to-reliability-weighted-fusion derivation with pruning off and pruned it with the normal selector, which resolves the implementation to `domains/combinatorial_recognizer.py`. The earlier open result was caused by the speech-only code surface. This validates mechanism-level closure, not any full VLM or visual-input capability. See the [transfer result](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_VLM_DOMAIN_SELECTOR_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md).

The VLM capability is now decomposed into source-backed facets: image/visual processing, text processing, narrow grapheme-speech association, and acoustic channel fusion exist; joint image-text scoring and zero-shot image classification are unestablished. A source-linked inventory and held-out image-caption benchmark protocol are recorded. The benchmark remains unexecuted until an image-text scorer and dataset custody are specified; no VLM capability claim is made. See the [facet audit and benchmark protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_VLM_FACET_AUDIT_AND_BENCHMARK_V1_20261001.md).

A held-writer Omniglot visual baseline scores 77/80 (96.25%) in three fresh workers on one alphabet using the substrate-backed grapheme Term. This verifies only seen-class/new-writer handwritten-character recognition. The nominal `run_cross_modal` facade aliases grapheme recognition and is not itself cross-modal evidence. FSDD is present under `data/fsdd-current`; only the standalone script's default `/tmp/fsdd/recordings` path is absent. A source- and data-locked run binds 10/10 co-presented pairs and scores 80% held-out read-aloud and 70% dictation across three workers. A paired cyclic-shift control on the same queries scores 0%/0% in all workers. This demonstrates dependence on the association labels for this narrow grapheme↔spoken-digit task; it is not a VLM result. See the [Omniglot result](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_OMNIGLOT_GRAPH_RECOGNITION_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [binding result](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GRAPHEME_AUDIO_BINDING_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), and [permuted-binding result](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GRAPHEME_AUDIO_PERMUTED_BINDING_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md).


## Thoughtexperiment concurrent adoption update (2026-10-01)

The strict-revision adoption gate now has an explicit concurrent-call result: 30 races across three fresh workers, with three same-revision receipts per race, produced exactly one atomic winner and two stale rejections every time. Full graph state matched the winner-only oracle in all races. Median barrier-to-return latency was 0.950 ms on this host, including thread scheduling; Python GIL serialization means this is not a native parallel-throughput result. This strengthens the harness's isolation and guarded writeback evidence. The harness still does not autonomously choose useful proposals, establish task utility, or claim self-improvement. Next gates remain a third task-family receipt replay, a blinded held-out utility oracle for proposal selection, and the broader open capability/tractability stages. See [concurrent receipt adoption results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CONCURRENT_RECEIPT_ADOPTION_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CONCURRENT_RECEIPT_ADOPTION_V1_PREREG_20261001.md).

## Capability inventory revalidation (2026-10-01)

The Markdown capability inventory and generated JSON mirror now pass the current-source builder check with 28 capability rows, 314 resolved source links, 65 linked test references, and 563 runner/analyzer references. The evidence catalog contains 235 result documents, 135 protocols/locks, and 292 raw/analysis records. No source links are unresolved and no validation errors remain. Three rows still have no linked functional tests: visual image ingestion, the Thoughtexperiment harness, and planning/action selection. Thoughtexperiment has extensive standalone measurement runners; the missing test mapping means functional test coverage is not audited, not that the capability lacks all evidence. The JSON mirror hash is `fbfbb099d11019b07deb912395a52d651d17c57b35abf8258ebca9b9ec6307cd`. This verifies inventory structure and link resolution, not completeness or whether linked tests reach the named production paths. See [capability map](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_MAP.md), [generated JSON](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/capability_map.json), and [builder](../../../scripts/build_substrate_capability_map.py).


## Graph-authored correction ranking status (2026-10-01)

The correction composer now fires from its graph-authored Rule seed and emits both an executable correction gate and an Argmin rank Program. A three-worker paired evaluation passes 14/14 gates across six episodes, including explicit rule-store isolation during candidate rehearsals and a third-wave held-out oracle. Both default-order and graph-cost-ranked arms adopt the same correct repair and pass the held-out continuation. The ranked arm tests two candidates versus one and has a 1,178 ms median correction time versus 695 ms for default order. The zero-cost candidate fails, so serialized edit-size cost does not help on this task. This is a bounded implementation result, not learning from accumulated outcomes or general self-improvement. The corrected source-linked capability inventory now includes the result; functional test coverage for the overall Thoughtexperiment row remains unaudited. See [ranking results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GRAPH_AUTHORED_CORRECTION_RANK_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GRAPH_AUTHORED_CORRECTION_RANK_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [updated capability map](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_MAP.md).


## 2026-10-01 update — outcome-informed correction ranking

The outcome-conditioned ranking continuation now passes 8/8 preregistered gates in three workers. Each worker derives typed success/failure outcomes from candidates actually tried during a training episode, then compares cold ranking with ranking on a fresh graph after explicit evidence transport. In all three pairs, transferred outcome history moves the successful `dedup_identity_fix` to first position; median candidate attempts decrease from 2 to 1, and both arms pass the held-out observation-continuation oracle. This is evidence that the graph-authored policy can use outcome fields to alter its ordering, and demonstrates the intended gradual split: the outside process transports records while the substrate evaluates the policy and chooses order. Scope remains narrow: same authored correction family, hand-defined utility attribution, and explicit transport. It does not establish autonomous self-improvement, broad transfer, or general capability. Next test independently labeled outcomes across fresh correction families and measure false attribution/noise sensitivity. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CORRECTION_OUTCOME_RANK_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CORRECTION_OUTCOME_RANK_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20261001.md).


## 2026-10-01 update — correction candidate breadth audit

A read-only, source-locked audit enumerated candidate sets for all 159 active rules in the standard core fixture, identically across three fresh processes; all five gates pass and enumeration preserves graph/rule state. It finds 544 candidate instances but only one task-shaped repair (`dedup_identity_fix` on `sd_mint_quantum`). Generic syntactic candidate generation across many rules does not amount to validated correction capability. This confirms that the current learning result is narrow and that a cross-family study first needs a second independently useful correction family with an external task oracle. No candidate was applied, so the audit makes no claim about convergence, preservation, or adoption safety. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CORRECTION_CANDIDATE_SURFACE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CORRECTION_CANDIDATE_SURFACE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md).


## 2026-10-01 update — independent recurrence correction task

The second task-shaped thoughtexperiment passes 7/7 gates in three fresh processes. A controlled single-guard fault in production-authored `mrl_offer_due_once` yields 75 repeated offers for one recurrence candidate. Restoring the original rule only inside a full-copy branch yields one offer and a fixed point, while complete primary graph and rule parity remain exact. This validates a second independent task oracle and proves the experiment branch can rehearse a recurrence-ledger repair without touching the primary. The repair is supplied from the known source rule; no autonomous proposal was discovered, and the general correction ranker has not yet demonstrated transfer to this family. Next integrate this candidate class into a cross-family outcome-transfer study, preserving separate semantic oracles for both domains. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_OFFER_IDEMPOTENCE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_OFFER_IDEMPOTENCE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md).


## 2026-10-01 update — cross-family correction evidence reaches the policy

The correction rank policy now has an authored shared class (`idempotent_mint_guard`) spanning shape-axis deduplication and recurrence-offer uniqueness, with a task-shaped candidate for each family. In the amended three-worker panel, all 9 gates pass: each transfer graph ranks with one success imported from the other target rule, while cold controls have zero; both arms preserve the primary and pass the independent recurrence oracle. There is no observed utility improvement: the structural-cost rank already chooses the recurrence repair first, so both conditions use one trial. This is cross-rule evidence consumption, not evidence of better decision quality, autonomous policy invention, or autonomous record transport. A development probe revealed the floor effect, so the final panel tests prior evidence in the trace and reports unchanged ordering; see the amendment. Next select a task with a preregistered cold-policy error before testing whether outcome history repairs it. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CROSS_FAMILY_IDEMPOTENCE_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CROSS_FAMILY_IDEMPOTENCE_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CROSS_FAMILY_IDEMPOTENCE_TRANSFER_V1_AMENDMENT_20261001.md).


## 2026-10-01 update — outcome history corrects a cold ranking error

A staged cross-family panel now measures an actual decision difference. The shape-axis correction trains an explicitly authored `idempotent_mint_guard` outcome class; a distinct recurrence-ledger task then presents an under-scoped episode-level guard and a candidate-level repair. In three fresh workers, the cold graph ranker chooses the smaller under-scoped option, which creates offer counts `[1, 0]` after the second candidate arrives and fails the independent task oracle. It then finds the correct repair on attempt two. The experience-informed policy carries one success across target rules, selects the candidate-level repair first, and passes `[1, 1]` on attempt one. All 9/9 gates pass, with primary-state isolation preserved. This establishes a narrow search-cost benefit for explicit outcome transfer (two trials to one) under authored shared classes and host-managed record transfer. The task staging amendment addresses a discovered same-pass matching effect; the exploratory simultaneous-candidate probe was not the confirmatory result. This remains a controlled two-family demonstration, not autonomous concept invention, utility labeling, or general self-improvement. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CROSS_FAMILY_IDEMPOTENCE_TRANSFER_V2_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CROSS_FAMILY_IDEMPOTENCE_TRANSFER_V2_PREREG_20261001.md), and [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CROSS_FAMILY_IDEMPOTENCE_TRANSFER_V2_AMENDMENT_20261001.md).

## 2026-10-01 update — production-persisted outcomes inherited by thoughtexperiment forks

A source-locked three-worker evaluation now calls the normal `scan_and_self_correct` production hook and observes its graph-resident outcome records. The cold branch, forked before training, sees no history and spends two trials on the staged recurrence oracle. A trained branch inherits the automatically persisted success and failure rows, ranks the correct candidate first, and passes in one trial; all 11 gates pass with branch isolation intact. This removes host-side outcome construction and transport from the demonstrated path and yields a narrow search-effort benefit. Candidate classes, repairs, task oracle, and experiment orchestration remain authored or host controlled, so the result is not autonomous self-improvement or broad general capability. Next evaluate multiple held-out correction families, label/noise robustness, negative transfer, automated experiment choice, and cost per useful update. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_AUTOMATIC_OUTCOME_PERSISTENCE_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_AUTOMATIC_OUTCOME_PERSISTENCE_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [source-linked capability map](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_MAP.md).

## 2026-10-01 update — one false outcome can overturn useful ranking transfer

A preregistered three-worker sensitivity panel starts with the actual two outcomes persisted by production correction, then adds one deliberately misattributed positive result for the tempting episode-scoped guard in a treatment branch. All 9 gates pass. Authentic experience selects the candidate-scoped repair and succeeds in one trial; the single false positive moves the bad guard first, fails `[1, 0]`, and requires the fallback repair on trial two. This establishes a concrete robustness gap in class-level outcome ranking, not a natural noise-rate estimate. Record confidence, corroboration, negative transfer, and dose-response are now priority learning gates. Existing selected mechanism tests are linked in the capability inventory, but end-to-end thoughtexperiment harness coverage remains unaudited. See [contamination results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_OUTCOME_CONTAMINATION_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_OUTCOME_CONTAMINATION_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [capability map](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_MAP.md).

## 2026-10-01 update — noise dose-response identifies a one-record ranking threshold

A five-condition evaluation over three fresh workers passes all 9 gates. Clean graph-resident experience chooses the useful repair in one trial. One false positive on the competing guard flips the first choice; a second false positive adds another unit of evidence but does not further change the two-trial task outcome. Reclassifying the true repair success as a failure has the same adverse effect. All branches recover through the second candidate and preserve primary state. This quantifies sensitivity on the authored task: outcome-class counts currently have a one-record decision threshold here, with no observed robustness from count corroboration. It motivates, but does not yet test, a confidence-aware graph-authored ranking policy. This does not estimate natural label-noise prevalence or establish learning beyond this task pair. See [dose-response results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_OUTCOME_NOISE_DOSE_RESPONSE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_OUTCOME_NOISE_DOSE_RESPONSE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [capability map](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_MAP.md).

## 2026-10-01 update — TypedGraph CoW measured at 200k nodes

The preregistered no-rebuild release extension covers 100k/200k path, star, and grid cells with five repeats each. All six pass exact graph-query parity and divergent-sibling/unchanged-primary checks, and the pre-run binary/source lock matches the raw capture. Shared-slab clone beats eager clone by 1.53–1.98×; clone plus a single attribute write by 1.26–1.80×; clone plus spaced 1% writes by 1.33–1.59×. Shared clone plus 1% writes still takes up to 48.2 ms at 200k. This extends only TypedGraph node/edge slabs on empty-attribute synthetic graphs; it does not establish full-Substrate scaling, memory residency, closure cost, or an asymptotic law. Historical 50,771 results are not pooled because the Rust source hashes differ. See [results](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_LARGE_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_LARGE_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/typed_graph_cow_large_scale_v1_lock_20261001.json), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/typed_graph_cow_large_scale_v1_raw_20261001.json).

## 2026-10-01 update — full-process snapshot fork memory at 200k graph nodes

A source/native-locked 100k/200k extension across eager, shared-record, and shared-graph-page backends completed 18 fresh workers. All branch writes are isolated and parent graph/rule revisions remain unchanged. At 200k, full-process fork PSS increases by median 57.3/26.3/1.7 MiB for eager/shared-record/shared-graph-page; graph-page sharing reduces this live-fork delta about 33× versus eager in the fixture. At 100k, the corresponding deltas are 26.5/10.9/0.31 MiB. After branch drop and GC, PSS remains near the fork delta, so prompt reclamation is not established. This includes the normal Python/native runtime and realistic loaded graph state, but candidate prediction and inference do not execute. It is one synthetic family and one host, not full operation cost or a general memory law. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_FULL_SUBSTRATE_MEMORY_LARGE_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_FULL_SUBSTRATE_MEMORY_LARGE_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_full_substrate_memory_large_scale_v1_lock_20261001.json), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_full_substrate_memory_large_scale_v1_raw_20261001.json).

## 2026-10-01 update — memory sampled during active thoughtexperiment rehearsal

The source/native-locked active rehearsal panel passes all five amended gates over 12 fresh processes at 100k/200k unrelated graph nodes. The actual positive self-correction path predicts and reads out a residual. Rules, source observations, and noise nodes/edges remain unchanged; the normal durable trace additions to the primary are allowed. Sampled peak-minus-baseline PSS is 98.3 versus 10.2 MiB (scoped native deep-copy versus shared graph pages) at 100k, and 190.8 versus 14.9 MiB at 200k. The reductions are about 9.7× and 12.8× in this fixture. A 10 ms sampling interval can miss short peaks and makes wall-time figures instrumented, so no runtime advantage is claimed. The first attempt's overstrict zero-edge-growth gate and raw capture are disclosed; the amendment restores the preregistered task-input invariants. This is one positive correction family, with no negative arm, candidate batch, natural graph, or second host. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ACTIVE_REHEARSAL_MEMORY_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ACTIVE_REHEARSAL_MEMORY_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ACTIVE_REHEARSAL_MEMORY_V1_AMENDMENT_20261001.md), and [capability map](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_MAP.md).

## 2026-10-01 update — active rehearsal memory across positive and negative candidates

A source/native-locked 2×2×2 panel (100k/200k graph sizes, positive/negative candidate, scoped deep-copy/shared graph pages) passes 6/6 gates over 24 fresh processes. The positive candidate converges in all 12 runs; the matched nonconverging candidate fails in all 12 without being inert. Backend outputs match, and task inputs/rules remain unchanged. Scoped deep-copy peak PSS delta is about 98.3 MiB at 100k and 190.8 MiB at 200k for either candidate. Shared graph pages range from 8.7–10.2 MiB and 13.8–14.5 MiB respectively. This indicates that, on this one positive/negative pair, branch backend drives nearly all peak growth; it does not characterize failed candidates broadly or natural workloads. The 10 ms current-PSS sampler can miss brief peaks, and instrumented timings are not used for speed claims. Next transfer the memory stages to the independent recurrence-ledger family and a batched candidate workload. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_REHEARSAL_MEMORY_OUTCOME_FACTORIAL_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_REHEARSAL_MEMORY_OUTCOME_FACTORIAL_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [capability map](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_MAP.md).


## Thoughtexperiment harness memory status (2026-10-01)

The next cross-family memory step is complete: a recurrence-ledger repair branch was run at 100k and 200k graph nodes on native full-copy and shared-graph-page backends, three fresh processes per cell. All 6/6 gates passed, including exact one-offer/fixed-point task oracles, backend semantic parity, and primary graph/rule/revision preservation. Median sampled peak ΔPSS was 32.11/62.94 MiB for full-copy and 2.68/4.05 MiB for shared pages at the two sizes. This indicates that the current harness can isolate and correctly execute a known repair while substantially reducing branch memory on this controlled recurrence shape. It does not establish autonomously found repairs, natural workload frequency, concurrent throughput, or general CPU cost; 10 ms sampling yielded 3–5 samples per run. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_REPAIR_MEMORY_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_REPAIR_MEMORY_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_repair_memory_v1.py), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_repair_memory_v1_raw_20261001.json).


## Thoughtexperiment memory topology follow-up (2026-10-01)

A 36-process panel repeats the known recurrence repair on equal-cardinality path, star, and balanced-tree noise graphs at 100k/200k nodes. All 6/6 gates pass. Shared-page branches have lower sampled memory in every cell; at 200k the median peak ΔPSS is 4.05 MiB (path), 6.30 MiB (star), and 3.66 MiB (balanced tree), against 58.82–62.95 MiB for full-copy. The star/tree difference suggests adjacency layout can affect shared-branch memory despite matched graph counts, but 3 repeats and 10 ms sampling on one host make it preliminary. It improves the harness’s scaling profile; it does not advance autonomous candidate discovery or natural-task capability evidence. Next use randomized degree-preserving graphs and simultaneous branch waves. The initial run omitted a native-binary pass/fail check and is preserved as a diagnostic; the confirmatory rerun adds and passes that lock gate ([amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_TOPOLOGY_MEMORY_V1_AMENDMENT_20261001.md)). See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_TOPOLOGY_MEMORY_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_TOPOLOGY_MEMORY_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_topology_memory_v1.py), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_topology_memory_v1_raw_20261001.json).


## Concurrent thoughtexperiment wave status (2026-10-01)

Task-shaped concurrent rehearsal now has direct evidence. Across 24 fresh processes, eight backend/fanout cells pass exact correction, per-candidate offer, fixed-point, sequential/concurrent parity, primary isolation, and source/native lock gates. Shared-page branches reach 73.6 rehearsals/s at fanout 8 versus 41.3/s sequential (1.78×), with 65.6 MiB peak ΔPSS; full-copy concurrent throughput is lower than sequential and uses 164.7 MiB at that fanout. This is 64 repeated candidates for one authored recurrence rule on one 100k-node synthetic graph; native closure runs concurrently but Python-exposed fork calls serialize. Host has 24 logical CPUs, while cgroup quota was unavailable. It demonstrates that CoW can support higher experiment throughput in this measured workload, not general task coverage or autonomous search. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_PARALLEL_WAVE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_PARALLEL_WAVE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_parallel_wave_v1.py), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_parallel_wave_v1_raw_20261001.json).


## Cross-family thoughtexperiment concurrency update (2026-10-01)

The recurrence task’s concurrency result does not generalize to a Python-heavy shape-decoding correction rehearsal. In a 48-process source/native-locked panel, all task and isolation gates pass. Shared graph pages still materially lower time and peak memory than scoped full-copy: at fanout 8, 7.80 vs 2.77 rehearsals/s and 116.2 vs 829.2 MiB ΔPSS. Yet threaded execution is slower than sequential for both backends at every fanout; shared-page concurrent/sequential throughput is 0.87× at fanout 8. This is useful negative evidence: CoW improves branch cost, but parallel throughput depends on the complete workload path. The panel does not yet locate whether Python orchestration, graph readout, prediction, or memory pressure dominates. Next instrument those phases and evaluate a native-orchestration path. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_PARALLEL_WAVE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_PARALLEL_WAVE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_shape_decoding_parallel_wave_v1.py), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_shape_decoding_parallel_wave_v1_raw_20261001.json).

## Shape-rehearsal phase attribution update (2026-10-01)

The follow-up six-process phase profile passes all six gates and locates the major measured single-rehearsal costs. On the 100k-node shape-axis task, shared pages reduce median branch snapshot/setup from 37.9 to 10.2 ms and model-after prediction from 162.9 to 68.1 ms; bounded forward look accounts for 149.0/51.0 ms of those prediction phases. Candidate patching remains about 19–20 ms. These figures explain where backend savings occur, but do not explain the cross-family threaded slowdown because this profile runs sequentially and does not measure GIL contention or concurrent phase overlap. The source-linked inventory has 28 capability rows and now validates 331 source links, with no unresolved source links, missing evidence, or map validation errors. Next instrument concurrent phase overlap and compare Python orchestration against native orchestration while retaining the same task oracle. See [phase-profile results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_PHASE_PROFILE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_PHASE_PROFILE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_shape_decoding_phase_profile_v1.py), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_shape_decoding_phase_profile_v1_raw_20261001.json).


## Shape-decoding concurrency profile update (2026-10-01)

A locked fanout-8 CPU/phase panel passes all seven gates. Across both backends and modes, summed worker `thread_time_ns` remains approximately equal to wave wall time (0.98–0.99×), suggesting the Python-heavy rehearsal does not exploit the host’s 24 allowed CPUs for simultaneous CPU work. During concurrent waves, shared-page median per-branch snapshot/setup and prediction phases expand from 8.9/60.3 ms to 116.1/637.1 ms. This is strong symptom-level evidence of serialization/contention, but does not distinguish GIL-held orchestration from native locks or memory/allocator contention. Next run GIL-aware profiling and collect native contention counters, then assess a native batch API against identical semantic gates. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_CONCURRENCY_PROFILE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_CONCURRENCY_PROFILE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_shape_decoding_concurrency_profile_v1.py), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_shape_decoding_concurrency_profile_v1_raw_20261001.json).


## GIL-bound cycle intervention status (2026-10-01)

The cycle binding now releases the GIL during `tick_graph_then_struct` while holding its native branch mutex. The source/native-locked v2 replay passes every semantic and isolation gate, but aggregate thread CPU/wall remains at approximately 1.0× and concurrent throughput does not improve. This falsifies the claim that this single GIL-held native cycle was sufficient to explain the whole pipeline serialization. Snapshot creation, candidate rule patch, Python frontier traversal/readout, native mutexes, and allocator contention remain candidate contributors; the current evidence does not isolate them. System perf events are denied by host policy. Next gather in-process Python/GIL attribution or move the complete hot orchestration into native code and replay the same task oracle. See [v2 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_CONCURRENCY_PROFILE_V2_RESULTS_20261001.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_CONCURRENCY_PROFILE_V1_AMENDMENT_20261001.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_shape_decoding_concurrency_profile_v2.py), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_shape_decoding_concurrency_profile_v2_raw_20261001.json).

## 2026-10-01 update — Python/native attribution narrows, but does not resolve, concurrency cost

The amended source/native-locked shape-decoding profile passes all 10 gates. Isolated sequential rehearsals attribute about 29.5%/70.5% Python/native self CPU for scoped full-copy and 49.9%/50.1% for shared graph pages. This identifies the isolated call profile, with `fork_full_copy_experiment` and rule export among the largest full-copy native costs. Concurrent fanout-8 remains near one CPU-ms per wall-ms (0.975× full-copy, 0.986× shared pages), and concurrent execution is slower than sequential. Concurrent cProfile attribution was excluded after profiler collisions and invalid cross-thread timer totals; see the explicit diagnostic and amendment. The one-call GIL-release intervention is therefore insufficient to explain the full pipeline. Next compare equivalent Python and native orchestration over the complete branch lifecycle while retaining outcome, parity, isolation, and provenance gates. The overall goal remains active; broad tractability and general-capability claims still require multiple task families and scaling evidence. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_PYTHON_NATIVE_PROFILE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [profile scope amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_PYTHON_NATIVE_PROFILE_V1_AMENDMENT_20261001.md), and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

## 2026-10-01 update — branch setup GIL release unlocks full-copy parallelism

The v3 same-task 12-process replay passes all 7 semantic, isolation, and provenance gates after releasing the GIL around native experiment forks and aperture setup. At synchronized fanout 8, scoped full-copy concurrent wall time falls from v2's 3.07 s to 1.62 s and aggregate worker CPU/wall rises from 0.98× to 2.00×; sequential timing is essentially unchanged. Shared graph pages remain near 1.21 s and 1.01× CPU/wall, so the gain is backend-specific. The result identifies a tractable scaling regime for costly isolated native copies on this 100k-node shape-decoding workload; it does not establish general speedup or solve the shared-page Python patch/prediction ceiling. Next determine why shared-page branches do not overlap, then expand across sizes and candidate families. See [v3 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_CONCURRENCY_PROFILE_V3_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

## 2026-10-01 update — native branch rule patch cuts avoidable serialization cost

A 24-process source/native-locked comparison passes all 8 gates. The opt-in Rust method resolves and replaces an active rule by name inside the isolated branch, avoiding Python export and decoding of the complete rule store. Outcome signatures match exactly across old/new patch routes and both backends/modes; primary state remains unchanged. Concurrent patch phase improves 7.5× for full-copy and 6.5× for shared graph pages. Total wave time improves 14% and 19%, respectively. Shared-page CPU/wall remains ~1.03×, so the prior GIL release plus this patch optimization reduces latency but does not remove its prediction/orchestration concurrency ceiling. A separate 36-process semantic panel then passed replacement, removal, absent-target, mixed, later-slot, and multi-rule controls on both backends. The native route is now the default while the Python export implementation remains available as a comparison control. Next measure prediction/readout and expand candidate/task/size coverage. See [v4 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_RULE_PATCH_V4_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

## 2026-10-01 update — native rule-patch path becomes default after parity gate

The follow-up six-case, two-backend semantic panel passes all six gates across 36 fresh processes, with exact paired outcomes for replacement, removal, absent names, mixed edits, later slots, and two-rule patches. Expected application/inertness and unchanged primary graph/rule state also pass. `imagine_and_check_convergence` now defaults to the direct native name-based patch; `_native_rule_patch=False` retains the full Python export/scan route as a control. This decision is supported for the exercised candidate forms and still needs broader rule-dialect and natural-task validation. See [semantic results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_NATIVE_RULE_PATCH_SEMANTICS_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

## 2026-10-01 update — native rule-store summary unlocks shared-page rehearsal throughput

The amended source/native-locked 12-process profile passes all 10 gates. Replacing repeated full rule-store export and Python scanning inside detached prediction branches reduces the shape-decoding shared-page eight-branch wave from 961 to 430 ms sequentially and 880 to 388 ms concurrently. The concurrent wave is now slightly faster than sequential, with modest 1.09× worker CPU/wall overlap. Model-after prediction and bounded forward look both shrink materially. Full-copy improves as well and reaches 2.70× overlap concurrent. This changes the prior assessment: a shared-page path can achieve useful concurrent throughput after redundant setup serialization is removed, although the measured CPU overlap remains modest and evidence is one task/size/host. Next test scaling across graph sizes and candidate diversity. See [profile results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_PYTHON_NATIVE_PROFILE_V3_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

## 2026-10-01 update — measured size-dependent thoughtexperiment backend regimes

The 60-process 0–200k-node panel passes all 9 gates across positive/negative candidates, sequential/concurrent modes, and full-copy/shared-page branches. Full-copy reaches 1.77× positive and 1.99× negative concurrent batch speed over sequential at 200k, with 2.74–3.04× summed worker CPU/wall. Shared pages retain much lower absolute latency (465 ms concurrent vs 1,573 ms full-copy for the positive arm at 200k) but remain 17–31% slower concurrent than sequential, with CPU/wall near 1×. The evidence supports two distinct operating points: low absolute branch latency from shared pages and increasing CPU-parallel throughput from expensive full copies. This is one scoped synthetic topology and two candidates; memory cost, other graph families, natural tasks, and >200k remain open. See [scale results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

## 2026-10-01 update — aperture boundary, not node count alone, determines local cost

The topology panel passes all 10 gates across 60 fresh processes. At 100k unrelated nodes, path and observation-spoke graphs have nearly identical total edge counts, but the spokes expose all noise nodes through the three focus observations. Shared-page sequential wave latency rises from 311 to 9,132 ms; concurrent execution takes 1,683 ms and reaches 6.47× aggregate worker CPU/wall. Setup and prediction costs rise in proportion to the exposed interface. Disconnected path/star/grid/random topologies remain substantially cheaper. This is direct synthetic support for boundary locality as a tractability condition, while the extreme spoke stress case does not estimate natural boundary prevalence or prove bounded treewidth. Next evaluate intermediate cut sizes and realistic nested frames. See [topology results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_TOPOLOGY_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).
