## 2026-10-01 update — fixed-size boundary sweep traces the locality cost curve

An 11-gate, 60-process intervention replaces path edges with focus-to-noise spokes at 0–99%, holding graph size exactly fixed at 100,771 nodes and 100,743 edges. Shared-page sequential positive wave latency grows from 305 ms at 0% to 5,851 ms at 99%; concurrent latency grows more slowly, from 407 to 1,154 ms, while summed worker CPU/wall rises from 1.07× to 5.81×. Concurrent shared-page execution changes from slower than sequential at 0% to 5.07× faster at 99%. This strengthens the evidence that exposed boundary work predicts cost and available parallel throughput in this synthetic pipeline. Edge replacement also fragments the original path; nested-frame and additional-task validations remain necessary. See [boundary results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SHAPE_DECODING_BOUNDARY_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md) and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

## Recurrence repair wave v2: second task-family concurrency replication (2026-10-01)

A pre-registered source/native-locked recurrence-repair run completed 24 fresh processes and passed all six gates, including exact sequential/concurrent branch-signature parity, all candidate-level offer oracles, unchanged primary state, and valid measurements. At fanout 8, shared graph pages process 1.72× as many branches per second concurrently as sequentially, with 2.40× aggregate thread CPU/wall and 85.2 MiB sampled peak ΔPSS. Full-copy concurrent execution is slower than sequential at all fanouts, stays near 1× CPU/wall, and reaches 261.0 MiB sampled peak ΔPSS. This replicates a backend-dependent concurrency benefit in a second task family; the GIL-release change does not unlock full-copy parallelism in this task. The shape-decoding family has a different performance profile, so the evidence now argues for workload-specific measurements rather than a blanket throughput claim. Generalization to natural tasks, independent hosts, and autonomous candidate discovery remains unproven. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_PARALLEL_WAVE_V2_RESULTS_20261001.md), [preregistration](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_PARALLEL_WAVE_V2_PREREG_20261001.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_parallel_wave_v2.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_parallel_wave_v2_lock_20261001.json), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_parallel_wave_v2_raw_20261001.json).

## Recurrence repair graph-size scaling v1 (2026-10-01)

The source/native-locked 48-worker factorial passes all six gates at 0/50k/100k/200k added noise nodes, two branch backends, and fanouts 1/8, with exact branch parity and unchanged primary state. At fanout 8, shared-page latency rises only 1.40× from the zero-noise to 200k-noise fixture (75.3→105.5 ms), and concurrency remains 1.64–1.75× faster than sequential. Full-copy concurrent latency rises 15.2× (72.2→1,094.6 ms); at 200k it is 10.4× slower and uses 3.2× more sampled peak PSS than shared pages. This shows a useful shared-page operating regime for one fixed, authored recurrence task through 200k unrelated nodes; it does not estimate candidate growth, real-task prevalence, or a formal asymptotic bound. Candidate-count scaling, a third independently authored task family, and independent-host replication remain next. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_scale_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_scale_v1_lock_20261001.json), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_scale_v1_raw_20261001.json).

## Recurrence candidate-count scaling v1 (2026-10-01)

A source/native-locked 48-worker factorial passes all seven gates at 1/16/64/128 authored candidates, two backends, and fanouts 1/8. The graph stays fixed at 100k noise nodes and branch readout uses a single-pass key index. At fanout 8 with shared pages, sequential time grows 24.9→216.1 ms from one to 128 candidates, while concurrent grows 38.1→72.0 ms; measured concurrent throughput rises from 0.65× to 3.00× of sequential, and summed CPU/wall reaches 3.90×. Full-copy concurrency stays slower, with about 251–265 MiB sampled ΔPSS. This establishes bounded parallel capability for a larger authored candidate set; candidates share one repair structure, so this is not semantic diversity or autonomous induction evidence. Third-family and independent-host replication, candidate-quality diversity, and lifecycle attribution remain open. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_CANDIDATE_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_CANDIDATE_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_candidate_scale_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_candidate_scale_v1_lock_20261001.json), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_candidate_scale_v1_raw_20261001.json).

## Confidence-aware correction ranking: no measured gain after verified negative (2026-10-01)

The source/native-locked three-worker policy comparison executes with 10/11 gates passing. All integrity, source/native, task-oracle, branch-parity, and primary-isolation gates pass. The failed expected discriminator is retained: after a controlled unverified positive for the known-bad recurrence guard was contradicted by an independently measured [1,0] oracle outcome and that negative was recorded, the current raw-count graph policy already switched to the candidate-scoped repair. A branch-local Beta(1,1) smoothed-mean graph Program made the same selection; both passed [1,1] in one trial. This is a negative treatment result, not evidence of a new learning advantage. It supports a narrow claim that verified negative evidence affects current ranking under this task's candidate costs. The harness supplied both the unverified positive and the verified negative record; normal production outcome persistence does not yet establish autonomous independent utility adjudication. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CONFIDENCE_RANK_POLICY_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CONFIDENCE_RANK_POLICY_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CONFIDENCE_RANK_POLICY_V1_AMENDMENT_20261001.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_confidence_rank_policy_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_confidence_rank_policy_v1_lock_20261001.json), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_confidence_rank_policy_v1_raw_20261001.json).

The four latest thoughtexperiment studies had been summarized in the map's introductory notes but were absent from the canonical Thoughtexperiment capability row. The row now links the recurrence wave, graph-size, candidate-count, and confidence-policy results to their protocols, runners, locks, raw captures, and the confidence amendment. The rebuilt inventory now catalogs 169 evidence references for this capability row (up from 160), with zero unresolved source links, missing evidence, or validation errors across the full 28-row map. ## Live-checkout status and thoughtexperiment capability boundary (2026-10-01)

The current capability-map check passes with 28 rows, 333 source links, 67 linked test references, and 546 result-linked runner/analyzer references. It reports no unresolved links, missing evidence, or validation errors. Thoughtexperiment is no longer among the rows without linked tests; production visual ingestion and planning/action selection remain without linked tests. This is an inventory-consistency result, not proof that each linked test reaches the full production path or that evidence spans representative workloads. The current generated JSON hash is `e25a6ef0ee5fbe6efdf259f83e2cbbff2445847c24a1f9bafe9bb4d907b46916`.

The Thoughtexperiment process now has evidence for the whole proposal lifecycle on bounded, authored fixtures: isolated candidate branches; productive nonempty residuals; revision-checked, atomic adoption; stale and malformed receipt rejection; and concurrent same-revision submissions with exactly one winner. A production correction path also persists typed outcome records in the primary, and a later fork inherits those records: in the three-worker study, cold branches required two trials while experience branches selected the prior successful repair and passed in one. Reusable snapshots reduce measured batch cost versus eager copies at +50k graph nodes, but do not beat direct shared-page forks in that panel. Task-generated receipt runs preserve canonical graph facts and adopted-primary parity, with serialized edge order differing from direct execution on some event states. These results establish useful isolation, replay, feedback-persistence, and guarded-writeback mechanisms, not open-ended autonomous experimentation or general task utility. The candidates, correction families, and outcome interpretation remain substantially authored; one false outcome record can overturn the observed ranking advantage.

The four-family source-locked panel now passes its complete held-out and cost gates; the earlier v1 and v1.1 captures remain explicitly descriptive for their separate protocol deficiencies. The panel shows graph-resident experience ranking can exploit an authored transfer class on shape-axis and recurrence, but it loses to the unchanged default candidate order and target memoization. The navigation study separately establishes prompted structural-edit composition on held-out directions. The next measured responsibility shift is to route the active A8 graph-generated structural variant through the thoughtexperiment evaluator as its own proposal arm, with no fallback to `_default_candidates`, and score it against the same held-out outcome matrix. The protocol must separately attribute the graph decision, host-side applicability feature extraction, host applier/rule serialization, branch evaluation, and oracle cost; this will identify which proposal-generation responsibility has actually moved into the substrate. The cue must be recorded as input, and a separate no-cue arm must measure honest abstention rather than treating prompted repair as autonomous discovery. Continue the existing structural, engine, learning, and scaling gates in parallel: include useful-discovery-per-resource, natural-task prevalence, lifecycle/cancellation, concurrent adoption, and independent-host replication; keep candidate count, backend, graph size, and exposed boundary as separate factors. Current one-host synthetic scaling does not establish natural-task prevalence or an asymptotic law. See [v1.2 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_BLINDED_PROPOSAL_UTILITY_V1_2_RESULTS_20261002.md), [fixture admission audit](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_BLINDED_PROPOSAL_UTILITY_V1_FIXTURE_AUDIT_20261002.md), [candidate-surface results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CORRECTION_CANDIDATE_SURFACE_V2_RESULTS_20261002.md), [navigation repair results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_NAVIGATION_POLARITY_REPAIR_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), and [A8 structural-composition results](MAC_FAC_A8_RESULTS.md).

Evidence replay qualification: the task-generated receipt v1.1 analyzer currently reports its captured 24-episode panel passing. The reusable-snapshot batch v2 capture is valid for its locked source revision, but its analyzer refuses to validate it against the live checkout because eight implementation files have changed since capture. Preserve that distinction: historical source-locked results are evidence about the captured version, not a replay of the current worktree. Re-capture that benchmark only after defining and locking the intended current source state; do not weaken its source-drift check. The full harness remains under active development, and the overall capability/tractability goal is not complete.

## Code-write example acquisition result (2026-10-01)

A preregistered, source-locked full-boot panel of ten fresh workers demonstrates that the substrate can absorb an externally supplied runnable function into its write codebook and reuse it. Before teaching, both starter-codebook requests abstain. After `teach_write_shape_from_example`, each request selects its newly taught `ShapeSignature`; the native Term and emitted Python both match an independent oracle on 305/305 held-out cases across parity and clamp. Median example teaching is about 28 ms, but full-seed boot costs 12.19 s and peak RSS is approximately 1.68 GiB. This is evidence of example-supervised acquisition and reuse, not self-originated candidate generation or open-ended program synthesis. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CODE_WRITE_EXAMPLE_ACQUISITION_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CODE_WRITE_EXAMPLE_ACQUISITION_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [pre-capture amendment](SUBSTRATE_CODE_WRITE_EXAMPLE_ACQUISITION_V1_PRECAPTURE_AMENDMENT_20261001.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_code_write_example_acquisition_v1_raw_20261001.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_code_write_example_acquisition_v1_analysis_20261001.json).

This adds a specific intermediate point in the responsibility transition: an external source example supplies the semantic proposal, while the substrate reads it, grows its own live codebook, selects the new construction, and produces executable code. The next gate is to compare this route with exposure-only and misleading-request controls, then test whether substrate-side mechanisms can originate candidate Terms from input/output evidence under a sealed utility oracle. Charge total proposal, search, verification, and reuse cost; retain false admission and refusal outcomes.

## Current primitive-building boundary audit (2026-10-01)

A fresh AST audit of the current [`substrate_extension.py`](../../../domains/substrate_extension.py) finds one buildable computational family in `build_primitive`: `spectral_value`. Both the forward log-magnitude Term tree and its nonselective negative-control twin are constructed by fixed Python builders, after which the resulting tree is materialized as graph data and evaluated by the native Term runtime. This is a useful mixed boundary: the substrate stores and executes a native composition of existing operators, while the recipe choice and formula are supplied by the host module. The current audit artifact records the source SHA-256 and explicitly scopes itself to this route ([audit script](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/audit_extension_construction_registry.py), [machine-readable inventory](../../../evaluation/reports/extension_construction_registry_v1.json)).

The older spectral forward/inverse study's v1.5 lock differed from the live checkout in four files, so it remains evidence for its captured 2026-09-29 revision. A v1.6 source-locked replay now covers current code/native state. Its amended analyzer checks all 12 arm/case records, re-evaluates each returned Term natively, independently computes the direct-DFT oracle, and confirms six exact forward builds across 18 signal checks plus six honest inverse refusals; the maximum magnitude ambiguity witness error is `9.77e-15`. The first analyzer attempt failed on an invalid uniqueness proxy (`codebook_epoch`); the raw capture is preserved and the correction is disclosed and separately hash-locked. See [v1.6 results](SUBSTRATE_SPECTRAL_IO_ORACLE_V1_6_RESULTS_20261001.md).

The next primitive-construction gate is now a genuinely independent construction family, with task descriptions and held-out oracle values sealed from the substrate-side selector. Compare registered construction, unregistered computation, and world-I/O requests; score exact held-out behavior, false admission, refusal quality, and full proposal/build/verification cost. The present fixed spectral recipe remains host-authored, so this replay is evidence of execution and boundary handling, not autonomous self-improvement.

## Code-write acquisition semantic-alignment control (2026-10-01)

A four-worker source-locked control distinguishes passive exposure from active teaching. Reading the parity example without teaching adds no construction and leaves the parity request unsupported in 2/2 workers. Teaching that same executable parity example under a clamp request is accepted and selected in 2/2; native Term and emitted Python then fail the independent clamp oracle on all 24 held-out cases. This demonstrates that the current teach path's successful parse and internal Term/write agreement do not verify request/example semantic alignment for this mismatch. It is not a calibrated false-admission rate. The next learning milestone is semantic validation or a conservative refusal check before codebook growth, evaluated over balanced aligned/mismatched families and independent oracles. Median boot was 18.05 s; median peak RSS about 1.81 GB. The analyzer field-path error was corrected post-capture, with the original lock preserved and amendment recorded. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CODE_WRITE_ACQUISITION_CONTROLS_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_CODE_WRITE_ACQUISITION_CONTROLS_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [analysis amendment](SUBSTRATE_CODE_WRITE_ACQUISITION_CONTROLS_V1_ANALYSIS_AMENDMENT_20261001.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_code_write_acquisition_controls_v1_analysis_20261001.json).

## Opt-in code-write contract validation (2026-10-01)

To address the demonstrated example/request mismatch, `teach_write_shape_from_example` now accepts optional independent `{inputs, expected}` contract observations. If supplied, every case must match both native Term evaluation and rendered Python, with exact argument-key/type/value checks, before any identifier grounding or ShapeSignature adoption. The source/native-locked 4-worker panel accepts aligned parity in 2/2 workers and gets 10/10 independent contract observations exactly right; it refuses same-arity parity-as-square examples in 2/2 workers before adding a construction (0 growth). Median boot is 17.95 s and peak RSS about 1.80 GB. This is a useful guarded-acquisition mechanism, not a proof that the contract examples are correct or complete. The new parameter is opt-in, so legacy callers can still admit mismatched examples; deciding where trustworthy contracts originate and how they become standard remains open. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CODE_WRITE_CONTRACT_VALIDATION_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CODE_WRITE_CONTRACT_VALIDATION_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_code_write_contract_validation_v1.py), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_code_write_contract_validation_v1_analysis_20261001.json).

## Code-write contract coverage gap (2026-10-01)

The opt-in contract gate was challenged with an identity function mislabeled as square. Two workers accepted it when given only `0→0` and `1→1`, selected the resulting construction, and scored 0/8 on independent held-out square inputs. Two other workers received the additional discriminating row `2→4` and refused before creating `taught_identity`. This shows that correct contract labels can still be insufficient when their inputs do not separate plausible hypotheses; contract checking alone does not guarantee semantic correctness. The next capability step is to measure who chooses useful probes: substrate-selected contrast/boundary inputs versus random and caller-chosen inputs, with an independent labeler and total query/adoption costs. The captured overall code-write ShapeSignature count is confounded by unlocalized changes during the combined read/teach call, so only the named construction is used for acquisition claims. The full goal remains active. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CODE_WRITE_CONTRACT_COVERAGE_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_CODE_WRITE_CONTRACT_COVERAGE_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [analysis amendment](SUBSTRATE_CODE_WRITE_CONTRACT_COVERAGE_V1_ANALYSIS_AMENDMENT_20261001.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_code_write_contract_coverage_v1_analysis_20261001.json).

## Substrate-side discriminating query result (2026-10-01)

A new bounded query selector now filters caller-supplied Term hypotheses against prior labeled examples, evaluates predictions natively, and proposes the unused input with maximum output-partition entropy. The source-locked eight-worker panel identified targets in three small arithmetic families using one query, but random choice was almost as effective (mean 1.071 queries). In the sparse coded family, only 3 of 9 unused inputs distinguished eight hypotheses: the selector chose the three useful inputs and identified the hidden target in 3 queries in both workers; 4,000 seeded random sequences averaged 7.496 queries. Measured selector loops had a 168.6 μs median on this host. This is evidence of substrate-owned probe choice and efficient use of external labels over a finite supplied hypothesis set. It does not generate hypotheses, select tasks, prove the correct hypothesis is present, or remove the need for an independent oracle. Next measure scaling with hypothesis count and expression depth, and test model misspecification. The random-control order correction is recorded; the original lock is retained. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_SELECTION_V1_1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_SELECTION_V1_1_PREREG_20261001.md), [analysis amendment](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_SELECTION_V1_1_ANALYSIS_AMENDMENT_20261001.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_selection_v1_1_analysis_20261001.json).

## Finite query selector scaling and model-adequacy gap (2026-10-01)

The source-locked scaling panel measures the native-Term maximum-disagreement selector through H=128 caller-supplied hypotheses and depth K=7: median native evaluations are 226/1,638/9,434 and median loops 2.16/22.85/178.59 ms at H=8/32/128. It identifies closed-set targets in K queries versus random medians 10/18/26. A label contradicting all candidates yields an empty set and refusal. But when a true target matches one hypothesis on all selected probes and differs only on an unqueried unanimous input, both runs reach a false singleton; a held-out x=15 check exposes 15 vs 115. Model-space closure remains a hard limitation, and these synthetic measurements do not establish natural task tractability. Next separate candidate count, term depth, and grid size; add residual or model-adequacy checks before adopting a singleton. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_SCALING_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_SCALING_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), [analysis amendment](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_SCALING_V1_ANALYSIS_AMENDMENT_20261001.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_scaling_v1_analysis_20261001.json).

## Query selector independent scaling factors (2026-10-01)

A source-locked 14-worker sweep separates H, Term depth, and candidate-grid effects. Baseline H=32/D=0/N=20 takes 24.96 ms median. Scaling to H=512/K=9/N=36 raises it to 1.30 s with 49,102 Term evaluations. At H=32, neutral expression depth 69 raises latency to 304 ms at unchanged evaluator-call count; increasing the input grid to N=500 yields 31,398 evaluations and 467 ms. This reveals separate cost drivers in a controlled binary-coded Term family; it is not a general asymptotic or natural-task estimate. The search's finite candidate-set limitation remains, and candidate evaluation cost will constrain large experiment batches. Next compare caching/batched evaluation under identical correctness and model-adequacy gates, then repeat on task-generated hypothesis families. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_SCALE_FACTORS_V1_RESULTS_20261001.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_SCALE_FACTORS_V1_PREREG_20261001.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_scale_factors_v1_analysis_20261001.json).


## Generated-pool lazy selector transfer (2026-10-02)

A fresh-process nine-worker panel compares uncached and lazy-matrix query selection on bounded Terms generated by the substrate for triangular, square, and Euler tasks. Pools are complete at 9/1/34 hypotheses; the independent analyzer passes all source/native, pool, prediction, oracle, parity, and held-out gates. The arms produce identical query traces and survivors, and the selected survivors are exact on the task-held-outs. Lazy evaluation reduces native calls by 18.8% for the one-query triangular pool and 39.4% for two-query Euler; its timing is tied on the former and 1.30× faster on the latter. Pool generation takes 46–683 ms and selection 0.9–3.6 ms, so this is a query-selector work reduction rather than an end-to-end learning speedup. The three families remain previously authored finite tasks. Next test held-out task families, larger/deeper generated pools, and explicit out-of-grammar targets while charging pool construction and selection together. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_GENERATED_POOL_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_GENERATED_POOL_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_contract_query_generated_pool_transfer_v1.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_generated_pool_transfer_v1_analysis_20261002.json), and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md). The overall capability/tractability goal remains active.


## Cubic generated-pool transfer (2026-10-02)

A locked three-worker follow-up on a new cubic scalar task family generated complete H=10 pools and retained the target across five held-outs. Independent replay passes source/native, target-label, query, parity, and held-out gates in all workers. The lazy query matrix reduces evaluator calls 18.75% (160→130), but selector time changes only 0.710→0.694 ms and synthesis takes 49.39 ms. This extends query-selector transfer beyond the original three authored task families while showing that the practical benefit is negligible at a one-query pool this small. It does not establish broad task generalization or protection against finite-grid aliasing. Next test larger/deeper pools, candidate-grid ablation, and an out-of-grammar target under total acquisition budgets. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_GENERATED_CUBIC_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_GENERATED_CUBIC_TRANSFER_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_contract_query_generated_cubic_transfer_v1.py), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_generated_cubic_transfer_v1_analysis_20261002.json). The overall capability/tractability goal remains active.


## Probe-grid sensitivity and arithmetic misspecification (2026-10-02)

A locked nine-worker panel compares deep cubic pools with broad versus one-point candidate grids and an absolute-value target excluded from the `Plus/Times/Minus/Div` vocabulary. Both cubic grids reach the correct held-out-exact survivor in one query (H=66/27); the out-of-grammar pool (H=9) is eliminated by its first selected negative probe in every worker, producing an empty version space rather than a false singleton. Independent replay and paired parity pass. Lazy matrix use cuts calls 16.7%/50%/30.8%, but wall time is generally tied, and synthesis costs 141–670 ms versus 0.6–6.7 ms selection. This demonstrates one useful contradiction/refusal route, not calibrated model adequacy; finite-grid aliasing can still preserve a wrong singleton. Next construct adversarial probe grids that cause aliasing, broaden independent targets and grammar depth, and measure rates of correct acquisition, refusal, and false singleton under total cost. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_GENERATED_GRID_MISSPEC_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_GENERATED_GRID_MISSPEC_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_contract_query_generated_grid_misspec_v1.py), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_generated_grid_misspec_v1_analysis_20261002.json). The overall capability/tractability goal remains active.


## Generated-pool false-singleton counterexample (2026-10-02)

A locked three-worker alias control uses target `x³`, known inputs 0 and 1, and only candidate `x=−1`. The complete 12-Term pool omits the target behavior representative: linear `x` and cubic `x³` coincide on all supplied inputs. Both uncached and lazy selectors query −1, produce the same singleton `x`, and score 0/4 on held-outs in every worker. The independent source/native and semantic replay checks pass, confirming this is a real coverage limit rather than arm divergence. Pool generation median is 99.20 ms; lazy batching halves native calls (72→36) but cannot fix model omission. This is a constructed failure example, not a prevalence estimate. It shows that empty-pool contradiction checks do not protect singleton acquisition; adequacy requires residual or independently selected probes before adoption. Next build and measure that gate with correctly represented, contradictory, and aliased targets across fresh task families. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_GENERATED_CUBIC_ALIAS_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_GENERATED_CUBIC_ALIAS_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_contract_query_generated_cubic_alias_v1.py), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_generated_cubic_alias_v1_analysis_20261002.json). The overall capability/tractability goal remains active.


## Independent post-selection adequacy gate pilot (2026-10-02)

A source/input-locked evaluation replays 24 acquired-model outcomes across six represented task configurations, one false-singleton configuration, and one empty-pool refusal configuration. Four fresh labeled probes are disjoint from acquisition/evaluation descriptors. At k=0, all 21 nonempty models pass vacuously, including all three wrong cubic alias singletons. At k=1, the fresh label rejects all 3/3 alias models; all 18/18 positive controls pass, and 3/3 empty pools remain refused. k=2/4 adds no decision changes; total native audit calls grow 21/42/84. Independent labels, candidate predictions, native/Python parity, parent locks, and probe freshness pass. This validates a simple finite-audit guard on a small control panel, not a model correctness certificate or calibrated false-accept/refusal rate. The gate is still evaluation harness logic that consumes externally labeled rows; there is no substrate-owned evidence acquisition or production adoption integration. Next build and measure a provenance-bound residual/audit evidence path, including ambiguous/noisy evidence and independently authored task families. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_INDEPENDENT_ADEQUACY_GATE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONTRACT_QUERY_INDEPENDENT_ADEQUACY_GATE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_contract_query_independent_adequacy_gate_v1.py), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_contract_query_independent_adequacy_gate_v1_analysis_20261002.json). The overall capability/tractability goal remains active.


## Provenance-bound adequacy receipts (2026-10-02)

A reusable request/receipt API now connects candidate Term identity, acquisition trace, fresh probe descriptors, declared label sources, and post-query residuals. Its locked 24-case evaluation accepts all 18 represented controls, rejects the generated false singleton in all three workers, and preserves refusal for three empty pools. Conflicting labels become `ambiguous`; missing evidence is `incomplete`; reused sources and changed descriptors are `invalid`; tampered request contents fail closed; a single incorrect label produces a rejection residual. Independent replay passes with zero errors. Total adjudication time is 2.12–4.22 ms for 21 candidate cases at budgets 1–4, excluding label acquisition. This is a useful evidence-recording mechanism, not authenticated provenance: providers are caller-declared, labels remain external, and no acquisition/adoption or Thoughtexperiment return path consumes the receipt yet. Next integrate the receipt into an explicit reviewable adoption gate and measure it on fresh/noisy domains. See [results](SUBSTRATE_PROVENANCE_BOUND_ADEQUACY_RECEIPT_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_PROVENANCE_BOUND_ADEQUACY_RECEIPT_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [API](../../../domains/independent_adequacy.py), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_provenance_bound_adequacy_receipt_v1.py), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_provenance_bound_adequacy_receipt_v1_analysis_20261002.json). The overall capability/tractability goal remains active.

## Overall status update — 2026-10-02

The broad capability and tractability programme remains active. The inventory and measurement plan are in place, and current evidence covers structural and engine behavior, branch isolation and scaling, code acquisition, query selection, adequacy controls, and selected-probe adoption. Thoughtexperiment branches can return graph residuals and use revision-checked staged adoption; residual application is no longer unimplemented. The measured branch and adoption mechanisms are useful on bounded fixtures, with copy-on-write and shared snapshots reducing branch cost at larger sizes and fanouts.

The latest learning result transfers a strict selected-probe adoption gate to parity and clamp in 18 fresh workers with all 15 independent checks passing. Correct examples adopted and passed held-outs; aliasing domains and contradictory labels led to refusal. This narrows a previously measured gap but does not solve open-ended structure learning. Challenger and domain generation remain supplied by the harness, labels/oracles remain external, and a trusted signer can still sign a false label. Runtime results come mainly from one host and synthetic/authored tasks; there is no established natural-task tractability rate or general capability estimate. Priorities are independently authored tasks, noisy and truthful-label controls, challenger quality/size sweeps, complete acquisition cost accounting, and replication on another host. These results do not establish self-improvement or general intelligence.

See the [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md), [source-linked capability map](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_MAP.md), and [selected-probe transfer results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_SELECTED_PROBE_TRANSFER_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md). The objective remains active pending wider task-family, learning, and scaling evidence.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 threshold transfer

The latest independent threshold-task panel strengthens selected-probe transfer evidence: truthful acquisition generalized outside the probe range to 100/100 held-outs in three fresh workers, and broad truthful evidence rejected the false candidate. Alias-only domains caused refusal, and conflicting signed providers produced ambiguous receipts. It also reconfirmed the main epistemic gap: one trusted provider signing a false label caused wrong adoption in 3/3 runs and 42/100 errors on shifted held-outs. Provider authentication is not a truth oracle, and agreement is not independence. The overall tractability/capability goal remains active; next measure controlled noise and domain shift across independent task families under matched acquisition budgets. See [threshold results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_THRESHOLD_NOISE_V1_2_RESULTS_20261002.md).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 stochastic evidence noise

A locked 2,160-trial panel now measures the live adequacy receipt policy's fail-closed noise law on parity and threshold examples. Across 1/3/5 simulated independent signers and noise rates 0/10/50%, all 48 source, replay, and per-cell consistency gates pass. More providers suppress unanimous false labels at the expected exponential rate but also reduce correct acceptance when any disagreement forces refusal. This characterizes a bounded decision tradeoff; it does not estimate natural provider error or certify label truth. Correlated error, real task distributions, broader structures, and autonomous proposal generation remain open. The full capability/tractability objective remains active. See [stochastic noise results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_STOCHASTIC_NOISE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 common-mode evidence noise

A source-locked 4,320-trial panel now varies the common-mode component of signed-provider error on parity and threshold tasks. All 84 replay/cell-consistency gates pass. Under the stipulated model, increasing provider IDs suppresses independent errors but cannot suppress shared errors: at five providers and 50% marginal noise, observed false eligibility rises from 4/120 at zero correlation to 55/120 at full common mode. Correct adoption rises too because unanimous channels disagree less often. This remains synthetic mechanism evidence, not a real provider-correlation estimate. Independent oracle governance, natural-task rates, and repeated live adoption remain open; the full tractability/capability goal is active. See [correlated-noise results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_CORRELATED_NOISE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 evidence-policy sensitivity

A post-capture replay compares unanimous, majority, and 80% agreement on identical signed-label budgets across the independent and common-mode noise captures. All 327 source/replay/model gates pass. Under the synthetic low-noise independent case, relaxed voting improves correct-candidate acceptance but permits more false acceptance; at 50% error both become approximately symmetric, and common-mode error defeats the value of extra provider IDs. This is an offline decision-frontier analysis, not an implemented or deployment-ready policy. Live adoption costs, calibrated real provider quality, and natural task distributions remain unmeasured. See [policy tradeoff results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_POLICY_TRADEOFF_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 strict live-route cost and graph boundary

The original 66-worker live-route panel passes its implemented decision and replay checks but omitted the preregistered full-graph invariant. The source-locked v1.2 confirmation captures full graphs in 66 fresh workers and passes 11/12 gates: expected accept/reject/ambiguous outcomes, including false adoption under unanimous trusted false labels, all reproduce. The graph-delta gate fails in every condition because the first `converse` call lazily adds 703–730 nodes and 475–512 edges and changes an existing `Agent` record. The cold call therefore mixes state loading with adoption, and the capture cannot attribute that delta to the adoption transaction. Per-cell median call timings range 171.9–178.6 ms on parity and 185.5–193.8 ms on threshold; they include that route and are not steady-state costs. Next separate initialization from a warmed adoption transaction and retain cold-path timings as their own capability cost. See [v1 results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_LIVE_ROUTE_COST_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [v1.2 results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_LIVE_ROUTE_COST_V1_2_RESULTS_20261002.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_live_route_cost_v1_2_raw_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_live_route_cost_v1_2_analysis_20261002.json). The overall capability/tractability goal remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 adoption-boundary audit

A separate locked audit places the graph snapshot immediately around `_teach_shape`, after parsing and evidence verification. All 8/8 checks pass across three fresh workers: rejected evidence never reaches adoption; accepted evidence adds exactly one code-write `ShapeSignature` and one pending `ShapeObservation`, updates only the Agent’s adoption metadata, and adds/removes no edges. The measured transaction boundary is 0.437 ms median. This resolves the narrow adoption-delta question for one authored square task; it does not remove the cold-route cost or establish all-caller/general-task behavior. Next expand the exact transaction gate to independent task families and separately profile a warmed full `converse`. See [adoption-boundary results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_ADOPTION_BOUNDARY_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [source-locked raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_adoption_boundary_v1_raw_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_adoption_boundary_v1_analysis_20261002.json). The overall capability/tractability goal remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 cross-family adoption boundary

The v2 source-locked audit extends the adoption-delta check to parity, clamp, and square across nine fresh workers. All 8/8 analysis gates pass: false labels refuse before the transaction; truthful labels produce the same exact two-node, one-Agent-field graph delta; and native held-outs match the independent oracle on 303/303 inputs. Median `_teach_shape` time is 0.483–0.526 ms by task. This strengthens transfer evidence for the code-write transaction across three authored Term families. It remains one host and does not cover cold/warm full conversation, other teaching callers, natural tasks, or autonomous induction. Continue with new task families, another caller, and a matched warmed-route measurement. See [v2 results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_ADOPTION_BOUNDARY_V2_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_ADOPTION_BOUNDARY_V2_PREREG_20261002.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_adoption_boundary_v2_raw_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_adoption_boundary_v2_analysis_20261002.json). The overall capability/tractability goal remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 warmed full-conversation cost

A source-locked 18-worker panel measures the first conversation turn and a subsequent task turn in matched adoption and missing-evidence arms across parity, clamp, and square. All 8/8 checks pass: truthful adoption adds one shape; missing evidence leaves the codebook unchanged; accepted native outputs match held-outs. First-turn median wall time is 94.6–100.3 ms by cell, and target-turn medians are 111.2–116.0 ms. Target process CPU medians are 1.65–1.70 s, which may exceed wall time because process CPU accumulates across threads; the matcher has a Rayon worker path, but this panel does not attribute CPU to it. This is a material cost signal, not yet a per-core or per-rule tractability estimate. The full-call graph continues to gain ordinary turn state after warm-up. A post-capture amendment records the request/receipt construction order deviation; both timed intervals exclude receipt construction. Next add per-rule/worker CPU attribution and thread-count controls, then replicate on more task families and another host. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_WARM_CONVERSE_COST_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_WARM_CONVERSE_COST_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_WARM_CONVERSE_COST_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_warm_converse_cost_v1_raw_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_warm_converse_cost_v1_analysis_20261002.json). The overall capability/tractability goal remains active.

**Thread-limit diagnostic (2026-10-02):** a six-worker locked comparison finds 25 maximum sampled OS threads under both default and `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1`; median target wall is 112.1/114.0 ms and aggregate process CPU is 1.47/1.65 s. Thus the env-var arm did not reduce measured process-level threads or wall latency, and process CPU remains far above wall time. With three workers per arm this is only diagnostic; it does not establish whether the Rayon pool applied the cap or which workers consumed CPU. Next expose the native pool's actual worker count and collect per-thread CPU or native per-rule worker counters. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_WARM_CONVERSE_THREAD_DIAGNOSTIC_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_WARM_CONVERSE_THREAD_DIAGNOSTIC_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_warm_converse_thread_diagnostic_v1_raw_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_warm_converse_thread_diagnostic_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

**Per-thread CPU attribution remains unresolved (2026-10-02):** the Rayon-pool getter confirms 24 versus 1 workers, and a separate caller-thread measurement finds 94.8% of target CPU outside the caller. A first Linux `schedstat` probe failed its preregistered sampling-quality gate: only three of six calls reached ten in-process samples; its analyzer also had a row-shape defect. The capture is retained as a failed diagnostic, not evidence assigning CPU to a subsystem. The next probe must sample `/proc` from a parent process outside the target's GIL and use target-call boundary markers. See [the preregistration](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_WARM_CONVERSE_THREAD_CPU_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_warm_converse_thread_cpu_v1_raw_20261002.json), and [measurement-plan entry](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

**External per-thread sampler follow-up (2026-10-02):** the parent-process probe samples 86–93% of aggregate process CPU by thread instance and preserves exact task/refusal controls. It reaches the 20-sample gate under capped Rayon, but default-Rayon runs fall short at 14–18 samples, so the nine-gate analysis passes 8/9 and remains diagnostic. Median target wall is about 136–137 ms and process CPU 1.20–1.24 s. It observes 25 distinct OS thread instances with Rayon=1 and 48 with Rayon=24; these are identities seen across samples, not simultaneous counts, and do not establish a second pool. OS `comm` values are generic. A one-process exploratory run saw no standard `threading.Thread` starts during the capped-Rayon target and only the main Python thread at the boundaries. Next instrument standard and low-level Python thread creation under a source lock, then trace native spawn sites or task/rule work if unexplained. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_WARM_CONVERSE_THREAD_CPU_V2_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_WARM_CONVERSE_THREAD_CPU_V2_PREREG_20261002.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_warm_converse_thread_cpu_v2_raw_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_warm_converse_thread_cpu_v2_analysis_20261002.json). The broader tractability and capability goal remains active.

**Thread-origin diagnosis (2026-10-02):** the source-locked pthread creation census identifies 23 OpenBLAS threads when its cap is unset and none with `OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1`, plus an invariant 117 native JSON parser threads per target call, each requesting a 256 MiB stack. Parser entry points are `add_production` (94), `add_rule` (16), `graph_from_dict` (6), and `import_rules` (1). The corrected independent review passes 12/12 checks, including exact task controls and complete source/probe hashes. Under interposition, the OpenBLAS cap cuts process CPU from 1.24–1.33 s to 0.11–0.125 s; this large intervention signal needs an uninstrumented confirmation before making a runtime cost claim. The other clear engine cost target is repeated large-stack parser thread creation. Next measure the BLAS cap without interposition, then test a reusable stack-safe parser worker. See [thread-origin results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_WARM_CONVERSE_THREAD_ORIGIN_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [corrected analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_warm_converse_thread_origin_v1_analysis_review_20261002.json), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_warm_converse_thread_origin_v1_raw_20261002.json). The broader goal remains active.

**Uninstrumented OpenBLAS confirmation (2026-10-02):** the fresh, six-process source-locked panel passes 9/9 gates with Rayon held at 24 and task outcomes unchanged. Setting `OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1` reduces median aggregate process CPU from 1.36 s to 0.102 s (13.35×) and other-thread CPU from 1.26 s to 4.4 ms; caller CPU stays close to 100 ms and median wall falls from 129.9 to 103.8 ms. This confirms the OpenBLAS thread pool is the main cause of the CPU/wall gap in this authored warm-conversation route. It does not generalize beyond this host and route. The 117 deep-stack parser threads per turn remain. Next evaluate that parser overhead without weakening deep-input correctness and expand the thread-cap panel across workloads/hosts. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_WARM_CONVERSE_OPENBLAS_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ADEQUACY_WARM_CONVERSE_OPENBLAS_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_warm_converse_openblas_v1_raw_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_adequacy_warm_converse_openblas_v1_analysis_20261002.json). The broader capability and tractability goal remains active.

**Navigation outcome-ranker integration (2026-10-02):** the production graph-resident rank Program now consumes candidate-specific typed outcomes from Up/Left training cases and orders six navigation edits for disjoint Down/Right held-out cases. Three fresh source/native-locked workers pass all 13 independent outcome/integrity checks. Experience rank selects the successful `flip_comparison` first and reduces attempts 2→1 relative to cold structural-cost ranking, but ties the unchanged default order, which already selects it first. The runner omitted preregistered ordering latency; the capture amendment discloses this, and no full-cost comparison is claimed. This establishes outcome-conditioned rank use in a third task context, not incremental value over default or cross-family transfer. **Blinded panel protocol clarification (2026-10-02):** before panel capture, v1.1 now explicitly separates information sets: experience rank receives other-family training outcomes only, while target memoization receives only the evaluation family's disjoint training outcomes; all orders precede held-out scoring. Keep panel capture at no-go until a new common runner produces four-worker-per-family data with blinded order capture and reconciled enumeration, ranking, branch, scoring, and adoption-verification costs ([results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_BLINDED_PROPOSAL_UTILITY_V1_READINESS_RESULTS_20261002.md), [audit runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/audit_thoughtexperiment_blinded_panel_readiness_v1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_blinded_panel_readiness_v1_20261002.json)).

The independent audit passes 12/12 integrity checks, including full candidate-matrix coverage, source parity, disjoint signatures, and unchanged primary revision. This closes the old shape fixture's 2/6 utility-score gap. It does not add an independent task family or establish ranking benefit; both passing edits solve the same authored duplicate-axis task. **Five-policy control-kind pilot (2026-10-02):** a source-locked two-stage pilot compares experience, frozen, default, seeded random, and target memoization on four fresh control-kind workers. Orders are captured before a separate scorer sees held-out cases; the independent review passes 14/14 integrity gates. Cross-family experience uses 20 recurrence/navigation/shape training records but receives zero matching candidate priors, matching frozen rank and requiring two attempts in every worker. Default and target memo succeed on the first candidate in all workers; random takes 1/3/5/1. All arms eventually solve the held-out cases. **A8 quantum-route transfer (2026-10-02):** after preserving v1.2's off-diagonal portal oracle defect, v1.3 corrects vertical/horizontal crossing coordinates and adds two fresh primary layouts. Four workers pass graph attribution, target preservation, no-cue abstention, source/receipt controls, and all six layout outcomes, including fresh H5/H6; source and fault controls discriminate and all 28 host candidates are scored.
