**General-rule proposal generation v1 (2026-10-02):** an opt-in A8 structural path now exposes non-frozen rule families to graph-resident target selection. In four blind order workers, the prompted proposal selected the non-action classifier `classify_kind_family`, chose `flip_comparison`, matched the independently enumerated host reference candidate, and preserved the target rule; the empty-cue path abstained. The proposal passes both held-out classifier cases in all four workers with valid branch receipts and primary preservation; source/fault controls discriminate and 24 host candidate outcomes were exhaustively scored. This moves target/operator selection beyond action rules for one authored classifier. Applicability extraction and candidate materialization remain Python-owned, the cue and oracle remain supplied, the harness calls the opt-in API directly, and no speed comparison is valid from the different-scope timing fields. Next integrate this path into the ordinary proposal dispatcher, separate those host costs, and transfer to fresh shape-axis and recurrence instances without a host-candidate fallback ([results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERAL_STRUCTURAL_PROPOSAL_CONTROL_KIND_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERAL_STRUCTURAL_PROPOSAL_CONTROL_KIND_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_general_structural_proposal_control_kind_v1.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_general_structural_proposal_control_kind_v1_analysis_20261002.json)).

**General-rule proposal dispatcher integration v1.1 (2026-10-02):** the same cued classifier probe now enters via ordinary `ground_concern` and `compose_experiment_rule`; four workers again select the correct non-action target and flip operator, match the host candidate, pass both held-outs, verify receipts/isolation, and abstain without a cue. The path still reports `served_by=python` because the legacy graph meta-rule is action-only; native Term Argmax selects target and operator after Python routes the explicit general-rule scope and exposes provenance, and Python materializes the edit. This is API integration, not a graph-native outer-dispatch migration. Median composer time is 25.87 ms but includes much more work than the 0.619 ms host-enumeration timing, so no speed comparison is valid. Next split cost phases and test distinct shape-axis and recurrence targets, with exact disclosure of any host-owned steps ([results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERAL_STRUCTURAL_PROPOSAL_CONTROL_KIND_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERAL_STRUCTURAL_PROPOSAL_CONTROL_KIND_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_general_structural_proposal_control_kind_v1_1.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_general_structural_proposal_control_kind_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json)).

**Current-source repeat and provenance-placeholder repair (2026-10-02):** after v1.1, `_ensure_general_rule_provenance` was extended to upgrade incomplete task-authored `RuleSummary` placeholders in place. A source-locked v1.2 repeat passes all ten gates: in four workers the normal `ground_concern` path selects the classifier target and flip operator, passes both held-out names, verifies candidate receipts/primary isolation, and abstains with no cue; all 24 host candidate outcomes are audited. V1.1 remains historical for its earlier exact source lock; v1.2 is the current-source result. The outer composer remains Python-served, with Python provenance exposure/materialization and a supplied cue/oracle. A shape-axis smoke found that the current deterministic key-swap site direction can select a failing variant; treat it as exploratory, and test directional site selection under a new locked protocol before claiming transfer. See [v1.2 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERAL_STRUCTURAL_PROPOSAL_CONTROL_KIND_V1_2_RESULTS_20261002.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERAL_STRUCTURAL_PROPOSAL_CONTROL_KIND_V1_2_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_general_structural_proposal_control_kind_v1_2.py), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_general_structural_proposal_control_kind_v1_2_analysis_20261002.json).

**Shape-rule site selection v1 (2026-10-02; diagnostic):** ordered attribute-key pairs are now exposed as `EditSite` graph nodes, and a native Term chooses a unique site from cue overlap or abstains on a tie. Across four workers, `key to signature` selects the known useful orientation and passes all three held-out axis cases plus fixed-point checks; `signature to key` selects the reverse and fails all three axis cases; an ambiguous cue abstains. All proposals match host references, and all 24 host candidate branches pass receipt/isolation checks. The analyzer passes 12/12 gates. Python still extracts sites and applies edits; the cue specifies the answer, and this task/outcome had prior exploratory exposure. Treat it as cue-conditioned selection plumbing, not learned direction discovery. Composer timing showed a consistent 255.77 ms vs 38.96 ms asymmetry that is unexplained and not used for a speed claim. Next test less answer-specific cues and substrate-owned probe selection, then move to recurrence/general operators ([results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERAL_STRUCTURAL_PROPOSAL_SHAPE_SITE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERAL_STRUCTURAL_PROPOSAL_SHAPE_SITE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_GENERAL_STRUCTURAL_PROPOSAL_SHAPE_SITE_V1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_general_structural_proposal_shape_site_v1.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_general_structural_proposal_shape_site_v1_analysis_20261002.json)).

**Active probe selection v1 (2026-10-02; bounded gate):** four source/native-locked workers passed 16/16 cases: the native-Term selector chose a discriminating input from finite pools with 0–99% alias examples, and the post-freeze oracle selected the correct supplied `key` hypothesis, which passed 20 separate held-out discriminators each time. Random one-shot probes discriminated in 0/4 cases at 90% and 99% alias rates. The test confirms useful bounded query selection over two supplied hypotheses; task hypothesis generation, input generation, oracle, and scoring remain host-defined. It is not evidence of autonomous structural improvement. Next integrate this selector with substrate-composed competing rule proposals on a fresh family, with proposal/oracle separation and full host-versus-substrate cost accounting ([results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ACTIVE_PROBE_SELECTION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ACTIVE_PROBE_SELECTION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_active_probe_selection_v1.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_active_probe_selection_v1_analysis_20261002.json)).

**Composed proposal active-query integration v1 (2026-10-02):** the normal prompted composer selected the `classify_kind_family` comparator repair in four workers, matched the independent host candidate, and a Term-based active selector found one-bit fault/proposal contrasts in all 16 candidate pools through 99% neutral names. After the query orders froze, the task oracle retained the proposal in every worker; it passed both held-outs while all fault controls failed, with receipts and primary preservation. Query-stage cost rose from 10.62 ms to 208.66 ms as nonmatching inputs dominated because both native graph branches were run across each pool. This connects composition, isolation, active query selection, and held-out evaluation in one authored family, while Python still supplies the task/fault/cue/pool/oracle and applies the edit. It is not self-improvement or fresh-family transfer. Next repeat on a newly authored rule family, then test recurrence/domain-specific operators and full cost accounting ([results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COMPOSED_PROPOSAL_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_COMPOSED_PROPOSAL_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_composed_proposal_active_query_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_composed_proposal_active_query_v1_lock_20261002.json), [orders](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_composed_proposal_active_query_v1_orders_20261002.json), [scores](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_composed_proposal_active_query_v1_scores_20261002.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_composed_proposal_active_query_v1_analysis_20261002.json)).

**Fresh-rule structural transfer v1.1 (2026-10-02):** on a newly authored `classify_meter_mode` rule, four source-locked workers selected `flip_comparison`, matched the independent generic candidate, and used active probes to retain the proposal in all 32 backend/neutral-rate cases. The proposal passed four held-outs; the fault failed all nonzero cases. Snapshot-CoW and full-copy outputs, queries, fixed points, and receipts matched. Mean CoW branch creation was 14.0 µs versus 29.9 µs for full copy, while total query time was 64.56 versus 65.12 ms across 200 isolated predictions. The initial v1 timing comparison was confounded by reusing branches across candidate pools; its correctness outcome remains intact, and the amendment withdraws its condition timing interpretation. This extends prompted structural transfer to one independent symbolic rule family and measures a local CoW fork-cost advantage, not general learning or system speed. Next transfer across multiple independently authored rule shapes, separate composition and prediction costs on larger graphs, and test whether the proposal vocabulary reaches recurrence repairs ([v1.1 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_FRESH_RULE_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [v1.1 protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_FRESH_RULE_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_1_PREREG_20261002.md), [v1 timing amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_FRESH_RULE_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_fresh_rule_active_query_v1_1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_fresh_rule_active_query_v1_1_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_fresh_rule_active_query_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json)).

**Attribute-site active-query transfer v1 (2026-10-02):** a second fresh symbolic rule uses a different proposal operator: four workers select `classify_meter_delta`, `swap_term_leaf`, and the ordered `bad_score -> good_score` site. Across 32 backend/alias conditions, active queries retain the proposal; all four proposal held-outs pass, while the fault fails nonzero cases. CoW and full-copy predictions, orders, receipts, and fixed points agree. Mean CoW branch creation is 14.6 µs versus 28.5 µs for full copy (1.95× on this tiny graph); mean total query time is 70.53 ms versus 69.46 ms, so no end-to-end speedup is shown. The cue explicitly gives the desired site orientation, so this is prompted transfer, not autonomous site discovery. Next remove answer-specific cue content and evaluate independently authored, disjoint edit sites with complete proposal/patch/closure/query cost accounting ([results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_attribute_site_active_query_v1.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_attribute_site_active_query_v1_analysis_20261002.json)).

**Attribute-site cue ablation v1 (2026-10-02):** across four fresh workers, the answer-bearing cue selects the forward site in 4/4 cases; direction-omitted, source-only, and destination-only cues each cause abstention in 4/4. Both opposing swaps are available, and the primary rule remains unchanged. This confirms that current EditSite selection is cue-conditioned and does not infer orientation from task outcomes. The corrected analysis passes 8/8 checks; its original exact-candidate-count check was too strict because the engine validly exposes other attribute swaps, and the post-capture correction is disclosed. Next construct competing site candidates, run branch predictions and substrate-selected probes before the oracle, and score source/branch patch and closure costs independently ([results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_CUE_ABLATION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_CUE_ABLATION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_CUE_ABLATION_V1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_attribute_site_cue_ablation_v1.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_attribute_site_cue_ablation_v1_analysis_20261002.json)).

**Attribute-site active choice v1 (2026-10-02):** following cue-free composer abstention, host-side generic enumeration provided fault, forward, and reverse hypotheses. The native-Term selector chose a 0.9183-bit 1-vs-2 maximum-entropy probe in all 32 backend/alias conditions; after frozen orders were scored, only the forward proposal survived. It passed all five held-outs in four workers; fault and reverse each failed nonzero cases in every worker. All branch receipts, fixed points, and primary-state checks passed, with 14/14 corrected analysis checks. Selector wall time averaged 107 ms per 100-input pool using CoW and 189 ms using full copy; these 300 isolated predictions dominate candidate enumeration (0.413 ms), and no broad speed inference follows. One-bit entropy in the original preregistration is impossible for three binary-output hypotheses. The analyzer also needed post-capture corrections, disclosed in the amendment; the final analysis is not a replay under the originally locked analyzer hash. This establishes substrate-led probe choice over explicit host-supplied candidates, not candidate invention or autonomous self-improvement. Next move proposal representation/selection into graph state and test cross-family transfer ([results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_ACTIVE_CHOICE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_ACTIVE_CHOICE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_ACTIVE_CHOICE_V1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_attribute_site_active_choice_v1.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_attribute_site_active_choice_v1_analysis_20261002.json)).

**Graph-resident attribute-site choice v3 (2026-10-02):** the native Term now aggregates proposal correctness from CandidateProposal/CandidateEvidence nodes in a snapshot branch and returns only a unique positive-evidence winner. Across 32 query conditions it chooses the forward repair, passes all five held-outs in each of four workers, and returns one compact ExperimentResidual per condition to primary without candidate/evidence leakage or changing active rule definitions. Tie and all-wrong cases abstain; incomplete coverage and reused experiment namespaces fail closed. The independent analyzer passes 18/18 checks. Graph evidence construction plus scoring takes 0.205 ms, snapshot branch creation 0.0238 ms, and residual return 0.0150 ms per condition on this fixture. The v2 pilot is censored because its tie fixture and primary isolation design were wrong; v3 is the valid gate. Candidate discovery, task/probe construction, prediction generation, and oracle labels remain host-defined, so the result is substrate-owned scoring within a bounded supplied proposal set, not autonomous structural invention ([v3 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_GRAPH_SELECTION_V3_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_GRAPH_SELECTION_V3_PREREG_20261002.md), [v2 censor note](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ATTRIBUTE_SITE_GRAPH_SELECTION_V2_CENSORED_20261002.md), [API source](../../../domains/contract_query.py), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_attribute_site_active_choice_v3.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_attribute_site_active_choice_v3_analysis_20261002.json)).

**Retrospective recurrence transfer of graph evidence selection (2026-10-02):** on the already published recurrence candidate-admission capture, three isolated replays each provide two worker-specific training episodes to the graph Term. It uniquely selects `dedup_identity_fix` from eight candidates and the selected proposal passes that worker's two held-out episodes; all seven alternatives are below the training maximum and fail at least one held-out. The amended review passes 11/11 integrity checks, but the original protocol incorrectly required six train and six held-out episodes per worker, so the literal preregistered gate did not pass. The outcomes were already visible and this is not a fresh or blinded replication. Treat it as retrospective API transfer only; preregister a new recurrence experiment with correct sample units before making cross-family capability claims ([results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_GRAPH_SELECTION_RETRO_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_GRAPH_SELECTION_RETRO_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_GRAPH_SELECTION_RETRO_V1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_graph_selection_retro_v1.py), [replay](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_graph_selection_retro_v1_20261002.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_graph_selection_retro_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json)).

**Prospective recurrence graph selection and early rejection (2026-10-02):** four source/native-locked workers score all eight generated candidates on four training and four disjoint held-out recurrence episodes. Each episode contains two due keys. Graph evidence Term selects `dedup_identity_fix` uniquely at 4/4 in every worker; it passes all 16 held-out episodes, reaches fixed point, and returns one compact residual without candidate/evidence leakage. Seven alternatives score below the training maximum and each fails held-out behavior. The selection subgate passes, but the original full tractability gate fails: five alternatives hit the runner's 100-call cap in all 160 train/held-out episodes. A paired bounded harness later produced identical pass/fail outcomes on all 256 candidate episodes and selected the same repair in every worker. It cut mean panel runtime from 116.384 s to 5.643 s (20.6×) and rule work from 176,288 to 32,288 units (5.46×) by rejecting proposals at a 200-call stage budget before admitting the second due candidate. This is an evaluation-harness improvement on one fixture; the rule engine's general closure semantics are unchanged, and cap/budget rejection does not prove nontermination. Corrected baseline episode means are 459 ms training and 467 ms held-out, not the earlier understated 115–117 ms; candidate enumeration remains about 109 ms per worker. The independent budget-calibration replication now passes 12/12 checks. Across arities 2/4/8, all four workers select the same repair for every budget; held-out candidate outcomes match the reference exactly and no reference-resolved candidate is falsely rejected. Forty of 64 proposals per arity/split are rejected at stage one. Pooled held-out medians improve about 18×/5.7×/1.5× at budgets 200/400/800, while the selected repair itself has no measured speedup. Arity increases its rule work 4/8/16 units, but does not change the pooled profile because poor proposals exhaust at the first stage. This supports adaptive proposal-evaluation economics on a synthetic recurrence workload, not general engine tractability; the same rule family and candidate generator remain a limit ([results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_BUDGET_CALIBRATION_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_BUDGET_CALIBRATION_V1_1_PREREG_20261002.md)). The selected-candidate fixed-point outcome is intact ([early-rejection results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_EARLY_REJECTION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_EARLY_REJECTION_V1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [original results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_GRAPH_SELECTION_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_GRAPH_SELECTION_V1_1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_early_rejection_v1.py), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_early_rejection_v1_2_analysis_20261002.json)).


**Thoughtexperiment Omniglot matcher transfer v1.1 (2026-10-02):** background-alphabet training evidence selected PointSetDist in all three workers. On evaluation alphabets held out until the frozen order stage, it scores 62.5%, 66.8%, and 60.4%, best or tied-best against the two other supplied graph-Term matchers in each worker. Aggregate accuracy is 63.2% across 20 alphabet clusters (descriptive cluster-bootstrap 95% interval 58.0–68.6%; 5% chance). The run passes 12/12 corrected analysis checks for source/native/data locks, split isolation, matching episodes, unique ranking, abstention controls, branch isolation, and residual return. Corrected analyzer v1.2 is post-capture because the frozen v1.1 analyzer expected evaluation specs in the order artifact; the protocol/sealing and cost gaps found in the earlier v1 pilot are separately documented. This establishes transfer of ranking over a supplied matcher set on real glyph data, not autonomous matcher invention or broad visual capability ([results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_OMNIGLOT_MATCHER_SELECTION_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_OMNIGLOT_MATCHER_SELECTION_V1_1_PREREG_20261002.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_OMNIGLOT_MATCHER_SELECTION_V1_1_ANALYSIS_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_omniglot_matcher_selection_v1_1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_omniglot_matcher_selection_v1_1_lock_20261002.json), [orders](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_omniglot_matcher_selection_v1_1_orders_20261002.json), [scores](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_omniglot_matcher_selection_v1_1_scores_20261002.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_omniglot_matcher_selection_v1_2_analysis_20261002.json)).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 thoughtexperiment proposal-scope transfer

The corrected v1.1 bidirectional edge-identity experiment passes 16/16 analysis gates across four workers and two authored rule families. In every worker, native graph-Term selection over opaque proposal IDs and training outcome evidence picks the edge guard at the right scope; the selected guards pass all 8 held-out trials per family. The harness generates proposals with a manually authored generic AST scan, so this supports substrate-side candidate selection and transfer, not self-directed proposal invention or self-improvement. Complete timing now covers held-out recurrence episodes and all named phases: candidate scoring medians range from 47.4–102.6 ms per family/split, while the fixed composer costs 29–36 ms and generic proposal generation 0.11–0.16 ms on this host. Treat these as diagnostic fixture timings, not general tractability estimates. Next remove the manual proposal-generation dependency in a locked residual-to-hypothesis evaluation, and broaden families before estimating transfer rates. See [v1.1 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_BIDIRECTIONAL_EDGE_IDENTITY_GUARDS_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [timing amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_BIDIRECTIONAL_EDGE_IDENTITY_GUARDS_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), and [analysis capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_bidirectional_edge_identity_guards_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json). The overall capability/tractability goal remains active.


## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 substrate-composed edge guards

The follow-up moves generic edge-site extraction into the reusable edit-operator layer and lets the general composer select a graph-held operator and site. The source-locked evaluation passes 12/12 gates across four workers: both authored families receive the expected proposal, all 16 training and 16 held-out episodes pass, and no-overlap/tied-site controls abstain 4/4. Median composition costs 30.5–36.8 ms on this host. This is prompted proposal generation from an externally supplied cue; the substrate does not yet decide that a residual exists or formulate its own cue. Next test wider edge-effect rule families and whether the cue can be grounded from residual evidence, while measuring false edits and end-to-end search cost. See [v1 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SUBSTRATE_EDGE_IDENTITY_COMPOSER_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SUBSTRATE_EDGE_IDENTITY_COMPOSER_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_substrate_edge_identity_composer_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_substrate_edge_identity_composer_v1_analysis_20261002.json). The overall capability/tractability goal remains active.


## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 edge-identity scope boundary

A three-family confirmation passes 13/13 preregistered gates. The composer continues to generate useful guards for recurrence and repeated replies (16/16 held-out episodes across the two families). On the real `mint_actor_goal_recipient` rule, its agent-wide edge guard passes one-verb training but suppresses a second distinct verb in all 8 held-out episodes; the source rule passes all 8. The demonstrated operator handles output existence when the bound edge key is sufficient, but it lacks identity over attributes of a freshly minted target. This narrows the next implementation gate to attribute-scoped guard hypotheses and retains the actor-goal failure as a regression boundary. The cue and target remain harness supplied, so autonomous residual discovery remains open. See [v1.1 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SUBSTRATE_EDGE_IDENTITY_COMPOSER_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SUBSTRATE_EDGE_IDENTITY_COMPOSER_V1_1_PREREG_20261002.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_substrate_edge_identity_composer_v1_1_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_substrate_edge_identity_composer_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json). The overall capability/tractability goal remains active.


## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 attribute-scoped guard repair

The actor-goal false-suppression gap is repaired in the measured proposal path. A new attribute-identity site vocabulary derives bounded signatures from direct bound `Attr` terms on `add_node` effects. In four workers, the native composer chooses `actor+verb`, passes all 8 held-out two-verb episodes, and keeps the prior recurrence/reply transfer results intact; the relation-only actor guard still fails 8/8 as a retained boundary control. The locked analysis passes 14/14 checks. Search over m exposed fields is O(m²) under the current singleton/pair/full-set grammar; nested/computed identity terms and scale remain unmeasured. Target, cue, examples, and oracle remain external. Next measure cost/coverage as m and graph size grow, then test residual-grounded cue formation. See [v1 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SUBSTRATE_EDGE_ATTRIBUTE_IDENTITY_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SUBSTRATE_EDGE_ATTRIBUTE_IDENTITY_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_substrate_edge_attribute_identity_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_substrate_edge_attribute_identity_v1_analysis_20261002.json). residual-grounded cue induction. The next tractability gate measures site
growth and composer cost as identity-field count and host graph size vary,
including unsupported computed/absent fields. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 current-source identity-site extraction scaling

The rule-local site finder was repeated against current `edit_ops.py` with a
complete seven-input lock and independent analyzer. Across nine field counts from
1 to 256, all 10 checks pass: the singleton/pair/full-set count formula holds,
every direct field is covered, and constant/computed fields are excluded. At
256 fields the finder produces 32,897 attribute signatures plus the relation
site in 44.39 ms median. This confirms quadratic proposal-pool growth within
the measured grammar. It does not measure graph-site materialization, native
selection, memory, or task cost; the function is graph-size independent by
interface. The initial pilot lock omitted the analyzer hash and is retained as
diagnostic only. Next combine field count with graph size through complete
site materialization and native choice, under explicit memory and candidate
caps. See [results](EDGE_IDENTITY_SITE_SCALING_CURRENT_SOURCE_V1_1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [preregistration](EDGE_IDENTITY_SITE_SCALING_CURRENT_SOURCE_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), [amendment](EDGE_IDENTITY_SITE_SCALING_CURRENT_SOURCE_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261003.md), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/edge_identity_site_scaling_current_source_v1_1_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/edge_identity_site_scaling_current_source_v1_1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/edge_identity_site_scaling_current_source_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json). ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 residual cue derivation boundary

The source-linked inventory currently validates 28 capability rows, 397 source links, 72 linked test references, and 546 result-linked runner records with no unresolved links or missing evidence fields. The broader programme contains measured structural, engine, learning, and scaling slices, but those fixture-bounded results do not establish an overall polynomial operating regime or general capability.

The newest learning gate passes 13/13 workers: an authored cardinal-action vector decoder recognizes four inversion residuals, supplies the existing A8 cue, and obtains four correct held-out proposals; nine negative/abstention controls emit no proposal. This narrows the raw-error handoff gap, while confirming that cue semantics remain hand-authored. Each operation also adds 24 lazily initialized rules to its fresh experimental context, with five more in cue-positive contexts. Target-rule preservation does not establish full graph isolation. The next priority is to repeat this path through the copy-on-write Thoughtexperiment harness, compare full graph and rule-store deltas, and return/adopt only the validated residual artifacts. The overall capability/tractability goal remains active. See [results](RESIDUAL_TO_COMPOSER_CUE_DERIVATION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_to_composer_cue_derivation_v1_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_to_composer_cue_derivation_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 residual return through CoW branch

The follow-up compares 13 paired task conditions under deep-copy and shared-graph-pages branches. Both retain the four successful held-out proposals and abstain across the nine negative controls. The host is unchanged during exploration. After validation, the harness applies only the branch's post-induction graph delta: 40 nodes and 2 edges per positive case, with exact graph-fact parity and no native rule-vector additions. Median fork time is 0.246 ms for shared pages and 0.515 ms for deep copy on this small fixture; this is not a broad speed claim. The partial-CoW branch therefore provides an experimentally verified isolation and graph-residual return path for this authored task, while independent usability of the returned graph residual, reclamation, full-state CoW, and large-scale economics remain open. The overall goal remains active. See [results](RESIDUAL_COW_RETURN_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](RESIDUAL_COW_RETURN_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_cow_return_v1_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_cow_return_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 returned-candidate rehydration

Four fresh shared-page workers now verify the next link: the passing candidate is serialized into the returned graph residual, read from the host, and scores the same held-out outcomes after deserialization. A stale graph revision is rejected without a partial write; a retry with the current revision succeeds. The candidate residual is therefore transportable and usable within this authored navigation workflow. This is an explicit harness packaging convention; the substrate still receives a hand-authored cue, native rule additions are not merged, and no production adoption has been tested. Reclamation, concurrent writers, other residual schemas, and larger graphs remain open. The overall programme remains active. See [results](RESIDUAL_COW_CANDIDATE_REHYDRATION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](RESIDUAL_COW_CANDIDATE_REHYDRATION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_cow_candidate_rehydration_v1_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_cow_candidate_rehydration_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 retained CoW fanout

At a 100k-node host, 18 fresh workers cover fanout 1/4/16 for full-copy and shared-graph-pages branches. All branch candidates pass held-out scoring; one exact residual returns to the host while the rule store remains unchanged. At fanout 16, validated branches add median current PSS of 209.9 MiB under full copy and 36.8 MiB under shared pages; post-drop PSS remains near those levels, so allocator-retained pages and live-state reclamation are unresolved. Median fork times are 17.32 ms and 0.186 ms, while induction plus grounding is 82–103 ms per branch. This provides workload-specific resource evidence for partial CoW, not general capability, parallel throughput, or a leak-free lifecycle. Next distinguish live branch allocations from allocator/kernel residency, then replicate at larger graphs and another task family. The overall programme remains active. See [results](RESIDUAL_COW_FANOUT_MEMORY_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](RESIDUAL_COW_FANOUT_MEMORY_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_cow_fanout_memory_v1_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_cow_fanout_memory_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 allocator-level branch drop

The glibc allocator probe now separates allocated/mapped bytes from process residency for the same 100k-node, fanout-16 workflow. Across three workers per backend, glibc-accounted bytes return to about 26.3 MiB after all branch objects are dropped, near the 25.8 MiB host baseline; live values before drop are 340.9 MiB full copy and 64.4 MiB shared pages. PSS remains high until `malloc_trim(0)` releases retained arena pages, and then falls below the original baseline because trim also releases prior free memory. The earlier PSS increase therefore did not indicate live glibc-accounted branches in this measured workload. This does not cover allocator paths outside glibc accounting or establish universal memory reclamation. The overall goal remains active. See [results](RESIDUAL_COW_ALLOCATOR_RECLAMATION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](RESIDUAL_COW_ALLOCATOR_RECLAMATION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_cow_allocator_reclamation_v1_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_cow_allocator_reclamation_v1_analysis_20261002.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 million-node Thoughtexperiment fanout

The source-locked retained-fanout workflow now passes at 1,000,000 unrelated host nodes across 18 fresh workers. Every branch generates and validates the authored navigation candidate; host state stays unchanged during exploration; exactly one 41-node/2-edge residual is adopted. At fanout 16, shared graph pages reduce median validated-stage PSS delta from 1,457.3 MiB to 41.9 MiB and median fork time from 184.7 ms to 0.573 ms. The million-node fixture itself peaks around 4.2–4.4 GiB at fanout one; full-copy fanout 16 peaks around 6.99 GiB. This extends partial-CoW workload evidence, not a complexity bound or autonomous self-improvement result. Other task families, concurrent throughput, and larger-scale allocator reclamation remain open. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 recurrence rehearsal at one million nodes

A second Thoughtexperiment family now has source-locked million-node evidence. Six fresh workers restore the recurrence ledger's negative-existence guard in an isolated branch, produce exactly one offer, reach a zero-progress fixed point, and leave the host graph/rule state unchanged. Peak PSS increases are 360.0 MiB for full copy and 19.3 MiB for shared pages; after explicitly deleting both branch and inner-substrate references, measured PSS remains 193.1 MiB and 1.3 MiB above baseline. The full-copy residual cannot yet be assigned to live allocations versus allocator retention. V1 post-drop readings were invalid because an inner-substrate alias kept its branch alive; v1.1 fixes that. This broadens structural/scale evidence from navigation to recurrence but still does not test autonomous repair discovery, natural task frequency, or concurrent service. See [corrected results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_REPAIR_SCALE_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_REPAIR_SCALE_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_repair_scale_v1_1_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_repair_scale_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json). ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 million-node recurrence allocator attribution

A glibc allocator follow-up resolves the full-copy post-release PSS elevation in the measured recurrence workflow. Across three workers per backend, all branch/rule/closure and primary-isolation gates pass. Full-copy branch peak adds 358.0 MiB of glibc-accounted in-use bytes and 360.0 MiB PSS; after explicitly dropping branch references, in-use bytes return within 0.04 MiB of baseline while free arena pages increase about 192 MiB. `malloc_trim(0)` releases those pages, moving PSS slightly below baseline. Shared pages peak at 19.3 MiB PSS and also return in-use bytes close to baseline. This supports allocator reclamation for this glibc/Linux recurrence workload; process-wide counters do not identify individual graph storage or generalize across allocator/runtime configurations. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_ALLOCATOR_RECLAMATION_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_ALLOCATOR_RECLAMATION_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_allocator_reclamation_v1_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_allocator_reclamation_v1_analysis_20261002.json). ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 concurrent recurrence rehearsal at one million nodes

The fanout-eight recurrence wave passes all six process/source/oracle gates at one million linked noise nodes, across full-copy and shared-page branches and sequential/concurrent modes. Shared pages increase throughput from 10.73 to 15.47 experiments/s (1.44×) but raise sampled peak PSS from 142 MiB to 1,129 MiB. Full-copy concurrency reduces throughput from 1.90 to 0.75 experiments/s (0.40×) and raises peak PSS from 360 MiB to 2,053 MiB. This shows why faster CoW forks alone do not guarantee cheap parallel exploration: concurrent residency and serialized Python branch setup matter. Native closure overlaps in this workload, but it is one synthetic recurrence task and does not establish open-ended experiment yield or natural-task rates. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_PARALLEL_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_PARALLEL_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_parallel_scale_v1_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_parallel_scale_v1_analysis_20261002.json). The overall capability and tractability programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 million-node cancellation of recurrence branches

At one million nodes, six source-locked workers create eight patched recurrence branches, discard four before closure, and verify the four survivors over all 64 candidates. All exact-once, fixed-point, source, and primary-isolation gates pass. Dropping half reduces sampled PSS by 726 MiB for full copy and 30.5 MiB for shared pages. After all branch references are cleared, shared-page PSS is 10.5 MiB above baseline, while full-copy PSS remains 1,683 MiB above baseline and needs allocator attribution. A concurrent shared-page transient peaks at 534 MiB despite a 72 MiB all-live checkpoint. Whole-wave medians are 11.74 s full copy and 0.353 s shared pages, including setup, cancellation, survivor closure, and readout. This measures discard-before-closure, not interruption mid-operation or general cancellation bounds. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_CANCEL_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_CANCEL_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_cancel_scale_v1_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_cancel_scale_v1_analysis_20261002.json). ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 allocator attribution for cancelled branches

The follow-up allocator probe resolves most of the million-node full-copy post-release PSS elevation in the cancel-four/survive-four recurrence wave. After all branch references are released, full-copy glibc in-use bytes return within 0.08 MiB of baseline, while free arena rises 1,617 MiB and PSS rises 1,619 MiB. `malloc_trim(0)` lowers PSS to 126 MiB above baseline. Shared-page in-use bytes return within 0.07 MiB and PSS remains 10.5 MiB above baseline before trim. Thus the large full-copy residual mostly reflects free glibc arena residency in this measured Linux workload, not live glibc-accounted branch allocations. Other allocator paths, object-level attribution, and cross-host behavior remain open. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_CANCEL_ALLOCATOR_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RECURRENCE_CANCEL_ALLOCATOR_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_cancel_allocator_v1_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_recurrence_cancel_allocator_v1_analysis_20261002.json). The overall capability and tractability programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 shared edit-op hypothesis generation

The recurrent and per-message conversation panels now construct edge-identity proposals through the shared edit-operation vocabulary rather than an evaluator-local AST transform. Four workers per family let the graph-Term selector choose among three generated edge scopes and one generic composer proposal from opaque candidate IDs and training outcomes. It selects the candidate-scoped recurrence guard and per-message reply guard in all eight cases; all selected candidates pass held-out checks, broader scopes fail, and tie/all-wrong controls abstain. Proposal generation costs 0.303/0.164 ms median; scoring all hypotheses costs 49.75/109.45 ms. This advances proposal construction and evidence-guided selection inside the substrate, while target selection, raw residual interpretation, cue, candidate task, and oracle remain host-authored. It is not autonomous self-improvement. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EDITOPS_EDGE_HYPOTHESES_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EDITOPS_EDGE_HYPOTHESES_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [orders](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_editops_edge_hypotheses_v1_orders_20261002.json), [scores](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_editops_edge_hypotheses_v1_scores_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_editops_edge_hypotheses_v1_analysis_20261002.json). ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 raw-residual causal observability audit

The first instrumentation increment is implemented: `run_rules_delta_report` now emits bounded per-invocation rule names, progress flags, and touched node/edge targets. A rebuilt temporary extension passed paired exact-delta smoke checks for direct node/edge writes and overlapping writers, including a no-op attempt; both cases reconciled under the exact graph oracle. This is single-host smoke evidence only. Binding identity, fact-level before/after values, attribute-key attribution, deferred-effect ownership, multi-process reconciliation, and trace overhead remain open. The trace identifies candidate rule-to-location links; target-blind residual diagnosis and learning remain unproven. The source audit records the implementation and next gates in [raw-residual causality audit](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RAW_RESIDUAL_CAUSALITY_AUDIT_20261002.md). The overall capability and tractability programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 source-locked rule-write attribution

The single-host smoke has been replaced by a source-locked three-process panel on mixed direct-write and overlapping/no-op fixtures. All semantic gates pass: ordinary, attributed, and exact-snapshot settlement agree; per-rule target unions cover every exact changed node and edge; aggregate touched targets cover all attributed targets; the no-op writer is represented; no trace events drop. Across 45 paired samples per arm and fixture, median attributed/ordinary wall ratios are 1.52× (mixed writes) and 1.60× (overlapping writers), with absolute medians below 0.181 ms on these small graphs. This is a diagnostic instrumentation cost estimate for two fixtures, not production overhead. Binding/value attribution, deferred-effect causality, and target-blind diagnosis remain open. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 deferred RuleEdit origin

One name-addressed `SetRuleClause` queue now retains its producer identity through drain. In three fresh workers, ordinary, attributed, and exact-snapshot reports agree at fixed point; one deferred event from `edit_target_clause` is marked progressed. Independent `export_rules_prefix` readback shows `target_rule` changed from revision 0 (`where=false`) to revision 1 (`where=true`), after which that rule writes the matched graph node. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 deduplicated RulePatch origins

RulePatch queue attribution now preserves the full producer set without changing node-level deduplication. Two one-shot producer rules submit the same graph-resident patch; in three fresh workers the drain emits one deferred event carrying both `submit_alpha` and `submit_beta`, commits exactly once, and moves the target rule from revision 0 to 1. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 residual-branch isolation and return

The cue-derivation mechanism now runs inside an immutable CoW snapshot branch. Across 13 fresh source-locked cases, all four opposite-action cues still produce the held `flip_comparison` repair and pass held-outs; nine non-inversion/ambiguous cases abstain. Every branch changes, but the primary graph, rules, and revision remain identical throughout exploration. Each worker returns exactly one `ExperimentResidual` node; all previous nodes and edges and the rule store remain unchanged, and positive residuals carry the serialized candidate. Median fork time is 0.263 ms for this small fixture. This closes the branch-containment and result-return question for the hand-authored decoder, not target-blind diagnosis: the host still maps action geometry to cue vocabulary. Next test diagnosis from substrate-observed, per-firing write evidence with correlated/competing causes, no supplied cue or target, and honest abstention; then continue cross-family held-out learning and scale measurements. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 trace-guided rule-site discovery

A three-worker source/native-locked pilot now feeds native per-firing write-target records into the shared edit-op site finder. In the one-step recurrence fault, 12 events with zero drops narrow the candidate source set to one progressed edge writer and generate its three edge-identity guard sites, without passing the evaluator target or cue into discovery; rule-store state is unchanged during proposal construction. The result is a reduction in structural search space on one authored case. It is not target-blind cause finding because rule identifiers retain semantic names, nor does it show that the writer was causally necessary or that any candidate helps held-outs. Next anonymize writer identity, include correlated and no-op writers, and require substrate-side candidate selection with utility/abstention controls across separate task families. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 anonymized trace-guided proposal selection

The follow-up now removes semantic rule identifiers from discovery and selection inputs, adds a correlated shadow writer with the same binding shape, and records an empty-effect no-op control. Four fresh workers each see 25 trace events with no drops and generate six proposals from two opaque writer identities. The substrate selector chooses the unique exact-once training winner in 4/4 workers; the chosen target repair passes 8/8 held-out episodes, while tie/all-wrong controls abstain in 8/8. Candidate selection costs a median 0.833 ms after 5.934 ms median training scoring over six proposals/two cases. These times omit setup, tracing, candidate generation, held-out checks, and label acquisition. This is evidence-guided selection over a small, externally labeled candidate set, not causal attribution from trace evidence alone: task construction, task oracle, candidate execution, and labels remain harness-controlled, and relation structure remains visible. Next vary task families and confounders, include field/value and binding-level provenance, and charge complete search/validation costs before expanding any autonomy claim. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ANONYMIZED_CAUSE_SITE_DISCOVERY_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_ANONYMIZED_CAUSE_SITE_DISCOVERY_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_anonymized_cause_site_discovery_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_anonymized_cause_site_discovery_v1_lock_20261002.json), [orders](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_anonymized_cause_site_discovery_v1_orders_20261002.json), [scores](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_anonymized_cause_site_discovery_v1_scores_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_anonymized_cause_site_discovery_v1_analysis_20261002.json). The overall goal remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 anonymized discovery transfer

The source-locked follow-up extends the blinded writer-site process from recurrence to conversation. In four workers per family, all 105 analyzer checks pass: six recurrence proposals and 18 conversation proposals per worker, one unique training winner in every cell, native selector agreement in all eight cells, selected-candidate success on every held-out episode, and correct tie/all-wrong abstention. The correlated shadow writer is visible in both trace sets; an empty-effect rule appears in non-progressing events without entering candidate generation. Median training candidate-scoring costs are 5.826 ms for the six-candidate recurrence cells and 601.746 ms for the 18-candidate conversation cells; selector medians are 0.865/0.866 ms. These timings are not end-to-end search costs and the family setups differ. The result supports transfer across two authored task structures with exact host labels; it does not establish autonomous causality, open-ended learning, or broad generality. Next broaden to independently designed tasks and task-free residuals, add stronger correlated/necessity controls, and measure complete branch, discovery, scoring, validation, and adoption costs. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 exact inference width envelope

The current-native width continuation passes all ten capture gates across 48 workers; independent analysis passes 40/40. At fixed N=120, exact binary pairwise JT reaches certified q=9 with median time 2.48 s on the grid and 210 ms on the edge-matched sparse q-tree. Relative to same-binary q=6 controls, certified JT time grows 9.00× and 3.81×; traced peak rises 8.20× and 1.84×. Min-fill overestimates all 18 q=7–9 grid cases but none of the q-trees, demonstrating that the current safe upper-bound gate can refuse certifiably tractable grids. The v1.3 native binary differed, so no ratio uses that historical capture. These results make the conditional boundary concrete while leaving natural topology prevalence, non-binary factors, task-derived decompositions, and larger-N behavior open. Next vary N independently at fixed q and obtain task-derived factor graphs; do not infer a cheap-cut prior from these synthetic fixtures. See [results](CAN_EDGE_MATCHED_INFERENCE_COST_V1_4_RESULTS_20261002.md), [preregistration](CAN_EDGE_MATCHED_INFERENCE_COST_V1_4_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_4.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_4_lock_20261002.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_4_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/can_edge_matched_inference_cost_v1_4_20261002_analysis.json). ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 exact inference node scaling

The fixed-width continuation passes all capture gates across 36 fresh workers, with 56/56 independent analysis checks. From N=126 to 504, certified-order binary pairwise JT grows 4.21×/5.01× at q=6 and 4.40×/7.60× at q=9 for grid/q-tree families; traced Python peaks grow 3.42–4.42×. At N=504,q=9, median cost is 12.00 s and 55.1 MiB traced for grids versus 1.56 s and 1.78 MiB for sparse q-trees. The fixed-width message profile and traced-memory changes are near node-linear over these fixtures, while min-fill order discovery can itself become material (q=9 q-tree: 16 ms at N=126, 516 ms at N=504). This sharpens the synthetic inference cost envelope; it does not establish natural frame locality, task-derived width prevalence, non-binary cost, or whole-system tractability. Next use representative task-derived factor graphs and record the sampling frame before stratifying by width, then vary domain size. See [results](CAN_EDGE_MATCHED_INFERENCE_NODE_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [preregistration](CAN_EDGE_MATCHED_INFERENCE_NODE_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/can_edge_matched_inference_node_scale_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/can_edge_matched_inference_node_scale_v1_lock_20261002.json), and [capture/analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/can_edge_matched_inference_node_scale_v1_20261002_analysis.json). ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 Bigville population inference census

The locked census rebuilt the complete committed 100-resident Bigville cast in three fresh workers and extracted each runtime 3-state CAN. All 300 per-resident MAP results match independent exhaustive oracles over 3^5 assignments, and whole-population JT matches the sum of resident oracle energies in all workers; the independent analyzer passes 16/16 checks. Each authored frame has five concepts, five factors, and width 2; there are no cross-frame factors. Median build/extraction/global min-fill/JT times are 56.9/7.50/75.6/10.05 ms. This validates exact-inference integration over one real-parameter authored population and reveals that ordering and setup are material. It is a disconnected-by-design positive control, not evidence that task-derived frames are naturally sparse or that partitioning reduces total cost. Next obtain a predefined sample of connected task-derived factor graphs and compare monolithic solving with discovered, oracle, and size-matched random partitions under exact semantic parity and fully charged costs. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 exact inference domain cost

The paired binary/ternary panel passes all capture gates across 36 fresh workers; independent analysis passes 55/55. With graph, fields, couplings, and certified width matched, ternary JT costs 4.41–16.26× binary JT across q=4–6, and its traced Python peak grows 1.82–12.18×. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 ternary exact-inference width envelope

The supported 3-state exact-inference path now reaches certified q=8 at N=120. All 18 current-native ternary runs pass extraction, oracle, state, width, mutation, and source gates; independent analysis passes 16/16. With identical fixture and current native/solver hashes, ternary certified JT is 35.55× slower than binary on q=8 grids and 24.02× slower on q-trees. Absolute ternary medians are 44.06 s/153 MiB traced and 2.59 s/3.17 MiB, respectively. From q=6 to q=8, time rises 9.75× on grids and 4.10× on q-trees. Min-fill estimates q=8 grid width at 10–12 despite the certified width of 8; its JT median is 67.0 s versus 44.1 s under the certified order. This sharpens the practical domain-width envelope and exposes order-quality cost, without establishing natural width or topology prevalence. Next measure ternary inference on task-derived connected factors; otherwise keep the claim bounded to the supported solver and tested synthetic envelope. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 cross-model extraction feasibility

The Qwen3-1.7B checkpoint is available locally in two Safetensors shards. The existing NumPy Qwen3 recovery path can consume it through an index-backed tensor lookup adapter: the embedding loaded with shape 151,936×2,048, and one short-input `_ffn_io` pass produced outputs at all 28 layers in 60.55 seconds on CPU. No substrate or repository code was changed for this smoke check. This shows that a second-model extraction is technically possible, but a full 60/10 corpus run has not been performed. The existing 0.6B chain export has no recoverable source text/token IDs, so the next valid comparison must preregister a fresh shared text corpus and split, preserve its text/tokenizer provenance, export both models, and then repeat the exact-inference and cost gates. The cross-model claim remains open; the goal remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 Qwen3 cross-model transport

A preregistered, shared-text evaluation now extends the four-hop fan-in assay from Qwen3-0.6B to independently fitted Qwen3-1.7B structure. Three fresh workers per model complete all 960 graph cases; the independent analyzer passes every gate, including exact junction-tree/exhaustive-oracle/direct-rescore agreement, topology repeatability, and isolation. Both models reproduce widths 1/2/4–6/8 for fan-in 1/2/4/8 and similar medians for JT cost (about 0.15/0.37/9/537 ms across fan-ins). Top-2 is about 1,400× faster than dense top-8 on this fixed 16-node solver workload. Held-out internal sign agreement does not improve consistently with pruning; BP differs from exact MAP in 3/240 cells for 0.6B and zero for 1.7B. This strengthens evidence that the engine cost effect is robust across two model scales, but edge pruning is explicitly imposed, the 70-text corpus is generated, and the metric is not task utility. It does not establish natural cheap-cut discovery, transfer between model-specific bases, or self-improvement. The overall goal remains active. See [results](LLM_CROSS_MODEL_TRANSPORT_V1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol](LLM_CROSS_MODEL_TRANSPORT_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [source-lock amendment](LLM_CROSS_MODEL_TRANSPORT_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/llm_cross_model_transport_v1_1_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/llm_cross_model_transport_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 task-derived taxonomy traversal

The corrected v1.1 assay passes source, worker, query coverage, graph-isolation, and independent-analysis gates across three fresh workers. On a harvested graph of 6,445 concepts and 5,772 `isa` edges, 9,640 queries per worker complete at a median 345,671 queries/s and 109.39 MiB peak RSS. All answers match the six-hop operating envelope; versus full closure, accuracy is 99.969%, with three expected misses at path lengths 7–8. The shuffled control scores 50.47%. This demonstrates cheap bounded traversal over a sparse graph with at most one outgoing `isa` edge per concept. It does not establish treewidth, natural cheap-cut discovery, external taxonomy correctness (the separate harvest audit reports 27.2% valid-ancestor yield), or task utility. The next locality gate is connected task-derived factors with automatic partition proposals, semantic parity, and fully charged discovery cost. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 task-graph separator audit

An exhaustive, source-locked audit of the 30-row Qwen3 mode-neuron inference census finds one connected K₄,₈ topology repeated across all held-out inputs. Enumerating all 4,096 variable subsets identifies a unique minimum separator of four mode variables. Conditioning on those modes gives 1,944 table-assignment operations versus 2,064 for a width-optimal monolithic elimination proxy, only 1.06× fewer. This is evidence that an explicit separator exists in this authored model representation, while also showing that separator size alone does not imply a large inference-cost reduction. No wall-time, memory, automatic discovery, or natural-task prevalence claim is made. The next gate must broaden to task graphs with varied topology and charge partition discovery alongside exact semantic parity and full execution cost. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 transport role-conditioned exact inference

The source-locked v1.1 follow-up compares a mode-conditioned exact solver with monolithic JT across 60 learned/shuffled held-out cells. All 60 conditioned solutions match JT energy and returned target signs; learned held-out sign agreement is 0.800 and shuffled is 0.413 for both. Median conditioned runtime is 2.41–2.39 ms versus 542–543 ms for JT, about 222–226× faster on this workload. The graph builder supplies the source/target split, and the new solver uses vectorized NumPy while JT is the Python implementation, so the timing difference combines structural decomposition and implementation strategy; it is not a substrate-runtime speedup or evidence of automatic cut discovery. This shows a promising exact solver specialization worth testing across less regular structures, but the cheap-cut hypothesis remains empirically open. Next compare partition discovery plus exact solving on varied task-derived graphs against optimized monolithic baselines, preserving semantic parity and charging all costs. See [results](LLM_TRANSPORT_ROLE_SEPARATOR_V1_1_RESULTS_20261002.md), [protocol and amendment](LLM_TRANSPORT_ROLE_SEPARATOR_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/llm_transport_role_separator_v1_1_lock_20261002.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/llm_transport_role_separator_v1_1_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/llm_transport_role_separator_v1_1_analysis_20261002.json). The overall goal remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 transport role-conditioned inference timing audit

The source-locked v1.2 repeat separates uninstrumented timing from traced allocation measurement and adds exact returned-sign parity against monolithic JT. All 60 cells pass energy, state, direct-rescore, sign-parity, source, coverage, and mutation gates. Median role-conditioned time is 2.29 ms (learned) and 2.27 ms (shuffled), against 540–543 ms for JT, for paired ratios of 236× and 241×. The builder supplies the source/target split; the solver uses vectorized NumPy; JT uses the Python junction-tree implementation. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 graph-only separator proposal cost

An exhaustive graph-only search over two locked task-derived topologies finds the unique four-variable separator in K₄,₈ after 794 tested subsets (5.78 ms median) and both eight-variable shores in K₈,₈ after 39,203 subsets (356.57 ms median). On the registered 20-cell K₈,₈ transport batch, proposal time amortizes to 17.83 ms per cell; proposal plus the previously measured conditioned solve is about 20.12 ms per cell versus about 540 ms for JT, around 26.9× faster for this batch. A single solve plus search is about 359 ms versus 540 ms. The follow-on conditioned solver did not consume anonymized candidates directly, and both families have fixed authored bipartite topology; exhaustive search cost will grow combinatorially. This is a first explicit accounting of proposal cost, not evidence of practical general frame discovery. Next pass graph-only proposals into a generic exact solver and test varied, independently sourced task graphs. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-02 anonymous proposal-to-solver integration

The v1.2 graph-only pipeline now searches every per-cell anonymized task graph and passes all minimum separators to a generic exact conditioning solver. Across 60 cells, independent analysis confirms all proposals and directly rescores every candidate; every candidate matches monolithic JT energy and returned signs. Yet the complete unamortized route takes a median 738 ms versus 539 ms for JT (about 1.37× slower): 299 ms proposal plus 440 ms solving both candidates. Precomputing per-component factor incidence cut candidate-solving time by about 64% versus the earlier pilot, but redundant candidate solves and repeated topology search remain costly. This is a concrete implementation gap, not a failure of exactness. Next solve one deterministic valid separator and cache it only while an anonymous topology signature remains identical, then compare end-to-end cost across the repeated held-out batch. The graph remains one fixed authored K₈,₈ family, so natural-task generality is open. See [v1.2 results](LLM_TRANSPORT_ANONYMOUS_SEPARATOR_SOLVER_V1_2_RESULTS_20261002.md), [base protocol](LLM_TRANSPORT_ANONYMOUS_SEPARATOR_SOLVER_V1_PREREG_20261002.md), [v1.1 amendment](LLM_TRANSPORT_ANONYMOUS_SEPARATOR_SOLVER_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [v1.2 amendment](LLM_TRANSPORT_ANONYMOUS_SEPARATOR_SOLVER_V1_2_AMENDMENT_20261002.md), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/llm_transport_anonymous_separator_solver_v1_2_lock_20261002.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/llm_transport_anonymous_separator_solver_v1_2_20261002.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/llm_transport_anonymous_separator_solver_v1_2_analysis_20261002.json). ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 anonymous separator caching

The v1.3 follow-up uses one opaque worker-level node permutation, exhaustive graph-only search, exact anonymous topology signatures, and one deterministic valid minimum separator per topology. In each of three workers, one proposal serves all 20 held-out/control cells (19 cache hits). Every selected state matches JT energy and target signs; the independent analyzer validates separator minimality and directly rescores the state. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 varied-topology separator census

The confirmatory v1.2 census passes source, reconstruction, independent separator recomputation, and three-worker repeatability gates across 960 reconstructed rows and 242 distinct model/fan-in/topology keys. The 16-node generated graphs show empty separators for the disconnected fan-in-1 cases, mostly disconnected fan-in-2 cases, and minimum separator sizes 1–3 for most connected fan-in-4 cases. Both dense fan-in-8 graphs require separators of size 8; exhaustive search takes 298–319 ms on this host, versus medians below 1.2 ms for fan-in 1–4. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 varied-topology exact solver integration

The confirmatory v1.3 run completes 320 unique generated factor graphs in each of three fresh workers. Independent analysis validates every graph-only minimum separator and directly rescores all 960 conditioned and junction-tree states; energy, returned-sign, topology, width, source-lock, vocabulary, and no-mutation gates pass. Each worker reuses 79 topology proposals. The full batch structural route, including seed initialization, factor-graph construction/extraction, proposal and component-order costs, per-row factor binding, and exact solving, takes 20.72–21.10 s versus 40.56–41.91 s for the current monolithic Python junction-tree route (1.95–1.99× faster). The average conceals a strong fan-in interaction: fan-in 1 is 1.06–1.11×, fan-in 2 is 0.91–0.96×, fan-in 4 is 0.32×, and fan-in 8 is 2.20–2.27×. V1.1 exposed the cost of enumerating a 15-node residual component; v1.2 exposed a factor-weight cache contamination bug and is excluded from timing claims. V1.3 fixes both through component-local exact inference and topology-only caching, with per-case factor rebinding.

This advances the structural tractability result from proposal-only to exact solver integration across varied generated topologies. It remains a small, imposed-fan-in workload using two model exports and a Python junction-tree comparator; no natural-task prevalence, larger-graph scaling, optimized-baseline advantage, or substrate-driven route selection is established. Next evaluate task graphs from natural or independently sourced tasks, increase graph size, optimize both paths, and then measure whether the substrate can learn a correct cost-aware choice. See [v1.3 results](LLM_TRANSPORT_VARIED_TOPOLOGY_SOLVER_V1_3_RESULTS_20261003.md), [v1.3 amendment](LLM_TRANSPORT_VARIED_TOPOLOGY_SOLVER_V1_3_AMENDMENT_20261003.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/llm_transport_varied_topology_solver_v1_3_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/llm_transport_varied_topology_solver_v1_3_20261003.json), and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/llm_transport_varied_topology_solver_v1_3_analysis_20261003.json). The overall goal remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 task-derived graph structure

The confirmatory task-graph census profiles 78 snapshots across three previously locked workload families and passes independent reconstruction, source, coverage, and min-fill-certificate gates. The projections are sparse: min-fill width upper bounds are 3 for Bigville, 6–7 for connected-conversation, and 6 for discourse; 36/63 Bigville snapshots are disconnected and 27 have articulation points, while all conversation/discourse snapshots are disconnected in the simple undirected projection. The complete run uses the shared manifest and records 10.17 s wall time, 10.17 s CPU time, and 278 MB peak RSS. This measures selected task-graph structure only. Edge semantics, factor-scope fidelity, preservation of inference or closure across cuts, native partition execution, solver speedup, and natural-use prevalence remain unverified. Next audit the source factors/dynamic dependencies, then compare a graph-derived split solver with a monolithic exact oracle on connected and high-coupling task-derived controls. V1–v1.2 failures remain available through the results revision history; only v1.3 is confirmatory.

### Edge semantics and access fidelity follow-up (2026-10-03)

A source-locked retrospective audit independently reconstructs typed-edge profiles and producer-derived frame interactions for all 78 snapshots. All 15 conversation/discourse full-node projections are disconnected, but all 15 frame-interaction graphs are complete after shared boundary nodes are included. Bigville's 317,056 observed causal edge/attribute reads match typed edges in the captured graph, but every worker reports a wildcard read and all 63 partition executions are refused. The conversation capture has no concrete access trace; discourse has bounded static rule scopes but only eight concrete attribute-read events. Low undirected width alone therefore does not establish a cheap computational cut. The next gate is a fresh access-instrumented workload followed by exact monolithic/partitioned state parity under shared-boundary and high-coupling controls. The audit is exploratory, not a natural-task frequency estimate or a factorization result. See [audit results](SUBSTRATE_TASK_GRAPH_EDGE_ACCESS_AUDIT_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [method](SUBSTRATE_TASK_GRAPH_EDGE_ACCESS_AUDIT_V1_METHOD_20261003.md), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/substrate_task_graph_edge_access_audit_v1_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_task_graph_edge_access_audit_v1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_task_graph_edge_access_audit_v1_analysis_20261003.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 connected-conversation access tracing

The v1.3 fresh paired capture runs three workers over four conversation cases with concrete tracing disabled/enabled. All 12 worker/case pairs match exactly at 198 closure checkpoints per arm and at final graph state; goals resolve and closure reaches fixed point. Trace-on records 6,240 event occurrences attributed to four bounded discourse-rule signatures. Most observed accesses touch shared/unowned state, making the selected frame interaction graph complete. This is trace-on/off parity on the current build, not proof of trace completeness, safe partitioned closure, speedup, or natural-task prevalence. Source review shows `run_rules_scoped` is scoped to a Microtheory's `has_concept`-owned nodes, while the conversation frame labels are separate harness metadata over one shared discourse state; the delta report also marks notifications incomplete. Current scoped closure therefore cannot serve directly as the partitioned comparator. The next gate is an explicit task ownership/shared-interface prototype followed by exact monolithic parity, with deferred effects and notifications gated, plus dynamic/high-coupling controls. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 task-scope closure parity pilot

The source-locked pilot maps the two concurrent-goal frames onto two seed-provided Microtheory roots by adding identical task-node ownership edges in both arms, then compares monolithic closure against repeated complete sweeps of `run_rules_scoped`. Across three fresh workers, all 24 checkpoint graph digests, all final graph digests, and goal statuses match; scoped quiescence reports no pending deferred effects. The scoped route uses 132 calls total and has a descriptive median checkpoint cost of 0.928 ms versus 0.044 ms monolithic. It still runs matching over the full graph and includes extra quiescence checks, so this is a narrow parity positive control and an overhead observation, not evidence for factorization or a fair speedup comparison. The first harness self-check mistakenly expected two cases per worker; that failed capture is preserved, and the corrected run passes. Next expand parity to shared-ground stress, 4/8 goals, dynamic ownership/deletion/reuse, and cross-frame negative controls; meter matcher work and ownership/scheduling overhead. See [results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_PARITY_PILOT_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [method](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_PARITY_PILOT_V1_METHOD_20261003.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_analysis_20261003.json). V1.1 records and independently checks per-scope quiescence at every checkpoint. Exact state parity remains 24/24 checkpoints and 3/3 final graphs. Scope closures account for 56,286 candidate visits and 192,078 Term evaluations, against 6,024 and 17,184 monolithic (9.34× and 11.18× higher), because the current implementation still runs full-graph matching for each scope call. ### Scoped matcher fallback attribution

All four current discourse lifecycle rules have four-edge patterns, so none qualifies for the existing one-edge scoped candidate paths. Across v1.4, 11,640 scoped matcher invocations take `FullFallback`, while local-owned-edge and Microtheory-seeded path counters remain zero. This source/counter match explains why the parity schedule multiplies candidate visits. Next test typed candidate seeding for owned Goals, with ordering and shared-boundary controls, before drawing any scalability conclusion.
