## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 frame-order sensitivity

V1.5 evaluates ascending, descending, and outside-in frame sweep orders across three workers per order and the same 2/4/8-goal plus common-ground panel. All 594 checkpoint states and all 36 final states match monolithic closure, and each traced/untraced scoped pair also matches. Candidate-work overhead stays between 8.90× and 9.34×; Term-work overhead stays between 11.15× and 11.18×. The existing 4-edge rules still invoke full matching, so this tests final-state invariance to scope scheduling order, not same ordered binding/effect semantics or a pruned matcher. Next add a declared typed anchor and measure an opt-in multi-edge candidate path under binding-order, high-coupling, and dynamic-owner controls. See [v1.5 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_ORDER_PARITY_V1_5_RESULTS_20261003.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_5_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_5_analysis_20261003.json). The overall goal remains active.

An exploratory all-variable seeded multi-edge path was then evaluated as v1.6. Exact graph parity remains 594/594 checkpoints and 36/36 final states, but candidate work increases 3.6% and Term work 13.7% relative to v1.5 (9.45× and 12.69× monolithic). The path runs only before edge deletion and is used 8,520 times; 26,400 invocations still take fallback. It is not a speedup and should not be treated as an adopted optimization. Next inspect per-rule/per-epoch costs and seek a sound typed anchor or a join-order strategy that avoids repeated enumeration; require work reduction as well as parity before continuing this branch. See [v1.6 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_LOCAL_PATTERN_V1_6_RESULTS_20261003.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_6_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_6_analysis_20261003.json). The overall goal remains active.

V1.7 serializes the per-rule counters and confirms all scoped work on this panel belongs to the four discourse goal lifecycle rules: resolve (46,467 candidates/171,306 Terms), select (44,547/158,922), execute (44,247/165,186), and fail (35,592/159,024). This locates the cost center but does not identify the fraction attributable to seeded enumeration, post-deletion fallback, or repeated closure epochs. Next add path-by-rule and epoch attribution before choosing another matcher design. See [v1.7 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_COST_ATTRIBUTION_V1_7_RESULTS_20261003.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_7_analysis_20261003.json). The overall goal remains active.

V1.8 adds path-by-rule counts: 8,520 local-pattern matcher calls versus 26,400 full fallbacks, consistently split across the four rules. The guard's graph-global `edge_delete_generation` means any prior deletion disables the local path for later calls, accounting for 75.6% fallback. The exact state gates still pass, but no work reduction has been demonstrated. Next evaluate deletion-safe match-order reconstruction or an explicit anchor contract, with work-count improvement as a required gate. See [v1.8 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_PATH_ATTRIBUTION_V1_8_RESULTS_20261003.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_8_analysis_20261003.json). The overall goal remains active.

V1.9 replaces the global deletion guard with live candidate-slice positions and passes the same exact-state panel, including 34,920 local-path invocations with zero fallback. However, candidate work rises to 12.52× and Term work to 22.62× monolithic, worse than v1.8's 9.45×/12.69×. Amended analyzer v1.9.1 corrects the obsolete nonzero-fallback expectation; raw capture and original analysis remain preserved. Do not adopt all-variable seeded traversal. The next candidate is a declared, semantically justified single anchor or a single-pass join reorder, measured against same-source fallback with exact parity and reduced work as gates. The anchor contract, cross-frame control, and fallback gate are preregistered in [v2.0 protocol](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_DECLARED_ANCHOR_V2_PREREG_20261003.md). See [v1.9 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_DELETION_SAFE_ORDER_V1_9_RESULTS_20261003.md) and [amended analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_9_1_amended_analysis_20261003.json). The overall goal remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 declared scope-anchor result

The v2.0.1 source-locked evaluation passes all semantic and guard gates across 54 paired cases and 738 checkpoints. Fallback, declared-anchor, and trace arms match exact checkpoint/final graph state; the aligned cases exercise the anchor path, and cross-frame/orphan controls exercise its conservative rejection path. The performance hypothesis fails: anchor and fallback both enumerate 81,228 Candidate pairs and evaluate 313,842 Terms, giving zero work reduction and ratios of 4.02×/5.34× versus monolithic closure. Do not claim or adopt a speedup from this matcher. It verifies the metadata and fallback semantics on this authored panel, while leaving efficient scoped matching unresolved. Next attribute work by pattern clause and candidate source, then change the algorithm to avoid repeated expansion rather than only reorder clauses. See [v2.0.1 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_DECLARED_ANCHOR_V2_RESULTS_20261003.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_anchor_pilot_v2_0_1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_anchor_pilot_v2_0_1_analysis_20261003.json). ## Overall status correction — fallback isolation in v2.0

The v2.0.1/v2.0.2 fallback arm did not strip anchor metadata because `substrate.boot_core` retains a module-level `manifest_for` import. Their state parity and anchor guard observations remain valid for anchor-enabled operation, but their claimed anchor-versus-fallback work comparison and equal-work conclusion are withdrawn. The corrected v2.1.1 source-locked selectivity evaluation patches both module references and asserts that every fallback checkpoint has zero declared-anchor attempts/rejections. Across 81 paired cases/954 checkpoints it passes exact state and trace parity; anchor work is 33.7% lower in Candidate pairs and 34.2% lower in Terms than full fallback. At 1,024 complete unowned decoys, reductions are 32.3%/33.3%. Anchor remains 2.61×/2.80× monolithic work across the full matrix. This is evidence for scoped pruning under an authored irrelevant-match distribution, not natural-task prevalence or general tractability. Next vary degree skew and partial decoy structures, then test a naturally harvested task graph. ## Overall status addendum — live task-graph harvest

A source-locked replay captures final graphs from five existing live conversation routes (two fresh workers each). All ten graph digests match their same-condition replicates and all worker/source checks pass. Independent ownership reconstruction from inbound Message identity plus Goal `comprehends` and Event `for_goal`/`origin_event` relations finds one request frame per process; only two of five route conditions have any assignable Goal. Frames contain one or five nodes inside 25k-node full-seed graphs, and touch 16–30 distinct unowned nodes. Because each process handles one request, zero cross-frame edges and width zero are design outcomes, not evidence for cheap cuts. Current task oracles pass 6/10; scene description unexpectedly routes to `self_report` in both runs, while concern authoring also fails twice. This source-specific result is not a natural-task prevalence estimate. The locked analyzer failed on a missing import; the preserved v1.1 post-capture analyzer passes all independent gates. Next audit missing Goal/Message identity links, then harvest multi-turn graph sequences with explicit owner labels. ## Overall status addendum — multi-turn task-frame scaling

The 12-worker repeated self-report scale capture passes source integrity, per-turn identity/route, and amended semantic-replicate gates. Each prompt creates a distinct resolved Goal linked to its own Message, with six owned nodes per frame. However, boundary-mediated interactions form a complete graph across the frames: exact interaction treewidth rises from 0 at one turn to 7 at eight turns. Seven direct cross-frame edges understate the coupling because shared unowned nodes induce 28 frame-pair interactions at N=8. The route therefore does not exhibit cheap-cut locality as repeated discourse state accumulates. Peak RSS stays near 1.72 GiB because this includes full-seed boot and the base graph; later turn latency is usually 130–160 ms after a ~330 ms first turn. A raw exact digest gate failed due solely to generated `SystemModel` timing attrs; the preserved v1.1 analysis normalizes only those attrs and passes semantic graph parity. This remains one repeated prompt family, not a natural conversation estimate. Next measure semantically distinct multi-turn interactions, separating independent tasks from dependent follow-ups. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_CONVERSATION_MULTITURN_FRAME_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_CONVERSATION_MULTITURN_FRAME_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), [digest amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_CONVERSATION_MULTITURN_FRAME_SCALE_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_conversation_multiturn_frame_scale_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json). ## Overall status addendum — distinct-turn frame scale

A source-locked six-worker panel compared three independent authored prompts with a three-turn context-dependent follow-up sequence. The amended semantic replicate gates pass after retaining the raw v1 digest failures caused only by generated `SystemModel` timing fields. The independent sequence produced three Message roots but only one explicitly linked Goal; the context-dependent sequence produced one linked Goal per Message, though its two follow-up `answer_topic` Goals stayed active and generated an information request / unknown-fact reply. Both arms have complete three-frame interaction graphs (treewidth 2), so this small panel provides no narrow-width evidence. Ownership varies with the live route, and explicit ownership does not establish successful grounded follow-up. Next use contrastive prior-answer controls to test whether follow-ups bind to the specific earlier answer, alongside a fresh-first-turn control. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_CONVERSATION_DISTINCT_TURN_FRAME_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_CONVERSATION_DISTINCT_TURN_FRAME_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), [digest amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_CONVERSATION_DISTINCT_TURN_FRAME_SCALE_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_conversation_distinct_turn_frame_scale_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json). The overall goal remains active.

## Overall status addendum — follow-up content binding

A preregistered nine-worker contrast holds the follow-up prompt fixed across a known organisation answer, an unknown-fact answer, and a fresh-turn control. All nine runs pass source and prompt integrity; v1.1 semantic replicate comparison passes after excluding only generated SystemModel timing attrs, while raw digest failures remain recorded for the two-turn arms. The exact follow-up reply is identical in all three conditions (`What information should I use to answer that?`), with zero preregistered content-token overlap to either prior answer. Yet explicit cross-turn edges (`responds_to`/`follows_event` or `follows_event`) exist in the graph. This shows event continuity without evidence of prior-answer content use for this prompt. Next test counterbalanced values where the correct follow-up answer must change with the preceding turn and verify against an external oracle. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_FOLLOWUP_CONTEXT_BINDING_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_FOLLOWUP_CONTEXT_BINDING_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), [digest amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_FOLLOWUP_CONTEXT_BINDING_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_followup_context_binding_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json). The overall goal remains active.

## Overall status addendum — counterbalanced value recall

A nine-worker source-locked test provided a plain user-stated secret code (14 or 29), asked the same question, and included a fresh-turn control. All 15 conversation turns across the nine workers returned `ERROR: no communicative response could be generated.` Both exact recall arms score 0/3, and the corrected fresh abstention control scores 0/3. The graph still has an `answer_topic` Goal linked to the question and `responds_to`/`follows_event` edges to the prior value Message, but the answer neither changes with the counterbalanced value nor succeeds. V1's numeral-only fresh oracle incorrectly called the error a pass; the post-capture v1.1 amendment fixes this and sorts only the empirically unordered Interlocutor `believed_concepts` set for semantic replicate comparison. This establishes a specific live conversational recall failure, not failure of the standalone episodic memory API. Existing episodic-memory rules/tests operate on explicitly structured `EpisodicEvent`/context/schema facts; the natural-language-to-memory bridge remains unestablished. Next test direct memory API storage/retrieval against conversational integration. See [results](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_COUNTERBALANCED_VALUE_RECALL_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_COUNTERBALANCED_VALUE_RECALL_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_COUNTERBALANCED_VALUE_RECALL_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_counterbalanced_value_recall_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json). The overall goal remains active.

## Overall status addendum — structured episodic recall

The isolated graph-native episodic path passes 9/9 source-locked workers across two known values and an unknown-cue control. With explicit observed-event/context/schema input, graph-tick rules bind one Episode, retrieve the correct state through one RecallCandidate at confidence .85/uncertainty .15, and reject a missing cue with one explicit insufficient-information outcome at uncertainty 1.0. This contrasts with the previous live `converse` value test (0/6 recall, all follow-up outputs communication errors): memory rule capability exists under structured inputs, while natural-language event binding and response integration remain unproven. The minimal 18-node structured graph and ~148 MiB process are not directly comparable to full-seed ~25k-node conversation workers at ~1.72 GiB. Next inspect `converse` graph evidence for event/context/schema and retrieval-request creation before designing any bridge. See [results](SUBSTRATE_STRUCTURED_EPISODIC_RECALL_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_STRUCTURED_EPISODIC_RECALL_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/structured_episodic_recall_v1_analysis_20261003.json). The overall goal remains active.

## Integration boundary audit

A post-capture inspection of all nine live value-recall graphs found zero `EpisodicEvent`, `MemoryContext`, `MemorySchema`, `MemoryRetrievalRequest`, `RecallCandidate`, or `RecallOutcome` nodes. The one/two `Episode` nodes per graph are legacy message episodes, not `episodic_working_memory_v1` records. The tested conversational route never activated the structured memory path; this narrows the failure from “memory retrieval failed” to “the measured graph-native memory interface was not invoked by this route.” The next gate is to specify and measure an explicit language-to-event/request bridge while preserving the current no-bridge baseline.

The cross-capture audit also finds the natural-language parse itself is asymmetric: `My secret code is 14.` has parsed object `14.`, while `My secret code is 29.` has `parsed_object=null`; both have missing relation support and unresolved grounding. `What is my secret code?` is classified as `table:know`, but no `MemoryRetrievalRequest` is created. Therefore the bridge gap begins before episodic binding: value extraction is inconsistent and no query-to-retrieval handoff exists in this route. See the [reproducible audit](../../../evaluation/reports/live_structured_memory_boundary_v1_audit_20261003.json) and [audit script](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/audit_live_structured_memory_boundary_v1.py).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 GenInfer acquisition and scaling

The relation-transfer v2 capture passes its strict three-map gate: the native
full-seed path acquires and reuses three distinct integer 2-D generators on
held-out signatures in 9/9 enabled cases, with disabled and identity controls
refusing minting and corrupted exact matches 0/9. A follow-up 84-worker sweep
crosses N=4–64 relations and B=3–6. All 60 positive cases acquire and transfer;
disabled and predicate-corrupt training controls acquire no target generator
(0/12 each). Coordinate-corrupted heldouts are recognized as a generator plus
an explicit local shift in 60/60 cases, while exact matrix-only matches remain
0/60. Training closure scales approximately with `N×(2B+1)^4`, reaching
1.78 seconds at N=64/B=6; held-out reuse at that cell takes 4.67 ms. This is
meaningful evidence for bounded substrate-side induction and reuse, plus a
single-frame cost profile. It does not establish autonomous goal selection,
operator-family invention, learner self-improvement, natural workload
tractability, or multi-frame scaling. The full-seed closure cost excludes
process and core boot; peak RSS is only a coarse delta from the booted baseline.
Next measure interactions across multiple eligible frames/codebook parts,
broader null families and memory, then test transfer outside geometric
2-D relations. See [transfer results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_RELATION_TRANSFER_V2_RESULTS_20261003.md),
[scaling results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_FULLSEED_SCALING_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md),
and [scaling analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_geninfer_fullseed_scaling_v1_analysis_20261003.json).
The overall programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 shared versus frame-isolated GenInfer

A paired follow-up compares the 15-group shared-codebook capture with 93
fresh-process single-part closures using the same matrices, frame inputs, and
native binary. Every isolated output is exact and matches the corresponding
shared output. At K=16, the shared closure takes 1.053 s versus 164 ms summed
isolated closure (6.42×); the direct candidate diagnostic reports 614,400
checks in shared scope versus 38,400 summed across isolated frames. This
supports cheap-cut/frame locality as a potential cost reduction and gives a
direct implementation gap: current GenInfer scans every eligible
`ShapeSignature` and ignores the supplied `frame_id`. Separate graphs supplied
the isolation; timing excludes boot, boundary discovery, scheduling, and data
movement. There is no production frame scheduler or end-to-end speed claim.
The next gate should implement and measure explicit codebook scope, including
the cost and error rate of choosing a scope and behavior when a useful
boundary is unavailable. See [comparison results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_FRAME_ISOLATION_COMPARISON_V1_1_RESULTS_20261003.md),
[protocol](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_FRAME_ISOLATION_COMPARISON_V1_1_PREREG_20261003.md),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_frame_isolation_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).
The overall programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 explicit GenInfer scope v1.3

A corrected source-locked production-rule run passes all 69 cases and five
gates. The exact output rate is 279/279 positive observations; required-scope
mismatch/missing controls produce zero target acquisitions in 24/24 cases. At
K=16, scoped closure checks 38,400 candidate matrices and takes a median
258.24 ms, compared with 614,400 checks and 1,056.98–1,068.07 ms globally
(about 4.1× lower closure time). This validates a bounded synthetic frame-local
operating regime for the implemented GenInfer path. The captured v1.2 failure
was caused by a missing production `observed` edge in the harness, not a
backward-compatibility failure; v1.2 artifacts remain intact. Scope discovery,
misassignment robustness, scheduling, realistic task prevalence, and full
end-to-end costs remain open. See [v1.3 results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_EXPLICIT_SCOPE_V1_3_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_EXPLICIT_SCOPE_V1_3_PREREG_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_geninfer_explicit_scope_v1_3_analysis_20261003.json). The overall programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 GenInfer scope provenance

The 36-worker locked probe varies only the provenance record while retaining
matching part and observation scope tokens. Exact target acquisition occurs in
12/12 valid-record, 12/12 missing-record, and 12/12 contradictory-record cases.
The native path filters on `scope_id` but does not validate `FrameScope`
provenance; required mode abstains on a missing token, not an unverified token.
This qualifies the v1.3 result: the 4.1× K=16 closure reduction is measured
with evaluator-verified synthetic boundaries, while boundary provenance and
correct assignment are not substrate-enforced. See [results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_SCOPE_PROVENANCE_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [amendment](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_EXPLICIT_SCOPE_V1_3_1_AMENDMENT_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_geninfer_scope_provenance_v1_analysis_20261003.json). The overall programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 GenInfer cross-frame dependencies

The 36-case paired comparison finds exact global acquisition in every case.
Scoped inference remains exact when its basis is local or explicitly included
as a cross-frame dependency (24/24); it misses every valid explanation whose
basis lies outside the supplied scope (0/12). At K=16, included-boundary
closure is roughly 4.5× faster than global search. The much faster excluded
condition (13.3×) is a false economy: it omits the explanation. The benchmark
proves the cost/coverage trade-off for this synthetic family and confirms that
scope discovery/dependency assignment remains upstream and unmeasured. See
[results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_CROSS_FRAME_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_CROSS_FRAME_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_geninfer_cross_frame_v1_analysis_20261003.json). The broader programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 GenInfer scope discovery

Source review finds that native GenInfer can consume a caller-provided scope
token, but no producer derives it from graph structure and no native path checks
`FrameScope` provenance. Existing locality gains use evaluator-authored
partitions. The earlier structural census computes graph properties over
projected captures but does not produce operational scopes or prove semantic
parity. Therefore cheap scoped application is measured; safe scope discovery
and cross-frame dependency preservation remain open capabilities. Next gate a
candidate discovery method on held-out oracle partitions, high-coupling
controls, dependency coverage, and scoped-first global retry cost before
scaling. See [source audit](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_FRAME_SELECTION_SOURCE_AUDIT_20261003.md), [cross-frame results](SUBSTRATE_GENINFER_CROSS_FRAME_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), and [structural census](SUBSTRATE_TASK_GRAPH_STRUCTURAL_CENSUS_V1_3_RESULTS_20261003.md). The broader programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 articulation frame-discovery baseline

A preregistered, label-blind articulation-cut baseline is independently
recomputed on all 78 snapshots from three task-graph captures. It recovers pure
Bigville components but splits same-owner nodes (median pairwise recall .508),
while leaving conversation and discourse owner groups joined (pairwise
precision .257/.379, cross-owner cut recall zero). This shows topology-only
articulation removal is not a general frame-discovery method for the selected
workloads. Because inputs discard edge semantics and owners are runtime
annotations, the next gate is typed factor-scope analysis and held-out semantic
parity, not a broad natural-task conclusion. See [results](SUBSTRATE_ARTICULATION_FRAME_DISCOVERY_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_ARTICULATION_FRAME_DISCOVERY_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_articulation_frame_discovery_v1_analysis_20261003.json). The broader programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 typed frame interactions

A typed-edge census of 78 locked snapshots passes independent per-edge
reconstruction. Cross-frame relations are concentrated in `follows_event`
for conversation/discourse and `causal_influence` for Bigville; shared boundary
nodes touch 966 and 177 conversation/discourse edge occurrences. This provides
specific dependencies for the next factor-scope audit and explains why simple
articulation removal can miss a multi-frame interface. Significant edge
annotation gaps remain (at least one endpoint unassigned on 35–70% of edges),
and graph relations do not capture full rule match/effect scopes. Next measure
actual rule-level dependencies and exact closure parity under partitioning.
See [results](SUBSTRATE_TYPED_FRAME_INTERACTION_CENSUS_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_TYPED_FRAME_INTERACTION_CENSUS_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_typed_frame_interaction_census_v1_analysis_20261003.json). The broader programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 rule predecessor dependencies

The typed/trace crosswalk confirms that all 90 cross-frame `follows_event`
links in the selected access-trace panel went unread by its four observed
discourse lifecycle rules; that harness loaded only the lifecycle seed. Source
inspection finds three other multi-edge communication rules that explicitly
read the event predecessor for action-target carry, correction grounding, and
reported-content recall. Their runtime partition behavior remains unmeasured.
This narrows the earlier 6,240-access finding to its actual loaded rule set and
makes predecessor preservation a concrete next gate. See [crosswalk results](SUBSTRATE_RULE_EDGE_SCOPE_CROSSWALK_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md) and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/substrate_rule_edge_scope_crosswalk_v1_analysis_20261003.json). The overall programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 predecessor-interface thought experiment

A source-locked native-rule experiment ran 10 repetitions of six conditions
(60 cases). The carry rule fired in all 10/10 retained-interface cases and
withheld carry in all 10/10 wrong-speaker, topic-interruption,
missing-predecessor-edge, and omitted-target-interface controls. It also
propagated through a chain of two eligible topicless events in 10/10 cases.
The added edge cost was one firing for a single carry and two for the chain;
tiny-graph medians were about 82 μs and 102 μs respectively and are not scale
estimates. This is evidence of a correctly gated local rule and chain behavior,
not evidence of general partition parity, copy-on-write speed, or autonomous
self-improvement. The runner explicitly loads the checked-in manifest because
`communication_intent_arbitration` is absent from the Python seed registry;
that gap has since been repaired and checked through `load_seeds_into`, which
installs the dependency closure and exactly one carry rule. A follow-on
scoped-closure capture compares 10 monolithic and 10 scoped retained-interface
cases with exact carry parity (10/10), plus 10/10 rejection for wrong speaker,
topic interruption, and omitted target interface. The predecessor was outside
the Microtheory-owned node set but remained globally readable, so this measures
scoped firing semantics, not physical isolation or Cow. Median closure was
109 μs monolithic and 50 μs scoped on these tiny graphs; no scaling claim is
supported. See [registry change](../../../domains/seed_registry.py), [scoped runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_scoped_closure_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_scoped_closure_v1_lock_20261003.json), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_scoped_closure_v1_20261003.json), alongside the original [rule runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_interface_v1.py) and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_interface_v1_20261003.json). The programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 correction-grounding scoped closure

A second seeded predecessor rule, `ground_definite_correction_from_scoped_action_target`, was exercised in a locked 70-case panel. The current event and interpretation belonged to one Microtheory while the immediately preceding event and target remained outside ownership but globally readable. Monolithic and scoped closure both grounded the valid correction in 10/10 cases, with the exact expected evidence path. Five scoped controls (wrong speaker, wrong referential head, missing predecessor edge, a topic-bearing intervening event, and omitted target) withheld grounding in 10/10 each. Median tiny-graph closure was 171 μs monolithic and 71 μs scoped; this is not a scaling result or full graph parity. An initial verifier assertion inspected the wrong predecessor in the interruption case; that capture was discarded and the corrected source-locked rerun passes all eight integrity/semantic checks. See [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_correction_grounding_scoped_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_correction_grounding_scoped_v1_lock_20261003.json), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_correction_grounding_scoped_v1_20261003.json). The programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 materialized correction-trace partition replay

A source-locked replay uses `domains.discourse_state` to materialize linked
instruction/correction pairs, assigns each prior instruction and correction
to separate Microtheories, and compares monolithic closure with scoped sweeps
in three orders. Across 36 paired cases (1/2/4/8 pairs, three orders, three
fresh processes per order), independently analyzed canonical node/edge facts
match in every cell, and exact grounding statuses/evidence paths match. At
eight pairs (533-node median graph, 16 frames), scoped closure takes 28.2 ms
versus 1.69 ms monolithic (16.6×), and uses 14.5× Candidate enumeration,
13.4× Term evaluations, and 40.1× rule enumerations. The effect count itself
matches; repeated whole-graph matching dominates. Raw serialization-order
parity falls to 1/3 at four and eight pairs, so consumers relying on ordered
graph serialization still need a deterministic ordering contract. This is a
production-materialized authored trace, not a natural task sample or scaling
law. Existing CoW residual experiments are separate; this run does not test
CoW integration. See [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1.py), [independent analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1.py), [work analysis](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_work_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_analysis_20261003.json). The programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 declared-anchor pruning on correction traces

An opt-in declared anchor (`CommunicationInterpretation i` owned by
`DiscourseEvent current_event` through `has_interpretation`) was compared with
same-source generic fallback and monolithic closure across the same 36-cell,
1/2/4/8-pair trace. Independent analysis confirms canonical graph parity and
exact grounded evidence in all arms, and verifies the declared-anchor path ran
while the fallback arm used no anchor path. The proof guard rejects most
scope/rule evaluations: at eight pairs the target rule records 3 declared
anchor-path evaluations and 53 fail-closed fallbacks. Total Candidate and Term
work falls only 0.5% and 0.7%; the anchored arm remains about 14× monolithic
work, and rule-enumeration counts do not change. This is safe but ineffective
pruning on a chained discourse graph, where each event can be a predecessor
for another frame's correction. See [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_anchor_v1.py), [independent analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_anchor_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_anchor_v1_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_anchor_v1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_anchor_v1_analysis_20261003.json).

Audit amendment: the preceding trace capture writes `owned_nodes_per_frame: 4`,
but source inspection shows two semantic nodes are attached to each frame root,
with the root itself making three nodes in the engine's owned set. That scalar
label and its dependent analyzer check are invalid; semantic outcome, graph
fact, and work-count comparisons are unaffected. Treat the runner source as
the ownership definition and exclude that scalar check from evidence. A
corrected ownership-count capture remains a follow-up.

## Audit amendment — corrected correction-trace ownership capture v1.1 (2026-10-03)

The original v1 materialized trace runner's `owned_nodes_per_frame: 4` field
was incorrect: it assigns two semantic nodes to each frame, plus the frame's
Microtheory root in the engine-owned set. V1.1 preserves the original as a
diagnostic and captures each root's actual `has_concept` members through the
native neighbor API. Across the same 36 paired cells, independent validation
now confirms two unique typed members per frame, three owned nodes including
the root, exact correction evidence, and canonical full-graph parity. Candidate,
Term, and rule-enumeration counts match v1.0. Timing is noisy between captures;
the corrected N=8 median is 25.25 ms scoped versus 2.48 ms monolithic (10.2×),
so use v1.1 timing as the current estimate. See [v1.1 runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_1.py), [work analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_work_v1_1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_1_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_correction_trace_partition_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 anchor topology comparison

The 72-cell chained-versus-independent-state comparison passes runner and
independent analysis for source lock, state counts, semantics, and canonical
graph parity. Independent states increase declared-anchor use at eight pairs
from 3 to 24 target-rule evaluations, but reduce Candidate and Term work only
5.1% and 5.4%; rule-enumeration work is unchanged, and median wall time shows
no end-to-end improvement. This narrows the optimization gap: eligibility
depends on topology, while repeated whole-graph matching remains the main
cost. Next gate: sound multi-role dependency indexing on predecessor-bearing
lifecycle cases, then combine it with CoW branch execution and residual
return. Full result and artifacts are documented in
[the topology comparison](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CORRECTION_TRACE_ANCHOR_TOPOLOGY_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 predecessor/CoW integration

Predecessor-dependent correction closure has now been executed inside both
full-copy and shared-graph-pages experiment branches, under monolithic and
scoped modes, across six positive/control conditions. All five arms preserve
canonical graph and evidence parity. The two positive conditions return one
derived interpretation-node change; negative controls return empty graph
deltas. The primary and rule store stay unchanged until revision-checked
return. Shared-page fork medians are about 0.20 ms versus 0.49–0.53 ms for
full-copy, while branch totals range from 0.90 to 1.32 ms with no stable
end-to-end winner on this tiny fixture. This closes
one integration gate while leaving multi-frame fanout, natural boundary
discovery, broader tasks, and scalable useful-improvement rate open. See [the
integration report](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_PREDECESSOR_COW_RESIDUAL_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 predecessor candidate fanout and allocator release

The v1.1 source-locked fanout panel passes 216/216 cells and the independent
analyzer passes 14/14 checks. Across 1/4/8 frames, chained/independent layouts,
1/4/16 supplied candidates, and 0/10,000 unrelated nodes, exactly one candidate
grounds with exact evidence; decoys abstain; scoped closure reaches quiescence;
selected branch and revision-checked return match the monolithic canonical
oracle; and stale residual retries are rejected atomically. This demonstrates
multi-frame outcome-guided selection over a supplied candidate set, not
candidate generation or self-improvement.

The corrected allocator samples show retained `mallinfo2` in-use memory growing
with fanout, then returning to baseline after all branch references are dropped.
At eight frames and 10,000 noise nodes with 16 candidates, median retained
in-use growth is 37.1 MiB for full-copy and 10.1 MiB for shared graph pages;
post-drop growth is approximately zero. PSS remains elevated by 39.5/12.2 MiB,
so resident pages must not be confused with live branch allocations. Lifecycle
medians are 311/269 ms for full-copy/shared pages; they include 16 closures and
return and are not comparable to the roughly 0.5-ms single-closure oracle.
Evidence is limited to one host, three replicates, and graphs up to 10,539
nodes. See [v1.1 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_PREDECESSOR_FANOUT_V1_1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_fanout_v1_1_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_fanout_v1_1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_fanout_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json). The next gate is outcome-blind evaluation on independently authored task families, then substrate-generated candidate acquisition and held-out benefit. The programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 current-source four-family proposal selection

The older four-family blinded utility capture was no longer source-current:
live hashes differed for domain edit/composition code, seven native Rust files,
the shape seed, and the loaded native binary. The current engine build itself
passes freshness checks, so the panel was repeated under a new lock rather than
relying on the historical capture. The v1.3 rerun freezes orders before held-
out scoring; all 104 candidate held-outs are scored exactly once and all 144
policy-prefix plus 104 audit branches preserve primary state and receipts. The
independent analyzer passes 7/7 checks, with all 49 source/build inputs stable
and current. Candidate set sizes and labels match the historical four-family
panel. Experience ranking reproduces 8/16 first-choice successes and 1.5
median attempts, beating frozen ranking (0/16; 2 attempts) but trailing default
order and target memoization (both 16/16, one attempt). Its 3.76-second median
selection cost includes cross-family outcome acquisition; default order costs
49 ms. This is evidence of bounded outcome use and cross-family record
availability, not broad transfer or self-improvement. Candidate generation is
still the fixed six-to-eight-candidate `_default_candidates` edit vocabulary.
Next: expand proposal coverage to a missing multi-edge identity-aware edit,
freeze generated pools before held-out scoring, and measure coverage, false
proposals, search cost, and utility against the current generator. See [v1.3
results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_BLINDED_PROPOSAL_UTILITY_V1_3_RESULTS_20261003.md), [amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_BLINDED_PROPOSAL_UTILITY_V1_3_SOURCE_REFRESH_20261003.md), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_blinded_proposal_utility_v1_3_lock_20261003.json), [orders](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_blinded_proposal_utility_v1_3_orders_20261003.json), [scores](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_blinded_proposal_utility_v1_3_scores_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_blinded_proposal_utility_v1_3_analysis_20261003.json). The programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 full edge-identity compose scaling

The current-source crossed microbenchmark varies eligible identity fields
(2/4/8/16/32) and unrelated graph nodes (0/1,000/10,000) through the full
composer. All 45 trials across 15 cells pass the exact actor+verb selection
oracle and primary-rule immutability check; the source materializer creates
exactly the enumerated sites. Candidate sites rise from 4 to 530. Median
compose latency remains in a broad 15–25 ms band; at 32 fields/10,000 nodes it
is 17.77 ms. This establishes a bounded synthetic cost envelope for supplied
rules and cues, including graph-site materialization and native selection. It
does not establish residual-to-cue grounding, autonomous candidate
acquisition, natural-task frame locality, or memory-capped scaling. v1 and
v1.1 captures are preserved as diagnostics after their analyzers caught,
respectively, private-copy counting and an unaccounted source-rule identity
field; only v1.2 is valid. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 numeric residual cue without action codebook

A new 13-case source-locked replay tests numeric outcome components through a
branch-only native relation rule, with no action-pair facts or action labels
on the candidate residual nodes or concern-induction input. Four opposite
vector cases derive the held cue and all four held-out repairs pass. Eight
ordinary controls abstain; the target-absent control derives the cue but does
not propose. Corrected independent analysis passes with zero false-control
cues/proposals and zero primary isolation/return failures. Median two-branch
fork time is 0.247 ms; native concern induction is 6.23 ms; composer grounding
is 0.377 ms. The evaluator still encodes task actions as numeric vectors, and
the opposition formula and cue vocabulary are authored. This is evidence for
numeric residual-to-rule transfer, not relation learning or autonomous
self-improvement. The relation-learning gate has now been attempted through
the native Term synthesizer. Five source-locked workers fit all 16 training
examples, but only one of five hypotheses is exact on 1,296 disjoint
fractional cases. Four workers prefer an x-only rule, each producing 180
false positives; the exact conjunction requires 9,862 explored terms and
1.19 s, while underfit hypotheses cost 5–68 ms. The independent reanalysis
passes measurement integrity and marks the generalization gate failed. This
shows the fixed-grammar learner can acquire the relation when examples
identify it, but the random training panels often omit a decisive counterexample
and synthesis cost rises sharply when the complete rule is required. Next
measure coverage-directed/active counterexamples, then integrate a frozen
synthesized Term into residual cue grounding and evaluate held-out task
families. See [results](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_RELATION_LEARNING_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [preregistration](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_RELATION_LEARNING_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/numeric_opposition_relation_learning_v1_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/numeric_opposition_relation_learning_v1_20261003.json), and [corrected analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/numeric_opposition_relation_learning_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json). The programme remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 learned-Term integration

The frozen coverage-directed relation Terms now run in the native residual
rule and structural composer. Across 65 locked cells (five learned Terms × 13
task cases), independent analysis passes exact AST binding, source stability,
and branch lifecycle checks. Cue recall is 20/20 for ordinary positives,
false cues are 0/40 ordinary negatives, all 20 held-out repair scores pass,
and all five target-absent controls withhold proposals. All 65 branches preserve
primary graph/rules/revision and return only the compact receipt. Median fork,
native induction, composer grounding, and held-out scoring are 0.258/6.245/
0.241/71.611 ms; the prior term acquisition median is 1.137 s (87.5 ms
amortized over 13 uses). This establishes an integrated supervised path for
one authored task family. The numeric labels, action-to-vector encoding,
cue-vocabulary mapping, and cue-to-edit connection remain supplied. Next
measure an active counterexample/query loop with a frozen probe pool and
separate held-out task families. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 counterexample coverage in relation learning

The follow-up fixes the specific training-coverage failure without changing
the learner, grammar, term-size cap, budget, or held-out grid. Five
source-locked workers receive 8 positive and 8 negative examples, including
x-only and y-only separating controls. All five synthesize the same size-9
two-axis conjunction and score 36/36 held-out positives plus 1,260/1,260
held-out negatives. Median search is 1.137 s and 8,908 explored terms. Against
the prior random cohort's 1/5 exact hypotheses and 5.5 ms median (dominated by
underfit rules), this supports the value of informative examples, with a
substantial search-cost tradeoff. The comparison is small and nonpaired and
does not establish a general active-learning policy. Next integrate a frozen
synthesized Term into isolated native residual cue grounding, retaining the
cue-to-operator association as an explicit authored input. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 active query over synthesized hypotheses

Five source-locked workers synthesize 9–10 candidate numeric relations from four sparse examples, with the exact relation present in each bounded pool. Maximum-entropy, fixed-first, and seeded-random selection all leave a unique survivor in every worker; every survivor is exact on the 1,296-case fractional grid and the 13-case navigation proposal/abstention panel. Median oracle-call counts are 2, 1, and 4 respectively, so maximum entropy does not beat the fixed-first control on this task. Median selector time is 3.13 ms for maximum-entropy versus 0.066 ms fixed-first and 0.14 ms random; version-space synthesis takes 5.83 s median. Integrity analysis passes, including current source/build hashes, independent regeneration of all five version spaces, exact typed prediction partitions, stopping behavior, and held-out replay. This is a bounded negative result for query efficiency, not a test of external oracle cost or open-ended learning. Next increase hypothesis ambiguity, include out-of-grammar/adversarial aliases, and replicate on unrelated rule/task families. The general capability and tractability programme remains active; natural-task locality, closure bounds, and broader learning capability remain unestablished. See [results](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [amendment](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_AMENDMENT_20261003.md), [protocol](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_PREREG_20261003.md), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/numeric_opposition_active_query_v1_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/numeric_opposition_active_query_v1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/numeric_opposition_active_query_v1_analysis_20261003.json).


## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 order-balanced query selection

The v1 follow-up addresses candidate-order bias in the preceding active-query result. Five source-locked synthesized pools (9–10 hypotheses each) were replayed under 30 paired randomized candidate orders per worker. Maximum entropy uniquely identified the exact survivor in 150/150 paired blocks; sequential randomized-order querying did so in 111/150. Across paired blocks, entropy used fewer labels in 102, tied in 32, and used more in 16; the median across five worker-level median differences was −2 labels. All 150 entropy-selected survivors pass the fractional-grid and navigation holdouts. Its median selection time is 3.74 ms compared with 0.40 ms sequential, and the locally simulated oracle means these call counts do not establish real-oracle economics. This is a bounded improvement over order-balanced sampling for one generated relation family, not evidence of general active learning or autonomous self-improvement. Next test larger/aliased hypothesis spaces, model misspecification, and unrelated task families. The full substrate tractability programme remains active; natural-task frame locality, general closure guarantees, and broad capability transfer are still open. See [results](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_1_RESULTS_20261003.md), [preregistration](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_ACTIVE_QUERY_V1_1_PREREG_20261003.md), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/numeric_opposition_active_query_v1_1_lock_20261003.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/numeric_opposition_active_query_v1_1_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/numeric_opposition_active_query_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).
## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 exploratory predecessor fanout at 100k nodes

An unpreregistered six-process extension of the predecessor-dependent thoughtexperiment fanout path reaches 100,511 nodes and 467 edges. Across three replicates for each backend, all candidate outcomes and residual returns match the monolithic oracle; primary isolation and stale-revision rejection hold. Shared graph pages reduce median four-branch fork time from 53.49 ms to 0.43 ms and retained allocator growth from 75.79 MiB to 4.61 MiB. Complete lifecycle medians are 2.52 s and 2.37 s, only about 1.06x apart, because whole-graph digesting and audits dominate. This is exploratory because it was not preregistered or locked before capture; it does not establish a scaling law. The next confirmatory gate is a prelocked 100k/1m topology/fanout sweep with audit costs separated from branch execution. See [exploratory result](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_PREDECESSOR_FANOUT_100K_EXPLORATORY_20261003.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_fanout_100k_exploratory_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_fanout_100k_exploratory_analysis_20261003.json).

The v1.1 source-locked gate extends shared edit-operator candidate generation to the previously omitted node-attribute identity sites. Across four fresh workers, all eight candidates are regenerated and exhaustively scored; the native graph-Term selector chooses the actor+verb guard from training outcomes in 4/4 workers, and all four selected held-out episodes pass. All seven broader or narrower generated guards fail held-out scoring; the unmodified source rule passes as a positive control. Median site generation is 0.78 ms, training scoring 22.19 ms, selection 0.65 ms, and scoring the full held-out pool 77.17 ms. V1 had a cross-product fixture defect and is explicitly excluded. V1.1 evaluates candidates in fresh task contexts rather than CoW branches, and still uses harness-supplied target, fault, examples, and oracle. The next integration gate is branch-local execution, evidence selection, residual return, and primary isolation across actor-identity, recurrence, and conversation families. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 node-identity candidates in CoW branches

The corrected v1.1 integration passes all 11 independent checks. Four workers evaluate all eight generated actor-identity guards in both full-copy and shared-page branches. Backend outcomes match; only actor+verb passes training, is selected by the native graph-Term mechanism, and passes held-outs. The selected training proposal returns a journaled `ThoughtExperimentProposal` receipt through revision-checked graph-delta adoption; stale retry is rejected, primary rules remain unchanged, and receipt content matches the branch. Median eight-candidate training lifecycle is 3.18 ms full-copy versus 3.08 ms shared pages; held-out lifecycle is 3.20 versus 2.98 ms; receipt adoption is 0.139 ms. This demonstrates integrated branch isolation and proposal-evidence return on one small authored family, not production rule installation or self-improvement. V1 is a diagnostic due to an analyzer field-name defect; v1.1 is the source-locked rerun. Next pair independently authored recurrence and conversation tasks, then measure a policy-approved rule adoption path with atomic graph/rule update semantics. ## 2026-10-03 update: cross-family thoughtexperiment branch evidence

The locked cross-family edit-op CoW experiment now passes across the recurrence
and conversation task fixtures (four workers each). The substrate selected the
unique training repair in all eight worker-family runs; both repairs passed
held-out cases under full-copy and shared-page branches. Proposal receipts
returned through the revision-checked graph-delta path while active primary
rules stayed unchanged, and stale retries were rejected. The independent
analyzer passed 13/13 checks. Measured candidate lifecycle medians were
19.37/3.68 ms (training) and 3.59/3.07 ms (held-out) for full-copy/shared pages;
these small workload timings are preliminary, not a scaling or speedup claim.

This advances the demonstrated substrate capability from one actor/verb task
to three authored task families with isolated candidate evaluation, evidence
return, and no primary rule activation. It supports the experimental harness
and substrate performing bounded parts of structural improvement; the LLM
still specifies the task, fault, cue, candidate family, and evaluation target.
Autonomous diagnosis, useful novel structure discovery, retained rule adoption,
and capability growth over time remain open. The next gate should add a
revision-controlled adoption experiment, followed by blinded task/fault
variation and increasing candidate and graph scale. See
`THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EDITOPS_CROSS_FAMILY_COW_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md`.

## 2026-10-03 update: revision-checked candidate-rule activation

The rule store now exposes an atomic revision-checked successor replacement
operation. It checks the current semantic graph/rule revision, parses the
candidate, rejects active-name collisions, and replaces the target while
holding one native lock. The source-locked follow-up activates the selected
recurrence and conversation repairs in eight fresh contexts; all eight recover
the expected behavior. Stale revisions and malformed candidates are refused
without changing the rule store in all cases. The independent analyzer passes
6/6 gates. Median activation is 0.193 ms and post-activation task execution is
0.629 ms.

This completes the mechanical transition from a validated branch proposal to
a newly active rule in a disposable substrate. Candidate quality, fault
diagnosis, and authorization remain harness controlled; live long-running
adoption, regression rollback, concurrent policy, and retained utility remain
open. Next measure rule activation across persistent multi-task sequences,
including regression detection and rollback, then test blinded task/fault
variation without a supplied candidate label. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RULE_ACTIVATION_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md),
[protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_RULE_ACTIVATION_V1_PREREG_20261003.md),
[runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_rule_activation_v1.py),
[lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_rule_activation_v1_lock_20261003.json),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_rule_activation_v1_analysis_20261003.json).

## 2026-10-03 update: persistent adoption and regression rollback

The source-locked follow-up runs selected recurrence and conversation repairs
across two sequential batches in the same substrate, with four workers per
task family. All 16 batch outcomes pass. A separate control deliberately
activates the known-bad recurrence agent-wide guard: all four workers detect
its output failure, restore the captured source rule using the current semantic
revision, and recover the expected behavior. Independent analysis passes 5/5
gates. Median selected activation is 0.189 ms and controlled rollback is
0.164 ms.

This proves persistence and rollback mechanics on two authored workloads. The
harness chooses candidates and treats the measured oracle failure as a rollback
trigger; no autonomous diagnosis, threshold calibration, broad task transfer,
or long-run net utility is established. Next add blinded task/fault variation
and an explicit policy that balances held-out benefit, regression risk, and
adoption cost over longer sequences. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_PERSISTENT_ADOPTION_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md),
[protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_PERSISTENT_ADOPTION_V1_PREREG_20261003.md),
[runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_persistent_adoption_v1.py),
[lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_persistent_adoption_v1_lock_20261003.json),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_persistent_adoption_v1_analysis_20261003.json).

## 2026-10-03 update: active-query model misspecification

A source-locked five-panel experiment sets the target to additive conservation,
outside the learner's Neg/Eq/And grammar. Synthesis returns 31 candidate Terms
in four panels and no candidates in one; none of the initial or final
hypotheses is exact on a disjoint 1,296-case fractional grid. The independent
analyzer passes 4/4 checks. Maximum-entropy selection cannot choose a query in
any nonempty panel because at least one Term evaluation fails at each candidate
input and the current selector discards that input. Sequential selection uses
9 simulated labels, then reports false singleton outcomes in 3/5 panels and an
empty version space in another. This is a bounded out-of-grammar result from
one authored relation, not a misspecification guarantee.

The result identifies a concrete selector gap: execution failure is currently
an input-level rejection rather than an explicit candidate prediction, which
can prevent all active querying. The next measurement should treat failed or
ill-typed candidate evaluation as a distinct outcome, repeat the same locked
panels, and check whether the selector then empties the version space or still
returns false confidence. Follow that with unrelated out-of-grammar targets.
See [results](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_MODEL_MISSPECIFICATION_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md),
[protocol](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_MODEL_MISSPECIFICATION_V1_PREREG_20261003.md),
[runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/numeric_opposition_model_misspecification_v1.py),
[lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/numeric_opposition_model_misspecification_v1_lock_20261003.json),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/numeric_opposition_model_misspecification_v1_analysis_20261003.json).

## 2026-10-03 correction and v1.1: out-of-grammar active queries

The earlier v1 query-policy conclusions are invalid: its runner passed row
lists to a selector that requires feature mappings, and the analyzer did not
check input shape. Keep v1 only as a synthesis/held-out diagnostic. V1.1 fixes
the binding and makes candidate evaluation failures an explicit prediction
outcome. The independent analysis passes 4/4 checks. On five additive-relation
panels outside the Neg/Eq/And grammar, the learner produces 31 hypotheses in
four panels and no fit in one; no initial/final hypothesis is exact on the
fractional holdout. Entropy uses one fewer simulated query than sequential
selection in one panel, with both policies ending in false singleton rules in
the same three panels and an empty version space in the other two. Explicit
failure buckets restore query availability, but do not detect that the grammar
is inadequate or prevent false confidence. This is one repeated relation and
not general active-learning evidence. Next test an independently justified
model-adequacy criterion or broader query-budget policy on unrelated
out-of-grammar relations. See [v1.1 results](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_MODEL_MISSPECIFICATION_V1_1_RESULTS_20261003.md),
[protocol](NUMERIC_OPPOSITION_MODEL_MISSPECIFICATION_V1_1_PREREG_20261003.md),
[runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/numeric_opposition_model_misspecification_v1_1.py),
[lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/numeric_opposition_model_misspecification_v1_1_lock_20261003.json),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/numeric_opposition_model_misspecification_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).

V1.1 also includes a controlled selector-level partial-Term case. It confirms
that an evaluator exception is emitted as an explicit error prediction and
that an oracle observation removes that candidate (5/5 analysis checks total).
The generated additive panels did not themselves raise native evaluation
exceptions at selected inputs; their core result is still false singleton
confidence and failure to detect that the bounded grammar misses the target.

## 2026-10-03 Model-adequacy audit across target families

The follow-up source-locked audit passes all six independent checks. It tests
five additive, five product, and five opposition panels. Challenge queries
reduce false singleton outcomes for additive conservation from 5/5 to 2/5 and
empty the product candidate set in 5/5 panels; they retain an exact opposition
model in 5/5 controls. The opposition control uses 16 coverage-directed
examples, while the out-of-grammar tasks use four random examples, so its
successful result confirms a candidate-available control but is not a matched
sample-size comparison. Synthesis median is 4.16 ms for opposition and
1.71–1.78 ms for the other tasks.

Current evidence supports bounded candidate search, useful query selection,
explicit detection of some grammar mismatch, and branch-based candidate
evaluation/adoption primitives. It does not show that the substrate can
diagnose all false singleton models, autonomously choose when to expand its
grammar, or improve itself across general tasks. The next learning gate should
measure policy choice among more queries, grammar widening, and external-help
requests, with held-out oracle separation and several distinct target families.
See [v1.1 results](NUMERIC_RELATION_MODEL_ADEQUACY_AUDIT_V1_1_RESULTS_20261003.md),
[protocol amendment](NUMERIC_RELATION_MODEL_ADEQUACY_AUDIT_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261003.md),
[source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/numeric_relation_model_adequacy_audit_v1_1_lock_20261003.json),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/numeric_relation_model_adequacy_audit_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json).

## 2026-10-03 Grammar widening and search-depth pilot

An exploratory worker-0 pilot adds the task-relevant primitive to the bounded
Term grammar. Plus/Eq and Times/Eq find one exact model each on the 1,296-point
fractional holdout in 5.03/4.18 seconds; adding
those primitives while retaining Neg/Eq/And at size 9 instead reaches the
12-second timeout with 17/28 partial candidates. The same expanded grammars at
size 7 find an exact model in 2.13/1.86 seconds with 2/11 candidates. The
source of the primitive hint is the harness, and this is one panel per family;
it demonstrates bounded recovery when the missing primitive is supplied, not
autonomous grammar expansion. The measured growth with grammar breadth and
term size is now a concrete target for the next all-panel search-cost audit.
See [pilot record](GRAMMAR_WIDENING_DEPTH_PILOT_20261003.md).

## 2026-10-03 Source-locked grammar widening depth result

A source-locked replay covers all five frozen additive and product panels.
With the task-relevant primitive supplied, size-7 expanded grammars find an
exact candidate on the 1,296-point holdout in all 10 panels without timeout;
median wall time is 1.82–2.21 seconds. Size-9 expanded grammars also find an
exact candidate in all 10, but every arm reaches its 12-second limit and
returns a larger partial space (134 additive and 65 product candidates across
five panels). The analyzer passes all four source, schedule, settings, and
candidate-score gates. This supports size-bounded recovery when a primitive is
provided, not autonomous grammar selection; both targets and operator hints
are harness-authored. Next measure operator-hint acquisition and a cost-aware
size/depth policy on more task families, with held-out labels and task identity
hidden. See [results](GRAMMAR_WIDENING_DEPTH_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md),
[preregistration](GRAMMAR_WIDENING_DEPTH_V1_PREREG_20261003.md),
[source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/grammar_widening_depth_v1_lock_20261003.json),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/grammar_widening_depth_v1_analysis_20261003.json).

## 2026-10-03 Supplied-menu primitive-family selection

A source-locked ten-panel follow-up supplies both Plus and Times grammar
extensions on each frozen additive/product task, then compares maximum-entropy
queries with fixed-order queries. Entropy selection ends at the correct exact
singleton in 10/10 panels (median two labels per task); fixed order does so in
6/10 and remains ambiguous in four, using median five or six labels. The
analyzer passes all six replay, score, setting, and binding checks. Median
synthesis costs are 2.10 s for Plus and 1.75 s for Times. This is bounded
operator-family selection from a two-item supplied menu using local oracle
labels, not open-ended primitive invention or autonomous help-seeking. Next
broaden operator families and task types, introduce irrelevant/harmful
extensions and a priced help-request option, and measure hidden-task family
selection with held-out separation. See
[results](PRIMITIVE_FAMILY_SELECTION_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md),
[protocol](PRIMITIVE_FAMILY_SELECTION_V1_PREREG_20261003.md),
[source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/primitive_family_selection_v1_lock_20261003.json),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/primitive_family_selection_v1_analysis_20261003.json).

## 2026-10-03 Multi-family learning and help-policy gate

The corrected v1.1 source-locked analysis passes 7/7 gates across 25 panels
covering arithmetic equality, ordering, opposition, and inequality. Entropy
queries resolve all additive/product panels (median two labels) and all
ordering panels (median three); fixed order resolves only 2/5 ordering panels
within six labels. The help-on-unresolved policy makes 5 Base and 5 Not teacher
calls for entropy arms. Post-capture 2,401-case zero-inclusive rescoring finds
that all opposition survivor sets are already exact on that finite grid, while
every inequality panel already has an exact candidate in the original menu
alongside at least one false candidate. Thus abstention/ambiguity alone
triggers unnecessary help on opposition, and the Not hint resolves rather
than expands inequality coverage. The menu, task set, labels, and helpful
teacher response remain harness-authored; no live LLM cost or general transfer
is measured. Next measure behavioral equivalence versus harmful ambiguity on
broader, zero-inclusive challenge domains and compare continued probes,
explicit equivalence-class return, and priced external help. See
[v1.1 results](PRIMITIVE_MENU_HELP_POLICY_V1_1_RESULTS_20261003.md),
[amendment](PRIMITIVE_MENU_HELP_POLICY_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261003.md),
[source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/primitive_menu_help_policy_v1_1_lock_20261003.json),
[primary analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/primitive_menu_help_policy_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json),
and [semantic addendum](../../../evaluation/reports/primitive_menu_help_policy_v1_1_semantic_addendum_20261003.json).

## 2026-10-03 Zero-aware querying and finite behavior classes

The source-locked 25-panel follow-up passes 7/7 analysis gates and exactly
replays the prior entropy transcript on its original input domain. Adding
zero-valued and boundary probes raises median inequality query cost from one
to four labels but removes selector abstention and leaves only exact survivors
in all five panels; the original pool left one false and one exact candidate
indistinguishable in every inequality panel. Opposition remains ambiguous at
the Term level, but its four survivors form one finite behavior class and are
all exact on the 6,560-case expanded holdout. Current help logic still makes
five unnecessary opposition hint calls in each domain. A finite equivalence
class appears useful for avoiding that spend, but the experiment does not yet
measure a downstream class consumer, and the inequality counterexample shows
that such a class can conceal error when its probe domain is weak. Next compare
zero-aware querying, coverage-qualified equivalence-class return, and priced
help on independently sampled task families. See
[results](PRIMITIVE_SEMANTIC_EQUIVALENCE_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md),
[protocol](PRIMITIVE_SEMANTIC_EQUIVALENCE_V1_PREREG_20261003.md),
[source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/primitive_semantic_equivalence_v1_lock_20261003.json),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/primitive_semantic_equivalence_v1_analysis_20261003.json).

## 2026-10-03 Finite behavioral-equivalence partition API

The query layer now exposes a typed finite-probe partition of hypotheses,
including a probe digest and a separate Term-evaluation-error bucket. The
source-locked H×P study passes 5/5 checks: all 50 captured survivor partitions
match the reference grouping, a bool/int/error control yields three distinct
classes, and measured evaluation calls equal H×P. Median partition time is
0.80–0.90 ms at H=1–4 on the actual 64-probe panels; synthetic H=64,P=64 takes
36.55 ms for 4,096 calls. This is a reusable finite behavior-class utility,
not a proof of semantic equivalence or a policy for safe return. Next measure a
policy that chooses between broadening probes, returning a coverage-qualified
class, and requesting help on the same locked task families, then expand to
independent families and holdouts. See
[results](BEHAVIOR_EQUIVALENCE_PARTITION_V1_RESULTS_20261003.md),
[protocol](BEHAVIOR_EQUIVALENCE_PARTITION_API_V1_PREREG_20261003.md),
[source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/behavior_equivalence_partition_v1_lock_20261003.json),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/behavior_equivalence_partition_v1_analysis_20261003.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-03 task-scope parity and closure work

The corrected conversation task-scope v1.3 capture and independent analysis pass. Across three fresh workers and four authored cases, 12/12 final graphs and 198/198 checkpoint pairs match monolithic closure, and scoped queues reach quiescence. The measured global resweep is increasingly costly: at 2/4/8 goals it performs 3.10×/5.83×/11.28× the candidate pairs and 3.33×/6.76×/13.99× the Term evaluations of monolithic closure. The current operation-work trace also exposes an instrumentation asymmetry: monolithic runs include scheduler event rows, while scoped calls expose operation markers only. Thus this is a parity and cost result for explicit annotations, not evidence that frame partitioning is already a tractable implementation. Next add scoped scheduler accounting, derive partitions from runtime activation/dependency evidence, and test coupled cross-frame changes on independent workloads with discovery costs included. See [v1.3 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_PARITY_PILOT_V1_3_RESULTS_20261003.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_3_20261003.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/conversation_task_scope_parity_pilot_v1_3_analysis_20261003.json). The overall goal remains active.

Provenance and independent analysis pass; all 12 paired cases and 198 checkpoint pairs preserve monolithic graph state. At 2/4/8 goals, the scoped runner records 144/528/2,016 passes and 540/2,040/7,920 rule attempts across three workers. Candidate and Term counts remain the comparable work measures and retain v1.3's result: global resweeps incur 3.10×/5.83×/11.28× candidate pairs and 3.33×/6.76×/13.99× Term evaluations. This improves observability without changing the finding that the present scoped sweep is more expensive on these fixtures. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 scoped binding funnel

The provenance-locked v1.5 replay passes with independent per-checkpoint conservation checks and preserves exact graph states across all 12 paired cases and 198 checkpoint pairs. Across three workers, the 2/4/8-goal cases return 27/90/324 successful matcher bindings; 9/54/252 fall outside the declared task scopes, while 18/36/72 reach application. No duplicate bindings are observed. The post-match rejection share rises from 33.3% at two goals to 77.8% at eight, but it does not measure pre-match candidates; candidate and Term counters still show that exhaustive scoped sweeps perform much more search. Later v2.1.1/v2.2 studies already exercise declared-anchor pruning: they reduce matcher work by about one-third versus verified fallback on controlled authored graphs, while still costing 2.67×/2.77× monolithic work in v2.2. Those later captures use an earlier locked engine. The next gate is transfer to independently harvested task graphs with reviewed ownership and coupled-mutation controls, under a new current-source lock and with discovery/memory costs charged. See [v1.5 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_PARITY_PILOT_V1_5_RESULTS_20261004.md), [v2.2 results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_DEGREE_SELECTIVITY_V2_2_RESULTS_20261003.md), and [v1.2 memory results](SUBSTRATE_CONVERSATION_TASK_SCOPE_SIZE_MEMORY_V1_2_RESULTS_20261003.md).
