## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 retained natural-graph ownership coverage

The retained live-conversation archive has ten usable full-seed final graphs from five repository-authored prompts, two workers per prompt. Graphs range from 25,036 to 25,216 nodes and 11,445 to 11,722 edges. Rechecking the independent v1.1 analyzer shows conservative prompt-linked Goal ownership in only two conditions (`scene_description` and `self_report_action`); `unknown_word_clarification`, `held_self_knowledge_answer`, and `concern_authoring` have no prompt-linked Goal under its current explicit-link criterion. Each graph therefore yields a single owned frame, and the remaining nodes are shared. This cannot support a multi-frame locality claim or test automatic ownership discovery. The archive remains useful for rehydration/API validation, but a transfer benchmark needs a larger task panel and blinded human-reviewed mappings with coverage and disagreement reported before current-source anchor replay. See [harvest analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_conversation_task_graph_harvest_v1_1_analysis_20261003.json) and [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_live_conversation_task_graph_harvest_v1_1.py). The overall goal remains active.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 typed task-anchor paths

A new retrospective typed-path audit finds a specific precision/recall tradeoff in recovering task structure. With high-degree intermediates excluded at degree 100, `scene_description` reaches five Goals: the intended `describe_scene` Goal through `about → Scene → describes`, three `produce_utterance` Goals, and `report_self`. The direct prompt `comprehends` link selects only `report_self`. `self_report_action` reaches only its directly linked `report_self` Goal; the other three conditions reach their prompt DiscourseEvent but no Goal. The unrestricted candidate path method is therefore too permissive for ownership, while the direct-edge rule misses a task-related Goal and most task cases. This is graph reachability evidence only; no ownership is inferred. The source script and JSON capture retain the archive digest and worker graph digests. Next compare mapping methods against independent reviewed annotations and score false ownership and abstention before any partition-cost claim. See [path analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_live_conversation_task_anchor_paths_v1.py) and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/live_conversation_task_anchor_paths_v1_20261004.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 task-anchor review packet

A blinded annotation packet has been generated from one retained worker graph per authored prompt. It suppresses condition and replicate fields, node labels are replaced with local aliases, and task-oracle outcomes are withheld. The packet includes each prompt, all Goal candidates, and typed path context for bounded reachable Goals and events; the second exact replicate is reserved for a separate reliability review. This prepares independent node-level annotation for measuring mapping coverage and false ownership, but provides no annotations and is not evidence that any mapping policy works. Its five-prompt authored scope and partial-graph context must remain explicit. See [builder](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/build_live_task_anchor_review_packet_v1.py) and [packet](../../../evaluation/reports/live_conversation_task_anchor_review_packet_v1_20261004.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 provisional anchor policy score

A provisional single-agent annotation separates request relevance from task alignment. On six non-uncertain Goal candidates, bounded typed-path reachability has 1.00 precision/recall for request-associated Goals against these labels, while direct prompt `comprehends` links have 1.00 precision and 0.33 recall. For task alignment, broad reachability scores 0.83 precision/1.00 recall and direct links 0.50 precision/0.20 recall; both include the misinterpreted `report_self` Goal as request-associated, while only alignment scoring treats it as wrong. This is an exploratory result on five authored prompts, with zero true-negative Goals and no annotation/policy independence. It does not validate an ownership method. The next gate remains independent multi-annotator labels plus held-back replicate reliability and broader tasks before current-source partition replay. See [annotation](../../../evaluation/reports/live_conversation_task_anchor_provisional_annotations_v1_20261004.json), [scorer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_live_task_anchor_provisional_annotations_v1.py), and [scores](../../../evaluation/reports/live_conversation_task_anchor_provisional_scores_v1_20261004.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 thoughtexperiment 100k current-source replay

The exploratory predecessor-fanout case now has a preregistered, current-source replay. Six fresh workers (four independent-state frames, four candidates, 100,000 noise nodes, three replicates per backend) pass all ten independent gates for candidate selection, branch/primary/oracle parity, isolation, rule preservation, stale-revision refusal, and quiescence. Median candidate-fork time is 75.0 ms for full-copy and 0.574 ms for shared graph pages; median end-to-end lifecycle is 3.13 s versus 3.21 s. The existing phase timers cover only 3.0% and 0.16% of those lifecycle medians. Remaining time is explicitly unattributed, so these data do not establish a full-lifecycle speedup or that hashing dominates. This confirms one supplied task and one graph scale on one host, not candidate generation or a scaling law. Next instrument graph export/digest/validation separately and then preregister a larger fanout/graph-size sweep. See [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_PREDECESSOR_FANOUT_CURRENT_SOURCE_REPLAY_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_PREDECESSOR_FANOUT_CURRENT_SOURCE_REPLAY_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_fanout_current_source_replay_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_fanout_current_source_replay_v1_lock_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_fanout_current_source_replay_v1_analysis_20261004.json). ## 2026-10-04 current release-gate readiness audit

The source-linked capability map still validates at 28 rows, 421 source links, 72 linked test references, and 549 result-linked runners (digest `ae8e3c273d74566ee06595395c890ad168ecede5d4554286af2dca8e86d9c4d3`). The P0-A package contains local protocol code and the archive contains a historical denial probe; the focused release-artifact inventory found no signed P2 phase-go, complete evaluator-signed P3 lock, or provisioned P0/P1 confinement evidence. The P3-R0 protocol itself therefore admits only local no-go/impasse. No evaluation worker was launched. This makes the current self-improvement status explicit: there is bounded substrate learning and a research thoughtexperiment loop, but no authorized or evidenced self-modifying adoption evaluation. The next governance gate is P0-A custody, mount/channel, and external-kill evidence, followed in order by P0-E1/E2 and P1; research-only engine and learning experiments remain useful but cannot substitute for those gates.

### P0-A runtime detail

Static/runtime inspection finds `bwrap 0.9.0` and a current shell with seccomp and no-new-privileges active. The P0 launcher still depends on evaluator-supplied `CgroupController`/`WorkerScope` protocols; no concrete controller exists in the package, and cgroup v2 is read-only to this user. The local content-addressed mount helper is explicitly an approximation and only sets staged files to mode `0400`. This is insufficient for the required external-kill and separate-custody claims. No evaluator worker or start/stop probe ran. See [P0-A readiness audit](P0A_LOCAL_RUNTIME_READINESS_AUDIT_20261004.md), which records source hashes and the exact gate boundary.

All 24 current-source workers passed the corrected edge-topology panel's 19 independent checks. ## 2026-10-04 touched-object parity audit

The current-source v1.1 panel passes 19 corrected checks across 18 workers and three graph sizes. The journal changes one or two nodes per candidate, and the complete state of every touched node is reconstructed and matched after residual adoption. This is 0.09–0.13 ms versus 128 ms/1.33 s/2.64 s for the exact whole-graph branch/primary parity check. Six deliberately untracked unrelated-node mutations show why the speedup cannot be promoted to exact parity: touched-object validation still passes while the global digest detects the difference. The local check is therefore useful as a conditional receipt consistency check, with an explicit completeness/isolation dependency; the exact global audit remains needed where arbitrary divergence must be excluded. Failed v1 instrumentation and the analyzer correction are retained. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_TOUCHED_OBJECT_PARITY_V1_1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [analysis amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_TOUCHED_OBJECT_PARITY_ANALYSIS_AMENDMENT_20261004.md), and [corrected analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_touched_object_parity_v1_1_1_analysis_20261004.json).

## 2026-10-04 current-source journal mutation matrix

A preregistered and source-locked Rust evaluator now covers 16 public `TypedGraph` mutation sequences. All 16 journal residuals equal their whole-graph diff; phase-specific output matches its phase interval oracle; and a deliberate post-capture write is detected by the global diff but absent from the earlier journal receipt. This directly validates the live-node restore before-image correction alongside node/edge add, change, delete, replacement, batch delete, repeated-write, and fresh/tombstoned restore cases. The prior touched-object speed result remains conditional: arbitrary later or untracked divergence still requires whole-graph parity. These finite cases do not establish concurrency safety or exhaustive mutation coverage. The overall tractability and capability goal remains active, with broader engine scaling, natural-task frame locality, learning capability, and governance-gated self-improvement unresolved. See [matrix results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_JOURNAL_MUTATION_MATRIX_V1_1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_JOURNAL_MUTATION_MATRIX_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), and [raw capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_1_20261004.json).

The subsequent source pass found `node_mut`, a public direct mutable-reference route omitted from the 16-case run. A separately locked v1.2 evaluator adds that case; all 17 rows pass journal/full-graph equality, including the checkpoint case, and the post-capture divergence control still demonstrates the local journal's limit. This is finite method coverage under single-threaded fixed sequences; retained mutable references and concurrency remain open. See [v1.2 results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_JOURNAL_MUTATION_MATRIX_V1_2_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_JOURNAL_MUTATION_MATRIX_V1_2_PREREG_20261004.md), and [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_journal_mutation_matrix_v1_2_20261004.json).

V1.3 strengthens that evidence by preserving the actual journal and oracle diffs in each raw row. ## 2026-10-04 live graph frame-locality probe

The retained production-route sample now has a source-locked prompt-neighborhood cut assay with 5 unique graphs, 12 radius/hub settings, and two control arms. The prompt-root cut ratio is usually lower than the few other Message nodes, but higher than the degree-ranked non-Message controls in 51/60 graph/settings comparisons. The same-type arm has only 2–8 candidates and degree gaps as large as 15–29; the broader arm is confounded by node type. Radius/cutoff sensitivity is substantial, and the sample is only five authored prompts. This is no positive general cheap-cut result; it motivates broader task sampling and independently reviewed task ownership before frame-local engine runs. See [results](LIVE_GRAPH_PROMPT_NEIGHBORHOOD_CUTS_V2_RESULTS_20261004.md), [preregistration](LIVE_GRAPH_PROMPT_NEIGHBORHOOD_CUTS_V2_PREREG_20261004.md), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/live_graph_prompt_neighborhood_cuts_v2_analysis_20261004.json).

The capability inventory now includes this structural assay and validates at 28 rows, 421 source links, 72 test references, and 549 result-linked runners (JSON SHA-256 `45e5a2a90db41f9aaa6445a191ab0c87f0399b9fcb807ba381c7007eaf984f98`).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 coverage-aware class return

The preregistered coverage-aware policy now has a 25-panel frozen capture and an independent replay. All survivor transitions, oracle labels, finite probe-class signatures, and 6,560-case holdout scores per survivor reproduce with zero discrepancies. The policy expands coverage on 10 panels, returns a holdout-safe class on 5/5 class-return panels, and reaches an exact single term on the other 20 panels; median oracle use is two labels, with no help callback. This supports evidence-gated finite-class return on the authored numeric benchmark. Candidate acquisition came from a frozen upstream pool; the result does not show that the substrate discovered a new dimension, used a CoW branch, returned a graph residual, or adopted a persistent rule. The next gate is to carry this decision through an isolated branch and a revision-checked receipt, then repeat on independently structured tasks. The generated capability inventory validates at 28 rows, 421 source links, 72 linked test references, and 549 result-linked runners, with no unresolved or missing evidence (JSON SHA-256 `83e8872023669d5e407803021fdca98f195ff354d567db7dccffcf2bfd5ea12f`). The overall goal remains active. See [results](COVERAGE_AWARE_CLASS_RETURN_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/coverage_aware_class_return_v1_1_analysis_20261004.json), and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

## 2026-10-04 Thoughtexperiment journal source audit

Static audit of `TypedGraph` mutation methods found a before-image coverage defect in `restore_node`: replacing a live ID was journaled as a new node. The current source now records the prior live node before replacement; fresh and tombstoned IDs continue to be journaled as additions. The source-linked inventory validates at 28 rows, 421 source links, 72 linked test references, and 549 result-linked runners, with zero unresolved links, missing evidence, or validation errors (JSON SHA-256 `6481c77dca53e77df42eb18781c813a08df40c0ce0609a17d8098391c0e57ed4`). No build, project tests, or thoughtexperiment workers were run for this correction. Thus journal-based local validation remains conditional, and exact graph parity remains the audit gate until a locked current-source mutation matrix covers all mutation paths and restore/checkpoint cases. The broader goal is still active: the substrate can run bounded, isolated structural experiments, but general tractability and substrate-led self-improvement remain unestablished. See [source audit](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_JOURNAL_SOURCE_AUDIT_20261004.md) and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 CoW class receipt

The five safe class-return decisions from the coverage-policy replay have now
been transported through shared-page CoW branches into primary graph receipts.

membership links, unchanged primary state before adoption, branch/primary
parity after adoption, unchanged rules, and rejection of stale retries. The

## Overall status correction — 2026-10-04 coverage boundary failure

The finite coverage policy has now failed a preregistered off-support case.
Across five opposition panels it returned an apparently single behavior
class containing the false candidate `(ax == -ex) AND (ax < 4)` after zero
labels. The candidate matches all selected probes and the registered
holdout, but a wider balanced domain shows a median 741 errors per panel.
Thus the current per-variable zero/±2 coverage rule does not establish
equivalence outside probe support. This narrows the preceding distinct-Term
positive control to correct class handling given covered outcomes, without
validating the coverage certificate itself. The next gate is candidate-term
boundary extraction and directed probe selection, compared against fixed
expansion and abstention controls. The map validates at 28 rows, 421 source
links, 72 linked tests, and 549 result-linked runners with no missing or
unresolved evidence (JSON SHA-256
`ff0708d1bcf747e483fdbc5c6b1451441fe3d3d9b999551b0fc77235d2b56a0d`). The
overall goal remains active. See [failure results](PROBE_COVERAGE_FALSE_EQUIVALENT_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md),
[independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/probe_coverage_false_equivalent_v1_analysis_20261004.json),
and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 distinct-Term class control

A preregistered positive control now checks class return with two structurally
different Terms, `ax == -ex` and `-ax == ex`, plus a same-sign distractor. In
all five frozen panels the policy uses one oracle label and one coverage
expansion, returns exactly the two equivalent Term trees, and excludes the
control. Independent replay verifies both members on the original holdout and
3,024 additional balanced wide-range/boundary examples per member. This
repairs the earlier class-return evidence gap at the mechanism level only:
the hypotheses remain harness supplied, and one authored relation is not
general induction. The inventory validates at 28 rows, 421 source links, 72

## Overall status correction — 2026-10-04 duplicate-Term audit

A canonical-Term audit narrowed the coverage-class and CoW receipt results.
Each of the five returned sets contains four candidate IDs but only one
distinct Term; the CoW receipts preserve those duplicate entries correctly,
but they do not transport a diverse equivalence class. Across all 25 upstream
panels, 39 of 90 candidate rows (43.3%) are exact Term duplicates. A locked
deduplication replay removes these rows, changes all five apparent class
returns to exact-term decisions, and keeps all 25 outputs correct on the
declared finite holdout. It uses 70 labels total versus 60 without
deduplication (median 3 versus 2), because the singleton challenge path costs
more labels. The prior class-return statements should therefore be read as
duplicate-handling results, not evidence for distinct-hypothesis ambiguity
resolution. The map remains valid at 28 rows, 421 source links, 72 linked
tests, and 549 result-linked runners, with no unresolved/missing evidence
(JSON SHA-256 `2ccef14cbc0c812bedb488ebffc0f75c5d7e912c910201138bdce90dfb15528a`).
The deduplication and distinct-Term mechanism gates are now complete. Next,
test a near-equivalent false hypothesis that differs outside the initial
probe coverage and assess substrate-side candidate generation rather than
harness-supplied formulas. Overall goal remains active. See [deduplication results](TERM_DEDUPLICATION_CLASS_RETURN_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md),
[independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/term_deduplication_class_return_v1_1_analysis_20261004.json),
[distinct-Term results](DISTINCT_TERM_EQUIVALENCE_CLASS_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md),
and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 boundary-directed repair

A targeted repair now addresses the registered `ax < 4` false-safe class.
Across all five panels, extracting the candidate's numeric bound and adding
three local probes lets the native selector choose `[4, 0, -4, 0]`. One
oracle label removes the restricted candidate while retaining both distinct
exact Terms. Independent wide-domain rescoring lowers false-safe returns
from five to zero in this controlled case. This demonstrates a focused
candidate-directed repair, not a generic probe synthesizer: the relation and
candidate family are still harness supplied. The map validates at 28 rows,
421 source links, 72 linked tests, and 549 result-linked runners, with no
unresolved/missing evidence (JSON SHA-256
`5273a2772a1b9e180baab4d3f2dda746cccabe39f0106a1fbf8d1316e6a38caa`). The

and [measurement plan](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_AND_TRACTABILITY_MEASUREMENT_PLAN.md).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 policy comparison

The follow-up comparison tested five frozen panels per arithmetic family
(additive and product), at three hidden thresholds. The current policy returned
an unsafe restricted candidate in all 30 panels. Candidate-directed probing
rejected it with one label and retained two exact candidates in all 30; the
fixed 17-point probe grid reached the same outcome with 5.7× more
candidate/probe evaluations. Abstention avoided false acceptance but solved no
panels. Independent analysis passed and wide finite scoring recorded zero
errors for both probing arms. Evidence remains limited to harness-authored
Terms, relation-preserving fixtures, and a finite seeded score set; there is
no generic extraction or autonomous candidate discovery result. The next gate
is syntax/domain variation that denies the policy an explicit parsed-bound
feature, followed by an end-to-end proposal and adoption measure. See [results](BOUNDARY_PROBE_POLICY_COMPARISON_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](BOUNDARY_PROBE_POLICY_COMPARISON_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/boundary_probe_policy_comparison_v1_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/boundary_probe_policy_comparison_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

`dd036e30ca35d60555d1e413fe6a49732737f9d90dfd90db248f91159fb9efca`). The

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 Term syntax transfer

The next locked gate varied how the threshold is represented in the candidate
Term rather than passing a parsed bound field. Across 120 cells, the small
affine syntax walker recovered the comparison boundary and the native selector
found a probe that rejected the restricted candidate after one label while
preserving both exact candidates. All four registered encodings passed; wide
scoring exercised the restricted candidate's failure in 17,726 cases, while
the exact candidates remained correct. This advances evidence on syntax
transfer but the walker and candidate pool remain authored, and no new
candidate was induced from raw task examples. The current capability bottleneck
therefore remains hypothesis generation and grounding of task-relevant
features. The next gate is to test synthesis-generated candidate coverage and
false-singleton/empty-set decisions across held task families, with complete
work accounting. See [results](TERM_DERIVED_PROBE_GENERATION_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](TERM_DERIVED_PROBE_GENERATION_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/term_derived_probe_generation_v1_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/term_derived_probe_generation_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 blinded operator choice

A new locked measure partially closes the operator-hint gap. Candidate
synthesis searched both `Plus` and `Times` expansions on the same examples,
without receiving the task-specific operator. Across five additive and five
product panels, native discriminating queries and labels left a task-appropriate
exact singleton in all ten. Single-grammar controls produced false singletons
or empty sets for the mismatched family. The joint search costs about twice one
grammar. This is bounded evidence of selecting a primitive from a two-item
declared menu, not open-ended invention or natural-task generalization. Next
test a wider operator menu or residual-derived proposals on independent task
families, including proposal rejection cost and a request-for-help baseline.
See [results](OPERATOR_HINT_BLINDING_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](OPERATOR_HINT_BLINDING_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/operator_hint_blinding_v1_20261004.json), and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/operator_hint_blinding_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 multi-operator search

The wider shared grammar was tested on four numeric families. Every search
reached its 12-second cap. Exact candidates appeared only for the absolute
relation (5/5) and were selected in all five panels. The bounded run produced
no exact candidates for additive, product, or minimum relations; the subsequent
selector returned false singletons in 13 of those 15 panels and ambiguity in
two. This indicates the measured search regime is highly sensitive to grammar
width and work cap, and candidate agreement is unsafe as an adequacy signal.
The targets may be expressible beyond size 7 or after longer search; current
evidence does not settle that. The next gate is staged, residual-triggered
grammar expansion with cumulative cost, confirmation labels, and a help/abstain
control. See [results](MULTI_OPERATOR_FAMILY_SEARCH_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](MULTI_OPERATOR_FAMILY_SEARCH_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/multi_operator_family_search_v1_20261004.json), and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/multi_operator_family_search_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 factored operator search

A matched follow-up partitions the same shared grammar into four smaller
operator-specific searches. At similar median total synthesis time (11.1–11.7
seconds versus the shared grammar's 12-second cap), it generates an exact
candidate and resolves all 20 panels across all four numeric families. The
shared grammar generated exact candidates in only 5/20. This is concrete
evidence that enumerative search organization materially affects measured
coverage, even when partial branches time out. It remains a closed four-branch,
small numeric result; the next test is adaptive branch budgeting and
resynthesis after query residuals, with a request-help action and full
cumulative-cost comparison. See [results](FACTORED_OPERATOR_SEARCH_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](FACTORED_OPERATOR_SEARCH_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/factored_operator_search_v1_20261004.json), and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/factored_operator_search_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 adaptive audit failure

The amended adaptive policy reduces median branch search work to 3.0–5.8
seconds, but accepts seven false singleton models among 19 acceptances after
two matching integer audit labels. Twelve accepted models are holdout-exact;
one panel requests help. The paired fixed policy spends about twice the search
time and resolves all 20 panels. This is a measurable cost/quality tradeoff,
with the adaptive acceptance gate failing on fractional inputs. It strongly
indicates that audit examples must cover the relevant input distribution and
constraint structure. Next test balanced task-conditioned continuous audits
against integer audit and help/abstention controls, with sealed holdout and
complete cost accounting. See [results](ADAPTIVE_FACTORED_OPERATOR_SEARCH_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](ADAPTIVE_FACTORED_OPERATOR_SEARCH_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/adaptive_factored_operator_search_v1_20261004.json), and [independent analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/adaptive_factored_operator_search_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 continuous singleton audit

The paired post-search audit shows that balanced continuous examples correct
the observed integer-audit failure on these 20 fixed proposals: all 12
holdout-exact candidates are accepted, all seven false integer acceptances are
rejected to help, and the remaining no-candidate panel also requests help.
This requires 62 labels versus 38 for the integer policy; immediate help
avoids false acceptance but accepts none. The result is bounded by its
task-conditioned example generator and fixed candidate pool. It neither
demonstrates autonomous adequacy detection nor residual-driven repair. The
next learning gate is generic or Term-directed probe generation without task
identity, followed by residual-triggered resynthesis and full cost/quality
measurement. See [results](CONTINUOUS_SINGLETON_AUDIT_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md),
[protocol](CONTINUOUS_SINGLETON_AUDIT_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/continuous_singleton_audit_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_continuous_singleton_audit_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/continuous_singleton_audit_v1_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/continuous_singleton_audit_v1_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/continuous_singleton_audit_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 generic continuous audit

The independent, task-blind uniform sampler did not detect any of the seven
false singleton candidates, including with 64 continuous labels per candidate.
At all three caps (4, 16, 64) the outcome remains 12 correct acceptances, seven
false acceptances, and one help request; the 64-label cap consumes 1,216 labels.
The paired task-conditioned audit detects all seven false candidates in 62
labels total, but relies on authored task-aware examples. This establishes a
sharp limitation of untargeted random auditing in these task distributions,
while leaving syntax-directed probing as the next test. Per-query timing is not
available in this capture. The assay audits fixed candidates and does not
resynthesize from residuals. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 Term-conditioned audit

Using candidate predictions to select a balanced finite-support audit stream
reduced false accepts from seven to one by 64 labels, while retaining all 12
holdout-correct candidates. The one miss is an absolute-value false candidate
that agreed across the sampled quarter-step support. Proposal construction
costs 21,039 native Term evaluations across 20 panels (median 731.5 each;
median 6.36 ms wall/CPU). This improves on uniform continuous sampling, which
caught none, but does not match the task-conditioned audit's zero false
accepts at 62 labels. It remains candidate-aware, finite-support, and
post-search; no residual is used to resynthesize. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 residual-triggered resynthesis

The corrected v1.1 capture passes independent source and semantic checks. Six
of 20 panels triggered a candidate mismatch. Paired native resynthesis with
and without that residual reaches the same final counts: one holdout-correct
acceptance, four false acceptances, one help request. Adding the residual
sometimes shrinks the candidate set and lowers selector queries, but does not
improve correctness at this budget. This is evidence that the current
resynthesis integration can consume a residual, while the measured search and
selection path did not turn it into a better adopted result. The initial v1
capture failed provenance after an analyzer correction and remains diagnostic;
only v1.1 is authoritative. Next distinguish expressivity, budget, and
selection failures and test incremental residual/budget policies with
quality-based controls. See [results](RESIDUAL_TRIGGERED_RESYNTHESIS_V1_1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](RESIDUAL_TRIGGERED_RESYNTHESIS_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [amendment](RESIDUAL_TRIGGERED_RESYNTHESIS_V1_1_AMENDMENT_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/residual_triggered_resynthesis_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_residual_triggered_resynthesis_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/residual_triggered_resynthesis_v1_1_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_triggered_resynthesis_v1_1_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_triggered_resynthesis_v1_1_analysis_20261004.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 residual resynthesis budget diagnostic

The v1.2 matched-budget replay increases each factored branch from 0.5 to 2
seconds. Both the original-example control and the one-residual arm now find a
holdout-exact candidate in all six triggered panels, accepting five exact
candidates and requesting help on the same unresolved panel. Neither arm has a
false acceptance. Candidate coverage and final correctness are equal; the
residual arm reduces the candidate pool and median selector labels (3 vs. 4)
without reducing total search time. This makes search truncation the primary
measured explanation for v1.1's poor outcome, and gives no quality advantage
to one residual at equal budget. See [results](RESIDUAL_TRIGGERED_RESYNTHESIS_V1_2_RESULTS_20261004.md), [amendment](RESIDUAL_TRIGGERED_RESYNTHESIS_V1_2_AMENDMENT_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/residual_triggered_resynthesis_v1_2.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_residual_triggered_resynthesis_v1_2.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/residual_triggered_resynthesis_v1_2_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_triggered_resynthesis_v1_2_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/residual_triggered_resynthesis_v1_2_analysis_20261004.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 selector-budget extension

Continuing native selection over the full registered query pool leaves the
same three candidates in one absolute-value panel after four labels; there is
no separating input in the remaining pool. At 8/16/32/full-pool caps, both
search arms have five exact acceptances, one help, and zero false acceptances.
The cap extension therefore identifies a probe-coverage limit, not a need for
more labels from the same pool. Candidate-directed novel queries are the next
step. The v1/v1.1 attempts are diagnostic due to analyzer-gate defects; only
the v1.2 source-bound replay is authoritative. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 Term-directed float-edge probes

The final source-locked v1.2 probe study passes all eight analyzer checks. A
candidate-Term-derived IEEE stress input `(ax,ex)=(1e-200,2e-200)` produces an
oracle mismatch for the squared-equality candidate due to underflow, removing
it with one label; the two remaining candidates agree on the probe slate and
are accepted as a behavior class. Both score exact on the ordinary 1,296-point
holdout and on a separate 4,096-case log-scale/ULP stress set, while the
squared candidate has 520 mismatches on that stress set. This is a narrow
success in candidate diagnosis and class return, bounded to one authored
absolute-value panel and finite validation domains. It does not prove universal
float equivalence or autonomous self-improvement. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 edge-probe v2 false-class control

The v2 transfer study reproduces the Abs/Times underflow and Div/Eq signed-zero
counterexamples using one query each. More significantly, its structured
literal-alias control is a false acceptance: a Term guarded on
`ax==0.125 AND ex==0.25` agrees with the variable-pair probes, is accepted as
part of a behavior class without any label, and then has two mismatches on the

acceptance criterion absent a bounded-domain proof. The next repair is a
Term-literal-directed probe arm plus abstention control. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 literal-directed probe repair

The new paired study passes seven independent replay gates. On the structured interval-alias control, edge-only probes accept a false behavior class with zero labels; direct Term-literal comparison boundaries expose its guard, and one label removes it while retaining identity. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 proposal budget and abstention

A noise-padded four-variable guard expands the direct-literal product to 256 rows. Exhaustive search rejects it with one query label. A 64-row cap misses the separating assignment, but explicit tracking of the incomplete proposal slate changes the false finite-class acceptance to `request_help`. This establishes a useful fail-closed behavior and exposes the remaining cost problem: exhaustive native policy work is 1,605 evaluations on this one case, while the truncated policy spends 1,364 evaluations and still abstains. The next gate is structure-aware proposal search that reduces work while preserving the same false-acceptance and abstention checks. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 clause-aware literal proposal

On the locked noise-padded four-variable case, a host-side clause solver finds the separating assignment with one proposal and one label, avoiding the 256-row Cartesian product. It uses 60 scalar constraint checks and 840 native Term evaluations; exhaustive selection uses 1,605 native evaluations. The capped policy abstains. This is a promising search-policy result, not autonomous substrate improvement: clause parsing and satisfying-assignment construction were authored by the harness, and end-to-end exhaustive generation timing was not measured. Broader generated formulas and negative/unsupported constraint controls remain necessary. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 contradiction and aliasing controls

A twelve-panel replay passes nine checks. Three direct unary comparison branches are proven unsatisfiable over finite binary64 values, including an adjacent-representable gap and the largest-finite boundary. Repeated bounds resolve; direct cross-variable aliases cause help while the false candidate survives. If another query removes that candidate, the system can accept the identity without solving the irrelevant remainder. Pairwise controls continue to falsely accept selected higher-arity, arithmetic, and alias cases; the clause-aware arm has no holdout false acceptance in this panel. This validates only a small host-authored unary proof fragment and fail-closed policy, not general substrate learning or equivalence. See [results](CLAUSE_CONTRADICTION_ALIASING_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](CLAUSE_CONTRADICTION_ALIASING_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/clause_contradiction_aliasing_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_clause_contradiction_aliasing_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/clause_contradiction_aliasing_v1_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/clause_contradiction_aliasing_v1_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/clause_contradiction_aliasing_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

A locked 1,024-case generated audit passes seven replay gates. All cases contain a variable-to-variable comparison, so the current direct-unary solver abstains on all 1,024. ## Thoughtexperiment predecessor fanout node scale v2 (2026-10-04)

The locked 12-worker sweep passes all 14 independent checks at 200k, 400k, and 1m unrelated nodes, with two fresh-process replicates per size/backend cell. At 1m, shared graph pages reduce candidate-fork median to 1.66 ms from 508 ms for full copy, and retained allocator growth to 21.8 MiB from 725.9 MiB. Yet lifecycle medians remain 21.85 s and 22.96 s because graph export and canonical digest dominate. Lifecycle rises about 4.9× from 200k to 1m in each backend; peak RSS is 3.58/4.29 GiB (shared-page/full-copy). This is two-replicate, one-host evidence on a sparse synthetic graph with four supplied candidates, not a general scaling or natural-task claim. It shows that reducing graph-wide export/digest work is now required for cheap forks to yield much higher experiment throughput. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_PREDECESSOR_FANOUT_NODE_SCALE_V2_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_PREDECESSOR_FANOUT_NODE_SCALE_V2_PREREG_20261004.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_fanout_node_scale_v2.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_predecessor_fanout_node_scale_v2.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_fanout_node_scale_v2_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_fanout_node_scale_v2_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_predecessor_fanout_node_scale_v2_analysis_20261004.json).

## Thoughtexperiment revision/receipt validation v1 (2026-10-04)

A source-locked 16-worker paired panel compares the existing full-digest lifecycle with O(1) graph/rule revision checks and journal receipts at 200k/1m nodes, both snapshot backends, and two replicates per cell. Every worker passes semantic selection, isolation, rule preservation, stale-receipt, quiescence, and exact post-lifecycle branch/primary parity gates; all match an independent monolithic oracle. The fast path removes five full graph exports/digests from the candidate lifecycle. Lifecycle-only median speedup is 28.2×/122.8× (full-copy/shared-pages) at 200k and 33.7×/319.8× at 1m. Including a timed exact branch/primary digest audit lowers this to 2.33×/2.42× and 2.47×/2.50×. Therefore high hot-loop throughput is supported only when revision/journal guarantees carry routine validation and full parity audits run separately; exact audit every batch still shows a measured 2.3–2.5× reduction on this task. This is conditional on tracked semantic writes and complete journal coverage; it remains one sparse synthetic workload, two replicates/cell, and one host. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_REVISION_RECEIPT_VALIDATION_V1_RESULTS_20261004.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_REVISION_RECEIPT_VALIDATION_V1_PREREG_20261004.md), [paired runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_revision_receipt_validation_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_revision_receipt_validation_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_revision_receipt_validation_v1_lock_20261004.json), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_revision_receipt_validation_v1_20261004.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_revision_receipt_validation_v1_analysis_20261004.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-04 runtime rule-audit attribution

The flat process-global `u32` rule index has been replaced at native matcher entry points by an audit key assigned from `(store instance token, local rule index)`. The owner token is carried through direct `Substrate` dispatch, scoped runners, the free rewrite runner, and Rayon worker context; a PyO3 snapshot maps audit IDs back to the source identity. Focused serial/Rayon matcher tests and a two-`Substrate` native test pass. The preregistered Python binding control v1.1 also passes all six gates, including audit-off isolation; its measured audit-on example has two candidate tests, zero unattributed tests, and two distinct store-qualified rows.

The new six-worker live capture v1.4 has full source stability, task/graph parity, and RuleUID resolution (4,788/4,788). Audit-on candidate attribution and candidate conservation pass. However, all three audit-off workers record 8,099 candidate tests and 86,114 Term evaluations on the first prompt, while the second prompt is zero; audit-on second prompts also contain 134 unattributed Term evaluations each. The panel therefore remains diagnostic and does not support a natural workload per-rule cost claim. These residual failures are documented in [the v1.4 diagnostic](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_RUNTIME_CLOSURE_COUNTERS_V1_4_DIAGNOSTIC_20261004.md), with the earlier collision and repair history in [the v1.3 diagnostic](SUBSTRATE_LIVE_RUNTIME_CLOSURE_COUNTERS_V1_3_DIAGNOSTIC_20261004.md). Next, locate the first-request audit reactivation and the 134 Term events before another live capture.

This advances engine instrumentation and its measurement contract; it does not complete the larger capability programme. Structural and synthetic scaling evidence is broad, while natural-workload tractability, learning of genuinely new dimensions, prospective thoughtexperiment residual discovery, and multi-host scaling remain open. The temporary Cargo build trees were cleaned after controlled builds and tests, recovering 4.5 GiB; the rebuilt source-matched native extension and provenance record remain installed.

**2026-10-04 follow-up on the live audit anomaly:** The capability map was regenerated with the namespaced identity API and v1.4 limitations; its independent `--check` passes 28 inventory rows, 422 source links, 72 linked test references, 552 runner links, and zero unresolved or missing evidence (JSON SHA-256: `21cd182e852413f2df0a6839872bf3a84b8de4f293052b89`). Source tracing confirms audit enablement has both a process-global mode and a thread-local override. The live runner can read only the process-global `scale_audit_enabled()` through PyO3; it does not observe the override on the thread doing native work. The thread-local setter currently appears only in Rust tests, so this is an observability gap and a diagnostic lead, not an established cause of v1.4's audit-off counts. Before another workload capture, expose and source-lock a diagnostic for both mode bits at the Python wrapper and native matcher/Term call sites, and directly trace the unattributed Term sites. The v1.4 panel remains invalid for audit-on/off cost comparison.

**2026-10-04 root-cause update:** The new PyO3 mode snapshot and opt-in setter trace show that the first-request audit-off counts are caused by nested instrumentation in the panel's base worker: its `_record_live_goal_event` wrapper turns on the same process-global audit for three event measurements and turns it off after each. The three toggles occur on the Python/native calling thread; the request's off-arm label therefore did not mean the whole request ran with audit disabled. The probe's zero-counter gate fails, but the trace and source inspection explain the failure. This resolves the suspected audit isolation defect as harness contamination. A corrected six-worker panel remains to be run without overlapping audit scopes; the 134 unattributed Term evaluations from the separate audit-on second-prompt condition also remain unresolved. Full evidence is in [probe v2 results](SUBSTRATE_RUNTIME_AUDIT_MODE_PROBE_V2_RESULTS_20261004.md).

V1.4's smaller first-prompt counters were the final producer-event window after repeated counter resets, not whole-request totals; v1.5 measures 805,415 Term evaluations on the first prompt and 374,222 on the second. **2026-10-04 closure-entry replay:** All 15 primary `run_rules` entry states restore their graphs and ordered rule definitions, but replay does not yet reproduce per-rule work: metric-cell exactness is 50.5%, with candidate/Term totals 10–21% above the live route. The scheduler trace directly shows fresh replay selecting all 2,357 rules where live calls select only 1,272 and 927, indicating that persistent dirty-rule state is required for a faithful replay. Most live rules are mutation-protected and the current Python restore binding clears that flag, another measured limitation. This is actionable evidence that observability has reached graph/rule state but not the full engine state required for deterministic independent replay. **2026-10-05 thoughtexperiment per-effect value trace prototype:** The event journal now records bounded before/after values at each effect boundary, exposing intermediate writes inside a single firing. A focused native smoke fixture confirmed `0→1→2` per-effect transitions while the enclosing event remains `0→2`, and separately captured edge removal. Rust compile checking and the Python/native smoke check pass. The build was development-profile; optimized overhead and memory cost have not been measured. The next gate is a source-locked four-mode comparison (plain, event attribution, per-effect trace, combined) with cap, fan-out, parity, and isolated-process memory controls. See [v1.2.13 amendment](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EVENT_VALUE_ATTRIBUTION_V1_2_13_AMENDMENT_20261005.md). This improves diagnosis of rule residuals, while rung-three/four structure induction and any claim of substrate self-improvement remain unaddressed.

**2026-10-05 effect observer cost gate and opt-in split:** The first complete capture showed that coupling effect snapshots to endpoint attribution was costly under a high-fan-out object-cap fixture. The observer is now independently opt-in; source review, compile check, and native parity probe confirm the endpoint API produces no effect snapshots while `run_rules_effect_delta_report` does. Three source-locked workers and 45 isolated arms pass corrected analysis. In the stress fixture, effect tracing costs a median 560 ms and 269,180 KiB retained RSS/PSS delta versus 223 ms and 118,120 KiB for endpoint events (2.51× and +151,060 KiB); the combined residual/effect path costs 1.069 s and +480,108 KiB. Small fixtures show only a few KiB difference. The build is optimized but non-LTO with 16 codegen units, and samples are three authored cases, so these values are diagnostic rather than general workload estimates. Next test whether effect detail improves held-out residual diagnosis enough to justify its stress-case cost. See [cost results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EVENT_EFFECT_COST_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [preregistration](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EVENT_EFFECT_COST_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_event_effect_cost_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_event_effect_cost_v1_lock_20261005.json), and [corrected analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_event_effect_cost_v1_analysis_correction_20261005.json). This is an observability and cost result; induction, general tractability, and substrate self-improvement remain open.

**2026-10-05 effect-trace diagnosis utility probe:** On 80 generated single-firing cases (20 each at 4/8/16/32 effects), three source-locked workers agree and corrected analysis passes. Given only the event report and a supplied failing node ID, effect traces identify the oracle effect index 80/80 times and reduce the candidate set from the full effect list (mean 15) to one. The event-only report carries changed node IDs but no binding-to-effect relation; its preregistered lowest-index choice matches 10/80 cases. This verifies interface-level information gain under that restricted query, not a fair comparison against full graph rematching/rule-source inspection, realistic diagnosis or repair, or natural-task prevalence. The next gate supplies those stronger baselines and measures trace-guided counterfactual branch reduction on held-out tasks. See [diagnosis results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EFFECT_TRACE_DIAGNOSIS_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EFFECT_TRACE_DIAGNOSIS_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_effect_trace_diagnosis_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_effect_trace_diagnosis_v1_lock_20261005.json), and [corrected analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_effect_trace_diagnosis_v1_analysis_correction_20261005.json). Structural induction, general tractability, and substrate self-improvement remain unproven.

**2026-10-05 full-rematch and counterfactual diagnosis baseline:** A source-locked 80-case, three-worker comparison now includes actual shared-graph-page branches and complete graph/rule rematching. Rematching and effect traces both select the correct effect and require one counterfactual branch; endpoint-only ordered search averages 7.775 branches. Pooled median diagnosis costs are 0.526 ms full rematch and 0.567 ms effect trace (CPU 0.534 vs 0.575 ms); the easier 32-effect conjunction stratum favors rematching 0.772 vs 0.919 ms. Thus the trace has not shown an advantage over source rematching on this workload. All 80 primary graph/rule revisions remain unchanged. This is still one synthetic single-rule family and excludes shared case/snapshot setup; deferred/dynamic effects, hard joins, realistic diagnosis utility, and natural-task prevalence remain open. Next assess production-derived binding complexity and a lightweight retained-binding/write-target event path before assigning effect traces any routine diagnostic role. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EFFECT_TRACE_COUNTERFACTUAL_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_EFFECT_TRACE_COUNTERFACTUAL_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_effect_trace_counterfactual_v1.py), [lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_effect_trace_counterfactual_v1_lock_20261005.json), and [corrected analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_effect_trace_counterfactual_v1_analysis_correction_20261005.json). This does not demonstrate substrate self-improvement or general tractability.

**2026-10-05 deferred `SetRuleClause` diagnosis:** Extended deferred effect provenance across the ordered rule-clause edit queue and recorded the committed target rule field, prior/new revision, and content hash. On the production `repair_apply_rule_clause` rule, three workers and twelve isolated runs pass source-lock and primary-isolation checks. In the authored held-out probe, target attribution and the rule-change receipt each identify the necessary effect in one omission branch; blind search and pre-state rematching require two. No-cause controls make rematching and receipt policies abstain. This closes one uniquely sourced deferred edit path and demonstrates useful evidence for a thoughtexperiment harness. It does not establish multi-writer attribution, natural repair frequency, self-directed learning, rung-three induction, or general tractability. Median policy timings are sub-millisecond to 1.43 ms in a tiny fixture and exclude setup, so they are exploratory. Next test multiple queued edits with competing and duplicate writers, including ambiguity/refusal outcomes, then move to a production-derived self-repair episode with target/cue withheld from the policy. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_DEFERRED_RULE_EDIT_DIAGNOSIS_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_DEFERRED_RULE_EDIT_DIAGNOSIS_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_deferred_rule_edit_diagnosis_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_deferred_rule_edit_diagnosis_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_deferred_rule_edit_diagnosis_v1_lock_20261005.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_deferred_rule_edit_diagnosis_v1_analysis_20261005.json). Overall goal remains active.

**2026-10-05 competing and duplicate deferred edits:** A follow-up source-locked gate passed three workers and nine isolated runs for competing edits, identical edits, and ambiguous target names. Two producers' origins and the ordered revision chain are retained across drain. Counterfactuals identify the final writer as necessary only when competing values differ; duplicate identical edits have no individually necessary source. Duplicate target names produce no commit, preserve both producer events, and leave the held-out marker enabled. The current receipt does not encode an explicit ambiguity code, so refusal classification relies on pre-state and unchanged target revision. This closes a multi-writer correctness/abstention fixture, not general learning or repair. Next, test production-derived self-repair behavior with target and cue withheld from the policy; preserve abstention and require held-out benefit across more than one rule family. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_DEFERRED_RULE_EDIT_COMPETING_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_DEFERRED_RULE_EDIT_COMPETING_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_deferred_rule_edit_competing_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_deferred_rule_edit_competing_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_deferred_rule_edit_competing_v1_lock_20261005.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_deferred_rule_edit_competing_v1_analysis_20261005.json). Overall goal remains active.

**2026-10-05 live configured self-repair repeatability:** The existing live ARC self-repair episode now passes in three fresh, source-locked processes. Each run observes the self-ID regression, auto-mints a Fault and commits the configured discriminator repair on frame 11, then reaches recovery and keeps the edit; final classification is `controllable`. Median wall time is 21.139 s. To enable the native segmentation path in this lean environment, `WorldAdapter._perceive_regions` now imports OpenCV only when the native `segment` primitive is unavailable; the three runs pass with no `cv2` installed, while the Python fallback remains untested. The episode still configures the broken target and corrected clause on the CapabilityAxis, so it validates execution/repeatability of specified repair, not target-blind discovery or novel learning. The next gate remains diagnosis with target/cue withheld and a held-out utility comparison. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_LIVE_SELF_REPAIR_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_LIVE_SELF_REPAIR_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_live_self_repair_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_live_self_repair_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_live_self_repair_v1_lock_20261005.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_live_self_repair_v1_analysis_20261005.json). Overall tractability/capability goal remains active.

**2026-10-05 graph-native blind self-change selection baseline:** Promoted the existing seven blinded FaultProbe cases from test-only evidence to a source-locked capability evaluation. Three workers pass 21 case runs: graph rules select the proof-admissible family in all five single-candidate cases, choose minimum MDL among two passing candidates despite the alternative's higher gain, and abort the correlation-only case because neither candidate beats both controls by the required margin. Median evidence-selection tick is 0.090 ms, excluding candidate/probe construction and evidence acquisition. This shows graph-native family selection and proof gating under supplied candidate representations and measured outcomes. It does not measure hypothesis invention, task-side evidence generation, external correctness, adoption, or self-improvement. Next combine candidate generation, observed write attribution, and externally measured held-out task outcomes without passing target/cue or a candidate-family label; include competing active rules and charge the full lifecycle. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SELF_CHANGE_BLIND_BASELINE_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SELF_CHANGE_BLIND_BASELINE_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_self_change_blind_baseline_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_self_change_blind_baseline_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_self_change_blind_baseline_v1_lock_20261005.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_self_change_blind_baseline_v1_analysis_20261005.json). Overall goal remains active.

**2026-10-05 graph-generated proposal through CoW rehearsal and guarded commit:** A source-locked end-to-end gate passes three workers, each paired across full-copy and shared-graph-page branches. Native graph rules generate a threshold candidate while the FaultProbe carries no live target UID; actual training branches beat no-op/placebo, the candidate passes distinct canary cases, then the harness binds the selected patch to the target CAS identity and Rust commits once. The target advances revision 0→1 and passes a fresh post-commit split. Full-copy and CoW branches match semantically. Tiny-graph timings are mixed and based on three pairs, so no speed conclusion is drawn. This closes lifecycle integration from a seed-generated candidate through held-out canary and commit on one authored task. The threshold-sensitivity cue, candidate grammar, and task oracle remain supplied. Target/cue-free diagnosis, broad hypothesis search, natural transfer, and substrate-led task/evidence acquisition remain open. Next add competing candidate families from the structural edit algebra, then test selection on independently generated task variants with no expected-family input and exhaustive held-out evaluation. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SELF_CHANGE_END_TO_END_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_SELF_CHANGE_END_TO_END_V1_PREREG_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_self_change_end_to_end_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_self_change_end_to_end_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_self_change_end_to_end_v1_lock_20261005.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_self_change_end_to_end_v1_analysis_20261005.json). Overall goal remains active.

**2026-10-05 blind structural repair gate preregistered:** The next bridge is specified in [the v1 protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_BLIND_STRUCTURAL_REPAIR_V1_PREREG_20261005.md). It removes family labels, target identity, corrected clauses, candidate clauses, and diagnosis flags from the policy inputs; uses a bounded, explicit structural edit language; requires independently authored cases, disjoint train/canary/final splits, no-repair and ambiguity controls, proposal accounting, CoW rehearsal, graph-native selection/abstention, and guarded CAS. Task generation and the correctness oracle remain harness-owned and are charged as external capability. The protocol is written, and a syntax-only enumerator implementation check has been performed; no source-locked task panel or outcome capture exists yet. The remaining integration check is whether the graph selection rules accept an opaque candidate pool without family-specific probe fields.

**2026-10-05 structural proposal enumerator implementation check:** Added `domains.edit_ops.enumerate_single_edit_candidates`, a bounded, deterministic syntax-only enumeration over the edit algebra. The initial smoke invocation produced 17 unique candidates from one small rule, without examples or oracle. At that point the operator pool supported comparison flips, match-edge deletion, attribute-key swaps, guard addition, and applicable edge-identity guards. The follow-up measurements below extend that initial operator pool and quantify proposal coverage. The remaining structural gap includes restoration of missing relations without graph evidence; task outcome, candidate selection, and commit integration are still open.

**2026-10-05 blinded structural proposal coverage v1:** The new source-locked subgate passes integrity and repeatability across three fresh workers. Syntax-only enumeration proposes the exact target rule in 1/5 authored fault pairs (comparison flip); it misses constant shift, restoring a dropped match edge, removing a guard, and replacing an effect value. Candidate counts are 9, 9, 0, 5, and 2; median enumeration time is 0.677 ms on these tiny rules. This is proposal-language coverage only—no task rehearsal, graph-native ranking, canary, or commit. The result makes the immediate boundary concrete: expand structural inverses for the missed families, then evaluate behavioral candidate discrimination without revealing the family label. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_BLIND_STRUCTURAL_REPAIR_PROPOSAL_COVERAGE_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/blind_structural_repair_proposal_coverage_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_blind_structural_repair_proposal_coverage_v1.py), [source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/blind_structural_repair_proposal_coverage_v1_lock_20261005.json), and [capture/analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/blind_structural_repair_proposal_coverage_v1_20261005.json).

On the same five authored fault/target pairs, three source-locked workers pass exact semantic repeatability and proposal accounting; exact target coverage rises from 1/5 to 4/5, while total proposals rise from 25 to 40 and median pool size from 5 to 10. Median enumeration time is 1.040 ms on these tiny rules. This is a paired engineering iteration on the v1 fixture, not held-out confirmation; the dropped-relation case remains uncovered, and there is still no task rehearsal or graph-native ranking in this gate. **2026-10-05 blind candidate tournament v1:** Candidate generation is now connected to actual CoW branch outcomes and graph-native proof/selection rules without passing operator names or target UID to the selector. Across three workers, the graph selects the sole control-beating candidate in a repair-needed case, passes disjoint canary examples, and commits via Rust CAS (revision 0→1); in a no-repair control it aborts with `no_causally_supported_patch` and preserves revision 0. The integration exposed a native parser omission for `set_attr_if_different` in RulePatch effects; `graph_rules.rs` now accepts this supported effect, the engine was rebuilt through `scripts/engine_build.sh --lib-only`, and provenance matches the source tree. This remains a two-condition, one-rule-family result with harness-owned task oracle and does not show general self-improvement. # 2026-10-05 blind candidate tournament v2

The expanded source-locked tournament passes three workers over six opaque
cases. A boolean-effect repair and a numeric-threshold repair each select one
control-beating proposal, pass disjoint replicated canary and final examples,
and commit by guarded CAS. Two no-repair cases, an ambiguous-training case,
and an irrelevant-relation proposal abstain without changing the live
revision. Independent analysis reports zero errors. Median measured full
lifecycle for the two repair cases is 89.989 ms; candidate-pool rehearsal is
30.209 ms median and graph selection 0.206 ms. Timings cover the named
in-process phases but exclude process boot, task construction, and oracle
work. This extends bounded candidate selection and safe writeback across two
edit families; harness-owned labels/oracle, tiny authored cases, and three
workers do not establish natural-task frequency, open-ended induction, or
self-improvement. Next broaden independently authored edit/task families,
scale graph/frame size, and move evidence and evaluation toward task-side
signals not encoded by the harness. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_BLIND_CANDIDATE_TOURNAMENT_V2_RESULTS_20261005.md),
[protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_BLIND_CANDIDATE_TOURNAMENT_V2_PREREG_20261005.md),
