## 2026-10-05 thoughtexperiment candidate branch scaling v1

A separate source-locked gate now measures one fixed-aperture rule rehearsal
over sparse directed-path backgrounds from 0 to 32,768 decoy nodes. Across 12
fresh processes and 120 no-op/candidate branches, task outputs match the
registered live and edited rules at every size; primary graph and rule
digests/revisions are unchanged. From 1,024 to 32,768 decoys (31.8×), median
shared-page fork stays at about 45–46 μs, closure at 0.138–0.189 ms, and
targeted read at 2.6–2.8 μs. Constructing the task's three nodes and two edges
roughly doubles from 19 to 39 μs; constructing the full path grows from 3.78
to 154.52 ms. The 0-node closure median is higher, and RSS snapshots are
noisy, so these are phase-local bounded observations rather than an asymptotic
or memory-scaling claim. This supports fixed-aperture CoW locality on one
sparse topology; high-degree structures, growing separators/apertures,
candidate-pool growth, second topology/host, and natural workload prevalence
remain open. Next cross background size with separator width and rule aperture,
then run a second topology and profile uninstrumented memory. See [results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CANDIDATE_BRANCH_SCALING_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md),
[protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CANDIDATE_BRANCH_SCALING_V1_PREREG_20261005.md),
[runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_candidate_branch_scaling_v1.py),
[analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_candidate_branch_scaling_v1.py),
[source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_candidate_branch_scaling_v1_lock_20261005.json),
[capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_candidate_branch_scaling_v1_20261005.json),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_candidate_branch_scaling_v1_analysis_20261005.json).

## 2026-10-05 thoughtexperiment candidate branch scaling v2

The follow-up closes v1's disconnected-background limitation with a
source-locked 54-cell cross of path/tree topology, 128/1,024/8,192 decoys,
2/8/32-node task apertures, and 1/8/32 crossing edges. Three fresh workers
per cell produce 972 timed no-op/candidate episodes plus 162 separate audit
probes. All pass output parity, source lock, and primary graph/rule isolation.
The instrumented per-rule counter verifies actual boundary degree and
`Exists` examined-neighbor work exactly (width × evaluation count). Shared
page fork medians stay near 50 μs; task-frame and crossing-edge construction
grow with their corresponding sizes. Closure medians remain about 0.14–0.28
ms and show no reliable monotonic latency trend, despite the verified scan
work increase. This is evidence for a fixed measured route and operation
count—not evidence that natural frames have narrow separators, nor a general
polynomial bound. Next prospectively sample real task graphs and boundary
widths, then repeat with a workload-derived topology and second host. See
[results](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CANDIDATE_BRANCH_SCALING_V2_RESULTS_20261005.md),
[protocol](THOUGHTEXPERIMENT_CANDIDATE_BRANCH_SCALING_V2_PREREG_20261005.md),
[runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_candidate_branch_scaling_v2.py),
[analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_thoughtexperiment_candidate_branch_scaling_v2.py),
[source lock](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/thoughtexperiment_candidate_branch_scaling_v2_lock_20261005.json),
[capture](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_candidate_branch_scaling_v2_20261005.json),
and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/thoughtexperiment_candidate_branch_scaling_v2_analysis_20261005.json).


## 2026-10-05 area-settlement parity update

The global versus barrier/live per-area settlement run completed three workers per arm over seven held-topic turns. Per-area calls reached zero residual without round-cap hits; area work reports, replies, and per-turn node/edge counts matched across arms. Exact graph parity remains unproven because the v1 digest includes numeric node IDs and the capture omitted graph records. Treat its failed digest checks as an inconclusive measurement gate, not as proof of divergent graph semantics. The next gate is an ID-independent structural comparison on retained typed graph snapshots. See [v1 results](SUBSTRATE_HELD_TOPIC_AREA_SETTLEMENT_PARITY_V1_RESULTS_20261005.md) and [next gates](SUBSTRATE_CAPABILITY_PROGRAMME_NEXT_GATES_20261005.md).


The 2026-10-05 v1.1 follow-up retained final graph snapshots and passed its ID-permutation/mutation fixture checks. It did not pass structural parity: WL fingerprints diverged from turn two onward even across repeated global workers. A pairwise global snapshot comparison found `SystemModel` record differences (including foreign sample IDs and target node-set sizes of five versus six), so the scheduler effect is confounded by baseline graph variation. The next action is to instrument the foreign-snapshot producer and establish baseline repeatability before testing priority settlement. See [v1.1 results](SUBSTRATE_HELD_TOPIC_AREA_SETTLEMENT_PARITY_V1_1_RESULTS_20261005.md).


## 2026-10-06 settlement parity resolution

The v1.1 raw digest failures were artifacts: the fingerprint did not normalize serialized NodeID references and included `SystemModel.ts` / `SystemModel.runtime_s`. The instrumented foreign-snapshot diagnostic found identical input graphs, reconstructed enumeration labels, and result fields across fixed seed 0 and seeds 1–3. An uninstrumented fixed-seed repeat showed raw hashes still vary, while reference-aware final-graph fingerprints matched. The decisive source-locked v1.2 panel passed normalized structural fingerprints, replies, node/edge counts, and settlement-termination checks at all seven turns across three arms and three workers per arm. This establishes bounded full-settlement semantic parity under the stated WL invariant; it does not establish response-priority deferral, latency benefit, natural-task prevalence, or learned-structure tractability. Next is the finite response-priority/residual-continuation gate. See [v1.2 results](SUBSTRATE_HELD_TOPIC_AREA_SETTLEMENT_PARITY_V1_2_RESULTS_20261006.md).


## 2026-10-06 response-priority follow-up

The v1 priority/residual experiment is not yet confirmatory. The first pilot bypassed adapter host hooks and was discarded as a harness confound. The corrected event trace matched replies but failed graph parity: native write events show default closure registers recurrence and review records before duplicate retirement, while selected settlement retires first and removes the required concern edges. Full continuation cannot restore them. This establishes schedule-order sensitivity for the tested graph. A pending shape-decode observation also remains in the priority graph. The single-worker latency difference is not evidence of a speedup. Next test a lifecycle-preserving rule partition and repeat the completeness gate before any multiworker panel. See [diagnostic results](SUBSTRATE_RESPONSE_PRIORITY_RESIDUAL_CONTINUATION_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md).


## 2026-10-06 response-priority API correction and candidate result

The selected-area Python binding now maps the canonical `global` label to `Sym::default()` for both selection and reassignment. `cargo check` and release extension installation succeeded. The first corrected pair exposed a missing last-progress update in selected settlement; after adding it, a separate three-worker-per-arm confirmation passed reply, held-topic outcome, normalized graph fingerprint, cardinality, and zero-residual gates across all seven turns. This validates finite semantic parity for the locked workload only. Instrumented medians show no speed advantage and are not a performance estimate. The prior v1/v1.1 runs are corrected for the alias confound. The subsequent mutation-journaling-off cost comparison found a 7.2% lower paired median on this one workload, with fixed arm order; treat it as a replication signal. Next counterbalance arm order and expand to independently sampled task families and frame sizes. See [v1.2 result](SUBSTRATE_RESPONSE_PRIORITY_RESIDUAL_CONTINUATION_V1_2_RESULTS_20261006.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/response_priority_residual_continuation_v1_2_candidate_20261006/capture.json.gz), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/response_priority_residual_continuation_v1_2_candidate_20261006/analysis.json).


## 2026-10-06 response-priority finite confirmation

The source-locked three-worker-per-arm confirmation passed all 71 analyzer checks. All seven turns matched on reply and held-topic outcome projections, normalized graph fingerprints, node/edge counts, and zero-residual continuation. This closes the finite semantic-parity gate for the selected partition and workload. It does not measure uninstrumented speed or establish how common the regime is in natural tasks. The next work is a preregistered uninstrumented cost comparison, followed by task-family and frame-size expansion. See [confirmation result](SUBSTRATE_RESPONSE_PRIORITY_RESIDUAL_CONTINUATION_V1_2_CONFIRMATORY_RESULTS_20261006.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_RESPONSE_PRIORITY_RESIDUAL_CONTINUATION_V1_2_CONFIRMATORY_PREREG_20261006.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/response_priority_residual_continuation_v1_2_confirmatory_20261006/capture.json.gz), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/response_priority_residual_continuation_v1_2_confirmatory_analysis_20261006.json).


## 2026-10-06 response-priority operational-cost measurement

With per-rule mutation journaling disabled, a separate six-worker capture passed the same finite reply, normalized graph, cardinality, and continuation gates. Median response plus continuation was 133.2 ms in priority and 143.6 ms under global closure; response medians were 125.1 and 136.1 ms, respectively. Priority deferred a median four dirty ambient rules per response and cleared them during continuation without progressing steps. Its term/where evaluation counters fell, while rule enumerations increased. Fixed arm order, one task family, and one machine limit the result; use counterbalanced, cross-family replication before claiming a speed advantage. See [cost result](SUBSTRATE_RESPONSE_PRIORITY_RESIDUAL_CONTINUATION_V1_2_COST_RESULTS_20261006.md), [preregistration](SUBSTRATE_RESPONSE_PRIORITY_RESIDUAL_CONTINUATION_V1_2_COST_PREREG_20261006.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/response_priority_residual_continuation_v1_2_cost_20261006/capture.json.gz), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/response_priority_residual_continuation_v1_2_cost_analysis_20261006.json).


## 2026-10-06 counterbalanced response-priority cost repeat

Four counterbalanced worker pairs passed finite reply, held-topic outcome, normalized graph, cardinality, and zero-residual gates. Priority had lower paired response-plus-continuation medians in all four pairs, by 6.4–10.3%, on the held-topic workload. This reduces the fixed-order explanation but remains one task family/host and includes normal adapter work. Next measure the same partition on five independent live-conversation routes with route-specific utility checks; do not generalize this timing to natural tasks or learned-structure acquisition. See [results](SUBSTRATE_RESPONSE_PRIORITY_RESIDUAL_CONTINUATION_V1_2_1_COUNTERBALANCED_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_RESPONSE_PRIORITY_RESIDUAL_CONTINUATION_V1_2_1_COUNTERBALANCED_PREREG_20261006.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/response_priority_residual_continuation_v1_2_1_counterbalanced_20261006/capture.json.gz), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/response_priority_residual_continuation_v1_2_1_counterbalanced_analysis_20261006.json).


## 2026-10-06 cross-family response-priority gate

Twenty source-locked workers covered five authored conversation routes with balanced arm order. The known 6/10 utility profile reproduced in both arms, with no priority regression. Four routes passed strict graph parity; scene description failed strict parity despite equal utility status and reply. A separate snapshot diagnostic localized that difference to a single `NativeReflectiveFacultyReceipt.native_steps` value for the shape-decode loop (10 global, 8 priority). Omitting only this recorded execution count yields matching bounded normalized fingerprints; strict full-graph parity remains failed. This separates task-state evidence from scheduler work telemetry without hiding either. The sample remains authored and narrow. Next measure pre-outcome frame support and actual native dependency boundaries across these routes, keeping task prevalence and new-structure learning as open gates. See [cross-family result](SUBSTRATE_RESPONSE_PRIORITY_CROSS_FAMILY_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [scene diagnostic](SUBSTRATE_RESPONSE_PRIORITY_SCENE_DIVERGENCE_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/response_priority_cross_family_v1_20261006/capture.json.gz), and [forensic analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/response_priority_scene_divergence_v1_20261006/analysis.json).

## 2026-10-06 multi-route outcome-blind frame and access gate

Ten fresh workers captured the fixed pre-outcome ingress frame and post-checkpoint native rule trace on the same five conversation routes. Integrity gates pass, and corresponding replicates reproduce exactly. Frames remain small (6–16 nodes; projected width upper bound 2), while traces touch 7,311–11,796 distinct nodes outside the frame and direct boundary. The task utility profile remains 6/10. A separately locked static metadata capture resolves all observed RuleUIDs and finds zero observed resource-key mismatches or concrete dependency pairs outside the static projection. But that conservative projection has 59,000–77,000 possible pairs versus 195–237 dynamically witnessed pairs, and some event kinds remain uncheckable. Source attribution identifies resident Concept scans for lexicon lookup and curiosity, plus scene recognition and ShapeDecode work. Their required interface size versus avoidable candidate-generation cost is unresolved. The immediate gate is exact-parity candidate indexing/caching with population growth and mutation invalidation controls, before defining a dependency-backed frame and testing partitioned closure. General tractability, natural-task prevalence, acquisition cost, and new-dimension induction remain open. See [frame results](SUBSTRATE_MULTIROUTE_FRAME_ACCESS_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [static reconciliation](SUBSTRATE_MULTIROUTE_STATIC_DEPENDENCY_RECONCILIATION_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](SUBSTRATE_MULTIROUTE_FRAME_ACCESS_V1_PREREG_20261006.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/multiroute_frame_access_v1_20261006/capture.json.gz), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/multiroute_frame_access_v1_20261006/dependency_reconciliation.json).

## 2026-10-06 COW value-index profile update

The substrate now has direct component evidence that COW value-shard size
controls first-write copy work: increasing shards from 64 to 512 reduced the
selected shard estimate from 793 to 97 entries at 50,771 distinct values and
reduced isolated fork-plus-delete median from 48.9µs to 5.3µs. This suggests
one targeted implementation opportunity in branch mutation. The measurement
is map-only; actual Graph/Substrate mutation, closure throughput, query cost,
and memory tradeoffs remain to be measured. It does not close the general
tractability claim. Capability evidence for held-structure execution continues
to improve, while natural task coverage and autonomous acquisition of new
structure remain the principal capability gaps. Next: graph-level shard
count comparison under exact parity, followed by workload/frame expansion and
residual-driven thoughtexperiment iteration.

## 2026-10-06 TypedGraph shard-count replication

The graph-level follow-up confirms the isolated value-map effect reaches real
TypedGraph branch writes at 50,771 nodes: candidate settings 128/256/512 show
roughly 0.53–0.60, 0.30–0.37, and 0.18–0.29 of 64-shard write times across
set/insert/delete/retype. Exact-query sweeps and fixture RSS remain close at
that scale. At 7,164 nodes, the combined insert-plus-validation timing is
7.6× slower at 128 shards and 12× at 256, while 512 remains close to baseline.
This is repeated across three fresh workers. Since the timed region includes
an exact post-insert lookup, split those phases before attributing the cause;
the default remains 64. Next test representative closure and full-Substrate
workloads. This strengthens the evidence for COW as a useful large-graph
mechanism, but does not establish general task tractability, frame-locality
prevalence, or the ability to acquire novel structure. See [v7 results](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_SHARD_COUNT_V7_RESULTS_20261006.md).

## 2026-10-06 insert-phase follow-up

The v8 phase profile separates indexed insertion from post-insert validation.
The validation lookup is only 0.09–0.14µs, and isolated insertion at 7,164
nodes is 8.6–11.1µs across the shard counts. Thus lookup overhead does not
explain the v7 128/256 small-graph timing, which remains unreplicated under
this different operation schedule. At 50,771 nodes, indexed insert declines
from 41.8µs at 64 shards to 13.1µs at 512; the matched no-index control remains
around 3.2–3.5µs. This supports COW value-index copying as a large-graph cost
center but leaves the mixed mutation-schedule interaction and end-to-end
benefit open. Next replay v7 operation ordering with split phase timers, then
measure representative closure and full Substrate routes. See [v8 results](TYPED_GRAPH_INSERT_PHASE_PROFILE_V8_RESULTS_20261006.md).

## 2026-10-06 exact-schedule shard-insert replay

The v9 phase timers replay the v7 set-attribute/insert/delete/retype and
alternating shared/eager sequence. The 7,164-node 128/256 insert spike does
not reproduce: combined insertion plus lookup is 10.97–11.57µs, while exact
lookup is about 0.12µs. Treat the earlier spike as non-replicating rather than
as a stable small-graph shard penalty. The 50,771-node shard-count write trend
continues to reproduce, but remains a graph microbenchmark. Next evaluate
candidate shard counts in counterbalanced rule closure and representative full
Substrate routes before changing the default. Natural workload prevalence and
novel structure acquisition remain open. See [v9 results](TYPED_GRAPH_COW_INSERT_SCHEDULE_PROFILE_V9_RESULTS_20261006.md).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-06 GenInfer validator fusion

An experimental feature-gated candidate validator combines structural validation and slot comparison within one traversal. Its source-matched comparison passes exact semantic projections and search-counter reconciliation across 88 fresh workers, spanning fullseed positive/held-out/corrupt cases and linear/coupled-quadratic induction fixtures. Audit-on GenInfer term units remain exactly equal between arms. Audit-off fullseed training medians are centered at parity (fused/baseline 0.997 across 18 cells, range 0.692–1.044); this spread does not support a reliable performance claim. The feature stays disabled by default. The result supports implementation parity but does not improve tractability evidence. Next profile candidate work by phase and rejection point, and only continue with an optimization candidate that predicts a measurable reduction in direct work. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-06 exact full-rank GenInfer feasibility

The native kernel still limits GenInfer to exactly two coordinate dimensions. A new exact-rational algorithm probe recovers authored full-rank maps in 1-D, 2-D, and 3-D, verifies all training and held-out points, and abstains on repeated-origin rank-deficient inputs. Its independent analyzer passes all source, exact-output, rank-control, and candidate-count gates. At the 3-D B=4 fixture, exhaustive search would consider 387,420,488 matrices; the prototype solves the full-rank case in about 0.105 ms on this host. These are authored feasibility fixtures and Python prototype timing only. They do not show that the substrate currently has higher-dimensional GenInfer. Next integrate behind an opt-in feature and gate the actual native graph route with exact receipts, false-acquisition controls, and uninstrumented cost. Rank-deficient induction and natural full-rank prevalence remain open. See [results](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_MATRIX_SOLVER_FEASIBILITY_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_MATRIX_SOLVER_FEASIBILITY_V1_PREREG_20261006.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/geninfer_full_rank_matrix_solver_feasibility_v1.py), [independent analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_geninfer_full_rank_matrix_solver_feasibility_v1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_matrix_solver_feasibility_v1_20261006.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_matrix_solver_feasibility_v1_analysis_20261006.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-06 native full-rank GenInfer path

A feature-gated native solver now recovers uniquely identifiable one- and three-coordinate integer linear maps from identity-aligned training relations, then uses the existing candidate validator, graph minting, and re-decode path. In the paired source-matched 20-worker capture, 1-D/3-D positives acquired the oracle matrix and reused it on held-out observations; the 2-D path retained exact semantic parity; rank-deficient and nonlinear controls abstained. The default build does not enable the feature. The analysis passes after correcting two post-capture issues (an arm-selection bug in the analyzer and a held-out rank-deficient fixture that was actually full rank), so classify this as exploratory integration evidence, not preregistered confirmation. Audit-off 3-D training is about 1.55 ms with the solver versus 0.84 ms in the refusing baseline on one worker per arm; capability is added at additional cost. Next lock the corrected analyzer in a repeated prospective comparison, widen geometry/identity controls, and measure memory and scale. Higher-dimensional coverage above three, rank-deficient acquisition, and natural prevalence remain open. See [results](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_NATIVE_COMPARISON_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_NATIVE_COMPARISON_V1_PREREG_20261006.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/geninfer_full_rank_native_comparison_v1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_geninfer_full_rank_native_comparison_v1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_native_comparison_v1_20261006.json), [corrected analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_native_comparison_v1_analysis_20261006.json), and [initial failed analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_native_comparison_v1_analysis_initial_failure_20261006.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-06 confirmatory full-rank GenInfer replication

The source-matched, feature-off versus full-rank-solver replication was preregistered with its analyzer frozen before capture and passes all gates across 96 fresh workers. Three repetitions each cover 1-D reflection, the unchanged 2-D path, three distinct 3-D integer matrices, rank-deficient, nonlinear, and ambiguous-ID controls, each with audit on/off. The solver arm acquires every positive 1-D/3-D matrix and reuses it on held-out observations; the 2-D semantic projection matches between arms; all negative controls abstain in both phases and add no generator. Search-accounting conservation, build hashes, source stability, and complete worker coverage pass. Across three 3-D positive families, median audit-off training closure is 1.46 ms solver versus 0.65 ms feature-off. The feature remains opt-in. Source review finds O(N²) row alignment before exact candidate checking; next measure relation-count scaling, memory, and work phases, then compare a key-sorted join under exact semantic controls. This confirms a bounded native capability but not natural prevalence, dimensions above three, or underdetermined induction. See [results](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_NATIVE_COMPARISON_V1_1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_NATIVE_COMPARISON_V1_1_PREREG_20261006.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/geninfer_full_rank_native_comparison_v1_1.py), [analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_geninfer_full_rank_native_comparison_v1_1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_native_comparison_v1_1_20261006.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_native_comparison_v1_1_analysis_20261006.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-06 ordered identity-join implementation

The next GenInfer scaling gate has advanced from source diagnosis to an isolated implementation candidate. The new opt-in `geninfer_full_rank_sorted_identity_join` feature sorts identity keys and merges them while retaining the existing nested-search route as the comparison arm. Default and candidate feature builds both pass `cargo check`; no runtime or scaling claim is made yet. The full-rank solver remains opt-in. Next freeze the runner/analyzer/protocol, then measure exact positive/held-out parity, refusal controls, audit-off latency, alignment work, and memory over increasing relation counts. The broader programme remains active: this candidate addresses one concrete engine cost while learning prevalence, natural task utility, higher-dimensional induction, and general capability remain open.

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-06 GenInfer identity-join scale

The ordered identity-join candidate completed its preregistered 144-worker comparison at N=8/32/128. Semantic parity, matrix acquisition and held-out reuse, refusal controls, source/binary provenance, and audit-counter conservation all pass. End-to-end closure timing is effectively at parity in this range, with paired train changes varying around zero; process high-water RSS does not isolate join memory. Therefore keep the feature opt-in: the candidate changes source-level alignment work from quadratic target scans to sorting plus merge, but a practical speedup/crossover has not been demonstrated. Next measure direct comparison counts and extend relation-count scaling while retaining semantic gates. This is one bounded engine improvement in a broader substrate capability programme; natural-task tractability, prevalence, rung 3–4 learning, and general capability remain open. See [v1 results](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_SORTED_JOIN_SCALE_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_SORTED_JOIN_SCALE_V1_PREREG_20261006.md), [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/geninfer_full_rank_sorted_join_scale_v1.py), [frozen analyzer](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/analyze_geninfer_full_rank_sorted_join_scale_v1.py), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_sorted_join_scale_v1_20261006.json), and [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_sorted_join_scale_v1_analysis_20261006.json).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-06 comparison-count replication and order-N scope

V2.1's corrected confirmatory 240-worker analysis passes exact semantic/refusal, source/binary, and counter-conservation gates. On ascending-ID 3-D frames, measured train identity-key comparisons fall from `4N²` on nested scans to 40,944 at N=2,048 on sorted alignment. Because these fixtures supply keys in sort-friendly order and the audit build affects timing, shuffled-order work and clean larger-N timing remain needed before a general scaling or speed claim. The original v2 analyzer failure is preserved. Scope clarification: the tested quadratic within-frame map is outside `TimeInfer`'s representation; it is not evidence that the substrate lacks nonlinear trajectory extrapolation. The separate order-N path fits finite differences of rigid-translation offsets and has measured order-2/order-3 cases. Arbitrary within-frame nonlinear operator induction remains open. See [v2.1 results](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_SORTED_JOIN_SCALE_V2_1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [v2.1 protocol](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_SORTED_JOIN_SCALE_V2_1_PREREG_20261006.md), [v2.1 analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_sorted_join_scale_v2_1_analysis_20261006.json), and [Generic Order-N results](GENERIC_ORDER_N_RESULTS.md).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-06 shuffled scaling and clean timing

The follow-up closes both prior measurement gaps. V2.3 passes the shuffled-order 144-worker semantic/refusal cohort and measures 129.5× fewer identity-key field comparisons at N=2,048. A separate clean uninstrumented 72-worker cohort on the same shuffled 3-D workloads shows median paired train-closure reductions of 9.7%/13.6%/29.8% at N=128/512/2,048; at N=2,048 all six positive pairs favor sorted by 26.4–36.2%. This is a concrete scaling improvement for large authored identity-aligned frames. Keep it opt-in until natural task distribution, key types, and broader dimensions are measured. Scope correction: order-N trajectory extrapolation already works for polynomial offset streams over rigid translations (measured order-2/order-3); the `x*y` negative control in the matrix experiment tests within-frame nonlinear map induction and says nothing adverse about that trajectory capability. See [v2.3 results](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_SORTED_JOIN_SCALE_V2_3_RESULTS_20261006.md), [clean timing results](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_SORTED_JOIN_CLEAN_TIMING_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), and [order-N results](GENERIC_ORDER_N_RESULTS.md).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-06 identity-key semantic parity

The sorted-join fast path now falls back to the exact baseline identity scan whenever it cannot prove complete, unambiguous key correspondence. A source-locked six-worker comparison passes train and held-out status/receipt parity for integer-to-float equal IDs, float-only IDs, and boolean-to-integer equal IDs. The last case covers the mismatch between `Value` equality and `cmp_value` ordering; the fallback preserves the learned/reused transform. This is focused semantic evidence, not broad key-domain or prevalence evidence. NaN, rational, other compound keys, duplicate/missing IDs, key-width scaling, and natural-task traffic remain open. See [key-semantics results](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_SORTED_JOIN_KEY_SEMANTICS_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](GENINFER_FULL_RANK_SORTED_JOIN_KEY_SEMANTICS_V1_PREREG_20261006.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/geninfer_full_rank_sorted_join_key_semantics_v1_20261006.json), and [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/geninfer_full_rank_sorted_join_key_semantics_v1.py).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-06 live-runtime inference frequency pilot

Re-analysis of three audit-on replicates of the existing live-runtime capture finds one of two repeated prompt routes invokes `DimInfer` on every replicate; the other route invokes neither `DimInfer` nor `ShapeDecode`. Generic `GenInfer` is absent in both. The active route performs two `DimInfer` term evaluations, three `ShapeDecode` term evaluations, and 17,392 `ShapeDecode` term units per observation. This is a fixed two-route self-report slice, not evidence that induction is rare or frequent in natural task distributions, and the counters do not provide latency. The immediate measurement gap is a task-diverse current-runtime panel with per-request counter attribution. See [pilot results](LIVE_RUNTIME_DIM_INFER_FREQUENCY_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](LIVE_RUNTIME_DIM_INFER_FREQUENCY_V1_PREREG_20261006.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/live_runtime_dim_infer_frequency_v1_20261006.json), and [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_runtime_dim_infer_frequency_v1.py).

## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-06 task-diverse inference counter panel

The follow-up runs ten fresh current-source workers across five authored conversation tasks. All five reach `DimInfer`/`ShapeDecode`, while none execute generic `GenInfer`; scene description uses five inference-argument calls and 63,133 shape-decode term units per request, versus one call and 17,392 units for each other condition. Eight of ten task contracts pass with repeat parity. Compared with the prior panel, scene description now passes in both current-source workers where it previously failed in both; the other four outcomes match. The capture therefore shows current-source task capability change and task-dependent inference work, but it remains selected authored traffic, not a natural prevalence estimate or timing result. See [results](LIVE_CONVERSATION_TASK_INFERENCE_COUNTERS_V1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](LIVE_CONVERSATION_TASK_INFERENCE_COUNTERS_V1_PREREG_20261006.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/live_conversation_task_inference_counters_v1_20261006.json), and [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/live_conversation_task_inference_counters_v1.py).

The full-counter v1.1 recapture preserves every native global audit counter for the same ten fresh workers. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-06 expanded identity-key parity

The v1.1 source-locked differential expands key coverage to six paired cases and 12 workers. Training and held-out status/receipt parity passes for mixed numeric, float, bool/int, unique compound, duplicate, and missing-key controls. The first three acquire and reuse transforms in both arms; the latter three preserve baseline refusal. ## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-06 changing-map extrapolation boundary

The corrected 15-worker `TimeInfer` v1.1 baseline passes its order-2 translation and random-refusal controls. It refuses changing linear, changing quadratic, and static quadratic map sequences in all three replicates, with no false minting. The quadratic-map query set takes about 46–47 ms across four direct Term fields on these small fixtures, showing a costly refusal path but not an asymptotic scaling law. The preserved v1 capture had a list-versus-tuple checker defect and is not counted as confirmatory evidence. This locates a boundary in `TimeInfer`; it does not test or rule out the separate `GenInfer` route or richer sequence-map learners. The next learning gate is a bounded changing-map hypothesis family with explicit grammar and work accounting. See [v1.1 results](TIMEINFER_CHANGING_MAP_EXTRAPOLATION_V1_1_RESULTS_20261006.md), [protocol](TIMEINFER_CHANGING_MAP_EXTRAPOLATION_V1_1_PREREG_20261006.md), [capture](../../../evaluation/reports/timeinfer_changing_map_extrapolation_v1_1_20261006.json), [analysis](../../../evaluation/reports/timeinfer_changing_map_extrapolation_v1_1_analysis_20261006.json), and [preserved v1 instrument-failure capture](../../../evaluation/reports/timeinfer_changing_map_extrapolation_v1_20261006.json).


## Overall status addendum — 2026-10-06 quadratic learner identifiability preflight

A bounded 2-D quadratic induction design is now concrete, but remains unimplemented. The exact-rational fixture audit finds the prior coupled-quadratic training geometry rank 4 in the five-feature basis because x is restricted to 0/1 and therefore x² aliases x. The revised training design has rank 5 and determinant 4, uniquely recovers both coefficient vectors, transfers exactly to six disjoint points, and has no affine integer 2×2 fit with entries bounded by 8. This removes a fixture-identifiability confound for the next learning gate; it is not evidence that the engine has acquired a nonlinear operator. The next step is a feature-gated native solver plus a paired source-locked evaluation with rank, identity, corruption, transfer, and work-accounting controls. See [design](SUBSTRATE_NONLINEAR_DIMENSION_INDUCTION_V2_DESIGN_20261006.md), [preflight](../../../evaluation/reports/nonlinear_quadratic_basis_preflight_v1_20261006.json), and [runner](../../../evaluation/substrate_capability/nonlinear_quadratic_basis_preflight_v1.py).
